### [1] [Electric-magnetic asymmetry of exact dyonic Euler-Heisenberg black holes: duality-blind shadows and magnetically controlled horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18694)
*Vahideh Memari,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how Euler-Heisenberg corrections break electric-magnetic duality in dyonic black holes, finding that exterior observables (shadow, ISCO, Lyapunov exponent) are nearly insensitive to the breaking—deviations are ~ppm—while interior structure (horizon existence, extremality) is strongly charge-asymmetric.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which physical properties of dyonic black holes retain memory of the electric-magnetic duality breaking induced by Euler-Heisenberg vacuum polarization.

Method: Analytic perturbative expansion of the Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg equations for dyonic Reissner-Nordström black holes; computation of metric corrections up to second order in the Euler-Heisenberg coupling; analysis of horizon structure, extremality condition via mass-function derivative; numerical validation via two independent integrations; comparison of predicted black hole shadow with EHT Sgr A* image.

Result: Metric function remains even under q ↔ g exchange to first order in coupling; first odd correction appears at second order and decays as r⁻¹⁰; shadow/ISCO/Lyapunov shifts < 10⁻⁶; magnetic charge always enforces a horizon (singularity repulsion), while sufficiently light electric configurations can be horizonless; extremality is governed by max(∂M/∂r), whose critical curve intersects electric axis at origin and magnetic axis at a closed-form value.

Conclusion: Electric-magnetic duality breaking is hidden in asymptotic observables but fundamentally reshapes causal and singularity structure—horizon formation and extremality are intrinsically asymmetric, revealing the physical imprint of quantum vacuum nonlinearity.

Abstract: Maxwell theory is invariant under electric-magnetic rotations, and the Reissner-Nordström black hole depends on its charges only through the sum of their squares. The Euler-Heisenberg vacuum-polarization terms break this symmetry, and an exact dyonic Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg black hole was found recently. We ask which properties remember the breaking. Outside the photon sphere, almost none do. The metric function is even under charge exchange through first order in the coupling, the first odd term appears at second order and falls off as the tenth inverse power of the radius, and the shifts it causes in the shadow, the Lyapunov exponent, and the innermost stable circular orbit stay at a few parts per million. The core behaves differently. Any magnetic charge drives the metric function to negative infinity at the center and so forces a horizon, while an electric configuration lighter than its field energy can be horizonless. Whether an extremal limit exists is decided by one function of the charges, the maximum of the derivative of the mass function, whose critical curve reaches the origin along the electric axis and meets the magnetic axis at a closed-form value. Two independent integrations confirm every number, and the shadow is tested against the Event Horizon Telescope image of Sagittarius A*.

</details>


### [2] [Transient Information in Moving-Mirror Quantum Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18695)
*M. Baran Ökten,Ash S. Turan,Michael R. R. Good,Kuan-Nan Lin,Pisin Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how transient dynamical history is encoded in quantum field correlations after a moving mirror returns to a thermal trajectory, revealing sign-sensitive interference effects in finite-bandwidth wave packets due to an even–odd grading induced by time-reversed Carlitz–Willey ray maps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite dynamical history persists in quantum field observables—specifically correlation signatures—even when asymptotic thermality (same Hawking temperature) is restored, probing information retention beyond equilibrium.

Method: Analyzes two compact deformations of the Carlitz–Willey ray map related by reversal of a control parameter; employs Bogoliubov transformation, peeling field decomposition, complex continuum covariance analysis, and linear/nonlinear response theory for finite-bandwidth wave packets (including Rindler/Milne and right-moving types) with varied window functions.

Result: Identifies exact even–odd grading in field and Bogoliubov coefficients; shows first-order covariance retains transient sign, while monochromatic quadratic response is even; derives bandwidth scaling laws: quartic (right-moving) and cubic (Rindler/Milne) for signed squared correlation; finds linear bandwidth dependence of cross-covariance phase, maximized by flat window; reveals finite-amplitude corrections add linear term to Rindler/Milne response.

Conclusion: Transient mirror dynamics imprint robust, sign-resolved interference structures in quantum field correlations that are detectable via finite-bandwidth measurements—demonstrating nontrivial information encoding despite asymptotic thermal equivalence.

Abstract: We study how a finite dynamical history remains encoded in a quantum field after a moving mirror returns to a common thermal trajectory. Two compact deformations of the Carlitz--Willey ray map are related by reversal of a control parameter and share the same asymptotic null line and Hawking temperature. The pairing induces an exact even--odd grading of the peeling field and Bogoliubov transformation. The complex continuum covariance retains the transient sign at first order, while the leading monochromatic quadratic response is even under reversal. Finite-bandwidth wave packets convert this distinction into signed interference with the thermal reference covariance. In linear response, the signed squared correlation is quartic in bandwidth for the selected right-moving packets and cubic for matched Rindler/Milne packets. The relative cross-covariance phase has a linear bandwidth dependence, with its leading coefficient maximized in magnitude by the flat window among normalized real symmetric windows. Finite-amplitude corrections introduce an additional linear bandwidth term in the squared Rindler/Milne response. The distinguishing packets sample the transient history in retarded time, while both trajectories share the same asymptotic Hawking temperature.

</details>


### [3] [Kalb-Ramond black bounces: throat-blind shadows and throat-sensitive neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18697)
*Faizuddin Ahmed,Ahmad Al-Badawi,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates observational signatures of a regularized black hole core (a 'bounce') in the Simpson-Visser model derived from a Lorentz-violating Kalb-Ramond background; it identifies which astrophysical observables are sensitive or insensitive to the throat geometry, derives universal scaling laws for subring demagnification and neutrino phase shifts, and demonstrates via ray-tracing and independent calculations that photon and neutrino lensing jointly constrain both mass and throat size.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable signatures that can distinguish a regular black hole with a nonsingular bounce (core) from a classical black hole, thereby providing empirical tests for quantum gravity-inspired spacetime regularization.

Method: Analytical derivation of observables (shadow, ISCO, photon ring instability, neutrino oscillation phase, neutrino pair annihilation energy) in the Simpson-Visser black bounce metric; exact geometric optics analysis for subring demagnification; weak-field expansion for light and neutrino deflection; polarized ray-tracing of a turbulent thin accretion disk; cross-verification via two independent computational methods.

Result: Shadow, ISCO, orbital frequencies, and gravitational redshift are insensitive to the bounce; photon ring instability, subring brightness, neutrino oscillation phase, and annihilation energy deposition scale universally with throat size; an exact subring demagnification law is derived; combined photon–neutrino lensing breaks degeneracy between mass and throat radius; ray-tracing confirms first subring reveals the throat while direct image hides it; neutrino flavor survival and energy deposition are modified near stellar-mass objects.

Conclusion: The throat of a regular black hole is not observationally silent: it imprints universal, measurable signatures in high-resolution interferometric substructures (photon rings) and multi-messenger neutrino phenomena; joint analysis of photons and neutrinos enables model-independent extraction of throat geometry.

Abstract: Regularizing a black hole removes its singularity, but which observations could detect the regularized core? We address this question for a Simpson-Visser black bounce built on the power-law hairy black hole that a Lorentz-violating Kalb-Ramond background produces. Measured in the areal radius, the bounce leaves the metric function untouched and changes only the radial measure. This single fact sorts the observables into two classes. The shadow, the innermost stable orbit, orbital frequencies and gravitational redshifts are blind to the throat. The instability of the photon ring, the brightness of its subrings, the oscillation phase of neutrinos and the energy deposited by neutrino pair annihilation respond to it through one universal factor. We derive an exact law for the subring demagnification that holds for any metric function. We also show that the throat enters the weak-field neutrino phase and the light deflection with different weights, so that combined photon and neutrino lensing can separate the mass from the throat. Ray-traced polarized images of a turbulent thin disk confirm that the direct image hides the throat while the first subring reveals it. For neutrinos emitted near a stellar-mass compact object the throat reshapes the flavor survival pattern and enhances the annihilation energy deposition. Two independent computations reproduce each headline number.

</details>


### [4] [Metric perturbations in Einstein-Hilbert gravity: An alternative perturbative formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18698)
*Sergey Bondarenko,Raghvendra Singh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares exponential and inverse-metric-density variable formulations of the Einstein–Hilbert action in 4D, deriving gauge-fixed quadratic operators on arbitrary backgrounds; it shows their equivalence at linearized and tree-level (e.g., 3-point amplitude) levels, confirms consistency on Schwarzschild spacetime, and clarifies quantum prerequisites.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in field-variable dependence of gauge-fixed gravitational actions—particularly how background choice, gauge condition, and field redefinition affect operator structure, determinant contributions, and physical observables such as scattering amplitudes and classical solutions.

Method: Derives quadratic and cubic gauge-fixed operators for Einstein gravity using exponential (h_{μν} = g_{μν} − η_{μν}) and inverse-metric-density (g̃^{μν} = √−g g^{μν}) variables; employs background-harmonic gauge transported via exact field map; computes Faddeev–Popov operator and functional determinants; evaluates on-flat-background cubic interactions and Schwarzschild solution with full boundary-bulk consistency checks.

Result: The density-variable quadratic operator is the trace-reversed Lichnerowicz operator; the exponential one includes an extra Ricci-curvature term vanishing on Ricci-flat/Einstein backgrounds; both yield identical three-point amplitudes (up to free-equation terms); Schwarzschild satisfies the gauge and is stationary under unrestricted variations; boundary and bulk terms match in both variables.

Conclusion: Exponential and inverse-metric-density variables yield physically equivalent classical and tree-level predictions when consistently gauge-fixed and regulated; quantum equivalence requires matching measures, regulators, and source prescriptions beyond operator-level agreement.

Abstract: We derive the ordinary gauge-fixed quadratic operators of the four-dimensional Einstein--Hilbert action in exponential and inverse-metric-density variables on an arbitrary reference background. With a single nonlinear background-harmonic gauge transported through the exact field map, the density operator is the trace-reversed Lichnerowicz operator, while the exponential operator contains an additional algebraic Ricci-curvature term. This difference arises from the nonlinear transformation of the background tadpole and vanishes on Ricci-flat backgrounds; a cosmological-constant check gives the corresponding cancellation at Einstein saddles. The construction retains the exact determinant contribution and specifies the associated Faddeev--Popov operator. At the first interacting order on a flat background, we show that the two gauge-fixed cubic actions differ by a term proportional to the free field equation, and independently evaluate the same nonzero three-point coefficient in both variables, with its normalization matched to the Einstein-gravity interaction. An exact Schwarzschild configuration satisfies the same background-harmonic gauge and is stationary under unrestricted exterior variations of the gauge-fixed action. Its density and exponential reconstructions are checked together with the bulk and boundary terms; a separate radial variation illustrates the loss of an equation after imposing a restricted ansatz. These results provide an explicit comparison of established field coordinates at the classical and tree levels; quantum loop calculations additionally require a common measure, regulator and source prescription.

</details>


### [5] [Optical Appearances and Multiple Lensed Rings of Self-Interacting Boson Stars with a Double-Well Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18700)
*Zefang Fan,Sen Guo,Xianggao Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the optical appearance and lensing features of self-interacting boson stars—horizonless black hole mimickers—as probes of dark matter microphysics, using a novel hybrid metric reconstruction method for high-precision ray-tracing; it finds a non-monotonic shadow-size dependence on scalar field amplitude due to competing self-interactions, establishes existence bounds for static solutions, and shows that VLBI observations can constrain scalar potential parameters (Λ, Γ).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable strong-field gravity tests and probe dark matter microphysics by characterizing observable signatures—especially shadows and photon rings—of self-interacting boson stars, which are horizonless, gravitationally compact alternatives to black holes.

Method: Developed a robust hybrid metric reconstruction: an interior 6-parameter Padé approximant matched via a globally C²-continuous cubic spline to the exact exterior Schwarzschild metric, enabling stable, high-precision null geodesic integration; performed systematic ray-tracing of thin-disk emission through this spacetime for a double-well scalar potential; scanned parameter space (φ₀, Λ, Γ) to identify solution existence bounds and shadow morphology trends.

Result: Identified strict upper bound φ₀ ≲ 0.317 for regular static boson star solutions; discovered non-monotonic shadow size evolution driven by macroscopic mass-amplitude turnover from microphysical attractive/repulsive self-interaction competition; observed prominent nested higher-order photon rings and separated secondary images in most compact configurations; revealed diagonal gradient in shadow size across (Λ, Γ) parameter space.

Conclusion: Next-generation VLBI observations—not only can distinguish boson stars from black holes, but also geometrically constrain the underlying scalar field potential parameters, thereby providing direct empirical access to dark matter self-interaction physics.

Abstract: As horizon-scale imaging opens a new era of strong-field gravity tests, self-interacting boson stars have emerged as compelling dark matter candidates and natural black hole mimickers. We systematically investigate the optical appearances and multiple lensed rings of such horizonless objects illuminated by a geometrically thin accretion disk. To overcome the severe numerical stiffness caused by the slowly decaying scalar tail, we introduce a robust hybrid metric reconstruction framework---seamlessly bridging an interior 6-parameter Padé approximant to the exact exterior Schwarzschild spacetime via a globally $C^2$-continuous cubic spline. This ensures high-precision ray-tracing across the entire domain. For the adopted double-well potential, we identify a strict upper bound ($\phi_0 \lesssim 0.317$) for the existence of regular static solutions. Crucially, we uncover a striking non-monotonic evolution in the shadow size, driven by a macroscopic mass-amplitude turnover that is fundamentally rooted in the microphysical competition between attractive and repulsive self-interactions. Within the most compact configurations, extreme gravitational lensing generates prominent nested higher-order photon rings and distinctly separated secondary images. Furthermore, exploring the scalar potential parameter space ($\Lambda, \Gamma$) reveals a distinct diagonal gradient in the shadow size. Our findings demonstrate that next-generation VLBI observations can move beyond merely identifying horizonless objects, offering a novel geometric avenue to directly probe the microphysical couplings of dark matter scalar fields.

</details>


### [6] [Fourier Transform of the Scalar Propagator with Quantum Light-Cone Fluctuations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18703)
*M. V. Takook*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the Lorentzian Fourier transform of the Gaussian-smeared light-cone part of the scalar propagator in Krein-space quantization, expresses it via a Meijer $G$-function, analyzes its distributional nature, reconstructs the massive propagator by incorporating a Bessel-function tail, and obtains an exact massless one-loop bubble kernel using position-space methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve infrared and ultraviolet divergences in quantum field theory by employing Krein-space quantization—a framework allowing indefinite metric Hilbert spaces—and to provide mathematically rigorous, finite expressions for propagators and loop kernels without ad hoc regularization.

Method: Analytic continuation and Lorentzian Fourier analysis; distributional characterization of smeared light-cone contributions; use of Meijer $G$-functions for closed-form representation; addition of Bessel-function tail to recover massive propagator; position-space evaluation of the massless one-loop bubble diagram.

Result: Exact Meijer $G$-function representation of the Lorentzian Fourier transform of the Gaussian-smeared light-cone contribution; full distributional characterization; reconstruction of the massive scalar propagator; exact closed-form expression for the massless one-loop bubble kernel in position space.

Conclusion: Krein-space quantization with Gaussian smearing yields finite, analytically tractable expressions for fundamental QFT objects—propagators and loop kernels—without conventional regularization, supporting its viability as a divergence-free quantization framework.

Abstract: We derive the Lorentzian Fourier transform of the Gaussian-smeared light-cone contribution to the scalar propagator in Krein-space quantization in terms of a Meijer $G$-function. We characterize its distributional structure, recover the massive propagator by adding the Bessel-function tail, and derive an exact expression for the massless one-loop bubble kernel using a position-space prescription.

</details>


### [7] [Thermodynamics and interior structure of density-responsive regular black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18705)
*Martin Drobczyk*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates regular black holes in density-responsive gravity (DRG), deriving an effective two-horizon metric family from a Gaussian matter profile mapped via a local running prescription; it calibrates extremal mass, analyzes nonmonotonic Hawking temperature, verifies universal near-extremal lapse scaling ∝(M−M_min)^(−1/2), computes cavity and entropy diagnostics, finds severe entropy loss at extremality, confirms de Sitter-like core but unresolved pressure mismatch, and reports bounded collapse configurations without full geometry formation—highlighting robust thermodynamic and scaling results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study regular black hole solutions motivated by the scalar sector of density-responsive gravity (DRG), addressing whether phenomenologically constructed matter profiles yield consistent static solutions and what thermodynamic and geometric features emerge—particularly near extremality—within a framework aiming to resolve singularities while preserving physical consistency.

Method: Algebraic mapping of Gaussian matter profile to a diagnostic density profile ρ(r)=ρ₀exp[−(r/r_c)ⁿ] via local running; pressure consistency check against full static DRG equations; extremal mass calibration using scalar response amplitude A; numerical fitting for exponent n; computation of Hawking temperature, static lapse Z_tr, coordinate-time cavity integral between horizons; area-entropy analysis across mass evolution; inverse source reconstruction for core geometry; exploratory dynamical collapse with imposed bounce factor.

Result: The diagnostic density profile fails the full DRG pressure equation, yielding only an effective two-horizon metric family; extremal mass calibrated to M_min ≈ 1.006 M_P; n=2 best fits ρ(r); Hawking temperature peaks at T_H^max ≈ 0.02547 M_P then vanishes at M_min; universal near-extremal scaling Z_tr ∝ (M−M_min)^(−1/2) confirmed for n=2,3,4,6 and Hayward/Bardeen controls; static cavity integral is geometric, not gravitational-wave echo prediction; entropy loss crosses 70.7% of initial BH entropy at M_cross/M_0 = 1/√2; endpoint area entropy drops to <2.6% (M_0=5M_P) or <0.03% (M_0=50M_P) of initial Bekenstein-Hawking entropy; inverse reconstruction confirms isotropic de Sitter core but pressure mismatch persists; bounce-imposed collapse yields bounded configurations with 1−2m/r<0 but no formation of calibrated geometry.

Conclusion: The DRG-motivated regular black hole model yields robust thermodynamic behavior—including calibrated extremal mass, nonmonotonic temperature, and universal near-extremal lapse scaling—but does not satisfy the full static field equations, lacks unitary Page-curve behavior, suffers drastic entropy loss, and fails to dynamically realize the calibrated geometry; thus, it serves as a phenomenological probe rather than a self-consistent solution of DRG.

Abstract: This work studies regular black holes motivated by the scalar sector of density-responsive gravity (DRG). An algebraic prescription with phenomenological local running maps a Gaussian matter profile to a diagnostic density profile. A pressure check shows that this profile does not solve the full static DRG equations. It serves only to select an effective metric family with two horizons. The extremal mass is calibrated to $M_{\min} = \sqrt{1+A/2}\,M_{\mathrm{P}} \simeq 1.006\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$, where $A=0.024$ is the historical scalar response amplitude. A fit to $\rho(r) = \rho_0\exp[-(r/r_c)^n]$ selects $n=2$. The temperature is nonmonotonic, reaching $T_H^{\max}=0.02547\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$ at $M=1.332\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$ before vanishing at $M_{\min}$. The calculation verifies the generic scaling near extremality $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{tr}}\propto(M-M_{\min})^{-1/2}$ of a static lapse diagnostic for $n=2,3,4,6$ and for the Hayward and Bardeen controls. A regulated integral of coordinate time between the horizons provides a static cavity diagnostic, not a prediction of echoes of gravitational waves. The remaining area entropy equals the area entropy already lost at $M_{\mathrm{cross}}/M_0=0.707107\simeq1/\sqrt{2}$. This geometric crossing does not establish a Page curve or unitarity. The endpoint area entropy is below $2.6\%$ of the initial Bekenstein-Hawking entropy for $M_0 = 5\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$ and below $0.03\%$ for $M_0 = 50\,M_{\mathrm{P}}$. An inverse source reconstruction confirms an isotropic core of de Sitter type but retains the pressure mismatch. An exploratory collapse calculation with an imposed bounce factor produces bounded configurations with $1-2m/r<0$ without establishing formation of the calibrated geometry. The robust results are the calibrated thermodynamics and generic scaling near extremality.

</details>


### [8] [Finite Energy, Event Horizons, and a Universal Bound on the Singularity Order in Born--Infeld-type Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18711)
*Tengyang Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies an interpolated Maxwell--Born--Infeld theory, showing finite electrostatic energy, constructing a smoothly interpolating charged black hole solution between Born--Infeld and Reissner–Nordström cases, analyzing horizon structure, and proving a universal lower bound on curvature divergence that rules out regular electric black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge Maxwell and Born--Infeld electrodynamics while preserving finiteness of energy and exploring singularity resolution in black hole solutions.

Method: Analytical construction of electrostatic solutions and black hole metrics; classification of event horizon existence/multiplicity for truncated nonlinear electrodynamics; curvature analysis (Kretschmann scalar) for generic Born--Infeld-type Lagrangians.

Result: Finite total electrostatic energy bounded uniformly across interpolation; monotonic interpolation of black hole solutions; singularity avoidance independent of interpolation parameters; classification of horizon configurations; universal lower bound on Kretschmann divergence order implying unavoidable curvature singularities.

Conclusion: No regular electric black holes exist in any Born--Infeld-type nonlinear electrodynamics satisfying minimal physical constraints—curvature singularities are generic and robust under interpolation.

Abstract: We investigate an interpolated Maxwell--Born--Infeld theory bridging the Maxwell and Born--Infeld limits. In the electrostatic sector the total energy is finite, and remains bounded above even as the theory approaches the Maxwell limit. We construct the electrically charged black hole, whose black hole solution interpolates monotonically between the classical Born--Infeld and Reissner--Nordström solutions; singularity can be relegated, independently of the weight parameters. For general interpolated theories with a truncation threshold, we analyze the existence and multiplicity of event horizons and classify the horizon configurations, and for general Born--Infeld-type nonlinear structures we prove a universal lower bound on the order of the Kretschmann divergence, thereby excluding regular electric black holes.

</details>


### [9] [Resonance Induced Chaos of Charged Particles Around a Magnetized, Periodically Time-Conformal Reissner-Nordstrom Black Hole with Quintessence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18712)
*Israr Ali Khan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies chaos onset in charged test particle motion around a periodically time-conformally deformed Reissner–Nordström black hole surrounded by quintessence, identifying nonlinear resonance—governed by an approximate Noether symmetry—as the primary mechanism; magnetic field $B$ induces chaos near resonant values and suppresses it at higher strengths, while quintessence mainly lowers potential barriers to enable escape.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how slow astrophysical evolution (e.g., accretion, mergers, gravitational radiation) affects integrability and triggers chaos in black hole spacetimes, using a controlled time-dependent deformation of a static, analytically tractable background.

Method: Models a time-conformal periodic deformation $\Omega(t)=1+\epsilon\sin(\omega_d t)$ of a Reissner–Nordström–quintessence black hole; treats charged particle dynamics as a two-degree-of-freedom non-autonomous Hamiltonian system; solves equations numerically with adaptive high-order Runge–Kutta; diagnoses chaos via Poincaré sections, bifurcation diagrams, largest Lyapunov exponent, and SALI; analyzes resonance structure using perturbative Noether symmetry breaking.

Result: Chaos onset is governed by nonlinear resonance overlap—not parameter magnitude—with $\epsilon$ promoting chaos monotonically and $B$ acting non-monotonically (chaos peak near $B \sim 0.012$, suppression beyond); the approximate Noether symmetry predicts $B_c \simeq 0.011$ (vs. measured $0.012$) without free parameters, and fits $\epsilon$-threshold after fixing one coupling constant; quintessence reduces confining potential, enabling non-resonant escape.

Conclusion: The transition from regular to chaotic motion in slowly evolving black hole spacetimes is fundamentally tied to resonance-driven breakdown of an approximate continuous symmetry, not generic instability; magnetic field and conformal amplitude act via resonance mechanisms, whereas quintessence provides a distinct, non-resonant destabilization channel through barrier reduction.

Abstract: Astrophysical black holes are never strictly static: accretion, mergers, and gravitational radiation drive a slow evolution of their geometry. We model this evolution through a periodic time-conformal deformation, $\Omega(t)=1+\epsilon\sin(\omega_d t)$, of a Reissner--Nordström black hole surrounded by quintessence, and study the resulting onset of chaos for charged test particles. The deformation converts the integrable static spacetime into a periodically driven nonlinear Hamiltonian system, so that the transition from regular to chaotic motion can be related to the breakdown of an approximate Noether symmetry. The resulting two-degree-of-freedom Hamiltonian system is solved using an adaptive high-order Runge--Kutta integrator, while the dynamical behaviour is analysed through Poincaré sections, bifurcation diagrams, the largest Lyapunov exponent, and the Smaller Alignment Index (SALI). Our results show that the onset of chaos is controlled primarily by nonlinear resonance rather than by the magnitude of any individual parameter. Chaos grows with the conformal amplitude $\epsilon$ through resonance overlap, while the magnetic field $B$ acts non-monotonically: it produces chaos near the field strength at which the radial frequency matches the drive, and suppresses it at stronger fields by detuning. The small-divisor structure of the approximate Noether symmetry predicts the magnetic threshold without free parameters ($B_c\simeq0.011$ against the measured $0.012$), and reproduces the conformal threshold once a single coupling constant is fixed. In contrast, the quintessence parameter mainly facilitates particle escape by reducing the confining potential barrier, providing a distinct non-resonant route to the breakdown of regular motion.

</details>


### [10] [Holographic Einstein rings of a covariant quantum corrected AdS black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18716)
*Zhenglong Ban,Xiaomei Liu,Rong-Jia Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies holographic Einstein rings in a quantum-corrected Schwarzschild–AdS₄ spacetime using a massless scalar probe, showing how the deformation parameter ζ affects photon trajectories and quasinormal modes while disentangling finite-radius corrections from boundary time rescaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and understand the physical effects of quantum corrections—encoded in the deformation parameter ζ—on null geodesics (e.g., Einstein rings) and quasinormal modes in a covariantly corrected black hole background, distinguishing intrinsic spacetime geometry changes from boundary time normalization artifacts.

Method: Analyzes a covariant quantum-corrected Schwarzschild–AdS₄ metric; performs ray tracing and local WKB analysis of massless scalar propagation; computes quasinormal frequencies under AdS boundary conditions; compares coordinate impact parameters and incidence angles in normalized boundary frame; examines invariants (photon sphere radius, surface gravity) versus ζ-dependent quantities (photon potential maximum, lapse coefficient A).

Result: At fixed horizon radius r_h, ζ leaves metric mass, coordinate surface gravity, and photon sphere radius invariant, but increases photon potential maximum and asymptotic lapse A = 1 + ζ²; critical coordinate impact parameter → A⁻¹/² for large black holes; boundary-frame incidence angle → π/2; ray tracing and WKB yield consistent geometric scale; quasinormal spectra confirm separation of finite-r_h effects from boundary time rescaling.

Conclusion: The deformation parameter ζ introduces a clean, interpretable scaling of boundary time via A without altering local null geodesic structure at the photon sphere; holographic observables (Einstein rings, QNMs) thus allow unambiguous attribution of quantum corrections to either bulk geometry or boundary conformal frame.

Abstract: We investigate holographic Einstein rings in a covariant quantum corrected Schwarzschild--AdS$_4$ spacetime using a massless scalar probe. At fixed horizon radius $r_h$, the deformation parameter $\zeta$ leaves the metric mass parameter, coordinate surface gravity, and photon sphere radius unchanged, while increasing the photon potential maximum and the asymptotic lapse coefficient $A=1+\zeta^2$. The critical coordinate impact parameter approaches $A^{-1/2}$ for large black holes, whereas the corresponding incidence angle in the normalized boundary frame approaches $\pi/2$. Ray tracing and local WKB barrier data recover the same geometric scale. Quasinormal spectra computed with AdS boundary conditions provide a comparison at finite multipole number. These results separate the finite radius effect of the deformation from the change in boundary time normalization.

</details>


### [11] [Polarized equatorial emission around regular compact objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18862)
*Tsanimir Angelov,Rasim Bekir,Galin Gyulchev,Petya Nedkova,Stoytcho Yazadjiev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates polarization signatures in images of regular compact objects (e.g., non-singular black holes and particle-like spacetimes) using a magnetized fluid ring model, identifying a distinctive second direct image at high inclinations—absent in Schwarzschild black holes—with enhanced polarization intensity and unique orientation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable electromagnetic signatures that distinguish quantum-gravity-motivated regular compact objects from classical black holes in general relativity, particularly via polarized imaging.

Method: Phenomenological modeling of a magnetized fluid ring orbiting in the equatorial plane of static, spherically symmetric, non-singular spacetimes; ray-tracing-based computation of direct polarized images across parametric families including transitions to particle-like geometries; analysis of polarization patterns, intensity, and direction as functions of observer inclination.

Result: At high observer inclinations, particle-like regular compact objects produce a second direct image of the fluid ring—unseen in Schwarzschild black holes—with up to three orders of magnitude higher polarization intensity and a characteristic polarization direction; the physical origin is linked to specific light-bending and emission geometry enabled by the absence of an event horizon and modified near-core curvature.

Conclusion: Polarized direct imaging, especially at high inclinations, provides a viable observational discriminant between regular compact objects and GR black holes, with the second direct image serving as a robust, high-contrast signature tied to horizonless spacetime structure.

Abstract: We study the polarization properties of regular compact objects motivated by quantum gravity. These geometries include static spherically symmetric non-singular black holes which undergo a continuous transition to regular particle-like spacetimes in certain regions of the parametric space. Applying a phenomenological model of a magnetized fluid ring orbiting in the equatorial plane, we search for characteristic signatures in the direct polarized images which could distinguish them from black holes in general relativity. At low inclinations the regular compact objects lead to a very similar polarization pattern as the Schwarzschild black hole. At high inclinations we observe a characteristic signature for the particle-like geometries. A second direct image of the fluid ring is formed which is absent for black holes. It may possess with three orders of magnitude higher polarization intensity and a specific polarization direction making it observationally significant. We elucidate the physical mechanism for the formation of the second direct image and pinpoint the plasma configurations which lead to amplification of its polarized emission.

</details>


### [12] [Landauer erasure and horizon information in an expanding universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18879)
*Babak Vakili*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper links Landauer's principle to cosmological horizon thermodynamics in FRW universes, showing that the thermodynamic cost of erasing one bit decreases during standard expansion while horizon information content grows; it distinguishes Landauer heat from Misner–Sharp energy despite their thermodynamic relation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the thermodynamic cost of information erasure in an expanding universe by integrating Landauer's principle with apparent-horizon thermodynamics, and to clarify physical interpretations of gravitational energy versus information-theoretic heat.

Method: Applies Landauer's principle using the apparent-horizon temperature as the effective cosmological temperature within Friedmann–Robertson–Walker spacetime; computes minimum erasure heat per bit and horizon entropy; compares Landauer heat with Misner–Sharp energy via thermodynamic identities.

Result: For non-phantom FRW expansion: (1) minimum heat to erase one bit decreases over time; (2) horizon entropy (information capacity) increases; (3) Landauer heat and Misner–Sharp energy are thermodynamically related but physically distinct quantities.

Conclusion: Information erasure cost is dynamically tied to cosmic expansion and horizon thermodynamics; this establishes a concrete bridge between information theory, black-hole-like horizon physics, and gravitational energy in cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate the thermodynamic cost of information erasure in Friedmann--Robertson--Walker cosmology by combining Landauer's principle with the thermodynamics of the apparent horizon. Adopting the apparent-horizon temperature as an effective cosmological temperature, we find that for standard non-phantom expansion, the minimum heat required to erase one bit decreases while the entropy-equivalent information content of the horizon increases. We further compare the Landauer heat with the Misner--Sharp energy and show that, despite their simple thermodynamic relation, they have distinct physical meanings. These results provide a simple connection between information erasure, horizon thermodynamics, and gravitational energy in an expanding universe.

</details>


### [13] [Primordial Non-Gaussianity and Correlation-Function Perturbativity in mLQC-I: The Hybrid Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18899)
*Rui Pan,Jamal Saeed,Anzhong Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the tree-level bispectrum of scalar perturbations in modified loop quantum cosmology I (mLQC-I), assessing primordial non-Gaussianity under two initial-state prescriptions (bounce and remote de Sitter). It finds small corrections to the power spectrum (Ξ ≪ 1), yet large pointwise non-Gaussian amplitudes (f_NL ~ 10³–10⁴ or higher), organized by rapid oscillations in total momentum k_t, with CMB projection implying strong phase averaging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the perturbative consistency and observational viability of mLQC-I by quantifying primordial non-Gaussianity—specifically whether large f_NL can arise without spoiling the validity of linear perturbation theory (i.e., while keeping two-point corrections small).

Method: Constructs the cubic interaction Hamiltonian in the canonical quadratic framework; derives an exact finite-momentum in-in expression for the tree-level bispectrum; evaluates it for two initial states (Gaussian at the quantum bounce vs. de Sitter vacuum at a remote contracting surface); computes the relative nonlinear-conversion correction Ξ = |ΔP_R / P_R^(0)| and equilateral f_NL; analyzes oscillatory structure via k_t = k₁ + k₂ + k₃ and envelope behavior; performs CMB-projection diagnostics for phase averaging.

Result: For the bounce state: Ξ < 6×10⁻⁶ (conservative estimate); equilateral f_NL has RMS amplitude ~10³–10⁴. For the remote de Sitter state: Ξ drops from ~9.9×10⁻² to ~2.3×10⁻⁴ across scales; f_NL can be much larger. Bispectrum exhibits dominant rapid oscillations with Δk_t ≈ 5.7×10⁻⁴ m_P, modulated by a slowly varying triangle-shape envelope. CMB projection implies substantial phase averaging, suppressing observable NG despite large pointwise values.

Conclusion: Large pointwise primordial non-Gaussianity (f_NL ≫ 1) is compatible with perturbative consistency (Ξ ≪ 1) in mLQC-I; however, CMB observables are suppressed by phase averaging, rendering such large f_NL unobservable in standard CMB bispectrum estimators—highlighting a key distinction between bare quantum amplitudes and observable signatures.

Abstract: We investigate primordial non-Gaussianity and the perturbative consistency of scalar perturbations in modified loop quantum cosmology I (mLQC-I) within the hybrid approach. We construct the cubic interaction Hamiltonian in the canonical representation of the quadratic theory and derive an exact finite-momentum in-in expression for the tree-level bispectrum. We consider two initial-state prescriptions: a Gaussian state specified at the bounce and the de Sitter vacuum specified at a remote contracting surface. We assess the retained nonlinear-conversion correction through $\Xi=|\Delta P_R/P_R^{(0)}|$. For the bounce state, a conservative, cancellation-independent, finite-window absolute-integrand estimate is below $6\times10^{-6}$ at all five sampled momenta. For the remote de Sitter state, the signed correction is dominated by cancellations and decreases from $\Xi\simeq9.9\times10^{-2}$ at one tenth of the bounce-curvature scale to $\Xi\simeq2.3\times10^{-4}$ at that scale. Although the retained two-point corrections remain below unity, the bispectrum can have large pointwise amplitudes: the bounce state yields an equilateral $f_{\rm NL}$ with root-mean-square amplitude over oscillation phase of order $10^3$--$10^4$, while the remote state can yield much larger values. The dominant rapid oscillation is organized approximately by $k_t=k_1+k_2+k_3$, with $\Delta k_t\simeq5.7\times10^{-4}m_{\rm P}$, while the triangle-shape envelope varies slowly. For the illustrative bounce-to-present mapping adopted here, CMB-projection diagnostics indicate substantial phase averaging. Thus, large pointwise primordial non-Gaussianity can coexist with a small retained correction to the two-point function.

</details>


### [14] [Asymptotic stability of axisymmetric perturbations of near-horizon extremal Kerr with a Schwarzian-deformed throat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18922)
*Marcela Cárdenas,Benjamín Cuevas-Tapia,Claudio Muñoz,Jessica Trespalacios,Hanne Van Den Bosch*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes asymptotic stability of axisymmetric gravitational perturbations on deformed near-horizon extremal Kerr (NHEK) geometries, where the AdS₂ factor is modified by a Schwarzian mode; it links background deformations to the KdV hierarchy, establishes energy/virial-based stability results—including conserved energy for NHEK and Lyapunov behavior for nonconstant Schwarzians—and introduces a novel horizon-localized monotone norm distinct from Aretakis type, while computing Regge–Teitelboim charges at late-time throat corners.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand gravitational stability in the near-horizon regime of (near-)extremal Kerr black holes, particularly how deformations of the NHEK geometry—encoded via Schwarzian modes—affect stability properties and symmetry preservation.

Method: Uses Killing equation analysis with locality constraints to identify Gelfand–Dikii polynomials as natural solutions; constructs a hierarchy of backgrounds via the Korteweg–de Vries (KdV) equation; linearizes Einstein's equations around this family; reduces axisymmetric perturbations to a wave equation on a nearly-AdS₂ base; applies energy and virial methods for stability analysis; computes Regge–Teitelboim charges using radial ADM foliation.

Result: For NHEK: conserved energy, integrated local energy estimate, and asymptotic stability in a weighted norm. For nonconstant Schwarzian backgrounds: energy becomes a Lyapunov functional whose monotonicity depends on the sign of its derivative—yielding uniform bounds for integrable Schwarzians and mild local decay along KdV solitons. Introduces a new monotone horizon-localized norm not of Aretakis type. Computes Regge–Teitelboim charges at late-time corner of the throat.

Conclusion: Gravitational perturbations on Schwarzian-deformed NHEK backgrounds exhibit rich stability behavior governed by the KdV hierarchy; stability is intrinsically tied to the near-horizon structure encoded in the Schwarzian mode, and the standard Aretakis picture is generalized by a broader class of monotone norms sensitive to background deformation.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic stability of axisymmetric gravitational perturbations of the near-horizon geometries of (near-)extremal Kerr black holes and consider the family of backgrounds in which the AdS$_{2}$ factor of NHEK is deformed by a Schwarzian mode. Requiring the Killing parameter to be local in the background makes the Gelfand--Dikii polynomials natural solutions of the Killing equation, and that locality requirement brings stationary solutions of the Korteweg-de Vries equation. The hierarchy selects a family of backgrounds, which remain $\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})\times \mathfrak{u}(1)$ throughout them: the first level gives NHEK and near-NHEK, the second, solitons that depart from one of these geometries and return to it. We linearize Einstein's equations around this family, where the axisymmetric sector reduces to a wave equation on the nearly-AdS$_{2}$ base, and analyze this equation by energy and virial methods. For NHEK we obtain a conserved energy, an integrated local energy estimate and asymptotic stability in a weighted norm; for a nonconstant Schwarzian the energy becomes a Lyapunov functional whose monotonicity is dictated by the sign of its derivative, yielding uniform bounds for integrable ones, and mild local decay along the KdV soliton. We also find a monotone horizon-localized norm, which is not of Aretakis type, showing that stability properties are directly sensitive to the near-horizon structure encoded in the Schwarzian background. We compute the associated Regge--Teitelboim charges at a late-time corner of the throat using a radial ADM foliation of the spacetime.

</details>


### [15] [The fate of hidden symmetries under magnetic Harrison transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19087)
*Shoulong Li,Fangkang Yu,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes universal perturbative inheritance laws for conformal Killing tensors (CKTs) and massless scalar separability under magnetic Harrison transformations, showing that hidden symmetries survive perturbatively even when exact separability or Killing-Yano structures are lost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether and how hidden symmetries—such as those encoded in conformal Killing tensors (CKTs) and separability structures—are inherited by new solutions generated via solution-generating transformations, particularly magnetic Harrison transformations.

Method: Perturbative analysis of conformal Killing tensors (CKTs) and scalar field separability under magnetic Harrison transformations, applied to Carter-type (e.g., Kerr) and conformal Carter-type (e.g., Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson) seed geometries.

Result: Universal perturbative inheritance laws for CKTs and massless scalar separability are derived; magnetized Kerr retains scalar separability perturbatively despite losing exact Carter separability, and conformal Killing-Yano structures are obstructed at the same order.

Conclusion: Hidden symmetries exhibit a hierarchy of perturbative survival: CKTs and scalar separability persist under Harrison transformations while exact Carter separability and conformal Killing-Yano structures fail; this provides a general framework for analyzing symmetry inheritance across solution-generating techniques.

Abstract: Solution-generating transformations construct new geometries from known seeds, raising the question of whether and to what extent the resulting solutions inherit the hidden symmetries underlying the seeds' integrability and separability. We have established universal perturbative inheritance laws for CKTs and massless scalar separability directly at the level of magnetic Harrison transformations, encompassing both magnetization of Carter-type seeds and demagnetization of conformal Carter-type seeds. In both magnetized Kerr and demagnetized Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson, this perturbative inheritance coexists with the loss of exact Carter-type separability, while seed-connected conformal Killing-Yano structures are already obstructed at the same perturbative order. These results reveal a hierarchy in the perturbative survival of hidden symmetries and provide a framework for investigating their inheritance in more general theoretical settings and under other solution-generating transformations.

</details>


### [16] [Drift of a Charged Particle in a Black Hole Magnetosphere: the Effect of Black Hole Spin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19098)
*Ye Shen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a covariant guiding-center approximation to study gravitational drift of charged particles in black hole magnetospheres, deriving a general drift formula that reduces to Newtonian gravitoelectromagnetic results in the weak-field limit and quantifying—via a defined 'drift rate'—the significant influence of black hole spin on particle drift in Kerr spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of a fully covariant formulation of the guiding-center approximation and gravitational drift for charged particles in strong-field relativistic regimes—particularly in black hole magnetospheres—motivates this work.

Method: A covariant guiding-center expansion is performed in curved spacetime; gravitoelectromagnetism is used to derive Newtonian drift analogs; a drift rate is defined and evaluated in Kerr geometry; consistency with weak-field/low-velocity limits is verified.

Result: A covariant drift formula is derived and shown to recover Newtonian gravitoelectromagnetic drift in the appropriate limit; the BH spin-induced drift is quantified via a dimensionless drift rate, revealing it can be comparable in magnitude to other drift drivers (e.g., electric or magnetic gradients) in extreme Kerr regimes.

Conclusion: Black hole spin significantly contributes to charged-particle drift in magnetospheres via frame-dragging effects, and a fully relativistic guiding-center framework is essential for accurate modeling in strong gravity—especially for astrophysical applications like jet formation or particle acceleration near rotating black holes.

Abstract: Sparse plasma immersed in a strong magnetic field is common in astrophysical scenarios, for which a collisionless description is more appropriate. The guiding-center approximation serves as an effective tool for describing the motion of charged particles in a strong magnetic field, revealing the drift, a sub-leading order motion that causes particles to deviate from the magnetic field lines. It is widely accepted that the charged particles drift under the induction of gravity in Newtonian framework. However, a covariant formulation of the guiding-center approximation, as well as the drift of a charged particle induced by strong gravitational field, has yet to be fully explored. As a preliminary study, we aim to analyze the drift of a single charged particle in a black hole magnetosphere via a covariant approach. Particularly, we focus on the effects of black hole spin. We first examine the drift caused by the rotation of a massive object in the Newtonian regime using the language of gravitoelectromagnetism. A covariant formula of drift is derived, which is confirmed to reduce to the Newtonian results in the weak gravitational field and low speed limit. The impact of BH spin on the drift is analyzed in detail in Kerr spacetime specifically, for which we define the drift rate whose value quantifies the significance of the drift. Our results indicate that the impact of BH spin on the drift of a charged particle in a BH magnetosphere can be comparable to that of other factors in some extreme cases.

</details>


### [17] [Chaotic motion of spinning particles in nonlocal gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19191)
*Jianhui Lin,Xiangdong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how spin-induced chaos in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals is affected by spacetime deformations in nonlocal gravity (NLG), finding that increasing the NLG deformation parameter ζ suppresses chaos—reducing chaotic trajectories from 37.5% to 0%—and that waveform mismatches between NLG and GR can be partially mitigated by adjusting initial conditions; however, chaos itself does not provide a clear advantage for distinguishing NLG from GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether spin-induced chaotic orbital dynamics in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals can serve as a discriminant between revised Deser--Woodard nonlocal gravity and general relativity, particularly through observable imprints in gravitational waveforms.

Method: Conservative test-body approximation in NLG spacetime; analysis via Poincaré sections, rotation curves, and fast Lyapunov indicators (FLIs); computation of approximate mass-quadrupole waveforms; quantitative mismatch comparison between NLG (varying ζ) and GR waveforms with adjusted initial conditions.

Result: Chaotic fraction decreases monotonically with ζ, vanishing at ζ = 0.1; FLI-classified chaotic trajectories exhibit higher spectral flatness in waveforms than regular ones; waveform mismatch between NLG (ζ = 10⁻⁶) and GR is reduced when initial radius is tuned to preserve dynamical classification; chaotic NLG waveforms retain larger minimum mismatches than regular ones, but sensitivity to initial conditions limits detectability.

Conclusion: Background-dependent orbital chaos is linked to radiation spectral features, yet chaos does not robustly enhance discrimination of NLG from GR—parameter degeneracies and initial-condition sensitivity undermine its utility as a smoking-gun signature.

Abstract: We study spin-induced chaos around a static, spherically symmetric black hole in revised Deser--Woodard nonlocal gravity (NLG), using the conservative test-body approximation relevant to extreme-mass-ratio inspirals. The deformation parameter $\zeta$ describes departures from Schwarzschild spacetime. Poincaré sections, rotation curves, and fast Lyapunov indicators (FLIs) reveal changes in resonant structures and a decreasing fraction of non-plunging trajectories classified as chaotic within the sampled family, from $37.5\%$ at $\zeta=0$ to zero at $\zeta=0.1$. The FLI classifications of selected trajectories are reflected in their approximate mass-quadrupole waveforms: neighboring chaotic and regular trajectories show a marked local spectral-flatness contrast, which weakens in backgrounds where both are classified as regular. To assess whether a transition from chaotic to regular motion caused by the background deformation helps distinguish NLG from GR, we compare NLG waveforms at $\zeta=10^{-6}$ with GR waveforms generated at different initial radii. For a trajectory whose classification changes between the backgrounds, this adjustment substantially reduces the mismatch. The selected NLG waveforms from chaotic orbits retain larger minimum mismatches than those from regular orbits, although sensitivity to initial conditions may also hinder signal recovery. These results connect background-dependent orbital dynamics with radiation spectra, but do not establish that chaos offers an advantage for identifying the background geometry.

</details>


### [18] [Numerical evidence for the validity of the inverse hoop conjecture for black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19240)
*Shahar Hod*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides evidence supporting the Inverse Hoop (IH) conjecture — that the irreducible mass of a black hole is bounded above by one-quarter of its smallest engulfing ring circumference divided by π — for non-stationary black-hole configurations, specifically verifying it for the recently constructed CGP solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the validity of the Inverse Hoop conjecture beyond stationary black holes to non-stationary configurations, addressing whether geometric wrinkling of horizons remains constrained dynamically.

Method: Analytical verification of the IH inequality ${\cal M}_{\text{irr}} \leq {\cal C}_{\text{s}}/4\pi$ on the family of non-stationary black-hole solutions recently constructed by Croft, García-Moreno, and Pani (CGP).

Result: All CGP non-stationary black-hole solutions satisfy the IH relation, providing concrete evidence for the conjecture's validity in dynamical settings.

Conclusion: The IH conjecture holds for the tested class of non-stationary black holes, suggesting its robustness beyond stationarity and reinforcing geometric constraints on horizon evolution.

Abstract: The inverse hoop (IH) conjecture, ${\cal M}_{\text{irr}}\leq {\cal C}_{\text{s}}/4\pi$, originally discussed in the context of stationary black-hole configurations, suggests that the surface area of a black hole cannot undergo unconstrained geometric wrinkling (here ${\cal M}_{\text{irr}}=\sqrt{{\cal A}/16\pi}$ is the irreducible mass associated with the horizon area ${\cal A}$ of the black hole and ${\cal C}_{\text{s}}$ is the circumference length of the smallest ring that can engulf the horizon of the black hole in every direction). In the present compact paper we provide evidence supporting the validity of the IH conjecture for non-stationary black-hole configurations. In particular, we show that all the non-stationary black-hole solutions recently constructed by Croft, García-Moreno, and Pani (CGP) [arXiv:2611.13266] satisfy the IH relation.

</details>


### [19] [Two-scale system: compactified ambient metrics and Scri/Spi/Ti](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19377)
*Xavier Bekaert,Maxim Grigoriev,Mikhail Markov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper unifies several geometric constructions—Fefferman-Graham ambient metric, Brinkmann spaces, and ultrastatic vacuum spacetimes—via a novel two-scale conformal framework built from weight-one scalars satisfying almost-Einstein equations; it yields a new ambient description of spatial (Spi) and temporal (Ti) infinity in Ricci-flat spacetimes, where Spi/Ti and null infinity appear as boundary face and corner of the ambient space, respectively, enabling unified asymptotic analysis of fields at all infinities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify disparate geometric constructions related to asymptotic structure of spacetimes—particularly Fefferman-Graham ambient metrics, Brinkmann spaces, and ultrastatic vacua—under a single, natural conformal-geometric framework that simultaneously captures spatial, temporal, and null infinity.

Method: Introduces a two-scale system consisting of two weight-one conformal scalars obeying almost-Einstein equations and regularity constraints; constructs conformal compactifications for the listed geometries; applies the framework to define an ambient space for Spi and Ti in Ricci-flat spacetimes, interpreting their geometry via boundary stratification (face vs. corner).

Result: A unified conformal-geometric framework embedding Fefferman-Graham, Brinkmann, and ultrastatic vacuum structures; a new ambient construction for Spi and Ti where spatial/temporal infinity is realized as a boundary face and null infinity as the corner; demonstration that the two-scale system serves as a background for analyzing asymptotics of gauge and matter fields at all three types of infinity.

Conclusion: The two-scale conformal framework provides a coherent, geometrically intrinsic unification of key asymptotic spacetime constructions, revealing Spi/Ti and null infinity as complementary boundary components of a single ambient space—enabling systematic, conformally invariant treatment of asymptotics across all infinities.

Abstract: We show that certain geometric constructions, including the Fefferman-Graham ambient metric, Brinkmann spaces, and ultrastatic vacuum spacetimes, admit natural conformal compactifications and can be unified within a single framework arising naturally from conformal geometry. The framework is based on a two-scale system comprising two weight-one conformal scalars satisfying almost-Einstein equations and certain regularity conditions. As a particular instance of this unifying framework, we obtain a new ambient construction for Spi and Ti, the extended spatial and temporal infinities of Ricci-flat spacetimes. A remarkable feature of this construction is that Spi/Ti and the null infinity of the corresponding spacetimes are naturally realised as a boundary face and the corner of the two-scale ambient space, respectively. We also demonstrate how the two-scale system can serve as a background ambient geometry for studying the asymptotic behaviour of (gauge) fields at spatial, temporal, and null infinity.

</details>


### [20] [A Stable Vector Bounce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19402)
*Nils A. Nilsson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a geodesically complete cosmological bounce in cubic generalized Proca theory with a canonical scalar field, ensuring stability of perturbations and adherence to the null energy condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize a nonsingular, geodesically complete cosmological bounce within a well-behaved effective field theory—specifically, cubic generalized Proca theory—while satisfying physical consistency conditions such as stability and energy conditions.

Method: The authors construct an exact background solution where a temporal vector field evolves along its algebraic constraint, enabling a smooth contraction-to-expansion transition; they analyze linear cosmological perturbations, examining kinetic coefficients, stability (via positivity of kinetic terms), and boundedness of solutions at infinite past/future times.

Result: A geodesically complete bounce is achieved; matter satisfies the null energy condition; derivative mixing is nonzero at the bounce; kinetic coefficients remain positive for all finite times and nonzero comoving wavenumbers; short- and long-wavelength perturbations are stable and bounded.

Conclusion: Cubic generalized Proca theory admits a physically viable, stable, and geodesically complete bouncing cosmology with a canonical scalar matter field, overcoming typical singularity and instability issues in early-universe models.

Abstract: We construct a geodesically complete bounce in cubic generalised Proca theory with a free canonical scalar as the matter field. The temporal vector evolves along its algebraic constraint and supports the transition from contraction to expansion while matter obeys the null energy condition. This evolution permits nonzero derivative mixing at the bounce. The kinetic coefficients are positive at every finite time for all nonzero comoving wavenumbers, and short-wavelength perturbations are stable throughout the solution. Following the linear equations to both infinite-time limits also shows that local perturbations are bounded for all nonzero comoving wavenumbers.

</details>


### [21] [Black holes in Myrzakulov $F(R,T)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19500)
*Alexandre Landry,Ratbay Myrzakulov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies vacuum black-hole solutions in metric-affine Myrzakulov F(R,T) gravity with torsion, deriving Einstein-like equations with effective cosmological constant under axial torsion; constructs smooth torsionful Schwarzschild-(A)dS solutions; classifies Ricci-symmetric connections; verifies field equations via independent curvature tests; identifies degeneracy in axial quadratic form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore viable vacuum black-hole geometries in metric-affine gravity where torsion contributes dynamically via a quadratic torsion invariant T (not the matter trace), extending beyond Riemannian GR and standard f(R) or f(T) frameworks.

Method: Variational principle applied to independent metric and affine connection in MG-I gravity; imposition of metric compatibility and totally antisymmetric (axial) torsion *after* variation; spherically symmetric ansatz; analysis of Ricci-symmetric connections via condition (fV−U)′=0; component-wise and coordinate-invariant verification of field equations; identification of null directions in axial quadratic form.

Result: Derivation of effective Einstein equations with Λ_*=γT_*^3/α on an exceptional torsion branch; local existence proof; explicit torsionful Schwarzschild-(anti-)de Sitter metrics smooth across a Killing horizon; classification of two distinct families of Ricci-symmetric connections yielding identical metric and T but differing affine curvature invariants; confirmation of field equation identities via curvature contractions and component tests; identification of degeneracy (not instability) in axial sector.

Conclusion: Vacuum black-hole solutions exist in this metric-affine F(R,T) framework with axial torsion, exhibiting GR-like behavior with emergent cosmological constant; geometric inequivalence of connections persists even when metric and torsion scalar coincide; results are local, with no claims about global structure, physical uniqueness of torsion profiles, or dynamical stability.

Abstract: We investigate vacuum black-hole solutions in metric-affine Myrzakulov $F(R,T)$ gravity (MG-I), where $R$ is the affine Ricci scalar and $T$ is a quadratic torsion invariant, not the trace of the matter energy-momentum tensor. For the polynomial model $F(R,T)=\alpha R+\beta T+\gamma T^3$, with $\alpha\neq0$, we first vary the metric and all components of the independent affine connection, and only then specialize to metric-compatible geometry with totally antisymmetric torsion. Nonzero axial torsion admits an exceptional branch $\alpha+\beta+3\gamma T_*^2=0$, on which the metric satisfies the Einstein equations with effective cosmological parameter $\Lambda_*=\gamma T_*^3/\alpha$. We establish a local existence result and construct torsionful Schwarzschild-(anti-)de Sitter representatives that are smooth through a selected future Killing horizon. Within the spherically symmetric axial ansatz, we characterize Ricci-symmetric affine connections by the condition $(fV-U)'=0$ and compare a constant-$W=fV-U$ family with a constant-$U$ family. Although these connections share the same metric and torsion scalar, their affine curvature invariants generally differ. Independent coordinate-level curvature contractions and component tests of the unrestricted metric and connection variations corroborate the identities used in the construction; these checks complement the analytic variational derivation. Finally, we identify null directions of the restricted axial quadratic form and distinguish this degeneracy from a complete metric-affine stability analysis. Our conclusions are local and do not establish global extendibility, physical inequivalence of all torsion profiles, or dynamical stability.

</details>


### [22] [Relativistic precession around rotating neutron stars. I. Slow rotation: Hartle--Thorne spacetimes, particle orbits and the QPOs of atoll and Z sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19502)
*A. C. Khunt*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies the Relativistic Precession Model (RPM) to QPOs in neutron star X-ray binaries using fully relativistic, Hartle–Thorne spacetimes (to second order in spin) for four realistic EOSs. It quantifies how neutron star quadrupole moments—2–4× larger than Kerr black holes at same spin—suppress nodal precession and constrain mass–spin–quadrupole inference from QPOs; RPM fits yield high masses (up to ~2.15 M⊙), rule out Kerr assumptions for moment-of-inertia estimation, and show horizontal-branch oscillations in Z sources are incompatible with 2×nodal precession under these EOSs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how realistic neutron star quadrupole moments—strongly dependent on equation of state (EOS) and significantly larger than Kerr black hole values—affect QPO modeling via the Relativistic Precession Model (RPM), and to determine whether observed QPO frequency ratios (especially 2:1) can be explained within physically viable neutron star spacetimes.

Method: Solved the Hartle–Thorne equations to second order in angular velocity for four nuclear EOSs (WFF1, G3, FSUGarnet, IOPB-I); computed exact (non-perturbative-in-spin/quadrupole) orbital and epicyclic frequencies in the exterior spacetime; modeled hot-spot emission on eccentric, tilted geodesics; fitted RPM predictions to QPOs in atoll sources (4U 1636−53, 4U 1728−34) and Z sources (GX 5−1, GX 340+0); performed Bayesian parameter estimation for mass, dimensionless spin j, and dimensionless quadrupole Q̄.

Result: Neutron star quadrupole moments at 1.9 M⊙ are 2–4× larger than Kerr BHs at same spin; nodal precession is suppressed by up to 25% near ISCO; RPM fits yield high NS masses (1.88–2.15 M⊙); for 4U 1728−34, low-frequency QPO favors large moment of inertia (~2.5×10⁴⁵ g cm²); without EOS, QPOs infer M = 1.920⁺⁰.⁰¹⁴₋₀.₀₁₂ M⊙, j = 0.162⁺⁰.⁰²³₋₀.₀₁₁, Q̄ = 2.2⁺¹.¹₋₀.₈; assuming Kerr spacetime underestimates I by 22%; 2×nodal precession is ruled out for HBOs in GX 5−1 and GX 340+0 under all four EOSs.

Conclusion: The quadrupole moment of realistic neutron stars strongly modifies RPM predictions compared to Kerr approximations; accurate QPO modeling requires EOS-informed spacetimes; observed QPOs in atoll sources are consistent with massive, rapidly rotating neutron stars, while Z-source HBOs cannot be explained as 2×nodal precession within standard EOS constraints—challenging RPM interpretations for those sources.

Abstract: We study the relativistic precession model (RPM) of quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) in the spacetimes of slowly rotating neutron stars computed from the equation of state (EOS). For four EOSs (WFF1, G3, FSUGarnet and IOPB-I) we solve the Hartle--Thorne equations to second order in the angular velocity and compute the orbital and epicyclic frequencies of the exterior metric without expanding in the spin or the quadrupole. At $1.9\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$ the spin-induced quadrupole is 2--4 times that of a black hole with the same spin; it lowers the nodal precession frequency by up to 25 per cent near the innermost stable circular orbit and limits the nodal frequency that any spin can produce. An eccentric, tilted geodesic around a $1.92\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$ star spinning at 363~Hz precesses at the epicyclic rates, and the power spectrum of a hot spot on it peaks at the orbital, periastron and nodal precession frequencies. Applied to the atoll sources 4U 1636$-$53 and 4U 1728$-$34, the RPM gives masses of 2.02--$2.15\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$ and 1.88--$1.92\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$. For 4U 1728$-$34 the low-frequency QPO favours stars with large moments of inertia, $I\simeq2.5\times10^{45}$~g\,cm$^{2}$. Without an EOS the QPOs give $M=1.920^{+0.014}_{-0.012}\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$, $j=0.162^{+0.023}_{-0.011}$ and $\bar Q=2.2^{+1.1}_{-0.8}$; a Kerr spacetime with the same nodal frequency would underestimate the moment of inertia by 22 per cent. For the Z sources GX 5$-$1 and GX 340+0 the horizontal-branch oscillations cannot be twice the nodal precession frequency for any of the four EOSs: the fits are driven to spins of 730--745~Hz and to the maximum mass.

</details>


### [23] [Scalar charge of neutron stars and accreting black holes in Starobinsky gravity: quasi-universal relations, the trace anomaly and gravitational-wave sensitivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19510)
*Yashmitha Kumaran,Ilídio Lopes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a master formula for the Starobinsky scalaron charge of static, spherically symmetric objects—neutron stars and accreting black holes—showing it depends on the stress–energy trace (ρ−3p), not vacuum geometry; for neutron stars, the charge is quasi-universal in compactness, correlates tightly with tidal deformability and moment of inertia, and remains stable under self-consistent Einstein-frame solutions; vacuum black holes carry zero charge, while disc-accreting ones acquire a finite charge governed by the disc’s outer radius; constraints from NICER, GW170817, and GW230529 are used to project sensitivity to scalaron mass (range) for next-gen detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the Starobinsky scalaron 'charge'—a gravitational scalar degree of freedom sourced by the stress–energy trace—for astrophysical compact objects, bridging theory (R + αR² gravity) with observables (tidal deformability, radii, gravitational waves) and distinguishing predictions from GR and other modified gravity models.

Method: Derives an exact master integral expression for the scalaron charge q in terms of the trace ρ−3p for arbitrary static, spherically symmetric sources; applies it to realistic neutron star interiors (6 EOSs, Eardley sensitivity formalism), computes self-consistent Einstein-frame stellar structure, evaluates q for black holes with thin accretion discs, and confronts results with NICER radius measurements, GW170817 Λ̃, and GW230529 dipole radiation bounds via Fisher analysis for Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer.

Result: Neutron star scalaron charge q is a quasi-universal function of compactness C = GM/(c²R); realistic EOSs lie ~0.007 above polytrope-calibrated fit but are well-refit; q tracks Λ̃ and I to ~0.1% and ~0.01%, respectively; self-consistent solutions alter q by <4% at fixed C; vacuum BH: q=0; disc-accreting BH: q = sinh(m_ϕ r_d)/(m_ϕ r_d), dominated by outer disc; light propagation insensitive to q at leading order; GW230529 dipole bound yields projected m_ϕ sensitivity ~10⁻²⁰–10⁻¹⁹ eV for ET/CE.

Conclusion: The Starobinsky scalaron charge is an observable, matter-sourced quantity—not geometric—enabling clean tests of R² gravity with multi-messenger data; its tight correlations with standard neutron star parameters make it a powerful probe of strong-field gravity, while its vanishing for vacuum BHs and controlled emergence in disc systems provides distinct signatures separable from alternative theories.

Abstract: The Starobinsky scalaron is sourced by the trace of the stress--energy, so its exterior charge is set by the matter around an object and not by the object's vacuum geometry. We derive a master formula for that charge for any static, spherically symmetric source, evaluate it first for neutron stars, where the interior is known, and then for accreting black holes, where it is not. For a star the pressure subtracts: on the interior metric with the Eardley sensitivity, and the charge is a quasi-universal function of the compactness. Six realistic equations of state confirm the relation but lie above the coefficient calibrated on polytropes; the refit reproduces their mean to 0.007. The trace $\rho-3p$ changes sign in the cores of massive stars, and the charge also tracks the tidal deformability and the moment of inertia to a few parts in 1000 and 100. Solving the Einstein-frame structure equations self-consistently moves a star along this relation and leaves its charge at fixed compactness within 4 per cent of the test-field value. We compare the charge with NICER radii and the GW170817 tidal deformability, and use the published massless dipole constraint from GW230529 to calibrate a Fisher estimate of the sensitivity of a similar binary to a finite scalaron range, projected to the Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer; the range is a free parameter, and the laboratory bound on pure $R+\alpha R^2$ is confronted explicitly. A vacuum black hole has q=0; a hole with a disc has $q=\sinh(m_\phi r_d)/(m_\phi r_d)$, controlled by the disc's outer edge, which makes it the counterpart in which the same integral is dominated by matter far from the centre; light is blind to the charge at leading order, and the correction beyond it is negligible for any self-consistent disc.

</details>

Abstract: We obtain a new static black string solution of Einstein's equations with a negative cosmological constant sourced by a Dymnikova-type anisotropic matter distribution. At large radial distances, the geometry approaches the Lemos black string, while the behavior near the central region is characterized by an effective curvature parameter. We examine the weak and strong energy conditions, the horizon structure, and the curvature invariants. The Ricci and Kretschmann scalars remain finite at the origin. We also investigate the Hawking temperature and heat capacity, with particular attention to the dependence of the thermodynamic behavior on the Dymnikova scale.

</details>
