<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore how topological complexity (multiple throats) arises from controlled geometric deformations in asymptotically conical wormhole spacetimes, and to identify consistent GR-compatible matter sources that support such configurations while characterizing their energy condition behavior and null geodesic structure.

Method: Analytic construction of a deformed areal-radius function; derivation of bifurcation curves in dimensionless parameter space; reconstruction of stress-energy via scalar fields (canonical + shift-symmetric phantom) and magnetic NED; pointwise and integrated evaluation of energy conditions (especially radial NEC); analysis of light rings via effective potential and radial stability criteria; computation of Nandi–Zhang–Kumar volume integral of (ρ + pᵣ).

Result: A single analytic family yields wormholes with 1–4 throats, bounded by saddle-node bifurcations; regular, asymptotically conical geometries; reconstructed source satisfying field equations; NED coupling resolves branch obstruction in uncoupled NED; leading asymptotic stress scales as r⁻² with string-cloud EoS; pointwise NEC satisfied in finite belly regions but violated at throats and in integrable tails; Nandi–Zhang–Kumar integral is quadratic in deformation amplitude and zero off bifurcation curves; each throat hosts an unstable light ring, each belly a stable one, with internal trapping in double-throat case.

Conclusion: The interplay between geometric deformation, source coupling, and asymptotic structure fully determines throat multiplicity, energy condition behavior, and circular null orbit stability—unifying topology, matter content, and geodesic dynamics within a coherent parameter-space framework for multi-throat wormholes.

Abstract: We study an ultrastatic, spherically symmetric family of traversable wormholes obtained by locally deforming a generalized Ellis--Bronnikov areal-radius profile in a spacetime with an asymptotic angular deficit. Depending on the deformation parameters, the same analytic family contains one, two, three, or four throats. The corresponding bifurcation curves are obtained in dimensionless form, and the resulting geometries are regular and asymptotically conical. We also reconstruct a source in General Relativity using a canonical scalar, a shift-symmetric phantom scalar, and a magnetic nonlinear electromagnetic field coupled to the canonical scalar. In the reference multi-throat geometry, the coupling removes a branch obstruction of an uncoupled single-valued nonlinear electrodynamics (NED); the same obstruction occurs whenever equal values of the magnetic invariant require different values of $\mathcal G_F$. For $\mathcal G(F)=F+\lambda\sqrt F$, the NED sector carries the leading asymptotic stress, which scales as $r^{-2}$ and approaches the string-cloud equation of state. The total stress tensor satisfies pointwise energy conditions in finite belly-centered regions of representative two- and four-throat solutions, while the throats violate the radial null energy condition (NEC) and the violation extends into integrable asymptotic tails. The Nandi--Zhang--Kumar covariant volume integral of $\rho+p_r$ is quadratic in the deformation amplitude; its zero is distinct from the bifurcation curves. Away from saddle-node boundaries, each throat supports a radially unstable background-metric light ring, and each intermediate belly supports a radially stable one, with an internally trapped null family in the double-throat case. These results place the topology, source reconstruction, integrated NEC content, and background-metric circular-orbit structure in a common parameter-space description.

</details>


### [26] [A hybrid log-likelihood-ratio statistic for single-detector SPIIR gravitational-wave searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19918)
*SPIIR Collaboration,Weichangfeng Guo,Sunil Choudhary,Victor Oloworaran,Linqing Wen,Jayden Doan,Qingyuan Liang,Tim Davies,Luke Davis,Irina Dvorkin,Apratim Ganguly,Shang-Jie Jin,JeongCho Kim,Yu-Chiung Lin,Ngai-Chun Lung,Alistair McLeod,Khun Sang Phukon,Andreas Wicenec*

TL;DR: This paper introduces a new single-detector SPIIR pipeline for BNS and NSBH merger detection, improving sensitive distances over prior GstLAL results—especially for BNS in H1 (+38%)—by incorporating glitch-aware signal consistency and detector-specific FAR calibration using background extrapolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve gravitational-wave detection sensitivity during periods when only one detector is operational (e.g., 13.3% of O3, 29.7% of O4a), where traditional coincident multi-detector pipelines are inapplicable.

Method: Develops a dedicated single-detector Summed Parallel Infinite Impulse Response (SPIIR) pipeline with a hybrid log-likelihood ratio: combining a glitch-robust signal-consistency model (calibrated per source category and trigger-rate class) and a Gaussian-noise profile likelihood ratio for SNR; assigns false alarm rates (FARs) via empirical background estimation and high-ranking tail extrapolation within the same detector and analysis chunk.

Result: At FAR ≤ 0.5 per month: BNS sensitive distances increase to 136.5+2.9−3.0 Mpc (L1) and 120.2+3.1−3.3 Mpc (H1), outperforming GWTC-3 GstLAL by ~24% (L1) and ~38% (H1); NSBH distances are competitive (164.1+6.7−7.3 Mpc in L1, 134.9+6.3−7.0 Mpc in H1). Triggers from GW190425, GW200105_162426, and GW230529_181500 are recovered below threshold.

Conclusion: The single-detector SPIIR pipeline significantly enhances BNS detection range in single-detector observing periods, validates robust FAR assignment via background extrapolation, and enables reliable identification of astrophysical signals missed by multi-detector-only searches.

Abstract: Periods with only one detector operating accounted for 13.3 percent of the third LIGO-Virgo observing run (O3) and 29.7 percent of the first part of the fourth observing run (O4a). We present the first dedicated single-detector implementation of the Summed Parallel Infinite Impulse Response (SPIIR) pipeline for binary neutron star (BNS) and neutron star-black hole (NSBH) merger searches. Our hybrid log-likelihood ratio combines signal-consistency models for non-Gaussian glitches with a profile likelihood ratio for the signal-to-noise ratio under a Gaussian-noise model. We calibrate the glitch model by source category and trigger-rate class. We assign false alarm rates (FARs) using the measured background from the same detector and analysis chunk, extrapolating its high-ranking tail. We check the FAR assignments by comparing observed and expected cumulative background counts for BNS in O3a and NSBH in O3b at LIGO Hanford (H1) and LIGO Livingston (L1). Using O3 data and the GWTC-3 injection set, we find that, at a threshold of one false alarm per two months, the BNS sensitive distances are $136.5^{+2.9}_{-3.0}$ Mpc in L1 and $120.2^{+3.1}_{-3.3}$ Mpc in H1. These central estimates are approximately 24 and 38 percent higher, respectively, than the GWTC-3 GstLAL values of $110.5^{+3.2}_{-3.4}$ Mpc and $87.2^{+3.6}_{-3.9}$ Mpc over the same data and injection parameter ranges. GstLAL provides the only published single-detector results in GWTC-3. For NSBH, SPIIR reaches $164.1^{+6.7}_{-7.3}$ Mpc in L1 and $134.9^{+6.3}_{-7.0}$ Mpc in H1, compared with $165.1^{+6.7}_{-7.3}$ Mpc and $156.0^{+5.9}_{-6.4}$ Mpc for GWTC-3 GstLAL, respectively. Targeted searches recover triggers associated with GW190425, GW200105_162426, and GW230529_181500 with assigned FARs below the same threshold.

</details>


### [27] [Quasinormal Modes and Environmental Trapping around Charged Letelier Black Holes with Perfect-Fluid Dark Matter and Quintessence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19935)
*Semra Gurtas Dogan,Abdullah Guvendi,Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quasinormal modes (QNMs) of massless scalar and electromagnetic fields on charged Letelier black holes immersed in logarithmic perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) and quintessence, for both asymptotically flat (Λ=0) and anti-de Sitter (Λ<0) spacetimes; it validates WKB and Chebyshev pseudospectral methods, analyzes parameter dependencies (charge, β, string cloud, quintessence), confirms absence of growing modes in test-field sectors, and identifies long-lived AdS modes due to barrier-boundary trapping.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate stability and dynamical response of charged Letelier black holes in realistic dark energy/dark matter environments—specifically logarithmic PFDM and quintessence—and to extend QNM analysis to asymptotically AdS cases where standard WKB fails due to divergent scalar potentials.

Method: For Λ=0: sixth-order WKB with Padé resummation and time-domain integration (for validation); eikonal approximation via null geodesics (Lyapunov exponent & angular frequency). For Λ<0: Chebyshev pseudospectral method in ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates, imposing horizon regularity and Dirichlet boundary condition at AdS infinity; cross-validated against time-domain evolutions and Schwarzschild-AdS benchmarks.

Result: WKB and time-domain agree within 0.06% for fundamental modes (Λ=0); stronger string cloud/quintessence reduce both oscillation and damping rates; larger charge or β>0 (negative PFDM energy density) increase both; eikonal limit matches ℓ=2 WKB at ~1% level; Λ<0 yields long-lived modes (damping rates span 5 orders of magnitude), correlated with ω/ω_barrier, indicating barrier leakage; no unstable (growing) modes found in test-field sector.

Conclusion: Charged Letelier black holes in logarithmic PFDM + quintessence show no linear instability in the scalar/electromagnetic test-field regime across studied parameters; however, full nonlinear gravitational stability remains unproven. The work establishes robust computational frameworks for QNMs in non-standard dark matter backgrounds and asymptotically AdS geometries with singular potentials.

Abstract: We compute the massless scalar quasinormal spectra of charged Letelier black holes surrounded by logarithmic perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM) and quintessence, for $\Lambda=0$ and for asymptotically anti-de Sitter (AdS) spacetimes, together with the test Maxwell spectra for $\Lambda=0$. For $\Lambda=0$, sixth order WKB values with Padé resummation agree with independent time domain evolutions to within $0.06\%$ for the fundamental modes. Stronger string cloud and quintessence lower both the oscillation frequencies and the damping rates, while a larger charge or a larger positive PFDM parameter $\beta$ raises both. In our convention $\beta>0$ means negative PFDM energy density; negative $\beta$ gives a positive density and reverses the shift. The angular frequency and Lyapunov exponent of the unstable circular null orbit reproduce the eikonal trends and approximate the $\ell=2$ WKB frequencies at the percent level. For $\Lambda<0$ the scalar potential diverges at the AdS boundary and the single barrier WKB formula does not apply. We therefore solve the eigenvalue problem with a Chebyshev pseudospectral method in ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates, with regularity at the horizon and a Dirichlet condition at the boundary, and check the modes against time domain evolutions and Schwarzschild-AdS benchmarks. The AdS spectrum contains long-lived modes trapped between the photon sphere barrier and the reflecting boundary, with damping rates spanning about five orders of magnitude. The damping correlates with the ratio of the oscillation frequency to the barrier top frequency, consistent with leakage through the barrier. No growing modes are found in the test field sectors and parameter ranges studied, which does not establish the stability of the fully coupled system.

</details>

*Si-Tong Peng,Anson Chen,Si-Yang Pu,Jun Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how superradiant clouds around stellar-mass black holes in binaries affect gravitational-wave signals across milli-Hertz (LISA) and ground-based bands, identifying phase corrections and merger-time shifts as probes of ultralight bosons and cloud dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the existence of ultralight bosons via their superradiant clouds around black holes, using multi-band gravitational-wave observations to constrain cloud formation, evolution, and depletion mechanisms.

Method: Modeling coupled superradiant cloud–binary orbital evolution; analyzing phase corrections from fine transitions (e.g., |322⟩→|31−1⟩) in LISA band; assessing Bohr transition effects on merger time; performing vacuum injection-recovery analysis for cloud mass fraction estimation.

Result: Fine transitions imprint detectable phase corrections in LISA; Bohr transitions shift merger time observable by ground-based detectors; cloud depletion can initiate before resonance; ionization accelerates (rather than delays) coalescence; posterior on initial cloud mass fraction is constrained to Mc^i/M ≲ 10⁻⁶ for a representative system.

Conclusion: Milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational-wave observations provide complementary, synergistic probes of superradiant cloud physics and ultralight boson properties, motivating dedicated search strategies across detector bands.

Abstract: We investigate superradiant cloud level transitions in stellar-mass black hole binaries with milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational-wave observations. Under the adopted cloud formation and survival conditions, the $\lvert322\rangle\rightarrow\lvert31-1\rangle$ fine transition can imprint phase corrections in the LISA band, while Bohr transitions occurring after the milli-Hertz band observation can be probed through shifts in the merger time. Modeling the coupled cloud-orbit evolution, we find that cloud depletion can begin before resonance and that ionization can advance rather than delay coalescence. For a representative nearby binary, a vacuum injection-recovery analysis yields a cloud-mass-fraction posterior concentrated at $M_c^i/M\lesssim10^{-6}$. Comparing the LISA-predicted coalescence time with a subsequent ground-based measurement provides a complementary test of later cloud evolution. These results motivate complementary searches for ultralight bosons with milli-Hertz and multiband gravitational-wave observations.

</details>


### [29] [Casimir-like wormholes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20168)
*Mauricio Cataldo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the most general spherically symmetric solutions of Einstein's equations for matter with energy density ρ ∝ 1/r⁴ and radial equation of state p_r = ωρ, identifying black holes, naked singularities, and traversable wormholes; it shows only Casimir-type (ω > 0) and phantom (ω < −1) matter support traversable wormholes, demonstrates finite exotic matter content scaling linearly with throat radius, and confirms human traversability for throat radii ≳ 10⁸ m.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify all spherically symmetric spacetimes supported by inverse-quartic energy density matter and determine which equation-of-state parameters ω permit traversable wormholes satisfying energy conditions and human traversability constraints.

Method: Analytical integration of the spherically symmetric Einstein field equations with ansatz ρ(r) = α/r⁴ and p_r = ωρ; application of flare-out condition, null/weak energy conditions, geodesic analysis for tidal forces and acceleration, and embedding diagram construction to characterize geometry.

Result: Exact general solution parametrized by ω and integration constants; identification of three distinct spacetime classes (BH, NS, WH); necessary and sufficient condition ω > 0 or ω < −1 for traversable wormholes; emergence of transverse pressure p_t → −ρ for ω = 3 without imposing full Casimir tensor a priori; finite total exotic matter ∝ r₀; radial free-fall traversability satisfied for r₀ ≳ 10⁸ m.

Conclusion: Traversable wormholes supported by ρ ∝ 1/r⁴ matter are viable classical geometries for ω > 0 (Casimir-like) or ω < −1 (phantom), with finite exotic matter and macroscopic traversability; they represent exact, parameter-controllable alternatives to quantum-derived wormhole models, bridging Planck-scale and astrophysical regimes.

Abstract: We investigate spherically symmetric spacetimes sourced by matter with energy density $\rho \propto 1/r^{4}$ and equation of state $p_r = \omega \rho$. We derive the most general solution of Einstein's equations for this class of sources, obtaining black holes, naked singularities and traversable wormholes, parametrized by the integration constants and $\omega$. We show that only two classes of matter can support traversable wormholes: Casimir-type matter with $\omega > 0$ and phantom matter with $\omega < -1$, while quintessence and conventional dark energy are excluded by the flare-out condition. For the Casimir value $\omega = 3$, the transverse pressure approaches $p_t \to -\rho$ near the central singularity, so the full algebraic structure of the Casimir stress-energy tensor, $\rho \propto -1/r^{4}$, $p_r = 3\rho$, $p_t = -\rho$, is not imposed but emerges from Einstein's equations. Treating the amplitude of the energy density as a free classical parameter, we obtain wormholes with throat radii from microscopic to astrophysical scales. These Casimir-like configurations share the functional form of the Casimir tensor but are not generated by the physical Casimir effect; Garattini's Planck-scale Casimir wormhole is recovered when the quantum Casimir energy scale is imposed. The total amount of exotic matter is finite for arbitrary $\omega$ and scales linearly with the throat radius. Analyzing the traveler's acceleration, tidal forces and transit times, we show that radial free fall satisfies all human traversability conditions for $r_0 \gtrsim 10^{8}$ m. Finally, embedding diagrams illustrate three geometric regimes depending on $\omega$.

</details>


### [30] [Canonical metrics on punctured spheres, II: compactified moduli spaces of black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20282)
*Boris Botvinnik*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies the asymptotic behavior of canonical scalar-flat metrics on punctured spheres as subsets of punctures (representing black holes) collide, revealing a tree-like degeneration structure; it introduces a renormalized mass matrix extending smoothly over the Axelrod–Singer compactification, with geometric limits yielding bubble trees joined by Schwarzschild necks and minimal spheres interpreted as apparent horizons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the canonical scalar-flat metric and its associated mass matrix behave under collision of punctures—modeling black hole mergers—and to relate this degeneration to geometric compactifications and physical notions like horizons and mass inequalities.

Method: Analysis of asymptotics of the canonical scalar-flat metric $g_{\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}}$ and its mass matrix $\eta \in H_\ell$ as punctures coalesce; use of rooted trees to encode nested cluster collisions; renormalization by subtracting logarithmic divergences; application of Axelrod–Singer compactification $\overline{M}{}^{\,\mathrm{AS}}_\ell$ and blow-up/rescaling techniques to extract limiting geometries; comparison with Riemannian Penrose inequality.

Result: The mass matrix diverges logarithmically along explicit rays $E(A)$ as clusters collapse; the renormalized mass matrix extends smoothly over $\overline{M}{}^{\,\mathrm{AS}}_\ell$ and splits across bubble tree vertices; rescaled metrics converge to complete scalar-flat metrics at each vertex, joined by Schwarzschild necks containing minimal spheres (apparent horizons) in dimension $n=3$; a conjectural criterion for outermost horizon components is formulated; the mass matrix descends to a Deligne–Mumford-type quotient.

Conclusion: The collision dynamics of punctures induce a hierarchical, tree-structured degeneration captured by renormalized mass data and bubble-tree geometry; this provides a rigorous geometric–analytic framework linking initial data for multiple black holes, compactifications of configuration spaces, and quasi-local horizon physics.

Abstract: Let $g_{\mathfrak{p}_{\ell}}$ denote the canonical scalar-flat metric on $S^n\setminus \mathfrak{p}_{\ell}$ introduced in Part~I, which for $n=3$ corresponds to the Brill--Lindquist initial data for $\ell$ black holes, and let $\eta\in H_{\ell}$ be its associated mass matrix. In this paper, we investigate the asymptotic behavior of the metrics and the mass matrix $\eta$ as subsets of punctures collide. Such collisions cause every mass to diverge; if a cluster $A$ of black holes collapses at a relative scale $t$, the mass matrix satisfies $\eta=E(A)\ln(1/t)+O(1)$, diverging along an explicit ray $E(A)\in H_{\ell}$. For nested families of clusters, this degeneracy structure naturally organizes into a space of rooted trees. Subtracting the divergence gives a renormalized mass matrix which extends smoothly to every stratum of the Axelrod--Singer compactified moduli space $\overline{M}{}^{\,\mathrm{AS}}_\ell= \overline C_{\ell-1}(\mathbb{R}^n)/\mathrm O(n)$ and splits naturally into one mass matrix per vertex of the bubble tree. Geometrically, at each vertex carrying punctures the suitably rescaled metrics converge to a complete scalar-flat metric with the nodes filled in, adjacent such pieces being joined by Schwarzschild necks, and the renormalized matrix acting as its mass data. For $n=3$, the necks contain minimal spheres, which are common apparent horizons of the merging holes. At vertices bearing two holes, we determine on which side of the limiting horizon each node lies, and we formulate as a conjecture the resulting criterion for a neck to be a component of the outermost horizon. We also compare the mass lower bound of Part~I with the Riemannian Penrose inequality. Because the mass matrix is invariant under rotations of the bubbles, it lives on a Deligne--Mumford-type quotient of the compactification.

</details>


### [31] [Repeated Gravitational-Wave Lensing from Binary Black Holes on Eccentric and Precessing Orbits around Spinning Supermassive Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20416)
*Jie Wu,Mengfei Sun,Jin Li,Zhoujian Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates repeated gravitational lensing of gravitational waves from a stellar-mass binary black hole (BBH) orbiting a supermassive black hole (SMBH), focusing on how outer-orbit evolution—eccentricity, inclination, and relativistic precession—affects lensing event timing, amplification, and parameter inference in LISA observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how orbital evolution in hierarchical triple systems (BBH-SMBH) affects repeated gravitational lensing of GWs, and to assess its impact on parameter estimation—particularly for SMBH spin and outer-orbit geometry—beyond static or circular approximations.

Method: Models the outer orbit using post-Newtonian dynamics; incorporates moving-source effects and de Sitter precession in the inspiral waveform; applies an event-local point-mass lensing model that tracks time-varying source–lens alignment and GW frequency; uses Fisher-matrix analysis and waveform residual comparisons for a one-year LISA observation.

Result: Outer-orbit evolution significantly alters lensing-event timing and magnification; many systems undetectable under fixed circular/nonprecessing assumptions become lensed; median fractional uncertainty reductions are largest for outer-orbit inclination and SMBH spin; lensing-induced residuals persist even after local unlensed-model fitting for some detectable systems.

Conclusion: Repeated lensing in evolving hierarchical BBH–SMBH systems provides unique, complementary constraints on SMBH spin and outer-orbit geometry in LISA data, invalidating static lensing approximations and enhancing multimessenger astrophysical inference.

Abstract: Gravitational waves (GWs) from a stellar-mass binary black hole (BBH) orbiting a supermassive black hole (SMBH) can experience repeated gravitational lensing as the BBH passes behind the SMBH. Our previous studies of orbit-induced GW modulation motivate this investigation of repeated lensing in evolving hierarchical triple systems. We investigate how eccentricity, inclination, and relativistic precession of the outer orbit affect successive lensing events and parameter inference. The outer orbit follows post-Newtonian dynamics, and the inspiral waveform includes moving-source effects and de Sitter precession. An event-local point-mass lensing model tracks the changing source--lens alignment and GW frequency. For a one-year LISA observation, we examine lensing-event identification, Fisher-matrix estimates of parameter uncertainties, and waveform residuals when lensing is omitted. Our results show that outer-orbit evolution changes the timing and amplification of successive lensing events and can bring initially unfavorable configurations into the lensing region. A fixed circular, nonprecessing approximation misses all lensing events for a substantial fraction of the selected lensed systems. The median fractional reductions in uncertainty are largest for the outer-orbit inclination and SMBH spin. For some systems above the adopted detection threshold, lensing-induced waveform differences persist after local adjustments to the parameters of the unlensed model. Repeated lensing can thus provide complementary constraints on SMBH properties and outer-orbit geometry in LISA observations of hierarchical BBHs.

</details>


### [32] [Harmonic branches of Einstein--ModMax--scalar field gravity: exact solutions and regularity II](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20529)
*Leonel Bixano,Tonatiuh Matos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit harmonic solution families in Einstein–ModMax–Scalar field gravity with non-constant electromagnetic phase Θ, classifies static and stationary configurations (including dyons, Taub–NUT, Schwarzschild–ModMax), proves structural theorems on phase constancy and rescaling obstructions due to twist, and recovers known and new ModMax extensions—including a Melvin–swirling background with exact ModMax Schwarzschild exterior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend known Maxwell electrovacuum solutions (e.g., RN, Taub–NUT, Melvin) to the nonlinear ModMax–scalar–gravity framework while allowing nontrivial electromagnetic duality phase tanΘ = G/F, and to clarify geometric obstructions (e.g., twist-induced failure of constant-phase or diagonal potential rescaling).

Result: Complete catalogue of explicit static and restricted stationary solutions: (i) ModMax generalizations of RN, Taub–NUT, single-charge, and dyonic configurations with scalar-dependent couplings; (ii) new ModMax–Schwarzschild exterior embedded in Melvin–swirling background; (iii) distinction between frozen (harmonic) vs. non-frozen (non-harmonic) stationary integrations; (iv) smooth horizon limits vs. constitutive continuation at null equatorial circle.

Abstract: We integrate harmonic solution branches of Einstein--ModMax--Scalar field gravity, keeping the electromagnetic phase \(\tan{\Theta}=\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{F}\) non-constant. For each family we first solve the field equations $(a(\s),b(\s),c(\s))$ and reconstruct the physical functions $f,\omega,k,A_t,A_\varphi,\phi$. In the same section we then select the Schwarzschild or Coulomb harmonic function, impose the regular horizon conditions, identify the ModMax solution, and state its Maxwell limit. Two theorems distinguish a constant electromagnetic phase from an exact static charge rescaling and show how twist prevents constant diagonal rescalings of the electromagnetic potentials at fixed metric and scalar. The catalogue covers known RN and charged Taub--NUT solutions, single-charge configurations, explicit static dyons with scalar-dependent couplings, and a restricted stationary family with an active scalar. We distinguish new explicit ModMax extensions of known Maxwell profiles from the nonfrozen stationary integration. The axial LWP reconstruction recovers the known Melvin--swirling background and gives an exact ModMax Schwarzschild exterior within that background. At its horizon, smooth metric and electromagnetic limits are distinguished from the constitutive continuation at a null equatorial circle. Uncharged configurations and earlier Maxwell fields are traced back to the preceding harmonic catalogue.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how semi-open black hole systems—specifically Kerr–de Sitter spacetimes with an artificial reflective inner boundary—behave dynamically, particularly regarding QNM spectrum structure, stability, and the physical interpretation of boundary conditions.

Result: 1) Increasing reflectivity generates echo branches approaching the real axis with regular spacing; 2) Rotation induces two QNM response types: some approach the real axis or retain finite damping, others shift horizontally tracking mΩ(r₊); 3) Two instability mechanisms identified—superradiance and energy injection from the wall; 4) Energy reflectivity diverges at mΩ(r₊) even for small Teukolsky amplitude reflectivity; 5) Asymptotic-amplitude boundary conditions produce unphysical pole-born resonances; exact finite-radius Robin condition eliminates them.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational quasinormal mode spectrum of Kerr--de Sitter black holes with a partially reflective inner boundary near the event horizon, by reducing the angular and radial Teukolsky equations to Heun equations. We vary different parameters and study the $s=-2$, $\ell=m=2$ modes numerically. As the reflectivity $\mathcal{R}_0$ increases, QNMs develop echo branches approaching the real axis with approximately regular spacing, while others approach $\operatorname{Re}\omega=m\Omega(r_+)$. As the rotation $a$ increases, some QNMs approach the real axis or retain finite damping, while others exhibit approximately horizontal shifts with real parts tracking $m\Omega(r_+)$. Two mechanisms driving the instabilities are identified in complementary regimes, namely superradiant amplification and energy injection from the reflective wall. We derive the ratio between Teukolsky amplitude and energy reflectivities, finding that the latter can diverge at $m\Omega(r_+)$ even when the former is small, which accounts for an active wall in this regime. We also find that formulating the reflective boundary condition in terms of asymptotic amplitudes gives rise to resonances near each pole of the ingoing connection coefficient, extending to arbitrarily large positive imaginary parts. An exact finite radius Robin condition imposed on the Sasaki--Nakamura variable removes these pole-born frequencies. These results show how the inner boundary prescription controls the spectrum structure and stability of semi-open Kerr--de Sitter systems.

Abstract: In this paper, we study spherically symmetric boson star solutions in Einstein gravity minimally coupled to Ayón--Beato--García nonlinear electrodynamics and a massive complex scalar field. We find that these solutions exist only when the electric charge $q$ is below a critical value. Within this range, solutions with relatively small charges can exhibit a gravitational blueshift, and the radial mass function becomes negative in some regions while the ADM mass remains positive. For larger charges, frozen boson-star solutions emerge as the scalar-field frequency $\omega$ approaches zero. In these configurations, the metric function $1/g_{rr}$ and $-g_{tt}$ become very small near a special position $r_{\mathrm{ch}}$, and the spacetime resembles an extremal black hole from the perspective of an observer at infinity. Moreover, timelike trajectories in these frozen boson stars are strongly deflected near $r_{\mathrm{ch}}$, and particles require a longer coordinate time to traverse the same radial distance than they do in ordinary boson stars.

Abstract: We perform the one of the most general tests of post-Newtonian (PN) inspiral in binary black holes to date by allowing all eight phase coefficients up to 3.5PN order to vary simultaneously from their general-relativistic values. We then identify the combinations of phase coefficients most strongly constrained by the data through a principal component analysis of the eight-dimensional deformation space. We find that the data constrain the three leading principal components beyond their priors, and that all three are consistent with the predictions of general relativity. These results demonstrate that exceptionally loud gravitational-wave events enable robust, high-dimensional tests of inspiral dynamics and provide direct access to the measurable subspace of post-Newtonian deformations.

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve recent doubts—raised by Taherasghari and Will's post-Newtonian analysis—that the spin-0 mode in Einstein-Aether theory is non-dynamical or fails to carry energy, by testing its radiative behavior in fully nonlinear, spherically symmetric collapse where it is the sole radiative degree of freedom.

Method: Fully nonlinear numerical evolutions of scalar-field collapse in spherical symmetry within Einstein-Aether theory, using appropriate initial data and gauge conditions; analysis of wave propagation speed, asymptotic falloff, energy flux, horizon mass vs. ADM mass, and trapped surface formation.

Conclusion: The spin-0 mode is confirmed to be a genuine, dynamical, energy-carrying gravitational degree of freedom; the emergence of transient white-hole–like behavior and persistent shell steepening at strong coupling suggests possible violations of cosmic censorship or new high-curvature phenomena beyond standard black-hole paradigms.

Abstract: Einstein-Aether theory is a theory of gravity with a preferred frame, in which Lorentz symmetry is broken by a dynamical unit timelike vector field. Both propagate a scalar (spin-0) mode besides the tensor mode and, in Einstein-Aether theory, the vector mode. The physical nature of the spin-0 mode has recently been called into question following the post-Newtonian analysis of Taherasghari and Will, whose post-Newtonian calculation of radiation reaction in compact binaries finds no trace of its speed in the energy loss, suggesting that the mode may not be a dynamical, energy-carrying wave. We test this with fully nonlinear evolutions of scalar-field collapse in spherical symmetry, where the spin-0 mode is the only radiative degree of freedom. The collapse emits an outgoing spin-0 wave that falls off as the inverse of the radius, travels at the speed predicted by the linear theory, and carries positive energy to infinity. The static aether configuration left around the black hole has negative energy instead, so that the horizon mass exceeds the ADM mass. For large couplings, collapse does not produce a black hole: we recover the transient white holes found by Akhoury, Garfinkle and Gupta, but at higher resolution the collapsing shell keeps steepening, not hidden behind any trapped surface, which may signal a naked singularity.

</details>


### [38] [Maximum entropy reconstruction of the black hole greybody factors from its Korteweg-de Vries integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21043)
*Noah Alberca,Michele Lenzi,Carlos F. Sopuerta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a maximum entropy method to reconstruct black hole greybody factors from KdV integrals—conserved quantities derived from the KdV hierarchy symmetry of BH perturbation equations—achieving high accuracy with few integrals, validated on Pöschl-Teller and Regge-Wheeler potentials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, positivity-preserving method for reconstructing BH greybody factors from the recently discovered KdV integrals—bypassing the need for exact solutions or high-order WKB expansions—especially where no closed-form solution exists (e.g., Schwarzschild Regge-Wheeler potential).

Method: Applies the maximum entropy principle to solve the moment problem posed by KdV integrals: constructs the transmission probability as the least-biased positive distribution consistent with a finite set of moments (KdV integrals), truncated at successive orders; validated on the exactly solvable Pöschl-Teller potential and applied to the Regge-Wheeler potential using numerically computed KdV integrals.

Result: The reconstructed greybody factors match fourth-order WKB results across multiple multipoles for Schwarzschild axial perturbations; the method guarantees positivity and converges rapidly—few KdV integrals suffice for high fidelity—establishing the first use of maximum entropy for BH greybody reconstruction.

Abstract: The dynamics of perturbed non-rotating black holes (BHs) can be described by master wave equations with a potential barrier. Recent work has shown that these equations possess an infinite number of symmetries generated by the Korteweg-de Vries (KdV) hierarchy, leading to conserved quantities (KdV integrals) that fully determine the BH greybody factors via a moment problem. In this paper, we introduce and validate a new method to solve this moment problem using the maximum entropy principle. The method reconstructs, at each order of truncation, the least-biased transmission probability compatible with a finite set of KdV integrals, guaranteeing positivity by construction. We first validate the technique against the exactly solvable Pöschl-Teller potential. We then apply it to the Regge-Wheeler potential governing axial perturbations of a Schwarzschild BH, where no exact solution is available; the resulting reconstruction agrees closely with the fourth-order Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB) approximation across several multipoles. This is the first application of the maximum entropy method to the reconstruction of BH greybody factors, demonstrating that a modest number of KdV integrals is sufficient to recover them accurately.

</details>


### [39] [On Chen's Extended Action for $κ(R,T)$ Gravity: Gravity in a Polarizable Medium, Material Folds and a No-Bounce Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21093)
*Ginés R. Pérez Teruel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes κ(R,T) gravity using Chen's local adjoint action, showing it yields field equations from a local variational principle and performs physical work. In flat FLRW cosmology, Dirac reduction yields a Hamiltonian independent of κ(T) on regular branches, with κ entering via a second-class matter constraint. It introduces a polarizable-medium interpretation for the effective source, links the fold in the matter map to a minimal-volume bounce endpoint at ρ_c/2, excludes smooth bounces for κ > 0, w > −1, and connects linear dust κ(R,T) dynamics to loop quantum cosmology (LQC) — matching its effective Friedmann equation but differing in conservation laws; an effective pressure −ρ²/ρ_c recovers LQC homogeneity, and the fold density matches LQC’s signature-change threshold. Quantization via Koopman–von Neumann transport is exact, with the adjoint field acting as a response variable, supporting an open-system view.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the physical content and Hamiltonian structure of κ(R,T) gravity—particularly whether it admits a consistent canonical formulation, supports cosmological bounces, and relates to established quantum-cosmological frameworks like loop quantum cosmology (LQC).

Method: Application of Chen's local adjoint action to derive field equations from a local variational principle; Dirac constrained Hamiltonian analysis in flat FLRW spacetime; exact decomposition of the effective energy-momentum source; polarizable-medium analogy; analysis of regular vs. singular branches including fold points; comparison with LQC effective dynamics; Koopman–von Neumann quantization of the reduced dust constraint.

Result: The Hamiltonian is independent of κ(T) on regular branches, with κ entering only through a second-class constraint; physical stress tensor non-conservation is compensated by conservation of a dressed particle number; the effective source admits a polarizable-medium interpretation; a past endpoint (bounce) occurs at a fold with finite volume and density ρ_c/2; smooth Hamiltonian continuation through the fold is forbidden by Dirac consistency; flat-FLRW bounces are excluded for κ > 0, ρ > 0, w > −1; linear dust model reproduces LQC’s effective Friedmann equation but violates standard conservation—restored by effective pressure −ρ²/ρ_c; fold density equals LQC’s signature-change threshold; exact Koopman–von Neumann quantization exists, with adjoint field as response variable.

Conclusion: κ(R,T) gravity, formulated via Chen’s adjoint action, constitutes a physically viable, non-conservative gravitational theory with emergent thermodynamic and open-system features. Its cosmological dynamics—especially the fold-induced minimal-volume endpoint and correspondence to LQC—suggest deep connections between modified gravity, quantum cosmology, and effective medium physics, while its canonical structure imposes strict limitations on singularity resolution mechanisms.

Abstract: Chen's local adjoint action reproduces the field equations of $\kappa(R,T)$ from a local variational principle, and we show that it does physical work. In flat FLRW, Dirac reduction gives a Hamiltonian independent of $\kappa(T)$ on regular branches, where the coupling enters through a second-class matter constraint. The physical stress tensor is not generally conserved, but a dressed particle number is. An exact decomposition of the effective source motivates a polarizable-medium interpretation: $\kappa=0$ corresponds to total screening, the repulsive phase to overscreening, and a fold of the matter map to saturation. For linear coupling and dust, the expanding low-density branch has a past endpoint at the fold, where the volume is minimal and the density reaches $\rho_c/2$ at finite curvature. Dirac consistency rules out smooth Hamiltonian continuation through the fold. More generally, a flat-FLRW bounce is excluded for $\kappa>0$, positive density and $w>-1$. The linear dust model reproduces the effective Friedmann equation of loop quantum cosmology (LQC), but the two models obey different conservation laws. An effective pressure $-\rho^2/\rho_c$ restores the homogeneous LQC dynamics, and the fold density coincides with the signature-change threshold in the deformed-algebra approach to LQC. The reduced dust constraint admits an exactly solvable Koopman--von Neumann transport quantization and the adjoint field acts as a response variable, motivating an open-system interpretation.

</details>


### [40] [Detectability of Eccentric Intermediate Mass-Ratio Inspiral in the Decihertz Band](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21323)
*Devesh Giri,Sajal Mukherjee,Apratim Ganguly,Sanjit Mitra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the impact of orbital eccentricity on gravitational-wave detection and parameter estimation for intermediate mass-ratio inspirals (IMRIs) in the decihertz band (~0.01–1 Hz), showing that circular templates fail significantly even at low eccentricities (e₀ ≳ 0.03), causing mismatches >3% and biases in mass parameters; it concludes that eccentric waveform models are essential for robust analysis with future observatories (B-DECIGO, LGWA-Si, IndIGO-D).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current GW search pipelines rely on circular templates, but IMRIs—expected in dense stellar environments—are likely eccentric; this work assesses whether circular approximations suffice for upcoming decihertz detectors.

Method: Using perturbative waveforms from FastEMRIWaveforms for a representative IMRI (M=10⁴ M⊙, q=100, 10-hr observation), the authors compute fitting factors between eccentric signals and circular templates, maximizing over total mass and mass ratio via differential evolution, under realistic noise curves (B-DECIGO, LGWA-Si, IndIGO-D) and flat PSD; they further apply a signal-to-noise-dependent distinguishability criterion to assess resolvability of eccentricity.

Result: Mismatch exceeds 3% at e₀ ≈ 0.03; best-fitting circular templates bias M and q by several percent at e₀ = 0.1; eccentricities as small as e₀ ≈ 5×10⁻⁴ (B-DECIGO/IndIGO-D) and 5×10⁻³ (LGWA-Si) are statistically resolvable; mismatch stems from excitation of radial Teukolsky harmonics and slower orbital decay in eccentric binaries.

Conclusion: Circular templates are inadequate for IMRI analysis in the decihertz band; robust detection and parameter estimation require eccentric waveform models and dedicated template banks for DECIGO, LGWA, and IndIGO-D.

Abstract: The proposed decihertz gravitational-wave (GW) band $(\sim 10^{-2}-1\,\Hz)$ will observe the late inspiral of intermediate mass-ratio inspirals (IMRIs), binaries with mass ratios $10^2 \lesssim q \lesssim 10^4$ that assemble dynamically in dense stellar environments and are expected to retain measurable eccentricity. Search and parameter-estimation pipelines are predominantly built around circular templates, and we test this assumption. Using black-hole perturbation theory waveforms from \textsc{FastEMRIWaveforms} for a representative IMRI ($M=10^4\,\modot$, $q=100$, observed for its final $10\,\hr$), we compute fitting factors between eccentric signals and circular templates, maximizing over the template's total mass and mass ratio with a differential-evolution search, for the B-DECIGO, LGWA-Si, and IndIGO-D noise curves and a flat PSD. The mismatch grows with initial eccentricity $e_0$ and crosses the standard $3\%$ threshold at $e_0\simeq3\times10^{-2}$, well within the range allowed by the waveform model and expected from dynamical formation. The best-fitting circular templates also bias the total mass and mass ratio by several percent at $e_0=0.1$. Combining the mismatch with the signal-to-noise-dependent distinguishability criterion, we find that for a source at $1\,\mathrm{Gpc}$, eccentricities as small as $e_0\simeq5\times10^{-4}$ (B-DECIGO and IndIGO-D) and $5\times10^{-3}$ (LGWA-Si) are resolvable, one to two orders of magnitude below where detection efficiency degrades. The loss of match arises from the excitation of radial Teukolsky harmonics, which spread power into sidebands that circular templates cannot represent, and from the slower orbital decay of eccentric binaries at fixed initial semi-latus rectum. Robust detection and parameter estimation of IMRIs with DECIGO, LGWA, and IndIGO-D will require eccentric waveform models and template banks.

</details>


### [41] [Critical curves and throat degeneracy in black bounces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21355)
*Francisco S. N. Lobo,Diogo Mesquita,Luís F. Dias da Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how static spherical areal-radius deformations affect null geodesics—particularly photon spheres and critical orbits—showing that while critical impact parameters and orbital frequencies remain invariant under such deformations, stability (Lyapunov exponent) and strong-deflection behavior scale with geometric factors like the radial Jacobian or throat curvature; it derives universal scaling laws for degenerate optical potentials and applies them to the Simpson–Visser spacetime, identifying a quartic merger at a=3M separating stable/unstable throat geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which features of null-orbit dynamics—especially photon sphere properties—are preserved under radial (areal-radius) deformations of spherically symmetric spacetimes, and to disentangle geometry-dependent stability effects from invariant critical-curve data.

Method: Analytical perturbation and scaling analysis of the effective optical potential for null geodesics under static, spherically symmetric areal-radius deformations; classification of critical point degeneracies via composition of extremal orders (m, q → p = mq); explicit computation for Simpson–Visser metric including Lyapunov exponents, strong-deflection coefficients, and bifurcation analysis at a = 3M.

Result: Critical impact parameter b_c and orbital frequency Ω_c are invariant under areal-radius deformations; coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent λ scales by radial Jacobian J_r; logarithmic strong-deflection coefficient scales as 1/J_r; at reflection-symmetric throats, stability factorizes into derivative of original optical potential and throat curvature; for order-m extremum composed with order-q radius minimum, optical degeneracy is order p = mq, yielding characteristic critical approach |x| ∝ |t|^{−2/(p−2)} and deflection Δϕ ∝ (b/b_c − 1)^{−(p−2)/(2p)}; Simpson–Visser family retains Schwarzschild b_c for a < 3M, with instability vanishing quartically at a = 3M, marking a bifurcation between stable (a > 3M) and unstable (a < 3M) throat branches.

Conclusion: Null-orbit critical curves (b_c, Ω_c) are robust under radial deformations, but dynamical stability and strong-field lensing signatures encode local geometric details—specifically the radial Jacobian and curvature at extrema—enabling a clean separation between topological/invariant and geometric/variable information in photon sphere physics.

Abstract: Identical critical curves need not imply identical null-orbit dynamics. For a class of static spherical areal-radius deformations, we show that a surviving original photon sphere retains its critical impact parameter and orbital frequency, while its coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent is multiplied by the radial Jacobian. The logarithmic strong-deflection coefficient acquires the inverse factor. At a reflection-symmetric throat, stability factorizes into a derivative of the original optical potential and the throat curvature. More generally, an order-$m$ extremum of the original potential composed with an order-$q$ areal-radius minimum produces an optical degeneracy of order $p=mq$, with critical approach $|x|\propto|t|^{-2/(p-2)}$ and reflected strong deflection $\Delta\phi\propto(b/b_{\rm c}-1)^{-(p-2)/(2p)}$ for $p>2$. For the Simpson--Visser family with mass $M$ and bounce parameter $a$, the critical impact parameter remains Schwarzschild-like for $a<3M$, although the instability varies with $a$. The merger at $a=3M$ is quartic, separating stable and unstable throat branches. These results isolate the null-orbit information preserved by a radial deformation from that encoded in its local geometry.

</details>


### [42] [The Stability of the Higgs vacuum inside a Schwarzschild Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21356)
*Paul G. Abel*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper studies Higgs field dynamics inside a Schwarzschild black hole, showing that due to timelike radial coordinate, the Higgs condensate evolves in time and remains in its broken phase until Planck-scale curvature is reached—regardless of black hole mass; inclusion of one-loop corrections modifies the solution only modestly (~10% reduction), preserving electroweak symmetry breaking throughout the interior within EFT validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how electroweak symmetry behaves inside black holes, where standard static analyses fail due to the timelike nature of the radial coordinate and strong curvature effects near the singularity.

Method: Analytical solution of the Higgs field equation in Schwarzschild interior with a Planck-suppressed Higgs–Kretschmann coupling; asymptotic analysis near singularity yielding Bessel’s equation; exact solution including one-loop vacuum fluctuation correction as a source term.

Result: Higgs condensate follows $H = v J_0(x)$ near singularity, remains frozen in broken phase until curvature reaches Planck scale; one-loop correction reduces condensate by ≤10% but does not restore symmetry.

Conclusion: Electroweak symmetry stays broken throughout the black hole interior within effective field theory validity—contrary to expectations from static or adiabatic approximations.

Abstract: I investigate the behaviour of the Higgs field inside a Schwarzschild black hole, including a Planck-suppressed coupling between the Higgs field and the Kretschmann scalar, the leading curvature invariant in a Ricci-flat geometry. Because the radial coordinate is timelike in the interior, the Higgs condensate evolves in time rather than tracking the minimum of its potential. Near the singularity the interior field equation reduces to Bessel's equation, with solution $H=vJ_0(x)$, where $x\propto\sqrt\xi\,t_p/\tau$ and $\tau$ is the proper time to the singularity. Contrary to static analyses, the field remains frozen in its broken phase while the potential changes shape, departing from its vacuum value only when the curvature becomes Planckian, independently of the black-hole mass. Including the one-loop correction from Higgs vacuum fluctuations yields the same Bessel equation with a source term, which I solve exactly; the condensate is reduced by at most about ten per cent. The electroweak symmetry therefore remains broken throughout the interior within the regime of validity of effective field theory.

</details>


### [43] [Geometry of squeezed states. The phase-space metric, relative vacua and cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21426)
*Alejandro Corichi,Jerónimo Cortez*

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets quantum squeezing and vacuum selection in linear quantum systems through the lens of phase-space geometry: it identifies the classical phase-space metric g — induced by a choice of vacuum/complex structure — as the fundamental object, showing that quantum uncertainties, coherent/squeezed states, and vacuum distances are all governed by g and its comparison to a reference metric g₀; it derives exact evolution laws for squeezing parameters rᵢ under quadratic Hamiltonians and applies the framework to field theory, resolving unitary implementability via finite metric distance (e.g., in de Sitter space).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and geometrically clarify the role of metrics in quantum theory—specifically, to elevate the phase-space metric g (associated with a vacuum choice) to primary status, bridging symplectic structure, quantum uncertainties, coherent/squeezed states, and vacuum comparisons in both quantum mechanics and QFT.

Method: Geometric quantization of linear systems; analysis of compatible complex structures and associated metrics on classical phase space; computation of quantum uncertainties and transition probabilities via Hamiltonian vector fields and g-norms; derivation of squeezing invariants from eigenvalues of g₀⁻¹g; application to harmonic oscillator and scalar field theory in curved spacetime (de Sitter), including comparison of Bunch–Davies and radiation-era vacua.

Result: 1. Quantum fluctuations of linear observables equal g-norms of their Hamiltonian vector fields; 2. The quantum metric on state space equals g/(2ℏ) along coherent states; 3. Squeezing is intrinsically a relative property between two phase-space metrics, characterized by eigenvalues e^{±2rᵢ} of g₀⁻¹g; 4. Quadratic evolution changes rᵢ only via the g₀-self-adjoint part of the generator, at an exact rate; 5. Unitary implementability of a vacuum relative to a reference is equivalent to finite geodesic distance in the space of metrics; 6. For massless fields in de Sitter space, Bunch–Davies vacuum has sinh rₖ = 1/(2|kη|) relative to standard reference (non-unitarily implementable), but sinh rₖ = 1/(2k²η²) relative to radiation-era vacuum (unitarily implementable, faster squeezing).

Conclusion: The phase-space metric g fully encodes quantum information about vacua, squeezing, and uncertainty in linear quantum systems; treating g as primary yields a geometric, coordinate- and representation-independent framework that resolves conceptual ambiguities—especially regarding unitary equivalence of vacua—and provides exact, physically interpretable measures of quantum deformation.

Abstract: Geometry enters quantum theory in two places. The space of quantum states carries a symplectic form, which generates unitary evolution, and a metric, which governs uncertainties and transition probabilities. The classical phase space acquires a metric of its own when a vacuum is chosen: quantizing a linear system amounts to choosing a compatible complex structure, equivalently a compatible metric $g$. We take this second metric as the primary object and show that it plays, on phase space, the role that the quantum metric plays on the space of states: fluctuations of linear observables are $g$-norms of Hamiltonian vector fields, and along coherent states $g/2\hbar$ \emph{is} the quantum metric. Squeezed states then have a simple description. A squeezed vacuum is the vacuum of another metric, and squeezing is a property of a pair of metrics, a relative vacuum, whose invariants are the eigenvalues $e^{\pm2r_i}$ of $L=g_0^{-1}g$. We work out the harmonic oscillator in detail, including why a rotated squeezed state no longer minimizes the product of the displayed uncertainties. Quadratic evolution deforms the metric, and only the part of the generator that is self-adjoint with respect to the reference changes the $r_i$, at a rate we give exactly. For fields, a vacuum is unitarily implementable relative to a reference exactly when it lies at finite distance from it in the space of vacua. For a massless field in de Sitter space we revisit the two canonical charts compared by Grain and Vennin: relative to the reference of the standard squeezing formalism the Bunch--Davies state has $\sinh r_k=1/(2|k\eta|)$, and relative to the vacuum that a radiation era beginning at time $\eta$ would select it has $\sinh r_k=1/(2k^2\eta^2)$. The second comparison squeezes twice as fast and is unitarily implementable; the first is not.

</details>


### [44] [Pulsar timing response and observational constraints on continuous-spin gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21512)
*Jing-Hong Han,Zhi-Chao Zhao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies continuous-spin particles (CSPs) as massless, integer-helicity excitations beyond standard gravitons, deriving their pulsar timing response and constraining the continuous-spin scale ρ_g using NANOGrav 15-year data; it finds ρ_g < 7.03×10⁻²³ eV (95% CL, uniform prior), and a spectral index γ_GW ≈ 4.89 consistent with circular SMBHBs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test fundamental extensions of general relativity by probing continuous-spin gravity — a theoretically consistent, Poincaré-invariant alternative to standard spin-2 gravity — using pulsar timing arrays sensitive to ultralow ρ_g via nanohertz gravitational-wave frequencies.

Method: Starting from gauge-invariant worldline coupling of linearized continuous-spin gravity, the authors compute the one-way redshift response and overlap reduction function (ORF) for arbitrary integer helicity h; they model an isotropic, unpolarized h = ±2 stochastic background in the solar-system barycentric frame and perform Bayesian joint inference on NANOGrav 15-year data including pulsar red noise, under uniform and log-uniform priors on ρ_g.

Result: The analysis yields 95% upper limits: ρ_g^U < 7.03×10⁻²³ eV (uniform prior) and ρ_g^{log U} < 4.79×10⁻²³ eV (log-uniform prior); the inferred gravitational-wave spectral index is γ_GW = 4.89^{+0.85}_{-0.96}, closer to the canonical SMBHB value 13/3 ≈ 4.33 than GR’s prediction for some astrophysical scenarios.

Conclusion: Nanohertz pulsar timing provides a uniquely sensitive probe of continuous-spin gravity; current NANOGrav data strongly constrain the continuous-spin scale ρ_g, and the inferred spectral index suggests continuous-spin effects may improve consistency with circular supermassive binary black hole predictions — motivating further theoretical and observational scrutiny of CSP gravity.

Abstract: Continuous-spin particles form the most general massless integer-helicity irreducible representations of the Poincaré group, characterized by a dimensionful continuous-spin scale $\rho_g$ set by a nonzero Pauli--Lubanski invariant. A single continuous-spin representation contains an infinite tower of integer helicities $h\in\mathbb Z$ that mix under Lorentz boosts. Since continuous-spin effects are controlled by $\alpha\equiv\rho_g/(\hbar\omega)$ at gravitational-wave angular frequency $\omega$, nanohertz pulsar timing arrays are naturally sensitive to ultralow values of $\rho_g$. Starting from the gauge-invariant worldline coupling of linearized continuous-spin gravity, we derive the one-way pulsar timing redshift response and the overlap reduction function of an isotropic stochastic background for arbitrary integer helicity. We show that while photon propagation is modulated across all helicities, only the vector modes ($h=\pm1$) perturb the four-velocity of a static observer, and that the tensor modes ($h=\pm2$) smoothly recover the Hellings--Downs correlation of general relativity as $\rho_g/(\hbar\omega)\to 0$. Modeling an unpolarized $h=\pm2$ background in the solar-system barycentric frame and jointly fitting the NANOGrav 15-year data with pulsar red noise, we obtain the $95\%$ upper limits $\rho^{U}_g<7.03\times10^{-23}\,\mathrm{eV}$ and $\rho^{\log {U}}_g<4.79\times10^{-23}\,\mathrm{eV}$ under uniform and log-uniform priors, respectively. Under the uniform prior, we obtain $\gamma_{\mathrm{GW}} = 4.89^{+0.85}_{-0.96}$, which brings the spectral index closer to the canonical value $\gamma_{\rm GW}=13/3$ expected for circular SMBHBs than standard General Relativity.

</details>


### [45] [On the Asymptotic Expansion of Massive Fields in Spherically Symmetric Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21557)
*Robin Fynn Diedrichs,Iris van Gemeren*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper challenges the standard Yukawa falloff assumption for massive real scalar fields sourced by a central object, showing it is generically invalid even for minimally coupled fields; it demonstrates that the asymptotically extracted coefficient is neither coordinate-invariant nor equal to the scalar charge in the point-particle action, and resolves this via matched asymptotic expansions (perturbatively) and numerical methods (non-perturbatively).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct the widespread but incorrect assumption that massive scalar fields sourced by a central object always exhibit Yukawa falloff at infinity, and to clarify the physical meaning and coordinate dependence of the asymptotically extracted scalar field coefficient.

Method: Perturbative matched asymptotic expansions to resolve the discrepancy between near-zone and far-zone scalar field behavior; supplemented by non-perturbative numerical methods to explore the full field structure.

Result: The Yukawa falloff is generically invalid for massive scalar fields; the asymptotically extracted coefficient differs from the true scalar charge (defined invariantly via the point-particle action) and lacks coordinate invariance.

Conclusion: The standard identification of the far-field coefficient with the scalar charge is unjustified; a consistent definition requires careful matching across scales and respect for coordinate invariance—highlighting subtleties in effective field theory treatments of scalar sources.

Abstract: The behavior of massive real scalar fields, when sourced by a central object, is usually assumed to follow a Yukawa falloff towards radial infinity. In this paper, we show that this is generically not the case, even for minimally coupled fields. Further, while the point-particle action provides a coordinate-invariant definition of the central object's scalar charge, we show that the coefficient one usually extracts at asymptotically large radii in the case of massive scalar fields is neither coordinate invariant nor identical to the scalar charge appearing in the point-particle action. We resolve this issue perturbatively by constructing matched asymptotic expansions and investigate the non-perturbative structure using numerical methods.

</details>


### [46] [Separation of gravitational perturbations of Myers-Perry black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21591)
*Jun-Kun Zhao,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs gauge-invariant, separated solutions for linearized gravitational perturbations of 5D asymptotically flat Myers–Perry black holes with two unequal spins, leveraging Killing–Yano symmetry to define angular operators; it proves completeness of angular harmonics, enables local metric reconstruction, and recovers known singly-spinning limits—providing a foundation for quasinormal mode analysis and stability studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous, separable, and complete framework for analyzing linearized gravitational perturbations of five-dimensional rotating black holes with two independent angular momenta—crucial for assessing their stability and computing quasinormal modes.

Method: Uses gauge-invariant formalism and separation of variables; exploits the Killing–Yano tensor to construct a first-order angular operator commuting with a second-order one; applies Carter interchange to link angular and radial equations; establishes completeness of angular eigenfunctions at real frequencies; verifies local metric reconstructibility from tidal components.

Result: Full set of five separated, gauge-invariant polarizations with shared radial amplitude; explicit angular operators induced by Killing–Yano symmetry; completeness proof for angular harmonics; local metric reconstruction guaranteed; recovery of Santos's results in singly spinning limit ($m_\psi = 0$, even $\psi$-parity).

Conclusion: A complete, symmetry-based separation framework for gravitational perturbations of doubly-spinning 5D Myers–Perry black holes is established—enabling future rigorous stability analysis and quasinormal mode calculations.

Abstract: We study linearized gravitational perturbations of five-dimensional asymptotically flat Myers--Perry black holes with two unequal spins. We construct separated solutions for the five coupled gauge-invariant polarizations for arbitrary azimuthal mode numbers. The key observation is that the Killing--Yano symmetry induces a first-order angular operator that, together with a commuting second-order operator, determines the angular profiles of all five gravitational polarizations. The Carter interchange relates the scalar angular and radial equations, and the five tidal components of each separated solution share a single radial amplitude. We prove completeness of the angular harmonics at real frequencies and show that the resulting physical perturbations admit local metric reconstruction. In the singly spinning limit, we recover Santos's angular operators and radial equation for perturbations with $m_\psi=0$ and even parity under $\psi\to-\psi$. These results provide a framework for studying quasinormal modes and gravitational stability of five-dimensional rotating black holes with two independent spins.

</details>


### [47] [A separation criterion for chiral systems and its application to Plebański--Demiański perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21603)
*Hideo Furugori,Daisuke Yoshida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides a constructive, necessary-and-sufficient criterion for local separation of first-order chiral systems coupling two complex doublets, using Gram decomposition and differential transport; it applies the framework to Einstein–Maxwell perturbations on rotating, charged, accelerating Plebański–Demiański spacetimes, yielding explicit separated radial/angular equations and spectral properties of angular operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous, constructive framework for separability of first-order chiral coupled systems—beyond standard second-order or scalar cases—particularly for physically relevant gravitational and electromagnetic perturbations in general rotating, charged, and accelerating black hole spacetimes with cosmological constant and NUT charge.

Method: Combines Gram decomposition of coupling sources (with decoupled coordinate dependence) and a differential transport test; restricts to mode-independent complex matrix transformations with unit determinant; analyzes uniqueness of Hermitian coefficient matrix reconstruction from sources; constructs explicit algebraic transformation for radiative Einstein–Maxwell perturbations; derives separated ODEs and angular endpoint indices via Sturm–Liouville theory under regularity assumptions.

Result: A necessary-and-sufficient local separability criterion for the specified chiral system class; explicit separated radial and angular equations for coupled Einstein–Maxwell perturbations on Plebański–Demiański backgrounds; uniqueness of Gram decomposition under genericity; angular operators shown to be self-adjoint with discrete spectrum and complete orthonormal eigenbasis under stated regularity and real mode parameter conditions.

Conclusion: Local separation of such chiral first-order systems is fully characterized by the interplay of source structure (via Gram decomposition) and geometric transport properties; the framework successfully extends separability theory to physically rich, highly nontrivial spacetimes, enabling rigorous spectral analysis and paving the way for stability studies and mode decomposition in generalized black hole backgrounds.

Abstract: We give a constructive criterion for local separation of first-order chiral systems coupling two complex doublets. It combines a Gram decomposition of the coupling sources with separate coordinate dependence and a differential transport test. For recoverable, nondegenerate source families, the criterion is necessary and sufficient within the specified class of mode-independent complex matrix transformations with unit determinant. For a fixed differential operator and admissible Gram decomposition, we identify conditions under which the sources uniquely determine the Hermitian coefficient matrix required for separation. As an application, we construct an explicit algebraic transformation and derive separated radial and angular equations for coupled radiative Einstein--Maxwell perturbations of rotating, charged Plebański--Demiański backgrounds with acceleration, NUT charge, and a cosmological constant, on specified local patches. Its Gram decomposition is unique under additional generic assumptions. We also derive angular endpoint indices for Bonnor and Misner identifications with conical deficits or excesses. Under the stated regularity assumptions, each specified self-adjoint angular realization at fixed real mode parameters has a discrete spectrum and a complete orthonormal eigenbasis.

</details>


### [48] [Self-interacting $p$-forms and the shear-free anisotropic cosmological model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07288)
*Nicolas Hernandez,Ben D. Normann,Yeinzon Rodriguez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends eFLRW cosmologies to non-Abelian p-forms (p=0–4) with self-interactions, identifying a unique untuned solution: a 3-form field with quadratic potential V(H²)=A+BH² balances anisotropic spatial curvature without fine-tuning—only in Bianchi class A, for both uncharged and appropriately charged cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To relax the cosmological principle by exploring minimal symmetry requirements—specifically isotropic expansion without vorticity—beyond standard FLRW, using eFLRW models where anisotropic curvature is balanced by anisotropic stress while shear vanishes.

Method: Analyzes non-Abelian p-form fields (p = 0,…,4) with self-interactions in eFLRW spacetimes; imposes vanishing shear and balance between anisotropic curvature and anisotropic stress; combines stress decay scaling a⁻² with gauge-field continuity equation and potential-dependent energy-momentum contributions; examines Bianchi class A backgrounds and distinguishes charged vs. uncharged 3-forms via connection choice.

Result: General non-Abelian p-forms require fine-tuned potentials to balance anisotropic curvature; exception is the 3-form H^a with V(H²) = A + B H², which achieves exact balance without tuning—uncharged in Bianchi class A, and charged with suitably chosen connection; this configuration mimics a cosmological constant plus curvature-like dark matter.

Conclusion: The quadratic 3-form model is the sole identified candidate enabling natural (untuned) realization of isotropic expansion in eFLRW cosmology, offering a geometrically motivated unification of dark energy and dark matter phenomenology without introducing shear or violating the observed isotropy of cosmic expansion.

Abstract: The cosmological principle may require more than observations demand: what is needed is isotropic expansion without vorticity. Extended Friedmann-Lemaitre-Robertson-Walker (eFLRW) cosmologies realise this with a non-vanishing anisotropic spatial curvature that is balanced by an anisotropic stress while the shear vanishes. Thorsrud and others investigated such scenarios for free differential $p$-forms. Here we extend the analysis to non-Abelian $p$-forms ($p=0,\dots,4$) with self interactions. The balance requires the anisotropic stress to decay as $a^{-2}$, $a$ being the cosmological scale factor, which we combine with the gauge-field continuity equation. The general results indicate that, in the presence of a potential, fine-tuning of the same is needed in order to balance the curvature. Notably, this is not the case for a $3$-form field $\underline{H}^a$ with a potential term $V(\underline{H}^2)=A+B\,\underline{H}^2$, where $A$ and $B$ are constants: in Bianchi class A, an uncharged 3-form balances the curvature with this potential without any tuning, and a charged one does so for a suitably chosen connection. This is the only candidate we have found where the potential does not require tuning. It is also an interesting candidate as it can be interpreted as a cosmological constant in combination with a dark-matter sector mimicking curvature.

</details>

### [49] [Confinement--Higgs Complementarity Viewed through Relative Quantum Reference Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19519)
*Kei-Ichi Kondo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates confinement-Higgs complementarity using quantum reference frames (QRFs) derived from gauge and scalar fields, provides a relational interpretation of scalar-color observables via exact continuum/lattice identities, explains the compatibility of a CDG-reduction-derived nonlocal boundary with Fradkin-Shenker continuity and Osterwalder-Seiler analyticity, and contrasts it with a Wilson-rooted observable that yields exact results at zero plaquette coupling but does not induce such a boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual tensions between confinement and Higgs phases in non-Abelian gauge-scalar theories by providing a unified, gauge-invariant, relational framework grounded in quantum reference frames—bypassing gauge-fixing artifacts and clarifying the physical meaning of nonlocal observables reported in lattice simulations.

Method: Construct two independent quantum reference frames—one from the gauge field (color frame) and one from the scalar field (Higgs frame)—and analyze their relative orientation; derive exact continuum and lattice identities for scalar-color observables; interpret the CDG-reduced nonlocal separation line as a relational boundary between QRFs; verify analyticity and continuity constraints using Fradkin-Shenker and Osterwalder-Seiler theorems; analytically compute a Wilson-rooted control observable at zero plaquette coupling.

Result: A relational QRF formulation of confinement-Higgs complementarity; a gauge-invariant interpretation of the CDG-derived nonlocal boundary as arising from QRF misalignment; proof that this boundary respects Fradkin-Shenker continuity and Osterwalder-Seiler analyticity for local gauge-invariant observables; demonstration that a Wilson-rooted observable remains local and exact at zero coupling without generating such a boundary.

Conclusion: Confinement and Higgs physics are not mutually exclusive phases but complementary descriptions tied to different quantum reference frames; the emergence of nonlocal structure (e.g., CDG separation lines) reflects relational degrees of freedom between QRFs, not intrinsic nonlocality; gauge-invariant observables must be interpreted relationally to reconcile continuum intuition with lattice findings.

Abstract: We formulate confinement--Higgs complementarity in terms of the relative orientation between two independently constructed quantum reference frames (QRFs): one derived from the gauge field and the other from the scalar field. Exact continuum and lattice identities give a relational interpretation of the scalar--color observables used in recent gauge-fixing-free $SU(2)$ simulations. In particular, we provide a QRF interpretation of the additional gauge-invariant nonlocal separation line obtained from the Cho--Duan--Ge (CDG) reduction reported in lattice simulations of the radially fixed fundamental $SU(2)$ gauge--scalar model. We explain why such a candidate nonlocal boundary is compatible with Fradkin--Shenker continuity and Osterwalder--Seiler analyticity for the appropriate class of local gauge-invariant observables. Separately, we show that a Wilson-rooted control observable admits an exact results at zero plaquette coupling and that it does not cause such a nonlocal separation line.

</details>


### [50] [Wilsonian renormalization group flow in QED with the Pauli term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19778)
*Sorato Nagao,Hiroshi Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the RG flow of QED with a Pauli term using GFERG; it identifies a large-$N_f$ fixed point with vanishing gauge coupling but nonzero Pauli coupling and photon anomalous dimension $\gamma_A = -1$, but shows this fixed point disappears at small $N_f$ (e.g., $N_f=1$) due to one-loop fermion mass renormalization, leaving the Pauli coupling irrelevant and induced as $\kappa_{\text{ind}} \propto e^3$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether QED augmented by a Pauli term admits nontrivial UV fixed points—potentially enabling asymptotic safety—while preserving gauge symmetry exactly via the GFERG framework.

Method: Applies the gradient flow exact renormalization group (GFERG) to QED with a Pauli coupling $\kappa_\tau \bar{\psi} \sigma_{\mu\nu} F_{\mu\nu} \psi$; enforces exact preservation of the gauge Ward--Takahashi identity; computes beta functions and anomalous dimensions in leading large-$N_f$ approximation and includes $N_f$-independent one-loop fermionic corrections (notably additive mass renormalization $\propto \kappa^2$).

Result: A nontrivial fixed point exists in the large-$N_f$ limit: $(e_*, \kappa_*) = (0, \kappa_* \neq 0)$ with $\gamma_A = -1$ and three relevant directions; however, at finite $N_f$, especially $N_f = 1$, this fixed point is destroyed by $\kappa^2$-driven mass renormalization—no fixed point exists for weak $\kappa$; instead, $\kappa_\tau$ flows to an induced value $\kappa_{\text{ind}}(e_\tau) \propto e_\tau^3$, while $e_\tau^2$ remains marginally irrelevant.
