Conclusion: The inclusion of the Pauli term does not generically yield a viable asymptotically safe QED at low flavor numbers; the putative UV fixed point is an artifact of the large-$N_f$ approximation and is invalidated by subleading but $N_f$-independent quantum corrections, reinforcing the marginal irrelevance of the gauge coupling and irrelevance of the Pauli coupling in physical ($N_f=1$) QED.

Abstract: We study the renormalization group (RG) flow of quantum electrodynamics (QED) with a Pauli term, $\kappa_\tau\Bar{\psi}\sigma_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}\psi$, by using the gradient flow exact renormalization group (GFERG). In the GFERG, the gauge Ward--Takahashi identity takes the conventional form and is exactly preserved, and the gauge coupling flows only through the photon anomalous dimension~$\gamma_A$. In the leading large-$N_f$ approximation, the vacuum polarization induced by the Pauli coupling yields a nontrivial fixed point with~$e_*=0$, $\kappa_*\neq0$, and~$\gamma_A=-1$. This fixed point has three relevant directions, as does one of the fixed points of the asymptotically safe QED proposed by Gies and Ziebell. At finite~$N_f$, however, the $N_f$-independent one-loop effects on the fermion line, in particular the additive mass renormalization proportional to~$\kappa^2$, eliminate this fixed point unless $N_f$ is at least several tens; for~$N_f=1$ there is no fixed point at weak Pauli coupling. Along the full flow for~$N_f=1$, $\kappa_\tau$ is irrelevant and is attracted to an induced value~$\kappa_{\text{ind}}(e_\tau)$, which is proportional to~$e_\tau^3$ at weak coupling, while $e_\tau^2$ is marginally irrelevant as in QED without the Pauli term.

</details>


### [51] [On reflection positivity of lattice chiral fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20084)
*Zhiyao Lu,Shu-Heng Shao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that the overlap Weyl fermion lacks reflection positivity in 2D and 4D at finite lattice spacing, preventing standard OS reconstruction of a unitary quantum theory; in 2D, it also exhibits boundary-induced nonlocality due to gravitational anomaly, unlike the reflection-positive overlap Dirac fermion—though its axial symmetry fails to be both compact and reflection-invariant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the viability of the overlap Weyl fermion as a consistent lattice regularization for chiral gauge theories, particularly regarding foundational quantum mechanical requirements (reflection positivity, locality with boundaries) and symmetry structure.

Method: Analytical examination of reflection positivity using the Osterwalder–Schrader framework; explicit analysis of boundary behavior and locality of the lattice action in two dimensions; comparison with the overlap Dirac fermion, including symmetry analysis under reflection and classification within the Onsager algebra.

Result: Overlap Weyl fermion is not reflection positive in 2D and 4D at finite lattice spacing; in 2D, its action becomes nonlocal with a boundary, signaling obstruction from gravitational anomaly; overlap Dirac fermion is reflection positive but admits no axial symmetry that is simultaneously compact and reflection-invariant—only noncompact reflection-invariant or compact non-reflection-invariant variants exist, both embedded in an Onsager algebra.

Conclusion: The overlap Weyl fermion fails key axiomatic requirements for defining a local, unitary quantum field theory on the lattice, undermining its use in rigorous chiral gauge theory constructions; the tension between compactness, reflection invariance, and anomaly constraints highlights deep structural incompatibilities in lattice chiral fermions.

Abstract: We point out that the overlap Weyl fermion, the spacetime lattice realization of a Weyl fermion underlying Lüscher's construction of lattice chiral gauge theories, is not reflection positive in two and four dimensions at finite lattice spacing. Consequently, the standard Osterwalder--Schrader reconstruction does not yield a Hilbert space with a self-adjoint Hamiltonian. In two dimensions, we further show that the overlap Weyl fermion cannot be cut open: the lattice action becomes nonlocal in the presence of a boundary, as expected from the gravitational anomaly of the continuum field theory. By contrast, the overlap Dirac fermion is known to be reflection positive. However, its compact axial symmetry is not invariant under the reflection, whereas a reflection-invariant axial symmetry is noncompact, and both are part of an Onsager algebra.

</details>


### [52] [Thermal deep inelastic scattering, universality and complex geodesics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21127)
*Andrei Parnachev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses holography to study current two-point functions in thermal strongly coupled QFTs in the deep inelastic scattering (DIS) regime, revealing universal near-lightcone behavior governed by conformal collider bounds and stress-tensor three-point couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the crossover from vacuum-dominated to thermally dominated dynamics in DIS at finite temperature within strongly coupled QFTs, where standard perturbative methods fail.

Method: Holographic computation of thermal current two-point functions in the high-virtuality (deep inelastic) regime; analysis of near-lightcone limits of thermal CFT correlators; connection to geodesic propagation in effective near-lightcone geometry.

Result: Thermal response functions exhibit universality: their vacuum–thermal crossover is controlled by a single channel-dependent coefficient tied to stress-tensor three-point couplings and conformal collider bounds; saturation of either bound restores vacuum form in the near-lightcone limit; low-temperature absorption exponent maps to a complex geodesic in the dual geometry.

Conclusion: The DIS kinematics at finite temperature in strongly coupled theories is governed by universal, geometrically encoded data—specifically stress-tensor OPE coefficients—and bridges thermal field theory, holography, and conformal collider physics.

Abstract: In deep inelastic scattering (DIS), a lepton scatters off a target hadron whose structure is probed by an exchanged photon with large spacelike virtuality. We consider two-point functions of currents in strongly interacting quantum field theories at finite temperature in this kinematic regime and use a holographic description to compute them. At large virtuality, the crossover from vacuum-dominated to thermally dominated physics corresponds to a transition from exponentially suppressed absorption to power-law behavior of the response functions. This regime is related to a particular near-lightcone limit of thermal CFT correlators. We show that the response functions exhibit a large degree of universality: the dependence on the three-point couplings of currents to the stress tensor enters through a channel-dependent coefficient multiplying the kinematic parameter that controls the relative size of vacuum and thermal effects. The same coefficient controls conformal collider bounds; when either bound is saturated, the corresponding response function takes its vacuum form in the near-lightcone limit. Furthermore, the absorption exponent in the low-temperature regime is related to a complex geodesic propagating in the near-lightcone geometry.

</details>


### [53] [Schwarzschild scattering from CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18826)
*Paolo Arnaudo,Benjamin Withers*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a correspondence between retarded CFT correlators on ℝ×S² (dual to a small AdS₄ black hole) and flat-space Schwarzschild S-matrix elements—including quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum—by identifying boundary operators whose dual bulk fields approximate plane waves at high frequency; it further proposes a toy model explaining how dissipative QNMs emerge transiently in otherwise Hermitian systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between AdS/CFT correlators and physical flat-space scattering data—particularly the Schwarzschild S-matrix and its quasinormal modes—by leveraging the flat-space limit (L → ∞) of AdS, and to clarify the emergence of dissipation (QNMs) in unitary quantum systems.

Method: Analytic continuation and asymptotic analysis of retarded CFT correlators dual to bulk fields near the AdS₄ boundary; identification of boundary operators whose dual bulk fields reduce to plane-wave solutions at high frequency; derivation of reflection coefficients from correlators; matching to flat-space partial-wave S-matrix and QNM spectrum via the L → ∞ limit; construction of a solvable toy model illustrating transient non-Hermitian behavior in a fully Hermitian setup.

Result: A rigorous analytic map from retarded CFT correlators (on ℝ×S², dual to small AdS₄ black hole) to Schwarzschild S-matrix elements and QNM frequencies in asymptotically flat space; explicit demonstration for spin-0 (conformally coupled), spin-1, and spin-2 fields; derivation of the flat-space limit behavior of reflection coefficients; a minimal Hermitian toy model reproducing short-time dissipative QNM-like dynamics.

Conclusion: Quasinormal modes and flat-space scattering data are encoded in boundary CFT correlators via a controlled high-frequency, large-radius limit; dissipation in QNMs arises effectively from time-limited boundary conditions or spectral truncation in an underlying unitary theory—not from fundamental non-Hermiticity.

Abstract: We show that retarded CFT correlators on $\mathbb{R}\times \mathbb{S}^2$ in a state described by the small black hole in AdS$_4$ can be used to derive asymptotically flat Schwarzschild partial-wave S-matrix elements, including its spectrum of quasinormal modes. This is achieved by identifying a class of operators for which the dual bulk fields behave as plane waves near the AdS boundary for sufficiently high frequencies. For such operators there is a simple analytic relation between CFT correlators and reflection coefficients which behaves gracefully in the flat space limit, $L\to \infty$. These include spin-1, spin-2, and conformally coupled scalars. We provide a simple toy model that captures how dissipative QNMs can appear for short times inside Hermitian systems, closely resembling the small black hole in AdS.

</details>


### [54] [Gravitational Memory Correlators and BMS Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18827)
*Enrico Herrmann,Ian Moult,Brett Oertel,Julio Parra-Martinez,Biswajit Sahoo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes n-point memory and mixed energy-memory correlators for scalar decay into gravitons using the in-in formalism in perturbative quantum gravity, and verifies the supertranslation flux balance relation linking memory, energy, and Bondi mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize asymptotic symmetry constraints—specifically supertranslation invariance—on infrared-finite observables in quantum gravity, by studying memory and energy correlators in gravitational radiation from scalar decay.

Method: In-in formalism at leading order in perturbative quantum gravity; computation of n-point memory and mixed energy-memory correlators for scalar-to-graviton decay.

Result: Compact analytic expressions for arbitrary-n correlators; explicit verification of the supertranslation flux balance relation among memory, energy flux, and Bondi mass detectors.

Conclusion: The supertranslation symmetry is realized as a constraint on infrared-finite observables, confirming consistency between soft theorems, memory effects, and asymptotic symmetries in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We compute the n-point memory and mixed energy-memory correlators for scalar decay into gravitons using the in-in formalism at leading order in perturbative quantum gravity. We obtain compact results for arbitrary n and explicitly verify the supertranslation flux balance relation between memory, energy, and Bondi mass detectors, realizing asymptotic symmetry constraints on infrared-finite observables.

</details>


### [55] [A Bound on Wormhole Probing Time from Holographic Multi-Entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19035)
*Takanori Anegawa,Norihiro Iizuka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper proves that increasing the number of parties in multipartite entanglement measures cannot indefinitely extend the time during which wormhole growth is probed—there exists a finite upper bound on probing time for nonrotating BTZ black holes, regardless of partition details.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bipartite entanglement entropy saturates and thus fails to track eternal wormhole growth; tripartite multi-entropy extends probing but it remains unclear whether arbitrarily many parties could extend probing time indefinitely.

Method: Uses the multiway maximin prescription for holographic entanglement entropy, assumes regular spacetime-stationary networks as representatives, and constructs a comparison network that assigns the entire wormhole interior to a single boundary party to derive an upper bound on probing time.

Result: For nonrotating BTZ black holes, any fixed finite boundary partition—regardless of number, sizes, or arrangement of parties—has a common finite upper bound on the time during which multi-entropy can probe wormhole growth; beyond this bound, no maximin-selected network enters the black-hole interior.

Conclusion: Multipartite entanglement entropy cannot indefinitely probe wormhole growth; its probing capability is fundamentally limited by geometry and causality, with a universal finite time cutoff for BTZ black holes.

Abstract: Bipartite entanglement entropy eventually saturates and therefore cannot track the continued growth of an eternal wormhole. Recently, it was shown that genuine tripartite multi-entropy can probe wormhole growth even after bipartite entanglement entropies have saturated. This naturally raises the question: can increasing the number of parties extend the probing time indefinitely? Adopting the multiway maximin prescription and assuming that its selected representatives are regular spacetime-stationary networks, we prove that the answer is no for nonrotating BTZ black holes. We establish a common finite upper bound on the probing time for every fixed finite boundary partition, independent of the number, sizes, and arrangement of its parties. For any network on any Cauchy slice, there exists a comparison network that assigns the entire central region containing the wormhole interior to a single party. A network longer than this comparison network cannot minimize length on that slice. The central length required by a stationary network connecting the two boundaries grows with time, whereas the central length of the comparison network remains uniformly bounded. At sufficiently late times, such a network therefore cannot minimize length on any Cauchy slice containing it and is excluded from the maximin prescription. Consequently, beyond this bound, no network selected by maximin enters the black-hole interior.

</details>


### [56] [Projected DKP spin-1 sectors in a $(2+1)$-dimensional static wormhole: Self-duality, helicity content, and spectral correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19058)
*Luis B. Castro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the projected spin-1 Duffin–Kemmer–Petiau (DKP) equation in 2+1 dimensions, revealing dual irreducible representations (projected vector and antisymmetric tensor) that select conjugate self-dual curl sectors corresponding to opposite massive helicities. Applied to static wormholes, it uncovers a previously missed normalizable bound state at ω² = M² in the first-order DKP system, shows Darboux equivalence between master and circular sectors, and establishes physically meaningful quasinormal mode pairing (ω, −ω*) in the complex momentum plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the physical interpretation of the two inequivalent 3×3 irreducible representations of the spin-1 DKP algebra in 2+1 dimensions, and to revisit the static wormhole scattering problem with full first-order DKP structure—beyond the standard second-order master-variable reduction—which may miss spectral features like bound states or alter quasinormal mode symmetries.

Method: Algebraic projection of the spin-1 DKP equation onto irreducible subspaces; identification of algebraic duality between projected vector and antisymmetric tensor representations; analysis of self-dual covariant-curl constraints; derivation and spectral analysis of the first-order radial DKP system for static wormholes; comparison with the second-order master-variable equation; application of Darboux transformation theory to relate sectors; pole analysis of reflection/transmission amplitudes in the complex radial-momentum plane.

Result: The two 3×3 DKP representations are algebraically dual and correspond to conjugate self-dual curl sectors yielding opposite massive helicities in flat space. In the static wormhole background, the first-order DKP system hosts an exceptional normalizable bound state at ω² = M² absent in the master-variable approach. The master and circular sectors are Darboux-equivalent, preserving scattering probabilities. Quasinormal modes split into two pole families in the complex k-plane, enforcing the physical symmetry (ω, −ω*) rather than naive ±ω.

Conclusion: The full first-order DKP framework reveals essential spectral and symmetry structures—such as helicity-selective representations, hidden bound states, and physically mandated quasinormal mode pairing—that are obscured in second-order reductions; thus, the DKP formalism provides a more complete and physically faithful description of spin-1 fields in topologically nontrivial 2+1D spacetimes.

Abstract: We use the projected spin-1 Duffin--Kemmer--Petiau (DKP) equation in $2+1$ dimensions to clarify the physical content of the irreducible $3\times3$ representations. The projected antisymmetric tensor is algebraically dual to the projected vector, and the two twin representations select conjugate self-dual covariant-curl sectors, which reduce to opposite massive helicities in the flat-space limit. We then apply this structure to the static wormhole problem previously studied with a single irreducible representation. The first-order radial DKP system contains an exceptional normalizable bound-state branch at $\omega^2=M^2$ that is absent from the master-variable reduction, whereas the generic master and circular sectors are related by a Darboux transformation. The latter preserves the transmission and reflection probabilities, while the quasinormal modes are organized into two pole families in the complex radial-momentum plane, leading to the physical pairing $(\omega,-\omega^*)$ rather than a global $\pm\omega$ prescription.

</details>


### [57] [Holographic Entanglement after a Global Quench in Noncommutative Yang-Mills Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19307)
*Elahe Hassanpour,Komeil Babaei Velni,M. Reza Mohammadi Mozaffar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies holographic entanglement entropy and mixed-state correlations after a global quench in 4D N=4 noncommutative Yang-Mills theory using Vaidya geometry; it identifies distinct early-time growth regimes (logarithmic, linear, quadratic) for strip-shaped regions aligned with the noncommutative direction, links growth coefficients and velocities to noncommutativity and energy density, analyzes equilibration via butterfly velocity and competing extremal surface branches, and shows mutual information cancels leading log terms while entanglement wedge cross section exhibits linear growth governed by the same entanglement velocity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum entanglement and correlations evolve dynamically after a global quantum quench in a noncommutative gauge theory—specifically, how spacetime noncommutativity modifies universal features of holographic entanglement growth, saturation, and information spreading.

Method: Holographic computation using a Vaidya-type black brane geometry dual to a global quench in four-dimensional N=4 noncommutative Yang-Mills theory; extremal surface prescriptions for entanglement entropy and entanglement wedge cross section; analytic and geometric analysis of time-dependent minimal surfaces for strip-shaped and disjoint spatial regions.

Result: Two early-time entanglement growth regimes (logarithmic then quadratic) for narrow strips, with logarithmic coefficient ∝ energy density × (noncommutative scale)²; intermediate linear growth for wide strips, with entanglement velocity increasing with noncommutativity; butterfly velocity governs continuous extremal surface evolution during equilibration; competing surface branches affect physical saturation time; mutual information removes leading width-independent log term; entanglement wedge cross section shows same linear-growth regime as entropy when connected wedge dominates.

Conclusion: Noncommutativity qualitatively enriches the time evolution of holographic information measures: it introduces new scaling regimes, modifies characteristic velocities (entanglement and butterfly), and alters equilibration dynamics through geometric competition—demonstrating that spacetime nonlocality encoded in noncommutativity has direct, quantifiable signatures in quantum information dynamics.

Abstract: The time evolution of holographic entanglement entropy and mixed-state correlations following a global quench in four-dimensional N=4 noncommutative Yang-Mills theory is investigated using a Vaidya geometry. For strip-shaped regions extended along a noncommutative direction, two distinct early-time growth regimes emerge, depending on the relation between the shell-crossing and noncommutative scales: logarithmic growth, with a coefficient proportional to the injected energy density and the square of the noncommutative scale, followed by quadratic growth. For sufficiently wide strips, an intermediate linear-growth regime appears, governed by an entanglement velocity that increases with noncommutativity. The approach to equilibrium is also examined, revealing the role of the butterfly velocity in the continuous evolution of extremal surfaces, while competing branches can modify the physical saturation time. For two disjoint strips, the leading width-independent logarithmic contribution cancels in the mutual information, whereas the entanglement wedge cross section exhibits an intermediate linear-growth regime governed by the same entanglement velocity, provided the connected entanglement wedge remains dominant.

</details>


### [58] [Off-shell one-loop counterterms of Palatini gravity with an independent background connection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20295)
*F. T. Brandt,G. A. Menezes*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop divergences of pure gravity in the first-order (Palatini) formulation using background field method in Goldberg variables, revealing novel counterterms involving nonmetricity invariants beyond curvature-squared terms; all counterterms vanish on-shell and are removable off-shell via field redefinitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically study quantum corrections in the Palatini formulation of gravity—where connection and metric are independent fields—and assess whether standard geometric invariants (e.g., Gauss-Bonnet) suffice for renormalization when the background connection is nonmetric.

Method: Background field method in Goldberg variables; independent background connection and metric; computation of one-loop divergences for two-, three-, and four-point graviton functions in arbitrary dimension and general gauge; verification of background Ward-Takahashi identities.

Result: Two-point function divergences require nonmetricity-dependent invariants (e.g., non-topological Gauss-Bonnet for nonmetric connections); three- and four-point functions in Feynman-de Donder gauge fully determine the counterterm Lagrangian up to fourth order in nonmetricity; all counterterms vanish on-shell and are field-redefinition equivalent to zero off-shell.

Conclusion: The Palatini formulation of pure gravity is one-loop on-shell finite and off-shell effectively renormalizable by field redefinitions; its quantum structure crucially depends on nonmetricity, distinguishing it from the metric formulation despite shared on-shell equivalence.

Abstract: We compute the one-loop divergences of pure gravity in the first-order (Palatini) formulation, using the background field method in Goldberg variables, with a background connection independent of the background metric. Background Ward-Takahashi identities are verified at one loop. The divergences of the two-point functions, obtained in arbitrary dimension and gauge, require, besides the curvature-squared terms, invariants built from the nonmetricity of the background connection; in particular, the Gauss-Bonnet combination, which is not topological for a nonmetric connection, does not suffice. Three- and four-point functions in the Feynman-de Donder gauge complete the counterterm Lagrangian up to fourth order in the nonmetricity and provide parameter-free tests of it. All the counterterms vanish on shell, as in the metric formulation, and off shell they can be removed by field redefinitions.

</details>


### [59] [Phase Structure and Thermodynamic Geometry of STU Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20328)
*Fil Simovic,Kenan Scavennec,David Kubiznak,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the thermodynamics and geometric structure of 4D STU black holes in gauged N=8 supergravity, deriving exact thermodynamic quantities, equation of state, phase diagram with up to two critical points, and using Ruppeiner geometry (with T and V as fluctuation variables) to infer attractive/repulsive microscopic interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the thermodynamic phase structure and microstructure of STU black holes in gauged N=8 supergravity via geometric thermodynamics, particularly for non-rotating, pairwise-equal-charge configurations.

Method: Derives closed-form thermodynamic quantities in terms of physical charges; determines equation of state and phase diagram; constructs Ruppeiner metric using temperature and thermodynamic volume as fluctuation variables; computes and renormalizes Ruppeiner curvature to probe microstructure.

Result: Identifies a rich phase structure with up to two critical points when charge ratio is large enough; maps regions of parameter space where renormalized Ruppeiner curvature indicates dominant attractive or repulsive microscopic interactions.

Conclusion: Ruppeiner geometry—appropriately renormalized—serves as an effective probe of black hole microstructure for STU black holes, revealing that thermodynamic interaction character (attractive vs. repulsive) depends sensitively on charge configuration.

Abstract: We study the thermodynamics of four-dimensional STU black holes, a family of charged solutions of gauged N = 8, SO(8) supergravity, from the perspective of geometric thermodynamics. We focus on non-rotating solutions carrying pairwise-equal charges, obtaining closed-form expressions for the thermodynamic quantities directly in terms of the physical charges, and use them to determine the equation of state and associated phase structure. We also construct the Ruppeiner geometry for the STU black hole, taking the temperature and thermodynamic volume as fluctuation variables, and study its renormalized curvature as a probe of the black hole microstructure. We find a rich phase structure with two critical points present whenever the charge ratio is sufficiently large, and identify regions in parameter space associated with attractive and repulsive microscopic interactions.

</details>


### [60] [Holographic Homogeneous Black Branes from Anisotropic Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20673)
*Markus A. G. Amano*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs homogeneous anisotropic black brane solutions in Einstein and Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton gravity across arbitrary dimensions, using radial scaling compatible with spatial Lie algebra; it derives thermodynamic quantities (mass, entropy, Euclidean action) and exact quasinormal spectra for scalar probes, and numerically explores relaxation dynamics in Nil, Solv, and Bianchi VI geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and analyze black brane solutions with homogeneous anisotropic scaling—beyond asymptotically locally AdS spacetimes—enabling study of novel thermodynamic and dynamical behavior in strongly coupled anisotropic systems.

Method: Imposes radial scaling symmetry compatible with spatial Lie algebra to reduce field equations to ODEs + algebraic constraints; constructs explicit neutral/electric/magnetic/dyonic solutions including Bianchi-type and Ricci nilsoliton-supported families; computes thermodynamics via Euclidean action and Smarr-like relations; solves Klein–Gordon equation exactly for scalar probes in specific scaling geometries; performs numerical analysis of quasinormal modes and relaxation in Nil, Solv, and Bianchi VI backgrounds.

Result: New families of anisotropic black branes in arbitrary D, including explicit 5D Bianchi and higher-D Ricci nilsoliton solutions; exact purely damped quasinormal spectra for a class of scaling geometries; asymptotic mass bound for probe scalars; numerical evidence for distinct momentum- and geometry-dependent relaxation behaviors (e.g., non-universal dynamical exponents).

Conclusion: A general framework is established for constructing and analyzing thermodynamic and linear response properties of homogeneous anisotropic scaling geometries—not restricted to AdS asymptotics—opening avenues for holographic modeling of anisotropic quantum critical systems.

Abstract: We construct homogeneous anisotropic black branes in Einstein gravity and Einstein--Maxwell--dilaton theory in arbitrary spacetime dimensions. By imposing compatibility between radial scaling and the spatial Lie algebra, we reduce the field equations to radial equations and algebraic constraints on the scaling exponents, curvature, and matter fields. We construct neutral, electric, magnetic, and dyonic solutions, including explicit five-dimensional Bianchi geometries and higher-dimensional families supported by Ricci nilsolitons. We investigate the thermodynamics of these solutions, obtaining their Euclidean action, mass, and entropy scaling. We also study the dynamics of a massive probe scalar, deriving an asymptotic mass bound and an exact purely damped quasinormal spectrum for a particular class of scaling geometries. Numerical calculations further explore the mass, dynamical exponent, and momentum dependence of scalar relaxation in Nil, Solv, and Bianchi type VI geometries. Our results establish a framework for studying thermodynamic and dynamical properties of homogeneous anisotropic scaling geometries that are generally not asymptotically locally anti-de Sitter.

</details>


### [61] [Doubly-spinning Black Saturns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20701)
*Jay Armas*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a six-parameter family of stationary, asymptotically flat black hole–black ring configurations ('black Saturn') in five-dimensional vacuum Einstein gravity, identifies the first regular asymptotically flat solution with two extremal disconnected horizons, analyzes thermodynamic equilibrium conditions, and confirms consistency with known limits and blackfold predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and analyze a new class of exact solutions in 5D vacuum general relativity that realize coexisting black hole and black ring horizons—particularly aiming to achieve simultaneous extremality of both horizons, which had not been realized before in asymptotically flat vacuum spacetimes.

Method: Analytic construction of a six-parameter family of exact stationary, axisymmetric, asymptotically flat solutions to the 5D vacuum Einstein equations using inverse scattering and/or solitonic techniques (implied by reference to Pomeransky–Sen'kov and Myers–Perry limits); thermodynamic analysis via horizon geometries, surface gravities, and angular velocities; comparison with blackfold effective theory predictions.

Result: Discovery of the first regular asymptotically flat vacuum Einstein solution with two extremal, disconnected horizons (doubly extremal black Saturn); demonstration that non-extremal black Saturns can satisfy thermodynamic equilibrium (zero net angular momentum transfer), but doubly extremal ones cannot; verification of smooth limits to known solutions (Myers–Perry, Pomeransky–Sen'kov, singly spinning Saturn); quantitative agreement with blackfold phase diagram predictions.

Conclusion: The black Saturn solution significantly extends the zoo of exact higher-dimensional black objects, provides a concrete realization of multi-horizon extremality in vacuum gravity, reveals a thermodynamic obstruction to equilibrium in the doubly extremal case, and validates blackfold methods as predictive tools for strongly interacting black object phases.

Abstract: We construct a six-parameter family of stationary, asymptotically flat solutions of five-dimensional vacuum Einstein gravity describing a spherical black hole surrounded by a black ring, with rotation in both independent planes. In a specific regime both horizons can become extremal, thereby providing the first example of a regular asymptotically flat vacuum Einstein solution with two extremal disconnected horizons. We also show that while non-extremal black Saturns can be in thermodynamic equilibrium, the doubly extremal Saturn cannot. The solution admits the doubly spinning Myers-Perry, the Pomeransky-Sen'kov black ring, and the singly spinning black Saturn as regular limits. We also show agreement with earlier blackfold predictions for the phase diagram of these solutions.

</details>


### [62] [A note on Padmanabhan's path-integral duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20731)
*P. Fernandez de Cordoba,J.M. Isidro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Padmanabhan's length-action duality to quantum field theory by deriving a nonlocal scalar field theory from the particle propagator, recovering Klein-Gordon behavior at low energies and exhibiting exponential UV suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Padmanabhan's duality principle — invariance under S → L₀²/S for proper length S — from point particles to quantum field theory.

Method: Starts from the particle propagator and its Schwinger proper-time representation; constructs a wave operator for a scalar field implementing the duality; derives a nonlocal operator involving modified Bessel functions; analyzes IR/UV limits.

Result: A nonlocal scalar field theory emerges, reproducing standard Klein-Gordon dynamics in the infrared and showing exponential ultraviolet suppression.

Conclusion: Padmanabhan’s duality can be consistently extended to QFT via a nonlocal field-theoretic framework, offering a natural UV regularization mechanism.

Abstract: We develop a field-theoretic implementation of Padmanabhan's principle of invariance under the duality $\mathcal S \rightarrow {L_0^2}/{\mathcal S}$, where ${\mathcal S}$ is the proper length (the action integral along a particle's trajectory) and $L_0$ is a quantum of length. Starting from the particle propagator and its Schwinger proper-time representation, we derive the wave operator associated with the duality transformation in terms of a scalar field, rather than a point particle. The resulting theory is governed by a nonlocal operator involving modified Bessel functions. We analyse the infrared and ultraviolet regimes, demonstrate the recovery of standard Klein-Gordon theory at low energies, and show the appearance of exponential ultraviolet suppression. Our analysis provides a natural extension, to quantum field theory, of Padmanabhan's duality for point particles.

</details>


### [63] [From Quasinormal Mode Poles to Branch Cuts at Finite $N$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20912)
*Navid Abbasi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that the first quantum (1/N²) correction to holographic thermal correlators in AdS₅ black brane introduces branch cuts—rather than poles—from integrated quasinormal mode (QNM) pairs, leading to a new late-time decay regime G(t) ∼ t^α e^{−Ct^{3/4}} that dominates over classical QNM decay and closes the spectral gap.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum corrections beyond the strict large-N (classical gravity) limit modify the analytic structure and late-time dynamics of thermal retarded correlators in holography—specifically whether finite-N effects introduce qualitatively new physics absent at tree level.

Method: Computes the one-loop bulk correction to a scalar retarded correlator on the planar AdS₅ black brane background; analyzes pole structure of the loop diagram before and after momentum integration; identifies QNM-pair singularities, their accumulation at zero frequency, and derives late-time asymptotics via saddle-point analysis incorporating thermal Boltzmann suppression.

Result: The 1/N² correction converts isolated QNM poles into branch cuts due to integration over continuous loop momentum; an infinite family of cuts accumulates at ω = 0, closing the spectral gap; late-time behavior is governed by a moving saddle yielding G(t) ∼ N⁻² t^α e^{−Ct^{3/4}}, C > 0, slower than any classical QNM exponential decay.

Conclusion: The large-N expansion is nonuniform at late times: bulk quantum fluctuations generate a distinct dynamical regime not captured by classical QNMs, fundamentally altering the long-time thermal response and spectral structure of holographic correlators.

Abstract: In the strict large-$N$ limit, holographic thermal retarded correlators are typigcally meromorphic, with their late-time behavior determined by isolated quasinormal mode (QNM) poles. We show that the first bulk-loop correction, of order $1/N^2$, qualitatively changes this structure. In a scalar theory on the planar AdS$_5$ black brane, a one-loop bulk diagram generates poles at sums of two QNM frequencies at fixed loop momentum $q$; integration over the continuous momentum turns these QNM-pair poles into branch cuts. At vanishing external spatial momentum, for pairs built from QNM branches with opposite real parts, the leading light-cone contributions cancel at large momentum, and the residual QNM-pair frequency scales as $q^{-1/3}$. Consequently, an infinite family of cuts accumulates at zero frequency, while all tree-level QNM poles remain at finite nonzero frequencies. The first finite-$N$ correction therefore closes the gap set by the classical QNM spectrum. Competition between the decreasing QNM-pair damping and thermal Boltzmann suppression produces a moving saddle and a finite-$N$ contribution $G(t)\sim N^{-2}t^\alpha e^{-Ct^{3/4}},\, C>0,$ which decays more slowly than the leading classical QNM contribution. Thus the large-$N$ expansion is nonuniform at late times, and bulk quantum fluctuations generate a distinct late-time regime beyond the classical QNM decay.

</details>


### [64] [Soft momentum expansion about the Stokes topological limit of Bogoliubov coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21271)
*Daniel J. H. Chung,Nidhi Sudhir,Junqi Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analytically computes the soft-momentum expansion of particle production amplitude near the Stokes topological limit in time-dependent cosmological backgrounds, using exponentially improved asymptotics and discrete symmetries of a power-law dispersion relation; it yields a nonperturbative leading-order momentum dependence and a semi-analytic estimate of particle number density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand nonperturbative particle production in time-dependent cosmological backgrounds via the Stokes phenomenon in analytically continued Bogoliubov transformations, particularly near the recently discovered Stokes topological limit.

Method: Applies exponentially improved asymptotic expansions incorporating automorphisms tied to the angular step function across Stokes sectors, combined with discrete symmetries of the dispersion relation ω_k²(z) = C(k) + A zⁿ (n even), to compute the soft-momentum expansion of the production amplitude.

Result: Explicit derivation of the leading soft-momentum dependence of the particle production amplitude—nonperturbative in the coupling A—and a semi-analytic expression for the resulting particle number density.

Conclusion: The Stokes topological structure governs the nonperturbative momentum scaling of particle production in this class of time-dependent backgrounds, enabling precise analytic control beyond standard WKB or perturbative methods.

Abstract: It is known that particle production in time-dependent cosmological backgrounds can be computed using the Stokes phenomena occurring in Bogoliubov transformations with an analytically continued time coordinate. We analytically compute the soft momentum expansion of the particle production amplitude about the Stokes topological limit that was recently discovered in this context. The computational technique relies on an exponentially improved asymptotic expansion that involves automorphisms in the space of functions defined partly by the angular step function intrinsic to the Stokes topological sectors. We combine this with special discrete symmetries tied to the time dependence of the dispersion relation $\omega_k^2(z) = C(k) + A z^n$ for even $n$ to explicitly compute the leading momentum dependence of the amplitude which is nonperturbative in $A$. We also use this result to give a semi-analytic estimate of the number density of particles in this class of scenarios.

</details>


### [65] [Carroll Double Copy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21388)
*Sucheta Majumdar,Silvia Nagy,Giorgio Pizzolo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a double copy for Carroll gravity by taking the c → 0 limit of the Lorentzian convolutional double copy, systematically handling electric/magnetic Carroll sectors, introducing regularisation for magnetic on-shell fields, and incorporating a Goldstone-type dressing to ensure Carroll boost covariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the double copy paradigm—established for Lorentzian gravity/Yang–Mills—to the ultra-relativistic (Carrollian) limit where c → 0, preserving symmetry compatibility and equations of motion.

Method: Starts from the convolutional double copy in Lorentzian spacetime, takes the Carrollian limit c → 0, distinguishes electric/magnetic Carroll sectors, introduces regularisation for time-independent magnetic configurations, and adds a Goldstone-type field to ensure covariance under Carroll boosts; develops formalism first with scalar toy models, then applies to linearised Carroll Yang–Mills and Carroll gravity.

Result: A consistent, symmetry-respecting double copy dictionary between Carroll Yang–Mills and Carroll gravity at the linearised level, covering all combinations of electric/magnetic parent theories, with explicit regularisation and boost-covariant field dressing.

Conclusion: The double copy survives the Carroll limit but requires sector-specific adaptations—regularisation for magnetic fields and Goldstone dressing for boost covariance—demonstrating its robustness beyond Lorentzian settings and opening avenues for Carrollian scattering amplitudes and geometric formulations.

Abstract: We construct a double copy for Carroll gravity as the ``square'' of Carroll Yang-Mills theory at the linearised level. Starting from the convolutional double copy prescription in Lorentzian spacetime, we take the Carrollian limit $c \rightarrow 0$ to establish a double copy dictionary compatible both with symmetries and the corresponding equations of motion. Since this limit yields two distinct sectors, namely the electric and magnetic Carroll theories, we systematically investigate double copy constructions involving all possible combinations of these parent theories. To accommodate the time-independent on-shell configurations of the magnetic Carroll fields, we introduce a suitable regularisation procedure for the convolutional double copy. In addition, the covariance of the double copy under Carroll boosts requires an appropriate dressing of the fields, leading to the inclusion of a Goldstone-type object in the construction. We first develop the formalism using toy models involving scalar fields and then apply it to the double copy between linearised Carroll Yang-Mills theory and Carroll gravity.

</details>


### [66] [Non-Topological Chiral Anomaly: The Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18841)
*Rémy Larue,Amaury Marchon,Jérémie Quevillon,Diego Saviot*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies a novel non-topological contribution to the chiral anomaly in thermal media, leading to new transport effects—including the Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect in Weyl semimetals—and establishes a generalized hydrodynamic framework for such anomalies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover previously overlooked non-topological contributions to the chiral anomaly in thermal media, especially for non-gauged axial symmetries, and explore their physical consequences in transport phenomena.

Method: Analytical derivation of the chiral anomaly in a thermal medium using quantum field-theoretic and hydrodynamic methods; identification and characterization of the non-topological term in the axial current continuity equation; application to relativistic hydrodynamics and condensed-matter systems (e.g., Weyl semimetals).

Result: Discovery of a non-topological, electromagnetic-field-independent contribution to the chiral anomaly; prediction of the Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect—generation of an electric field ∝ magnetic field in Weyl semimetals without external electric field; formulation of a broader hydrodynamic framework accommodating such anomalies.

Conclusion: Non-topological chiral anomalies are physically significant and generically modify hydrodynamic constitutive relations, enabling new anomaly-driven transport phenomena in both high-energy and condensed-matter physics.

Abstract: We derive the chiral anomaly in a thermal medium and show that, in addition to the well-established topological piece, the anomaly acquires a previously overlooked non-topological contribution, relevant for non-gauged axial symmetries. Remarkably, this modification of the continuity equation is independent of the electromagnetic fields, thereby giving rise to novel transport effects in relativistic hydrodynamics. We further propose an experimentally accessible new effect in Weyl semimetals which we call the Chiral Magnetoelectric Effect: an electric field proportional to the magnetic field is generated by the non-topological anomaly, even in the absence of an externally applied electric field. Beyond this concrete prediction, our results establish a broader hydrodynamic framework for non-topological chiral anomalies, opening the door to a range of previously unexplored anomaly-induced transport phenomena across condensed-matter and high-energy systems.

</details>


### [67] [Triple Exchange at the Cosmological Collider in Closed Form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20356)
*Taushif Ahmed,Sumit Banik,Syed Mehedi Hasan,Andreas Rapakoulias*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives exact closed-form expressions for the quasi-single-field inflation bispectrum using Feynman-integral methods and multiple polylogarithms, improving upon the Planck template; it shows the latter overestimates amplitude and underestimates mass, and further combines bispectrum and collapsed trispectrum to tighten bounds on field self-coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact, momentum-resolved analytic forms of the quasi-single-field inflation bispectrum—especially for light fields near the Hubble scale—where prior approximations (e.g., Planck’s template) are inaccurate and limit cosmological parameter inference from CMB and LSS surveys.

Method: Applies Feynman-integral techniques in de Sitter space to compute the triple-exchange bispectrum exactly in momenta, expanding around massless and conformally coupled limits using multiple polylogarithms; computes the collapsed trispectrum and jointly constrains self-coupling with the bispectrum.

Result: First closed-form bispectrum expressions valid for arbitrary momenta; quantifies up to 80% overestimation of amplitude and systematic mass bias in the Planck template; demonstrates improved mass and coupling recovery with the new template; shows combined bispectrum–trispectrum analysis tightens self-coupling bounds at weak mixing.

Conclusion: Accurate analytic templates are essential for robust inference of heavy-field masses and couplings from non-Gaussianity; the derived polylogarithmic forms enable precision tests of inflationary particle physics with current and next-generation surveys.

Abstract: Particles with masses near the Hubble scale leave a characteristic pattern in the primordial bispectrum, turning inflation into a cosmological collider. For light fields, where this signal is largest, no closed form is known for the full shape of the quasi-single-field triple exchange. Using Feynman-integral methods, we obtain the first closed-form results, exact in the momenta, as expansions in terms of multiple polylogarithms around the massless and conformally coupled limits. We show that the template used in Planck analyses overestimates the amplitude by up to a factor of 1.8 and underestimates the mass, whereas our new template recovers both, as needed for current and upcoming CMB and galaxy surveys. We also compute the collapsed trispectrum and, combining it with the bispectrum, find that at weak mixing it bounds the self-coupling more tightly.

</details>


### [68] [Stochastic gauge fixing from the holographic renormalization group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19083)
*Soner Albayrak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the stochastic quantization–holographic Wilsonian renormalization dictionary to 4D AdS Yang-Mills theory at tree level, identifying the radial bulk gauge field component with Zwanziger’s gauge-fixing function and radial gauge with Parisi–Wu quantization; it computes finite-cutoff triple- and quadruple-trace gluon couplings in closed form, showing transverse couplings depend only on a single bulk zero mode that vanishes in the flat-space limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify stochastic quantization and holographic Wilsonian renormalization for non-Abelian gauge theories in curved spacetime—specifically, Yang-Mills theory in four-dimensional anti-de Sitter space—and to clarify the role of radial gauge and bulk gauge degrees of freedom in the boundary RG flow.

Method: Applies stochastic (Parisi–Wu) quantization to bulk Yang-Mills in AdS₄, identifies the radial component of the gauge field with Zwanziger's gauge-fixing functional, interprets radial evolution as stochastic time, and computes boundary effective couplings (triple- and quadruple-trace) via tree-level holographic renormalization with finite radial cutoff.

Result: Derives closed-form expressions for finite-cutoff triple- and quadruple-trace gluon couplings; shows transverse couplings depend solely on a single bulk zero mode of the gauge field, which decouples in the flat-space (AdS radius → ∞) limit.

Conclusion: The stochastic quantization framework provides a natural holographic interpretation of Wilsonian RG flow for Yang-Mills theory in AdS₄: radial gauge corresponds to Parisi–Wu quantization, the radial coordinate acts as stochastic time, and the longitudinal bulk propagator encodes the random walk of gauge modes; the resulting boundary couplings are fully determined by bulk zero-mode data, reinforcing the bulk-boundary correspondence at finite cutoff.

Abstract: We extend the dictionary between stochastic quantization and holographic Wilsonian renormalization to Yang-Mills theory in four-dimensional anti-de Sitter space at tree level. The radial component of the bulk gauge field is Zwanziger's gauge-fixing function; radial gauge is Parisi-Wu quantization, whose random walk of gauge modes is the longitudinal bulk propagator. We obtain the triple- and quadruple-trace couplings of gluons at finite cutoff in closed form. On transverse data, their dependence on the bulk gauge resides in a single zero mode, which drops out of the flat-space limit.

</details>


### [69] [Conditional Principle of Least Action: Gauge Algebra and Noether's Second Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19097)
*V. A. Abakumova,S. L. Lyakhovich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Noether's second theorem and its converse by analyzing how gauge symmetry algebras of constraint differential equations relate to those of the action restricted to fields satisfying those constraints, illustrated via linearized gravity with Cotton-equation constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between gauge symmetries of differential constraints on fields and gauge symmetries of the action restricted to those constrained fields, enabling a generalized Noether-type correspondence.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic derivation establishing algebraic relations between the gauge symmetry algebras of the constraint equations and the restricted action; application to linearized gravity with Einstein-Hilbert action and Cotton equation constraints.

Result: A generalized Noether's second theorem and its converse, linking gauge identities of constrained-field actions to the structure of the constraint equations' gauge algebra.

Conclusion: The gauge symmetry algebra of the constraints fully determines (and is determined by) that of the restricted action, yielding a structural extension of Noether’s second theorem applicable beyond standard variational settings.

Abstract: We consider the problem of a conditional extremum of an action within a class of fields constrained by differential equations. For this setup, we establish the interconnection between the gauge symmetry algebra of the equations constraining the admissible fields and that of the action restricted to this class of fields. Based on this relation between the two gauge algebras, we derive an extension of Noether's second theorem and its converse. The general method is illustrated by the example of linearized gravity with the Einstein-Hilbert action and the class of admissible metrics restricted by the Cotton equation.

</details>


### [70] [Renormalization Group Flow from 4d Chern-Simons Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19517)
*Meer Ashwinkumar,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives the one-loop renormalization group (RG) flow for 2D integrable QFTs realized via 4D Chern-Simons theory with surface defects—both order and disorder types—using intrinsic 4D Feynman diagrammatics and BV formalism, recovering the known asymptotically free beta function of the principal chiral model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand RG flow of 2D integrable QFTs from a unified, higher-dimensional perspective—specifically, using the 4D Chern-Simons framework with surface defects—as an alternative to conventional 2D perturbative methods and to clarify the role of symmetry (e.g., affine Kac-Moody) and defect type in governing flow.

Method: Performs first-principles Feynman diagram computations within the 4D Chern-Simons theory on Σ × C; employs order surface defects (with affine Kac-Moody symmetry) for generic 2D integrable models and disorder surface defects (in BV formalism) for the principal chiral model (PCM); computes one-loop amplitudes explicitly and also uses an analogue of analytic torsion in 4D CS theory.

Result: Derives the one-loop RG flow for both general 2D integrable QFTs with order defects and specifically for the PCM without WZ term using disorder defects; reproduces the correct asymptotically free beta function of the PCM via two independent methods.

Conclusion: The 4D Chern-Simons framework with surface defects provides a consistent, symmetry-respecting, and computationally viable setting for deriving RG flows of 2D integrable QFTs from first principles; the distinction between order and disorder defects maps to distinct symmetry realizations and renormalization behaviors.

Abstract: The four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory coupled with surface defects is known to realize two-dimensional integrable quantum field theories (QFTs). We discuss the renormalization group (RG) flow of such two-dimensional integrable QFTs from first-principles Feynman diagram computations intrinsically in the four-dimensional framework. We first derive the one-loop RG flow of two-dimensional QFTs realized by the four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory on $\Sigma \times C$ coupled with the order surface defects supported on $\Sigma $. Here, the affine Kac-Moody symmetry of the defects underlies the one-loop RG flow. We then derive the one-loop RG flow of the principal chiral model (PCM) without Wess-Zumino term, which is realized using disorder surface defects, within the Batalin-Vilkovisky formalism. We obtain the nontrivial one-loop RG flow of the PCM, reproducing its known asymptotically-free beta function, using two complementary methods: by explicitly evaluating one-loop Feynman amplitudes, and by computing an analogue of analytic torsion for four-dimensional Chern-Simons theory.

</details>


### [71] [A proof of Nakamura's conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19558)
*Arsen R. Melikyan,Aleksandr Pinzul*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves Nakamura's conjecture linking the 2D Toda model and the Ernst equation, leveraging Pfaffian solutions from prior work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish Nakamura's conjecture concerning the mathematical connection between the integrable 2D Toda model and exact solutions of the Ernst equation in general relativity.

Method: Analytical proof using Pfaffian solution structures previously derived for the 2D Toda hierarchy, applied to verify functional and structural correspondence with the Ernst equation.

Result: A rigorous proof confirming Nakamura's conjecture: specific Pfaffian solutions of the 2D Toda model indeed yield solutions to the Ernst equation.

Conclusion: The conjectured integrable correspondence between the 2D Toda model and the Ernst equation is validated, strengthening the bridge between soliton theory and exact gravitational solutions.

Abstract: We prove Nakamura's conjecture on the relation between the 2D Toda model and solutions of the Ernst equation, using the Pfaffian solutions obtained in our previous work arXiv:2610.27420.

</details>


### [72] [On $ι$Quiver (BPS) Algebras and (Strict) Partitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19856)
*Jiakang Bao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies ι-quiver algebras of split type associated with quiver BPS algebras of affine type A, proposing their identification with twisted affine Yangians and elliptic quantum toroidal algebras arising from D-branes on C×C²/Zₙ with orientifolds; it establishes minimal presentations (rational case), coideal structures, orientifold-induced quiver realizations, and combinatorial representations—especially strict/symmetric partitions for n=1,2, recovering known BPS counts for n=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and characterize algebraic structures (ι-quiver algebras) that geometrically model BPS states in string theory setups involving D-branes on orbifolds with orientifolds, bridging quiver representation theory, quantum groups, and enumerative geometry.

Method: Algebraic construction of ι-quiver algebras of split type via quiver BPS algebras of affine A-type; analysis of presentations (minimal rational case), coideal subalgebra structures, quiver-theoretic realization under orientifold actions, and explicit construction of combinatorial representations using coloured strict/symmetric partitions.

Result: Established minimalistic presentations for rational ι-quiver algebras; revealed coideal structures; provided quiver-theoretic identification criteria under orientifold actions; constructed combinatorial representations labeled by (coloured) strict/symmetric partitions for n=1,2; recovered known BPS counting for n=1 in appropriate parameter limits.

Conclusion: ι-quiver algebras of split type provide a unifying algebraic framework for twisted affine Yangians and elliptic quantum toroidal algebras in orientifolded D-brane systems; their representation theory connects to partition combinatorics and BPS state counting, especially for low-rank orbifolds (n=1,2).

Abstract: We consider the $\iota$quiver algebras of split type associated with the quiver BPS algebras of affine type A. They are supposed to give twisted affine Yangians and (elliptic) $\iota$quantum toroidal algebras that could possibly arise from D-branes on $\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^2/\mathbb{Z}_n$ with orientifolds. We mention some properties of these algebras, such as the minimalistic presentations in the rational cases and the coideal structures. We also investigate how one might identify these algebras from the quiver theories with orientifold actions. Moreover, we discuss some representations with combinatorial bases of these algebras. In particular, the algebras with $n=1,2$ in certain limits of the parameters have representations labelled by (coloured) strict/symmetric partitions, which in the case of $n=1$ recovers the known BPS counting of certain theories.

</details>


### [73] [Rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models from a tetrality web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18831)
*António Antunes,Jin Chen,Junchen Rong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models using chiral symmetry folding, $S_3$-based 'moving up/down' procedures, revealing a tetrality web; it identifies 24 boundary states (18 factorized + 6 transmitting), computes their energy transmission ($35/48$), boundary entropies, bulk correlations, operator spectra, and full annulus amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and classify rational conformal interfaces between distinct 2D CFTs—specifically Ising and tricritical Ising models—by exploiting chiral symmetry folding and coset extensions with $S_3$ automorphism, thereby uncovering structural relations ('tetrality web') among related theories.

Method: Uses folded boundary state construction via differing chiral symmetries; implements 'moving up' to an auxiliary coset with $S_3$ automorphism yielding twisted Cardy states, and 'moving down' to the $S_3$-invariant subalgebra; pulls back Cardy states to the physical tensor product; computes annulus amplitudes, energy transmission, boundary entropy ($g$-function), and operator content.

Result: Identifies 24 conformal boundaries: 18 factorized and 6 transmitting interfaces; the latter match four transposition- and two 3-cycle-twisted boundaries in the largest theory, include Gaiotto's RG wall and its duality-line fusion; all six transmitting interfaces share identical energy transmission coefficient $35/48$ but are distinguished by boundary entropy, bulk correlation functions, and operator spectra; full annulus partition functions are explicitly calculated.

Conclusion: The $S_3$-driven tetrality web provides a unifying framework for rational interfaces between Ising and TIM models; the six transmitting interfaces realize the regular representation of the TIM fusion ring, demonstrating that identical energy transport can coexist with richly distinct boundary CFT data—highlighting the necessity of full modular data (not just transmission) for interface classification.

Abstract: We construct rational conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising (TIM) models by using different chiral symmetries to prepare their folded boundary states. We use a moving up procedure which extends the product theory to an auxiliary coset with an $S_3$ automorphism group, where permutations give twisted Cardy states. We complement this with a moving down approach which reduces to the $S_3$ invariant algebra. Overall, these procedures uncover a tetrality web relating four different theories. Pulling back ordinary Cardy states of the smallest theory in the web to the physical tensor product system gives eighteen factorized interfaces and six transmitting interfaces. The latter agree with four transposition and two 3-cycle twisted boundaries in the largest theory. They include Gaiotto's RG wall and the interface obtained by fusing an Ising duality line into it. The six interfaces carry the regular action of the TIM fusion ring and have the same energy transmission coefficient, $35/48$. Their boundary entropies, bulk correlations and operator spectra distinguish them. We calculate these observables and the complete annulus amplitudes between the twenty four boundaries.

</details>


### [74] [Constants and Nonperturbative Corrections in $V^{5,2}$ Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18832)
*Seyed Morteza Hosseini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes perturbative and nonperturbative (exponentially small) corrections to the effective twisted superpotentials of two 3D N=2 theories linked to cyclic quotients of the Sasaki–Einstein manifold V^{5,2}; it identifies modular structures (Eichler integrals of weight-4 Eisenstein series) governing exponential remainders, derives convergent integral representations and explicit Clausen/ζ(3) evaluations for rank-independent constants, and reconciles results via M-theory localization and squashed-sphere limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve subleading nonperturbative structure—beyond the universal N^{3/2} scaling—in 3D N=2 supersymmetric gauge theories dual to M-theory on quotients of V^{5,2}, particularly distinguishing between free- and fixed-locus orbifold actions and their impact on twisted superpotentials.

Method: Perturbative expansion and Borel resummation of twisted superpotentials; exact evaluation of rank-independent constants via convergent integrals; identification of exponential remainders with Eichler integrals of weight-four Eisenstein series; high-precision numerical validation using Bethe ansatz solutions; comparison with M-theory localization on squashed S^3 and bulk AdS_4×V^{5,2}/ℤ_n geometry.

Result: Convergent integral representations and explicit Clausen-function/ζ(3) expressions for rank-independent constants at integer parameters; conjectured exponential remainders as Eichler integrals for k,N_f ∈ {1,3}; determination of minimal modular levels and natural boundaries for the associated Eisenstein primitives; recovery of Chern-Simons shifted rank and constant from b→0+ limit of Z_{S_b^3}; generalized localization prescription incorporating fixed-locus contributions reproduces flavored-theory result.

Conclusion: The two theories share a common modular architecture in their nonperturbative coefficient laws (governed by weight-4 Eisenstein series), but differ in perturbative shifts and exponential corrections—resolving distinctions invisible at leading N^{3/2} order—and are unified under a refined M-theoretic localization framework accommodating both free and fixed-locus orbifold actions.

Abstract: We study perturbative and exponentially small corrections to the on-shell effective twisted superpotentials of two three-dimensional $\mathcal N=2$ theories associated with free and fixed-locus cyclic quotients of the non-toric Sasaki-Einstein manifold $V^{5,2}$. These are the two-node Chern-Simons theory at levels $(k,-k)$ and the one-node flavored theory with $N_f$ fundamental pairs, respectively. At superconformal chemical potentials, we propose the Chern-Simons counterpart of the all-order perturbative expression for the flavored theory and obtain convergent integral representations for the rank-independent constants of both theories. At integer parameters, these constants admit explicit evaluations involving finite sums of Clausen functions and $\zeta(3)$, while their large-parameter expansions Borel sum to the defining integrals. At $k=1,3$ and $N_f=1,3$, we conjecture complete exponential remainders expressed through Eichler integrals of weight-four Eisenstein series. Supported by high-precision Bethe solutions, the resulting completions coincide at unit quotient order but differ at order three. We determine the minimal modular levels of the Eisenstein generators and establish convergence and natural boundaries for their primitives. Within the relative M-theory localization prescription, a complementary bulk analysis recovers the Chern-Simons shifted rank and constant in the $b\to0^+$ limit of the squashed-sphere partition function. We propose a generalization incorporating fixed-locus contributions that yields the corresponding flavored-theory result. The two theories therefore share a modular structure in their nonperturbative coefficient laws, but distinct perturbative shifts and exponential corrections resolve differences invisible in their common leading $N^{3/2}$ behavior.

</details>


### [75] [Quadratic Sum Rules for Scalar Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18836)
*Karol Kampf,Jiří Novotný,David Podivín,Jonah Stalknecht*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper discovers new quadratic-sum rules linking scalar amplitudes across different multiplicities, derived via recursion, kinematic shifts, and symbolic regression; these rules generalize from the nonlinear sigma model to string-inspired extensions, inverse KLT matrix diagonal elements, and planar scalar theories with nonderivative interactions and general propagators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover novel amplitude constraints inspired by the success of Kleiss–Kuijf and BCJ relations—and their string monodromy generalizations—in governing partial amplitudes.

Method: Combines on-shell recursion, kinematic shifts (e.g., momentum or Mandelstam-variable deformations), and symbolic regression (data-driven discovery of algebraic identities) to identify and verify quadratic-sum relations.

Result: New quadratic-sum rules connecting scalar amplitudes of varying multiplicity; validated for the nonlinear sigma model and extended to its string-inspired variants, diagonal entries of the inverse KLT matrix, and broad classes of planar scalar theories with arbitrary nonderivative interactions and general propagators.

Conclusion: Quadratic-sum rules constitute a previously unrecognized structural feature of scalar amplitudes, significantly expanding the landscape of amplitude relations beyond linear BCJ/KK-type constraints and enabling new analytic and computational leverage in effective field theories and string theory.

Abstract: Kleiss--Kuijf and Bern--Carrasco--Johansson relations, and their string monodromy generalizations, have long provided some of the most powerful and useful constraints on partial amplitudes. Motivated by their success, we uncover a new class of quadratic-sum rules that connect scalar amplitudes at different multiplicities. Combining recursion, kinematic shifts, and symbolic regression, we establish them for the nonlinear sigma model and its string-inspired extensions. We then extend these relations to diagonal elements of the inverse Kawai--Lewellen--Tye matrix and subsequently to planar scalar theories with nonderivative interactions and general propagators.

</details>


### [76] [Generalised global symmetries of 6d SCFTs on a circle from the blow-up equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18837)
*William Harding,Noppadol Mekareeya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes 6d (1,0) SCFTs compactified on a circle (with possible twist) via their 5d blow-up equations, establishing a precise map between 6d and 5d frames—including the KK/instanton mass ratio r—and shows that higher-form symmetries, mixed anomalies, and 2-groups are encoded in fluxes through the exceptional curve of the blown-up C²; tensor fluxes encode Green–Schwarz couplings and fractional exponents yield mixed anomalies with KK U(1), gravity, flavor, and SU(2)_R; forced fluxes distinguish 2-groups from mixed anomalies, illustrated for twisted su(4) theories and SU(2)-gauged E₁ constructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize higher-form symmetries, mixed anomalies, and 2-group structures of 6d (1,0) SCFTs upon circle compactification—especially in twisted setups—by leveraging the geometric and algebraic structure of their 5d effective descriptions via blow-up equations.

Method: Construct explicit 6d-to-5d frame maps including the Kaluza–Klein/instanton mass ratio r; interpret flux sectors on the exceptional curve of the resolved C² as ’t Hooft sectors of 5d 1-form symmetries; extract mixed anomalies from fractional exponents of Green–Schwarz coupling weights on tensor fluxes; determine whether forced fluxes imply non-split 2-groups or only mixed anomalies; apply method to twisted su(4) models and SU(2)-gauged E₁ theories (yielding SU(4)_0, pure Spin(8), and untwisted so(8) KK theory).

Result: Higher-form symmetries, mixed anomalies, and 2-groups of circle-compactified 6d (1,0) SCFTs are fully determined by flux data on the exceptional curve; concrete 2-group structures are identified—for example, a non-split Z₄ 2-group in twisted su(4)₀,₄+4Λ², versus mixed anomalies only in su(4)₂+4Λ² and su(4)₆+2Λ²; for SU(2)-gauged E₁ theories, fluxes reveal symmetry extensions, accidental symmetry breaking, locking backgrounds, and reduction of the 6d S₃-invariant 3-group to a 5d 2-group.

Conclusion: The geometry of the blown-up C² provides a unified, computable framework for classifying global symmetry structures—including 2-groups and mixed anomalies—in circle-reduced 6d (1,0) SCFTs; this bridges 6d anomaly inflow, 5d effective dynamics, and M-theory geometry, enabling systematic classification beyond Lagrangian descriptions.

Abstract: We study 6d $(1,0)$ SCFTs on a circle, possibly twisted, through the blow-up equations of their 5d descriptions. For every theory we consider, we construct the explicit map between the 6d and 5d frames, including the ratio $r$ of the Kaluza--Klein (KK) mass to the instanton mass, which depends on the frame and not only on the twist. Our main result is that the higher-form symmetries of these theories, their mixed anomalies and 2-groups can all be read off from the fluxes through the exceptional curve of the blown-up $\mathbb{C}^2$. The flux sectors are the 't$~$Hooft sectors of the 5d 1-form symmetries. On the tensor fluxes, the 6d Green--Schwarz couplings fix the weights, whose fractional exponents we read as the mixed anomalies of the 6d 2-form symmetry with the KK $\mathrm{U}(1)$, gravity, flavour and $\mathrm{SU}(2)_R$. The fluxes forced in each sector decide between a 2-group and a mixed anomaly. For instance, the twisted frames $\mathfrak{su}(4)_{0,4}+4\Lambda^2$ carry a non-split $\mathbb{Z}_4$ 2-group, while, in $\mathfrak{su}(4)_2+4\Lambda^2$ and $\mathfrak{su}(4)_6+2\Lambda^2$, the instanton symmetry is locked to the flavour centre and only a mixed anomaly remains. The same method applies to the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ gauging of two, three and four copies of the $E_1$ theory, which describes, respectively, $\mathrm{SU}(4)_0$, pure $\mathrm{Spin}(8)$ and the untwisted $\mathfrak{so}(8)$ KK theory. There, the fluxes exhibit the extensions of 1-form symmetries inherited from the $E_1$ 2-groups, identify the states that break each accidental 1-form symmetry and the background to which it is locked, and give the 5d 2-group to which the $S_3$-invariant 6d 3-group reduces.

</details>


### [77] [Classification of Conformal Ising/Tricritical Ising Interfaces Preserving $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$ Symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19129)
*Bufan Zheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models preserving the $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$ chiral algebra, identifying 24 fundamental boundary states via the folding trick and Cardy condition; among them, 6 break full product Virasoro symmetry and are explicitly constructed, with annulus partition functions computed and linked to topological defect lines and Gaiotto’s RG domain wall.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and construct conformal interfaces between two distinct rational CFTs (Ising and tricritical Ising) that preserve an extended chiral symmetry ($\\mathcal{W}_3^{\\mathbb{Z}_2}$), bridging structural CFT theory with physical relevance in RG flows and defect physics.

Method: Employs the folding trick to map interfaces to boundary states in the folded CFT; imposes the Cardy condition to select physically consistent boundary states; identifies the unique NIM-rep compatible with the bulk modular invariant; constructs explicit interface operators for non-factorized cases using topological defect line dressings; computes annulus partition functions.

Result: Finds exactly 24 fundamental conformal interfaces; 18 are factorized (preserving full product Virasoro symmetry), 6 are non-factorized (breaking it); all six non-factorized interfaces are explicitly realized, their annulus partition functions derived, and shown to be related by topological defect line actions; one matches Gaiotto's proposed RG domain wall.

Conclusion: The classification is complete and exact within the $\\mathcal{W}_3^{\\mathbb{Z}_2}$-preserving sector; the six non-factorized interfaces constitute novel, symmetry-breaking conformal interfaces with direct connections to renormalization group flows and topological defects, enriching the landscape of exactly solvable boundary/interface CFT data.

Abstract: We classify conformal interfaces between the Ising and tricritical Ising models that preserve the chiral algebra $\mathcal{W}_3^{\mathbb{Z}_2}$. Using the folding trick, we formulate the problem in terms of boundary states satisfying the Cardy condition and determine the unique NIM-rep compatible with the bulk partition function. We obtain a complete set of 24 fundamental boundary states, of which 18 correspond to factorized interfaces preserving the full product Virasoro symmetry, while the remaining six break this symmetry. We construct these six interfaces explicitly and determine their annulus partition functions. They are related by topological defect line dressings, and one coincides with the RG domain wall proposed by Gaiotto.

</details>


### [78] [Large-$N$ expansion in SU($N$) Yang-Mills-like theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19144)
*Marco Bochicchio,Mauro Papinutto,Francesco Scardino*

Main category: hep-th
