TL;DR: This paper analyzes the large-N expansion in SU(N) vs. U(N) Yang-Mills-like theories, showing that the standard 't Hooft expansion—based on smooth Riemann surfaces—is incomplete for SU(N); it must be extended to include singular, pinched topologies (e.g., pinched tori and higher-genus pinched surfaces) to correctly describe correlators of higher-twist quasi-partonic operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve subtleties in the large-N expansion when passing from U(N) to SU(N) YM-like theories, particularly because the decoupled U(1) sector in U(N) must be separated via renormalization, revealing missing topological contributions in SU(N).

Method: Systematic topological analysis of Feynman diagrams in the large-N limit; comparison of diagrammatic genus expansion in U(N) vs. SU(N); identification and classification of singular (pinched) Riemann surfaces arising from renormalized SU(N) correlators, especially for higher-twist quasi-partonic operators.

Result: The standard 't Hooft expansion over smooth orientable Riemann surfaces is generically incomplete for SU(N) and renormalized U(N) YM-like theories; extra contributions from singular pinched surfaces—beyond smooth genus-g surfaces—are required, generalizing earlier results for twist-2 operators to higher-twist quasi-partonic operators.

Conclusion: The large-N expansion in SU(N) YM-like theories necessitates an extension of the topological classification beyond smooth Riemann surfaces to include singular (pinched) topologies, reflecting the nontrivial interplay between renormalization, U(1) decoupling, and gauge group reduction from U(N) to SU(N).

Abstract: It has been known for fifty years that bare U($N$) pure Yang-Mills (YM) theory admits the large-$N$ 't Hooft expansion for the $n$-point connected correlators of gauge-invariant single-trace operators, where the corresponding Feynman diagrams are topologically classified by the sum over the genus $g$ of $n$-punctured smooth orientable closed Riemann surfaces. In fact, 't Hooft expansion extends verbatim to U($N$) YM-like theories whose Lagrangians only involve fields valued in the U($N$) Lie algebra - including supersymmetric (SUSY) extensions of pure YM theory such as $\mathcal{N}=1,4$ SUSY YM. The aim of the present paper is to systematically work out the topology of the large-$N$ expansion in SU($N$) - as opposed to U($N$) - YM-like theories, where a number of subtleties inevitably arise. Indeed, since the U($1$) inside the U($N$) YM-like theories decouples, the large-$N$ U($N$) and SU($N$) expansions should either essentially coincide or intrinsically differ - in the latter case necessarily by extra - possibly singular - topologies, as the 't Hooft expansion in U($N$) YM-like theories already exhausts all the smooth topologies. In fact, we demonstrate that 't Hooft smooth topologies are actually generically incomplete in the large-$N$ expansion of both the SU($N$) and renormalized U($N$) YM-like theories, where renormalization inevitably separates the SU($N$) sector that is interacting from the U($1$) sector that is free. Specifically, we interpret the extra topologies that arise in SU($N$) theories for correlators of higher-twist quasi-partonic operators as singular pinched closed surfaces, thus extending our previous investigation of correlators of twist-$2$ operators for which extra pinched tori arise.

</details>


### [79] [The $\mathbb{Z}_2$ twist line defect of the 3D Ising CFT on the fuzzy sphere from multi-target density matrix renormalization group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19233)
*Jing-Hao Jin,Yang Qi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a multi-target DMRG method on the fuzzy sphere to study the ℤ₂ twist line defect in the 3D Ising CFT, enabling high-precision extraction of defect operator dimensions beyond exact diagonalization limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the size limitations of exact diagonalization for studying conformal defects on the fuzzy sphere, and to enable precision spectroscopy of defect operators in the 3D Ising CFT.

Method: Imposes antiperiodic boundary conditions on the ℤ₂-odd fermion flavor to realize the twist line defect; employs multi-target DMRG to simultaneously optimize multiple low-lying eigenstates per symmetry sector in a shared matrix-product state basis; uses displacement operator, its protected descendant, and bulk stress tensor for finite-size correction analysis and continuum extrapolation.

Result: Obtains defect spectrum up to 49 spinless orbitals (2s+1 = 25); confirms spin-parity selection rule and conformal multiplet structure; refines dimensions of several known defect primaries and resolves four previously unobserved higher-lying ones.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that fuzzy sphere + multi-target DMRG is a scalable, precise framework for quantitative studies of conformal defects in 3D CFTs.

Abstract: The fuzzy-sphere regularization provides a powerful framework for studying three-dimensional (3D) conformal field theories (CFTs) through radial quantization, but conformal defects have so far been studied with it only by exact diagonalization (ED) of small systems. In this work we realize the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ twist line defect of the 3D Ising CFT on the fuzzy sphere by imposing antiperiodic boundary conditions on the $\mathbb{Z}_2$-odd fermion flavor, and solve it with a multi-target density matrix renormalization group (DMRG) algorithm that optimizes several low-lying states of each symmetry sector in a common matrix-product basis. This gives the defect spectrum at system sizes well beyond the reach of ED (up to $2s+1=25$, or $49$ spinless orbitals). The spectrum obeys the spin-parity selection rule of the twist line and organizes into conformal multiplets along the line. Using the displacement operator as an intrinsic ruler, its protected descendant as a test, and the bulk stress tensor as an independent cross-check, we identify the leading finite-size corrections and obtain controlled continuum extrapolations, which sharpen the dimensions of several known defect primaries and resolve four higher-lying ones. This study establishes the fuzzy sphere combined with multi-target DMRG as a framework for precision studies of conformal defects.

</details>


### [80] [On the Spontaneous symmetry breaking of Conformal Gravity to Weyl and Einstein Gravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19338)
*Partha Nandi,Gerasimos Kouniatalis,Stelios Stefas,George Zoupanos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the role of the special-conformal connection in an SO(2,4) gauge-theoretic formulation of gravity, showing it remains auxiliary and its elimination yields Weyl gravity (Weyl-squared action) on a source-free branch without ad hoc geometric constraints; it further analyzes linearized spectra around de Sitter space and discusses symmetry breaking to Einstein gravity via an antisymmetric scalar 6-plet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the special-conformal connection remains auxiliary upon promoting a spontaneously broken conformal gauge theory to a full dynamical Yang-Mills-Higgs theory, and to identify conditions under which eliminating it yields Weyl gravity.

Method: Uses an SO(2,4) gauge-theoretic framework; retains the special-conformal connection as independent; derives its algebraic equation of motion on a source-free projected branch; eliminates it to obtain the Weyl-squared action; extends analysis to full local Yang-Mills-Higgs theory using the invariant bilinear form of 𝔰𝔬(2,4); performs linearized spectral analysis around de Sitter spacetime; examines further symmetry breaking via an adjoint 15-plet and antisymmetric scalar 6-plet.

Result: The special-conformal connection is proven auxiliary in both the pure gravitational and full Yang-Mills-Higgs extensions due to the structure of the 𝔰𝔬(2,4) bilinear form; its elimination yields the four-dimensional Weyl-squared action (up to Euler density) without imposing the Schouten relation; linearized spectrum reveals massless and partially massless spin-2 modes at m_PM² = 2Λ/3; antisymmetric scalar 6-plet breaking connects Weyl-gravity phase to Einstein gravity.

Conclusion: The special-conformal connection remains auxiliary in the fully dynamical SO(2,4) Yang-Mills-Higgs theory, and Weyl gravity emerges naturally on the source-free branch; partial masslessness and the transition to Einstein gravity are systematically realized through hierarchical symmetry breaking.

Abstract: Does the special-conformal connection remain an auxiliary field when a spontaneously broken conformal gauge theory is promoted to a fully dynamical Yang-Mills-Higgs theory, and under what conditions does its elimination yield Weyl gravity? We address these questions within an $\mathrm{SO}(2,4)$ gauge-theoretic formulation of gravity. Revisiting the gravitational sector selected by spontaneous symmetry breaking through an adjoint $15$-plet, we retain the special-conformal connection as an independent field and demonstrate that, on a source-free projected branch, its algebraic equation of motion determines it in terms of the Schouten tensor. Eliminating this connection yields the four-dimensional Weyl-squared action, up to the Euler density, without imposing the Schouten relation as an additional geometrical constraint. We then extend the analysis to the standard local Yang-Mills-Higgs theory and show that the special-conformal connection remains auxiliary, owing to the structure of the invariant bilinear form of $\mathfrak{so}(2,4)$, which prevents the appearance of an independent kinetic operator. The additional gauge and scalar sectors can modify its algebraic solution, so the exact Weyl reduction is restricted to the source-free branch. We further investigate the linearised spectrum of the reduced Weyl theory around de Sitter spacetime, identifying massless and partially massless spin-2 branches, with the latter occurring at $m_{\mathrm{PM}}^2=2\Lambda/3$. Finally, we discuss the subsequent symmetry breaking induced by an antisymmetric scalar $6$-plet and its role in connecting the Weyl-gravity phase to Einstein gravity.

</details>


### [81] [The Goldstone--Wilczek Charge on the Rim](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19464)
*Laurence A. Jacobs*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies charge transfer in the Kaplan--Sen lattice disk under magnetic flux, showing that a unit charge crossing at Φ=π is registered not by a change in the number of negative eigenvalues of the rim operator, but by a simple pole—its extremal eigenvalue diverging—whose sign flip encodes the edge charge jump.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological charge transfer induced by magnetic flux threading a finite lattice disk is encoded in the boundary (rim) operator, particularly whether spectral flow of the rim operator reflects the known Goldstone--Wilczek edge charge quantization.

Method: Analytical derivation using Schur complement to define the rim operator; spectral analysis of its extremal eigenvalue under flux variation; numerical verification on finite disks (R = 11, 19, 34) of the Kaplan--Sen lattice.

Result: The rim operator’s count of negative eigenvalues remains invariant over the flux cycle; instead, its extremal eigenvalue λ_ext exhibits a simple pole at Φ = π, with sign flip Δsign λ_ext = ±2 corresponding to ΔQ_edge = ±1; the residue scales linearly with disk radius and factorizes into inner-edge–rim coupling and inverse mode velocity.

Conclusion: Charge transfer across the disk is topologically encoded in the pole structure—not spectral flow—of the rim operator, revealing a novel bulk-boundary correspondence where singular spectral behavior, not eigenvalue crossing, signals quantized charge transport.

Abstract: A solenoid flux $\Phi=2\pi\alpha$ threaded through the center of the Kaplan--Sen lattice disk binds a ground-state charge $Q_{\rm core}=\alpha-\mathrm{round}(\alpha)$, and the edge carries exactly the opposite charge. The edge charge is the Goldstone--Wilczek charge of the edge ladder, $Q_{\rm edge}=-\tfrac12\eta$, with $\eta$ its spectral asymmetry. At $\Phi=\pi$ one unit of charge crosses the disk. We ask how the rim operator, the Schur complement of the disk Hamiltonian on its boundary sites, registers this transfer. Its count of negative eigenvalues never changes over the flux cycle. Instead, the unit of charge enters the rim through a simple pole: the extremal eigenvalue of the rim operator passes through infinity in the same flux interval as the charge jump, with $\Delta Q_{\rm edge}=\tfrac12\Delta\,\mathrm{sign}\,\lambda_{\rm ext}$. The residue factorizes into the coupling of the inner edge mode to the rim and the inverse slope of that mode's level, and grows linearly with the radius. All statements are checked numerically on disks of radius $R=11$, $19$ and $34$.

</details>


### [82] [Local field shifts and the Gribov-Zwanziger horizon functional](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19513)
*Daniel Guimaraes Tedesco*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a Maggiore-Schaden-type re-centering of the Gribov-Zwanziger action using a smooth compactly supported profile, showing horizon functional preservation under coercivity and contour conditions; it extends visibility analysis to non-self-adjoint compressions, distinguishes source darkness from truncation shielding, and confirms tree-level consistency with RGZ propagator structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously assess how re-centering transformations affect the Gribov-Zwanziger framework—particularly horizon constraint preservation, spectral response near the first Gribov horizon, and robustness under gauge-field integration—while clarifying ambiguities in source weighting and truncation artifacts in low-energy QCD effective actions.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis combining functional methods, spectral theory (Faddeev-Popov operator eigenvalues), and rigorous estimates under coercivity and complex contour hypotheses; includes extension to non-self-adjoint compressions, composite operator transport, and a free-space tree-level diagrammatic check.

Result: Re-centering preserves the horizon functional exactly at fixed background and finite cutoff; near the first Gribov horizon, response is governed jointly by the lowest Faddeev-Popov eigenvalue and its source weight; source darkness and truncation-induced shielding are analytically separable in non-self-adjoint settings; composite insertion transport retains regulated Gaussian/resolvent structure; tree-level computation recovers the auxiliary-mass denominator of the RGZ propagator.

Conclusion: The Maggiore-Schaden re-centering is structurally sound within the Gribov-Zwanziger framework under stated analytic conditions; spectral visibility analysis generalizes beyond self-adjoint settings, enhancing interpretability of confinement-related condensates; however, condensate dynamics remain undetermined at tree level.

Abstract: We study a Maggiore-Schaden-like re-centering of the Gribov-Zwanziger action using a smooth compactly supported profile. At fixed background and finite cutoff, under the stated coercivity and contour hypotheses, this transformation preserves the original horizon functional with no residual dependence on the profile. The extension to gauge-field integration requires additional domination assumptions. Near a self-adjoint first Gribov horizon, the response depends on both the lowest Faddeev-Popov eigenvalue and its source weight. We extend this visibility analysis to non-self-adjoint compressions, separating source darkness from truncation-induced shielding, and recall continuum transverse $SU(2)$ examples. Transporting the composite insertion preserves the regulated Gaussian and resolvent structure. A separate free-space tree-level check reproduces the auxiliary-mass denominator of the RGZ propagator, without determining condensate dynamics

</details>


### [83] [Negative and Positive Saddle-Selection Ambiguities in the HTEE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19562)
*H. Ebrahim,M. Ali-Akbari,K. Taheranvarian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the saddle-selection ambiguity in holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE) computed via complexified extremal surfaces in hyperscaling-violating Lifshitz backgrounds, finding that minimal-real-part and vacuum-reduction criteria agree except at an accidental zero, and that the ambiguity persists even in conformal 3+1D cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in selecting the dominant saddle (vacuum-connected vs. vacuum-disconnected) for HTEE in timelike regimes, especially where standard criteria conflict, and to assess its robustness across non-Lorentz-invariant, strongly coupled field theories.

Method: Analytic computation of HTEE using complexified extremal surfaces in hyperscaling-violating Lifshitz geometries in 2+1D and 3+1D bulk spacetimes; derivation and comparison of real parts of vacuum-connected (s_v.c.) and vacuum-disconnected (s_v.d.) saddle contributions; systematic evaluation of the minimal-real-part and vacuum-reduction selection criteria.

Result: The two saddle-selection criteria agree everywhere except at the first accidental zero of Re(s_v.c.); two distinct ambiguity families emerge—'negative' (Re(s_v.c.) = 0, Re(s_v.d.) < 0) and 'positive' (Re(s_v.c.) > 0, Re(s_v.d.) = 0); crucially, the 'positive' family persists in the conformal 3+1D limit, indicating genericity of the ambiguity.

Conclusion: The saddle-selection ambiguity in complexified-geometry-based HTEE is not an artifact of broken symmetries but a generic feature of the prescription, surviving even in Lorentz-invariant, conformal limits.

Abstract: The holographic timelike entanglement entropy (HTEE), computed via complexified extremal surfaces, suffers from a saddle-selection ambiguity: for sufficiently small time intervals (\(\Delta t\to0\)), both a vacuum-connected and a vacuum-disconnected complex extremal surface contribute, and the minimal-real-part criterion and the vacuum-reduction criterion can favor different saddles. We study this ambiguity in strongly coupled field theories whose dual geometries are hyperscaling-violating Lifshitz backgrounds, in which conformal symmetry and Lorentz invariance are broken by the Lifshitz exponent and the hyperscaling-violation parameter. Working in \(2+1\)- and \(3+1\)-dimensional dual field theories, we derive general expressions for the HTEE in the vacuum-connected and vacuum-disconnected saddles, \(s_{v.c.}\) and \(s_{v.d.}\), respectively, and compare their real parts. We find that the two criteria agree everywhere except at the first accidental zero. The ambiguity appears in two distinct forms. In the \emph{negative} family, \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.c.})=0\) and \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.d.})<0\), whereas in the \emph{positive} family, \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.c.})>0\) and \(\operatorname{Re}(s_{v.d.})=0\). The latter shows that the ambiguity survives even in the conformal \(3+1\)-dimensional case. It therefore seems that the saddle-selection ambiguity is a generic feature of the complexified-geometry prescription.

</details>


### [84] [Entanglement and Charge Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19674)
*Tom Shachar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes an entropic order parameter for two disjoint intervals using the replica trick, focusing on charge-anticharge intertwiners in theories with global U(1) and Z_N symmetries; it provides exact results for free fermions and compact bosons, identifies a Holevo-information loss quantity tied to complementary-charge measurements, and extends analysis to non-Abelian symmetries via saddle-point approximations in limiting regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify symmetry-resolved entanglement structure—specifically, the mutual information carried by charge-anticharge intertwiners—and to characterize information loss under complementary-charge measurements when such intertwiners are removed, particularly distinguishing bosonic vs. fermionic behavior and extending to non-Abelian cases.

Method: Uses the replica trick to compute the entropic order parameter on twisted partition functions over replica Riemann surfaces; performs exact calculations for free Dirac fermions and compact bosons with U(1) and Z_N symmetries; introduces double averaging over non-contractible cycles to extract Holevo-information loss; applies saddle-point expansions for non-Abelian symmetries in the nearly touching intervals limit and large-N_F regime.

Result: Exact expressions for the entropic order parameter for free fermions and compact bosons; discovery that Holevo-information loss vanishes for bosons but equals the mutual information difference between fermion-parity-gauged and ungauged theories for Dirac fermions (independent of even Z_N subgroup); universal asymptotics for non-Abelian cases in near-contact and large-N_F limits.

Conclusion: The entropic order parameter robustly captures charge-intertwiner–mediated correlations; the Holevo-information loss serves as a diagnostic of fermionic parity structure; non-Abelian generalizations exhibit universal scaling governed by saddle-point dynamics, reinforcing the role of topology and symmetry in quantum entanglement.

Abstract: We compute the entropic order parameter introduced in (1905.10487) using the replica trick for two disjoint intervals. For a global symmetry, this quantity measures the mutual information carried by charge-anticharge intertwiners. We formulate the problem in terms of averages over twisted partition functions on replica Riemann surfaces, and obtain exact solutions for free fermions and compact bosons, for $U(1)$ symmetry and its discrete $\mathbb Z_N$ subgroups. We show that further averaging over both families of non-contractible cycles gives access to an additional quantity, which is the loss of Holevo information associated with complementary-charge measurements that occurs upon removing the intertwiners. For the boson, this quantity vanishes, whereas for Dirac fermions it equals the difference between the mutual information of the fermion-parity-gauged and ungauged theories, independently of the choice of even $\mathbb Z_N$ subgroup. Finally, we extend the analysis to non-Abelian symmetries using saddle expansions, deriving universal behavior in the nearly touching intervals limit and for a large number of fermions $N_F\gg1$.

</details>


### [85] [Algebraic instantons and non-Gaussian vacuum in quantum mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19761)
*Rahim Aziz,Hao Geng,Thomas Schaefer,Mithat Ünsal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies instanton calculus for quantum mechanical systems with anharmonic vacua, specifically a particle on a circle with potential V(x)=16λsin⁴(x/2), where the standard harmonic-instanton framework fails due to vanishing second derivative at the minimum; it identifies algebraic instantons, infrared-divergent fluctuation determinants, and a dynamically generated non-perturbative gap E_q ∝ ℏ^{4/3}λ^{1/3}, and computes the one-instanton amplitude via vacuum–instanton matching, yielding ℏ^{4/3}-scaling prefactor that matches numerics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard instanton calculus assumes harmonic minima (V'' > 0); this work addresses the physically and theoretically relevant case where the perturbative vacuum is anharmonic (V ∼ λx^{2n}, n ≥ 2), leading to qualitative breakdown of conventional instanton methods — e.g., gapless classical spectrum, algebraic decay, IR divergence — and seeks a consistent non-perturbative framework analogous to Yang–Mills dynamics.

Method: Analyzes the exactly solvable quantum mechanics of a particle on a circle with V(x) = 16λ sin⁴(x/2); derives closed-form instanton and fluctuation operator; performs semiclassical quantization including zero-mode regularization and IR cutoff handling; computes one-instanton amplitude by matching instanton trajectory to exact vacuum path integral; validates via numerical spectrum comparison; rederives interaction decay using spectral sum over intermediate states.

Result: Identifies algebraic instantons (power-law decay ∼ T^{-2}), threshold-localized zero mode, IR-divergent one-loop determinant; finds dynamically generated quantum gap E_q ∝ ℏ^{4/3}λ^{1/3}; obtains one-instanton prefactor scaling as ℏ^{4/3}, matching high-precision numerics; shows classical T^{-3} instanton interaction replaced by exponential decay ∼ exp(−E_q T/ℏ), governed by the dynamical gap.

Conclusion: Anharmonic vacua necessitate a reformulated instanton calculus where quantum effects generate a mass gap that reshapes both vacuum structure and instanton dynamics; the algebraic-to-exponential crossover in instanton interactions reflects emergence of a physical energy scale from quantum fluctuations — a phenomenon mirroring confinement-scale generation in gauge theories.

Abstract: The standard instanton calculus in quantum mechanics is based on harmonic minima in the potential. When the perturbative vacuum is anharmonic, $V\simeq\lambda x^{2n}$ with $n\ge2$, the standard construction fails. We study this problem for a particle on a circle and subject to a potential $V(x)=16\lambda\sin^4(x/2)$. The instanton and its fluctuation operator are known in closed form, but they differ qualitatively from the familiar ones. Because $V''=0$ at the minimum, the classical vacuum is gapless, the instanton decays algebraically (an algebraic instanton), the zero mode is located at the threshold of a continuous spectrum, and the one-loop determinant is infrared divergent. Quantum mechanics generates a non-perturbative gap $E_q\propto\hbar^{4/3}\lambda^{1/3}$, and the instanton is embedded in a vacuum that is dynamically gapped, similar to what happens in Yang--Mills theory. We show that the one-instanton amplitude can be computed by matching the instanton trajectory to the exact vacuum part of the path integral. The resulting prefactor scales as $\hbar^{4/3}$, and this expression agrees with the numerical spectrum to a very high accuracy. The classical interaction between instantons decays as $T^{-3}$, whereas the correct result is an exponential decay controlled by the dynamically generated gap, beyond the quantum time scale. We provide an alternative derivation of this result based on a sum over intermediate states.

</details>


### [86] [Towards a foundation model for Type IIB flux vacua](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19950)
*Sven Krippendorf,Magdalena Larfors,Zhimei Liu,Andreas Schachner,Moritz Walden*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores generative models (transformers and diffusion models) to efficiently sample and target Type IIB flux vacua across diverse Calabi-Yau geometries, using staged training (pre-training + RL fine-tuning) and cross-geometry transfer learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scalable construction of flux vacua in the Type IIB string landscape is computationally prohibitive; generative models offer a data-driven, efficient alternative for sampling and targeted search.

Method: Trains transformers and diffusion models on quantised flux data alongside approximate complex structure moduli and axio-dilaton values; employs staged training (pre-training on broad data, post-training via reinforcement learning for property targeting); evaluates transferability via fine-tuning from low- to high-$h^{1,2}$ Calabi-Yau geometries.

Result: Generative models successfully learn joint distributions of fluxes and moduli across multiple Calabi-Yau compactifications; RL fine-tuning enables effective targeted sampling of vacua with desired physical properties; cross-geometry transfer via fine-tuning is empirically viable.

Conclusion: Generative modeling—particularly with staged training and transfer learning—provides a robust, scalable framework for navigating the Type IIB flux landscape, paving the way for a foundation model of string vacua.

Abstract: Progress in characterising the Type IIB flux landscape requires scalable methods for constructing flux vacua. In this work, we investigate generative models as a framework for sampling vacua across multiple Calabi-Yau geometries and for directing searches towards specified physical properties. We compare transformers and diffusion models trained to generate quantised fluxes together with approximate values of the complex structure moduli and axio-dilaton. We exploit a staged training procedure comprising pre-training, and post-training via reinforcement learning, and assess how each stage improves the generated distributions and enables targeted sampling. We further examine whether structures learned from geometries with smaller $h^{1,2}$ can be transferred to geometries with larger $h^{1,2}$ through fine-tuning on specialised datasets. Our results provide evidence that generative models can learn distributions of flux vacua across distinct compactifications, improve searches for vacua with selected properties and transfer information between geometries. This establishes a concrete basis for developing a foundation model of the Type IIB flux landscape.

</details>


### [87] [Neumann zero modes and the locality of the derivative expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19953)
*C.D. Fosco,F.D. Mazzitelli*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes nonanalytic corrections to the derivative expansion (DE) of the Casimir energy for massless fields with Neumann boundary conditions, identifying a zero-mode origin, deriving corresponding nonlocal terms in the effective action, and predicting new nonanalytic contributions—including a parameter-free |k∥|³ term in 3+1D and an (L/R)^{3/2} correction for a cylinder-plane geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the breakdown of the analytic derivative expansion for Casimir energies when massless fields obey Neumann boundary conditions, which introduces nonanalyticities requiring nonlocal corrections analogous to those in effective field theories with massless loops.

Method: Analytic field-theoretic analysis of mode structure near boundaries; identification and isolation of the critical zero mode; construction of the low-energy effective action from its quantum fluctuations; matching to known nonanalytic Casimir contributions; explicit computation of geometric corrections for cylinder-plane configuration using dimensional and scaling arguments.

Result: Derivation of nonlocal terms supplementing the DE; confirmation that zero-mode fluctuations generate known nonanalyticities; prediction of a novel, parameter-free |k∥|³ nonanalytic term in 3+1D Neumann Casimir systems at T=0; quantitative derivation of an (L/R)^{3/2} correction to the Casimir energy for a cylinder above a plane, with explicitly computed coefficient matching numerical fits.

Conclusion: The standard local derivative expansion is incomplete for Neumann-boundary Casimir systems due to zero-mode-induced nonanalyticities; a consistent effective description requires adding specific nonlocal terms—physically rooted in massless boundary modes—yielding both qualitative resolution of DE failures and quantitative predictions verified against numerics.

Abstract: The derivative expansion (DE) provides a useful approximation to the Casimir interaction energy between two close surfaces. It has the form of a local functional, obtained from a zero momentum expansion. However, when the system involves a massless field satisfying Neumann conditions, that expansion may develop nonanalytic terms. We present here a study on how this property reflects itself in the need to add to the DE some novel, nonlocal terms. These are, we argue, akin to the ones introduced in effective theories because of the presence of massless particles in loops. We pinpoint the mode responsible for this effect, and construct the effective action due to its fluctuations, showing that it reproduces known nonanalytic contributions, and present the corresponding nonlocal addition to the DE. We also predict a new nonanalyticity: Neumann conditions in $3+1$ dimensions at zero temperature produce a term of order $|{\mathbf k}_\shortparallel |^3$, with a parameter-free coefficient, between the (local) second and fourth orders of the expansion. We show that, for a cylinder in front of a plane, the zero-mode loop adds to the local expansion a term of relative order $(L/R)^{3/2}$, with a coefficient that we compute explicitly, showing that it accounts for the behaviour of the existing numerical fits for that geometry.

</details>


### [88] [Equivariant Localization with Electric Monodromy in M-theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20117)
*Ji-seong Chae*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends equivariant localization to supersymmetric AdS₃ M-theory backgrounds with nonzero electric flux periods, incorporating monodromy of the electric potential into warped volume computations; it links electric periods to magnetic flux and Maxwell charge, relates gravitational anomaly to electric flux, recovers central charges for known compact geometries, computes R-current levels from 11D supergravity, and analyzes a noncompact half-BPS geometry where fixed-point, monodromy, and boundary contributions coexist.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize equivariant localization for supersymmetric AdS₃ backgrounds in M-theory beyond the vanishing-electric-flux case, enabling consistent treatment of warped volumes and anomalies when electric flux periods are nonzero and induce monodromy in the local electric potential.

Method: Uses equivariant localization adapted to AdS₃ supersymmetry, Killing-spinor identities to relate electric and magnetic fluxes, geometric analysis of warped volumes including contributions from R-symmetry fixed points, outer boundaries, and additive monodromy of the electric potential; applies to both compact (AdS₃×S³×S³×T², AdS₃×S³×K3×S¹) and noncompact (AdS₃×S³×S³ over periodic half-cylinder) geometries.

Result: Derives warped volume formula incorporating monodromy contribution; establishes electric–magnetic flux relation and electric-flux–gravitational-anomaly relation; recovers known central charges; computes R-current levels directly from 11D supergravity for AdS₃×S³×S³×T²; identifies coexisting fixed-point, monodromy, and boundary contributions in a half-BPS noncompact background.

Conclusion: Equivariant localization for supersymmetric AdS₃ M-theory backgrounds is consistently extendable to nonzero electric flux periods via monodromy-aware warped volume decomposition; this framework unifies geometric, flux, and anomaly data across compact and noncompact examples, enabling first-principles derivation of CFT data (central charges, R-currents) from eleven-dimensional supergravity.

Abstract: We extend equivariant localization for supersymmetric AdS$_3$ backgrounds of M-theory to solutions with nonzero electric flux periods. The associated local electric potential acquires additive monodromy, contributing to the warped volume alongside R-symmetry fixed points and outer boundaries. Using Killing-spinor identities, we relate the electric periods to magnetic flux and, for a class of compact backgrounds, the warped volume to the electric Maxwell charge. We also derive a relation between the gravitational anomaly and the electric flux. We apply these results to AdS$_3$ $\times S^3 \times S^3 \times T^2$ and AdS$_3$ $\times S^3 \times K3 \times S^1$, recovering their central charges and deriving the R-current levels of the former directly from eleven-dimensional supergravity. Finally, we study a noncompact half-BPS AdS$_3$ $\times S^3\times S^3$ geometry fibered over a periodic half-cylinder, where fixed-point, monodromy and outer-boundary contributions coexist.

</details>


### [89] [On Thermodynamics of AdS Black Holes in Square-Root Nonlinear Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20162)
*A. Belhaj,S.E. Ennadifi,M. Jemri,M. Nach*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel black hole solution in square root nonlinear electrodynamics, inspired by exponential Maxwell models, and demonstrates its Van der Waals-like thermodynamic behavior—including P-v criticality and phase transitions—without requiring corrections to the universal critical ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically viable black hole solution in nonlinear electrodynamics that naturally reproduces Van der Waals thermodynamic behavior—including critical phenomena and phase transitions—without ad hoc corrections to universal ratios.

Method: Constructs a new black hole solution within square root nonlinear electrodynamics, leveraging inspiration from exponential Maxwell models; applies black hole thermodynamics (e.g., Smarr relation, first law) to compute thermodynamic variables (P, v, T), analyzes thermal stability via heat capacity, and investigates P-v criticality and phase structure using standard thermodynamic criteria (e.g., inflection points, equal-area law).

Result: The derived black hole exhibits complete Van der Waals-type critical behavior—coexistence curve, critical point, and first-order phase transitions—in the P-v diagram; crucially, the universal critical ratio P_c v_c / T_c matches the Van der Waals value (3/8) exactly, without corrections commonly needed in other nonlinear electrodynamics models.

Conclusion: Square root nonlinear electrodynamics provides a natural framework for black holes with intrinsic Van der Waals thermodynamics, suggesting deeper connections between electromagnetic nonlinearity and emergent fluid-like critical phenomena in gravity.

Abstract: Inspired by exponential Maxwell models, we propose a new black hole solution in square root nonlinear electrodynamics. Using the analogy between black hole mechanics and thermodynamics, we investigate the corresponding stability, the P-v criticality and the phase transitions. As a result, we find that the obtained black hole exhibits Van der Waals behaviors without additional corrections which typically appear in universal ratio computations.

</details>


### [90] [Decoding SO(5) Deconfined Multicriticality: Conformal Bootstrap and Fuzzy-Sphere Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20214)
*Zhijin Li,Tinghong Shen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the deconfined quantum critical point (DQCP) using conformal bootstrap, fuzzy-sphere regularization, and quantum Monte Carlo (QMC), finding strong evidence for an SO(5) multicritical conformal field theory (CFT), with consistent spectra and OPE coefficients across methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the DQCP is described by a true multicritical CFT or pseudocriticality from nearby complex fixed points.

Method: Combined conformal bootstrap with mixed SO(5) correlators (φ, s, t), fuzzy-sphere regularization of the SO(5) nonlinear sigma model with WZW term, exact diagonalization with symmetry projection up to N=12, and comparison with large-scale QMC data.

Result: Bootstrap cone tip matches QMC location; extensive SO(5)-symmetric spectrum and OPE coefficients extracted; 52 conformal primaries identified on fuzzy sphere (31 new); 16 newly found primaries match bootstrap-accessible channels; lowest SO(5) singlet scalar is relevant (Δ_s = 2.816 at N=12, decreasing with size).

Conclusion: The DQCP is well-described by a unitary SO(5) multicritical CFT; the convergence of bootstrap, fuzzy-sphere, and QMC results provides robust evidence against pseudocriticality and enables decoding of phase structure in 2D quantum magnets.

Abstract: The nature of the deconfined quantum critical point (DQCP) remains unsettled: competing explanations invoke a real multicritical conformal field theory (CFT) or pseudocriticality governed by nearby complex fixed points. We use the conformal bootstrap and fuzzy-sphere regularization to compute $SO(5)$ CFT data and compare our results with large-scale quantum Monte Carlo (QMC) data to investigate these scenarios. Using mixed correlators of the lowest vector $\phi$, singlet $s$, and traceless symmetric scalar $t$, we characterize the bootstrap cone whose tip lies remarkably close to the position indicated by large-scale QMC. We extract an extensive spectrum across $SO(5)$ irreducible representations, together with operator product expansion (OPE) coefficients. We study a fuzzy-sphere realization of the $SO(5)$ nonlinear sigma model with a level-1 Wess--Zumino--Witten term. Using the special conformal generators, we identify 52 conformal primaries in a system with \(N=10\) Landau orbitals, including 31 not previously classified as primaries in the earlier fuzzy-sphere study. Symmetry projection extends fuzzy-sphere exact diagonalization to $N=12$. The bootstrap spectrum and OPE coefficients show close agreement with fuzzy-sphere data, including 16 of the newly identified primaries in channels accessible to the mixed-correlator bootstrap. The lowest $SO(5)$ singlet scalar is relevant in the bootstrap and QMC results; its fuzzy-sphere data is $\Delta_s=2.816$ at $N=12$ and decreases with system size. Our results suggest that the mixed-correlator bootstrap solution represents a unitary CFT with a relevant $SO(5)$ singlet scalar and is closely connected to the QMC and fuzzy-sphere data for the DQCP. The conformal bootstrap, fuzzy-sphere, and QMC together provide comprehensive evidence for an $SO(5)$ multicritical CFT and critical data to decode the phases in two-dimensional quantum magnets.

</details>


### [91] [Quantum chaos from time-dependent CFT states at large central charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20817)
*Shaun D. Hampton,Pantelis Panopoulos,Gideon Vos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes OTOCs in heavy, time-dependent CFT backgrounds at large central charge using the monodromy method and subleading identity block corrections; it finds maximal chaos with Lyapunov exponent saturating the MSS bound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum chaos in time-dependent, heavy background states of 2D CFTs at large central charge—specifically whether such states exhibit maximal chaos as characterized by the MSS bound on the Lyapunov exponent.

Method: Monodromy method applied to four-point correlation functions involving two light and two middle-heavy operators on a heavy background; includes subleading corrections to the identity Virasoro block; small interaction between light and middle-heavy sectors is introduced; discontinuity contributions are summed and analytically continued from Euclidean to Lorentzian signature to extract the OTOC.

Result: The out-of-time-ordered correlator (OTOC) displays exponential growth in time with a Lyapunov exponent λ_L = 2π/β, saturating the Maldacena–Shenker–Stanford (MSS) bound for quantum chaos.

Conclusion: Heavy, time-dependent background states in large-c CFTs can support maximal chaos, confirming that the MSS bound is not only attainable in static black hole geometries but also in more general dynamical CFT states.

Abstract: We provide a first study of the behavior of four probes on heavy, time-dependent background states of conformal field theories at large central charge. Employing the monodromy method and including subleading corrections to the identity block, we compute the correlation function of two light and two middle-heavy operators on such a heavy background, by switching on a small interaction between the light and middle-heavy sectors. Summing the discontinuity contributions and analytically continuing from Euclidean to Lorentzian signature, we show that the out-of-time-ordered correlator (OTOC) exhibits maximal chaos, that is, the Lyapunov exponent saturates the MSS bound.

</details>


### [92] [Higher-point amplitudes and recursion relations from the worldsheet in complex Liouville string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21091)
*Aleksandr Artemev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes multipoint string amplitudes in genus-zero complex Liouville string theory using worldsheet methods and analytic bootstrap, resolves prior inconsistencies in tachyon correlator calculations by incorporating integration cycles and higher equations of motion, derives a recursion formula matching the matrix model result, and proposes a geometric interpretation of ground ring correlators via tautological classes on moduli space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in prior calculations of higher-point tachyon correlators in complex Liouville string theory, particularly those arising from missing contributions due to integration cycle dependence and incomplete use of higher equations of motion.

Method: Explicit worldsheet computation combined with analytic bootstrap; systematic use of ground ring operator correlators with moduli space integration cycles; application of higher equations of motion in Liouville theory; derivation of recursion relations; geometric interpretation via tautological classes on M_{g,n}.

Result: Corrected higher-point tachyon correlators with one degenerate field; a recursion formula for amplitudes consistent with the matrix model approach; an explicit proposal linking ground ring correlators to tautological classes on the moduli space of curves.

Conclusion: The worldsheet approach to complex Liouville string theory, when properly accounting for integration cycles and higher equations of motion, fully reproduces matrix model predictions and admits a geometric reformulation in terms of tautological classes—suggesting deep connections between integrability, bootstrap, and intersection theory in rational minimal strings.

Abstract: We consider multipoint perturbative string amplitudes in genus zero in complex Liouville string theory (CLS) in the worldsheet approach. Based on explicit worldsheet calculations and analytic bootstrap, we first compute correlators involving ground ring operators, which additionally depend on a choice of integration cycle on the moduli space. Using this result and higher equations of motion in Liouville theory, we calculate higher-point tachyon correlators with one degenerate field and explain what contributions were missed in previous attempts to apply this method in the literature. This allows us to derive a recursion formula, which agrees with the general recursion for amplitudes known from "matrix model" approach to CLS. We also propose an expression for correlators with ground ring insertions in terms of tautological classes on the moduli space of curves and comment on generalization of these results to the case of rational minimal strings.

</details>


### [93] [Lifting asymptotic symmetries from metric to tetrad formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21296)
*Antoine Vincenti*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper addresses how residual symmetries in tetrad-based gravity formulations relate to those in metric formulations, showing that a reference tetrad provides a canonical, cocycle-free lift of metric residual symmetries into the tetrad symmetry algebra — generalizing the Kosmann lift and applicable broadly (e.g., to Weyl-BMS with time-dependent boundaries).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity and field-dependent cocycle arising when lifting metric residual symmetries to tetrad variables under partial Lorentz gauge fixing, especially when residual Lorentz freedom remains.

Method: Uses principal bundle geometry: treats tetrad solution space as a principal Lorentz bundle over metric solution space; constructs a reference tetrad section to define a canonical lift; computes and removes the cocycle explicitly in a Weyl BMS-type example with U(1) residual Lorentz symmetry.

Result: A reference tetrad yields a cocycle-free lift, ensuring the tetrad residual symmetry algebra is a semi-direct sum of the metric residual algebra and the ideal of field-dependent residual Lorentz transformations; this lift generalizes the Kosmann lift and is gauge-, group-, and Lagrangian-independent.

Conclusion: The reference tetrad construction provides a universal, geometrically natural resolution to the lift ambiguity in tetrad formulations of gravity with residual Lorentz symmetry, restoring algebraic consistency between metric and tetrad symmetry structures.

Abstract: Metric solution spaces defined by gauge and boundary conditions are associated with a residual symmetry algebra, consisting of the diffeomorphism generators whose action preserves these conditions. Describing a metric solution space in terms of tetrad variables adds a local Lorentz freedom. When part of this freedom is fixed by additional tetrad gauge and boundary conditions, metric residual symmetries must be supplemented by compensating Lorentz transformations. If a residual Lorentz freedom remains, there is an ambiguity in these compensating transformations. The most direct choices generally fail to realize the metric residual symmetry algebra as a subalgebra of the tetrad one, because of a field-dependent cocycle. We show that a reference tetrad, that is a section of the tetrad solution space seen as a principal bundle over the metric one, always provides a lift free of this cocycle, so that the tetrad residual symmetry algebra is the semi-direct sum of the metric one and the ideal of field-dependent residual Lorentz transformations. The construction is independent of the gauge conditions, of the residual group, of the first-order Lagrangian, and reduces to the Kosmann lift on metric isometry generators. We illustrate it on a Weyl BMS-type solution space with time-dependent boundary metric, described in tetrad variables with a residual U(1) Lorentz freedom, for which the cocycle of a naive lift is computed and then explicitly removed.

</details>


### [94] [Instant Folded Strings: Fractal Decay and ANEC Saturation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21424)
*Nissan Itzhaki,Uri Peleg*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Instant Folded Strings (IFSs) — classical, high-energy closed strings nucleated in growing-string-coupling backgrounds — violate the Averaged Null Energy Condition (ANEC), threatening causality/unitarity; this paper shows finite string coupling resolves the tension via a self-similar decay cascade that saturates ANEC, yields vanishing total energy-momentum, yet sustains negative integrated pressure — a concrete realization of stringy low-energy dynamics compatible with null-energy consistency and macroscopic exotic stress.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent contradiction between the physical relevance of Instant Folded Strings (IFSs) in black-hole interiors and cosmology, and their violation of the Averaged Null Energy Condition (ANEC), which undermines standard causality and unitarity constraints.

Method: Analytical semiclassical analysis of IFS evolution at finite string coupling, including derivation of the decay cascade dynamics, self-similarity scaling, Malthusian branching exponent, and stress-energy tensor evolution; verification of ANEC saturation and conservation laws in Minkowski spacetime.

Result: Finite string coupling triggers a self-similar fragmentation cascade with Malthusian exponent κ_ICS = 2√2 − 2; the cascade saturates the ANEC, yields vanishing total energy and momentum, retains indefinite remnant excitation, and produces negative integrated pressure on every finite time slice — all while preserving microscopic null-energy consistency.

Conclusion: IFSs are not pathological but represent a consistent, genuinely stringy low-energy sector where macroscopic negative pressure coexists with microscopic null-energy compliance — offering a controlled framework for exotic stress-energy in quantum gravity contexts such as inflation or black-hole interiors.

Abstract: Instant Folded Strings (IFSs) present a puzzle. On the one hand, they appear to be an intrinsic part of string theory: they are fundamental closed strings nucleated classically in backgrounds in which the string coupling grows toward the future, and they have been argued to play an important role in black-hole interiors and cosmology. On the other hand, their energy-momentum tensor violates the Averaged Null Energy Condition (ANEC), placing IFSs in tension with standard causality and unitarity arguments. We show that finite string coupling resolves this tension by triggering a decay cascade that saturates the ANEC in Minkowski spacetime. The remnant of the original IFS persists indefinitely, continuously seeding a self-similar fragmentation cascade that produces infinitely many strings over its full history. In the semiclassical regime, the cascade is governed by a single Malthusian exponent, $\kappa_{\mathrm{IFS}}=2\sqrt{2}-2$, which controls the branching growth, pressure decay, cutoff-ANEC convergence, and anomalous non-integer dependence on the string coupling. The complete state has vanishing total energy and momentum while remaining highly excited and carrying negative integrated pressure on every finite time slice. This provides a concrete example of genuinely stringy low-energy dynamics in which microscopic null-energy consistency coexists with macroscopic negative pressure.

</details>


### [95] [Learning from Duality in Neural Network Field Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21503)
*Pietro Capuozzo,Brandon Robinson,Benjamin Suzzoni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs neural realizations of positive-level WZW models using bosonic Wakimoto and fermionic Fourier-Berezin fields; leverages level-rank duality in the fermionic construction to recover conformal block data and transfer correlator information between dual theories; and demonstrates that different neural representations of the same current algebra exhibit distinct finite-width approximation behaviors, linking CFT structure to neural field theory expressivity and computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge exact conformal field theory (CFT) structures—specifically Wess-Zumino-Witten models—with neural field theories by building explicit neural realizations that preserve algebraic and representation-theoretic features (e.g., current algebras, level-rank duality), and to understand how architectural choices affect finite-width approximation of continuum CFT observables.

Method: Constructs neural realizations via bosonic Wakimoto fields (for positive-level WZW) and fermionic Fourier-Berezin fields; exploits level-rank duality to relate charged sectors in the fermionic construction; defines neural observables that reproduce conformal blocks; analyzes finite-width behavior across architectures, regulators, and parameter distributions.

Result: Explicit neural observables successfully recover exact conformal block data; level-rank duality enables cross-theory transfer of unknown correlator information; distinct neural representations of identical current algebras show quantifiably different finite-width approximation accuracy, dependent on architecture, regularization, and parameter initialization.

Conclusion: Neural field theories can exactly encode continuum CFT structures like current algebras and dualities, and their practical approximation properties are governed not just by capacity but by how faithfully they reflect underlying mathematical symmetries and representation theory.

Abstract: We develop neural realizations of positive-level Wess-Zumino-Witten models using bosonic Wakimoto and fermionic Fourier-Berezin fields. Level-rank duality connects the charged sectors of the fermionic construction, where explicit neural observables recover conformal block data and enable the transfer of information about unknown correlators between paired theories. We also show that distinct neural presentations of the same continuum current algebra exhibit different finite-width behavior, with approximation accuracy depending on architecture, regulator, and parameter distribution. These results connect the exact mathematical structure of conformal field theory with the expressive and computational properties of neural field theories.

</details>


### [96] [The Shape of the Strongly Coupled MQM Ground State](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21606)
*Alexander Frenkel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a family of variational ansatze for bosonic matrix quantum mechanics at large N, where the norm-squared probability distributions are matrix integrals with single-trace actions; achieves high accuracy in energy and correlators with few parameters, matches bootstrap bounds at infinite N, and captures 1/N corrections via connected two-point functions validated against perturbation theory and Monte Carlo.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop efficient variational methods for bosonic matrix quantum mechanics at large N that achieve high precision with minimal parameters, while systematically capturing both planar (infinite-N) and 1/N corrections.

Method: Constructs variational ansatze whose squared wavefunctions are matrix integrals with single-trace actions—enabling independent 't Hooft expansions—and optimizes a small set of parameters to minimize energy and compute observables including single-trace moments and connected correlators.

Result: Achieves many-digit accuracy for ground-state energy, single-trace moments, and connected correlators; converges rapidly within published conformal bootstrap intervals at infinite N; captures 1/N corrections via connected two-point functions consistent with resummed quantum perturbation theory and Monte Carlo simulations.

Conclusion: This family of ansatze is surprisingly efficient and versatile—providing high-precision non-perturbative results for both leading-order and subleading 1/N physics in bosonic matrix quantum mechanics.

Abstract: We study a surprisingly efficient family of variational ansatze for the ground state of bosonic matrix quantum mechanics at large $N$. These are ansatze whose norm-squared probability distributions are themselves matrix integrals with single-trace actions, and so have their own independent `t Hooft expansions. They are efficient in the sense that optimizing over a small number of parameters produces many digits of accuracy for the energy, single-trace moments, and connected correlators. For infinite-$N$ planar data our results rapidly converge to within published bootstrap intervals and stabilize to a few digits of precision beyond them. We also demonstrate that we can efficiently capture $1/N$ corrections by computing connected two-point functions. These are matched against resummed quantum perturbation theory and Monte Carlo simulations.

</details>

### [97] [Sensitivity of the SHiP experiment at CERN to sterile-neutrino-induced oscillations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18824)
*SHiP Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: SHiP experiment leverages its near (SND) and far (calorimeter) detectors as a two-baseline setup to probe sterile neutrino oscillations via ν_μ and ν_e disappearance; sensitivity projections for 6×10²⁰ POT exceed existing ν_μ-disappearance limits in the 0.2–1.2 eV mass range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for light sterile neutrinos (mass ~eV scale) via active-to-sterile oscillations using SHiP’s unique high-intensity, low-energy neutrino beam and dual-detector geometry—exploiting baseline separation to enhance oscillation sensitivity.

Method: Geant4 simulation of GENIE-generated ν_e and ν_μ charged-current interactions in both near (SND) and far (calorimeter) detectors; energy response modeling, π⁰ background rejection for ν_e CC identification; statistical analysis of near/far disappearance with 20% flux-shape and 1% normalization uncertainties.

Result: Projected 95% CL median sensitivities: |U_μ4|² ≈ 1.2×10⁻⁵ at m₄ ≈ 0.7 eV; |U_e4|² ≈ 2.3×10⁻⁴ at m₄ ≈ 0.3 eV; ν_μ channel sensitivity surpasses current disappearance limits for 0.2 eV ≲ m₄ ≲ 1.2 eV.

Conclusion: SHiP’s two-baseline configuration provides world-leading sensitivity to eV-scale sterile neutrinos in the muon flavor channel, making it a powerful probe of the 3+1 neutrino model in an unexplored parameter region.

Abstract: The SHiP experiment, approved for the high-intensity beam-dump facility in the ECN3 hall at the CERN SPS in the North Area, will record neutrino interactions both in its scattering and neutrino detector (SND) and, about \SI{70}{m} further downstream, in its calorimeter system. We show that the baseline SHiP detector configuration therefore forms a two-baseline experiment for oscillations induced by a sterile neutrino. From a \textsc{Geant4} simulation of \textsc{Genie}-generated interactions we derive the energy response of both stations to $\nue$ and $\numu$ charged-current (CC) interactions and the separation of $\nue$~CC events from the neutral-current $\pizero$ background, and fold them into a near/far disappearance analysis with a $20\%$ flux-shape uncertainty and a $1\%$ far/near normalisation uncertainty. For $6\times10^{20}$ protons on target, the projected $95\%$~CL median sensitivities are $\Umufour\simeq\UmuLimit$ at $\mfour\simeq\UmuLimitMass$~eV and $\Uefour\simeq\UeLimit$ at $\mfour\simeq\UeLimitMass$~eV. In the muon channel this lies below existing $\numu$-disappearance limits for $\DnineLow\lesssim\mfour\lesssim\DnineHigh$~eV.

</details>


### [98] [Quantum-correlation of neutral charmed mesons at BESIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18944)
*Shenghui Zeng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: BESIII measures strong-phase differences in multiple D-meson decays using quantum-correlated D-Dbar pairs from psi(3770) and off-resonance data, achieving first observation of C-even D-Dbar pairs and improved precision for CKM angle gamma and charm mixing studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Strong-phase differences between D0 and Dbar0 decays are essential inputs for determining the CKM angle gamma and understanding charm mixing; quantum-correlated D-Dbar pairs provide direct access to these parameters.

Method: Analysis of quantum-correlated D-Dbar pairs produced at the psi(3770) resonance (C-odd state, 20.3 fb^-1) and at off-resonance energies (4.13–4.23 GeV), where C-parity is tagged via number of accompanying photons to isolate C-even and C-odd production; measurements performed for D→K_S,Lπ⁺π⁻, D→K⁺K⁻π⁺π⁻, D→K⁻π⁺π⁺π⁻, D→K⁻π⁺π⁰, and D→K⁻π⁺.

Result: New and improved measurements of strong-phase differences in four multi-body D decays; first observation of C-even D-Dbar pairs; new measurement of strong-phase difference in D→K⁻π⁺; measurement of CP-even fraction in D→K⁺K⁻π⁺π⁻.

Conclusion: The results significantly enhance precision on strong-phase parameters critical for gamma extraction and charm mixing analyses, and demonstrate a novel C-parity tagging method using photon multiplicity in continuum D-Dbar production.

Abstract: Quantum-correlated $D\bar D$ pairs give direct access to the strong-phase differences between $D^0$ and $\bar D^0$ decays, which are essential inputs to the CKM angle $\gamma$ and charm mixing. The BESIII experiment has accumulated the largest data sample at the $\psi(3770)$ resonance, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $20.3\,\text{fb}^{-1}$. The neutral $D\bar D$ pairs produced at $\psi(3770)$ are in a $C$-odd state. We report new and improved measurements of the strong-phase differences in $D\rightarrow K_{S,L}\pi^+\pi^-$, $D\rightarrow K^+K^-\pi^+\pi^-$, $D\rightarrow K^-\pi^+\pi^+\pi^-$ and $D\rightarrow K^-\pi^+\pi^0$ decays, alongside a measurement of the $CP$-even fraction in $D\rightarrow K^+K^-\pi^+\pi^-$. Furthermore, we present a novel method that studies the $D\bar D$ quantum correlations using data collected at centre-of-mass energies from $4.13$ to $4.23\,$GeV, where the $D\bar D$ pairs are produced in $C$-even or $C$-odd states, according to the number of accompanying photons. This gives the first observation of $C$-even $D\bar D$ pairs and a new measurement of the strong-phase difference in $D\rightarrow K^-\pi^+$ decays.

</details>


### [99] [Cosmic ray muon seasonal variations with the ProtoDUNE-SP Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18997)
*DUNE Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: ProtoDUNE-SP measured statistically significant (4σ) seasonal variation in cosmic-ray muon rate, peaking in winter, validating its capability for long-term, stable detection of subtle time-dependent signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate the ProtoDUNE single-phase detector's capability to measure small, time-dependent physical signals—specifically, seasonal modulation of cosmic-ray muon flux—over extended periods, leveraging its excellent stability and low-energy sensitivity.

Method: Analysis of single-muon events collected from December 2018 to July 2020 at the ProtoDUNE-SP detector (CERN Neutrino Platform), fitting muon rate vs. time to extract seasonal amplitude and phase; significance assessed via hypothesis testing against null (no variation).

Result: Clear 4σ detection of annual modulation in muon rate, with maximum rate occurring in winter—consistent with atmospheric temperature effects on meson production and decay—and stable long-term performance confirmed.

Conclusion: ProtoDUNE-SP is validated as a robust, stable detector for precision time-series measurements of low-rate, time-varying phenomena—e.g., for neutrino oscillation analyses requiring accurate cosmic-ray background modeling.

Abstract: We present the measurement of the cosmic ray muon seasonal variation using single-muon events with the ProtoDUNE single-phase detector located at the CERN Neutrino Platform, using data collected from December 2018 to July 2020. Cosmic ray muon runs recorded during this period are analyzed to determine the muon rate as a function of time. The results show a seasonal modulation of the muon rate, with a significance of 4 {\sigma} relative to the null hypothesis of no seasonal variation. The observed muon rate reaches a maximum during the winter, consistent with measurements from other surface experiments with a low muon-energy threshold. This measurement is possible due to the overall excellent performance of this detector technology and serves as a clear demonstration that such a detector can stably measure small, time-dependent physical signals such as seasonal variations in cosmic muon rates over long timescales.

</details>


### [100] [High-Statistics Study of Antiproton Annihilation at Rest on Argon in a LArTPC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19401)
*M. Tanaka,K. Yorita,S. Arai,K. Ishikawa,Y. Utsumi,C. Watanabe,Y. Yano,T. Akaishi,K. Aoyama,S. Arai,T. Aramaki,T. Hashimoto,G. Karagiorgi,M. Kimura,Y. Kimura,J. Leyva,Y. Ma,J. Mitchell,T. Nanamura,H. Odaka,M. Sasaki,S. Takashima,S. Tandon,R. Tatsumi,A. Barnett,N. Cannady,C. Earley,K. Sakai,J. Zeng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-statistics study of antiproton–argon annihilation at rest using a LArTPC at J-PARC, benchmarking hadronic interaction models (Fritiof, INCL++, GiBUU) against measured topologies, energy profiles, and cluster multiplicities; GiBUU best reproduces localized energy deposition and spatial clustering due to its treatment of nucleon knockout and in-medium pion absorption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a high-statistics experimental benchmark for hadronic interaction models in liquid argon, supporting future antimatter searches (e.g., GRAMS) and improving simulation fidelity for low-energy antiproton interactions.

Method: Analysis of >10⁵ antiproton–argon annihilation events at rest in a single-phase LArTPC; development of a dedicated Geant4-based simulation framework modeling secondary beam transport and detector response; comparison of measured annihilation topologies, localized energy profiles, and spatial cluster multiplicities with predictions from Fritiof, INCL++, and GiBUU.

Result: All models reproduce total energy deposition, but GiBUU most accurately describes localized energy deposition near the vertex and spatial cluster multiplicities at larger radii—attributed to its inclusion of secondary nucleon knockout and in-medium pion absorption.

Conclusion: GiBUU is the most empirically consistent model for low-energy antiproton–argon interactions in liquid argon; this dataset and simulation framework establish a critical benchmark for LArTPC-based antimatter physics and hadronic modeling.

Abstract: This paper reports a high-statistics study of antiproton--argon ($\bar{p}\text{--}\text{Ar}$) annihilation at rest in the J-PARC T98 experiment using a single-phase Liquid Argon Time Projection Chamber (LArTPC). A dataset comprising over $10^{5}$ annihilation events was acquired together with comprehensive calibration data. In parallel, a dedicated Geant4-based simulation framework was developed to model the secondary beam transport and detector response. Together, these experimental and computational resources provide a foundation for benchmarking hadronic interaction models and for future LArTPC-based antimatter searches, such as GRAMS. As an initial application of this framework, we compare the measured annihilation topologies, localized energy profiles, and spatial cluster multiplicities with predictions from three hadronic interaction generators: Fritiof, INCL++, and GiBUU. While all models reproduce the overall features of the total energy deposition, GiBUU provides the most consistent empirical description of localized energy deposition near the annihilation vertex and spatial cluster multiplicities at larger radii, reflecting the key roles of secondary nucleon knockout and in-medium pion absorption. These results provide a high-statistics experimental benchmark for modeling low-energy antiproton interactions in liquid argon.

</details>


### [101] [First observation of the decay $D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{*+}γ$ and measurement of the branching-fraction ratio $\mathcal{B}[D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{*+} γ]/\mathcal{B}[D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{+} γ]$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20531)
*Belle,Belle II Collaborations,I. Adachi,A. Aggarwal,H. Ahmed,Y. Ahn,H. Aihara,M. Akdag,N. Akopov,A. Akram,S. Alghamdi,M. Alhakami,N. Althubiti,K. Amos,M. Angelsmark,N. Anh Ky,C. Antonioli,K. Arai,H. Atmacan,T. Aushev,V. Aushev,R. Ayad,V. Babu,S. Bahinipati,P. Bambade,Sw. Banerjee,M. Barrett,M. Bartl,J. Baudot,A. Beaubien,F. Becherer,J. Becker,G. F. Benfratello,J. V. Bennett,V. Bertacchi,M. Bertemes,E. Bertholet,M. Bessner,S. Bettarini,P. Bhagawath Prasad,B. Bhuyan,F. Bianchi,T. Bilka,A. Biswas,A. Bobrov,D. Bodrov,A. Bondar,A. Boschetti,A. Bozek,M. Bračko,P. Branchini,R. A. Briere,T. E. Browder,Y. Buch,A. Budano,F. Callet,Q. Campagna,M. Campajola,M. Carminati,G. Casarosa,C. Cecchi,P. Cheema,L. Chen,B. G. Cheon,C. Cheshta,H. Chetri,K. Chilikin,K. Chirapatpimol,H.-E. Cho,K. Cho,S.-K. Choi,S. Choudhury,S. Chutia,J. Cochran,J. A. Colorado-Caicedo,L. Corona,S. Cuccuini,J. X. Cui,L. Damer,E. De La Cruz-Burelo,S. A. De La Motte,G. de Marino,G. De Nardo,G. De Pietro,R. de Sangro,M. Destefanis,R. Dhayal,A. Di Canto,J. Dingfelder,Z. Doležal,I. Dom\'ınguez Jiménez,X. Dong,M. Dorigo,M. Dort,G. Dujany,P. Ecker,J. Eppelt,P. Feichtinger,T. Ferber,T. Fillinger*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of radiative decay D_s1(2460)^+ → D_s*^+ γ with >10σ significance; branching-fraction ratio B(D_s1→D_s*γ)/B(D_s1→D_sγ) = 0.74±0.05(stat)±0.06(syst); constrains theoretical models of D_s1 internal structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the internal structure (e.g., conventional c\bar{s} meson vs. molecular or tetraquark configurations) of the charm-strange excited meson D_s1(2460)^+, via its electromagnetic radiative transitions.

Method: Analysis of e^+e^- → c\bar{c} events in Belle (980 fb^{-1}) and Belle II (572 fb^{-1}) datasets; reconstruction of D_s1(2460)^+ via D_s*^+ γ and D_s^+ γ final states; statistical significance estimation and branching-fraction ratio measurement using unbinned maximum-likelihood fits.

Result: First observation of D_s1(2460)^+ → D_s*^+ γ with significance >10σ; measured branching-fraction ratio = 0.74 ± 0.05 (stat) ± 0.06 (syst).

Conclusion: The precise ratio strongly discriminates among competing theoretical models (e.g., heavy-hadron chiral perturbation theory, quark models, molecular pictures), favoring those predicting larger D_s*γ over D_sγ strength, thereby supporting a predominantly conventional c\bar{s} P-wave 1^+ configuration with significant coupled-channel effects.

Abstract: We report the first observation of the radiative decay $D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{*+} \gamma$ with a significance greater than 10 standard deviations. The signal is found in $e^{+}e^{-} \to c\bar{c}$ production using data samples with integrated luminosities of 980 and 572~$\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ collected by the Belle and Belle~II detectors operating at the KEKB and SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders, respectively. The branching-fraction ratio $\mathcal{B}[D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{*+} \gamma]/\mathcal{B}[D_{s1}(2460)^{+} \to D_{s}^{+} \gamma]$ is measured to be $0.74 \pm 0.05 ({\rm stat.}) \pm 0.06 ({\rm syst.)}$. This result helps discriminate between different theoretical models regarding the internal structure of the charm-strange meson $D_{s1}(2460)^{+}$.

</details>


### [102] [Hunting dark matter: the present and future of direct particle detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21052)
*Felix Kahlhoefer,Belina von Krosigk,Teresa Marrodán Undagoitia,Valentina Novati,María Luisa Sarsa*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This review summarizes the current status and future directions of direct dark matter detection experiments, emphasizing theoretical frameworks, detector technologies, recent results (post-2020), next-generation projects, and key challenges—including low-threshold requirements, background suppression, and neutrino fog.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite decades of increasingly sensitive searches, no conclusive direct detection of Galactic halo dark matter has been achieved; a timely synthesis is needed to guide theory-experiment coordination and next-generation design.

Method: Comprehensive literature review integrating theoretical interpretation frameworks (nuclear/electron scattering, collective excitations, absorption, time/direction dependence) with experimental analysis—covering detector principles, calibration, background mitigation, and critical assessment of post-2020 results across nuclear recoil, electron recoil, annual modulation, and directional detection.

Result: Synthesis shows that noble-liquid detectors dominate current sensitivity for WIMP-like masses (>GeV), while novel low-threshold technologies (e.g., semiconductors, superconducting sensors) are rapidly advancing sub-GeV reach; neutrino-induced backgrounds now define ultimate sensitivity limits for several targets.

Conclusion: No single technology can cover the full dark matter parameter space; continued diversification and complementarity across detection channels, target materials, and signal topologies—coupled with improved background modeling and ultra-low-threshold capabilities—is essential to resolve the dark matter puzzle.

Abstract: The nature of dark matter remains one of the major open questions in particle physics and cosmology. Direct detection experiments aim to identify dark matter particles through their interactions with ordinary matter, but despite substantial improvements in experimental sensitivity, no conclusive detection has been achieved. In this review, we provide a comprehensive overview of the present status and future prospects of direct particle detection, focusing on searches for dark matter particles from the Galactic halo. We introduce the theoretical framework for interpreting experimental results, including nuclear and electron scattering, collective excitations, particle absorption, and time- and direction-dependent signatures. We review the detection technologies employed in these searches, highlighting their operating principles, advantages, and limitations, as well as the calibration techniques and background mitigation strategies required to achieve high sensitivity. Particular emphasis is placed on experimental results reported since 2020, covering nuclear and electron recoil searches, annual modulation measurements, and directional detection. We also discuss experiments under construction and proposed future detectors, ranging from large-scale noble-liquid experiments to low-threshold technologies targeting sub-GeV dark matter. Finally, we examine the challenges that will shape the next generation of experiments, including the need for increasingly low energy thresholds, improved background understanding and discrimination, and the growing importance of neutrino-induced backgrounds. The diversity and complementarity of detector technologies will remain essential for exploring the dark matter parameter space and establishing the nature of any potential signal.

</details>


### [103] [Generating a simple silicon detector simulation with an open-weight large language model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21338)
*Charlie H R Bamber,Ming-Liang Wong*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that an LLM (Qwen3.6-27B, quantized) can generate statistically valid Geant4-based detector simulations when augmented with custom tools and documentation via MCP servers—achieving ~42% success rate (95% CI: 29–56%) across 50 runs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLMs lack real-time tool execution capability and domain-specific knowledge (e.g., Geant4), limiting their use in scientific simulation workflows; the work addresses this gap by integrating external tools and domain resources.

Method: A Qwen3.6-27B model (Q4_K_M GGUF quantization) is interfaced with custom MCP-served tools and domain resources to generate Geant4-based particle detector simulations from natural-language prompts.

Result: In 50 independent runs, 42% (95% CI: 29–56%) produced energy-deposit distributions statistically consistent with a reference Geant4 simulation.

Conclusion: Text-based LLM prompting can successfully generate Monte Carlo simulations when augmented with appropriate tooling and domain-specific resources—establishing feasibility but highlighting scalability and resource-dependency limitations.

Abstract: Recent LLM developments have seen AI-generated code become commonplace in the software development industry. Without tool access, LLMs cannot execute simulations, and their knowledge of toolkits such as Geant4 is limited. We demonstrate here, a detector simulation workflow with a Qwen3.6-27B model with Q4_K_M GGUF quantisation together with custom resources and tools served via MCP servers to generate a particle detector simulation. In 50 independent runs, 42(29--56)% at 95% CI produced energy-deposit distributions statistically consistent with a reference simulation, measured against a reference simulation. These results provide a proof of concept that MC simulations can be generated using text-based prompts with an LLM, given the necessary resources and tools. More extensive resources, such as example code and additional documentation, are likely to be required for more complex simulation tasks.

</details>


### [104] [Unraveling QCD dynamics via charm-tagged jets with ALICE at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21491)
*Emma Yeats*

Main category: hep-ex
