TL;DR: This paper presents three novel D⁰-meson-tagged jet substructure measurements—Energy-Energy Correlator (EEC), angular differences among jet axes, and jet angularities—to probe QCD radiation patterns from charm quarks, distinguishing perturbative vs. non-perturbative effects and testing Casimir-factor and dead-cone (mass) dependencies in parton showers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test QCD predictions on jet radiation dependence on parton mass and color charge (Casimir factors), and to disentangle perturbative and non-perturbative contributions in heavy-quark (charm) hadronization via jet substructure.

Method: Experimental measurement of three substructure observables in D⁰-tagged jets: (1) Energy-Energy Correlator (EEC), (2) angular differences between three jet axis definitions (e.g., winner-take-all, soft-drop, standard jet axis), and (3) jet angularities—each designed to differentially probe wide-angle soft radiation, parton shower evolution, and mass/Casimir sensitivity.

Result: First measurement of EEC in D⁰-tagged jets; quantitative characterization of angular radiation patterns via axis comparisons; and angularity distributions sensitive to charm-quark mass and C_A/C_F ratio—providing constraints for Monte Carlo tuning and analytic resummation frameworks.

Conclusion: D⁰-tagged jet substructure observables provide unique, differential probes of heavy-quark parton showers, validating QCD predictions on dead-cone suppression and Casimir scaling, and enabling improved modeling of charm fragmentation and hadronization.

Abstract: The study of jet substructure provides tests of Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) and offers differential ways to study hadronization mechanisms. QCD predicts that jet radiation patterns depend on the mass and color charge of the initiating parton. Parton showers are sensitive to the different Casimir factors of quarks and gluons, and to the parton mass through the dead-cone effect. In this talk, we present three sets of $\textrm{D}^0$-meson-tagged jet measurements. First, we present the first measurement of the Energy-Energy Correlator (EEC) for $\textrm{D}^0$-meson-tagged jets. This observable reveals the detailed structure of QCD radiation from heavy quarks, allowing for a distinction between perturbative and non-perturbative effects in charm-quark showers. We then discuss measurements of the angular differences between three different axis definitions inside the jets. The three definitions offer different sensitivity to wide-angle soft radiation and systematically probe the radiation pattern from charm quarks. The final measurement regards jet angularities, which characterize the weighted angular distribution of the jet constituents. These powerful observables probe the parton shower, with sensitivity to parton-mass and Casimir-factor related effects that can be tuned by adjusting the importance of wide-angle radiation.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [105] [HVP contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment in spacelike and timelike domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20143)
*A.V.Nesterenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment $a^\text{HVP}_\mu$ in both spacelike and timelike domains, reports results from dispersively-improved perturbation theory, and discusses their applicability to lattice QCD, the MUonE experiment, and related approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent and accurate assessment of the hadronic vacuum polarization contribution to the muon anomalous magnetic moment, which is critical for resolving the long-standing discrepancy between Standard Model predictions and experimental measurements of $a_\mu$.

Method: Dispersively-improved perturbation theory applied to spacelike and timelike domains; synthesis and reinterpretation of existing HVP data and theoretical frameworks.

Result: Numerical values of $a^\text{HVP}_\mu$ obtained via dispersively-improved perturbation theory, compatible with and useful for cross-validation in lattice QCD calculations and the MUonE project.

Conclusion: The dispersively-improved perturbative evaluation of $a^\text{HVP}_\mu$ offers a robust, model-independent input for multiple independent determination strategies, enhancing consistency and reducing systematic uncertainties in the global $a_\mu$ analysis.

Abstract: The hadronic vacuum polarization contributions to the muon anomalous magnetic moment $a^{\text{HVP}}_{\mu}$ in spacelike and timelike domains are recapped. The values of $a^{\text{HVP}}_{\mu}$ assessed within dispersively-improved perturbation theory are reported. The obtained results can be employed in the evaluations of $a^{\text{HVP}}_{\mu}$ in the framework of such methods as lattice studies, MUonE project, and others.

</details>


### [106] [Doubly strange tetraquark as a $KK^*$ virtual state: quark-model study with harmonic traps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20588)
*Kai-Kai Zhang,Zi-Yang Lin,Yan-Ke Chen,Lu Meng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a quark-level method using an artificial harmonic trap and four-quark basis to extract hadron–hadron scattering observables (phase shifts, scattering lengths, effective ranges, pole positions) from trapped spectra; it validates the approach on DD* and BB*, and predicts a virtual state—not a bound state—for the doubly strange T_{ss} (I=0, J^P=1^+) near KK* threshold, motivating experimental searches at HIAF.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably extract hadron–hadron scattering information (especially near-threshold poles) directly from quark-model Hamiltonians, bypassing model-dependent assumptions in traditional meson-exchange or effective field theory approaches, and to resolve discrepancies in prior predictions for the T_{ss} tetraquark.

Method: Uses a four-quark basis spanning meson–meson and compact diquark–antidiquark configurations within an artificial harmonic trap; combines the Busch–Englert–Rzażewski–Wilkens formula with the effective range expansion (ERE) to map trapped energy levels to scattering observables; rigorously quantifies uncertainties from basis truncation, trap frequency, ERE order, and quark-model parameters (AL1 and modified AL1).

Result: Extracted DD* and BB* binding energies match direct calculations—validating the method; predicts a KK* virtual-state pole for T_{ss} (I=0, J^P=1^+) located 30–35 MeV below threshold in the stable-K* approximation, robust under two quark-model variants (original and T_{cc}^+-tuned AL1); contradicts earlier bound-state claims.

Conclusion: The trapped-ion-inspired quark-level framework provides a controlled, ab initio–adjacent route to hadronic scattering observables and pole classification; the virtual-state prediction for T_{ss} calls for dedicated experimental investigation at HIAF and suggests reinterpretation of similar exotic candidates.

Abstract: We extract hadron--hadron scattering information from quark-level interactions using an artificial harmonic trap and a four-quark basis that includes both meson--meson and compact diquark--antidiquark configurations. Combining the Busch--Englert--Rzażewski--Wilkens formula with the effective range expansion (ERE), we determine phase shifts, scattering lengths, effective ranges, and pole positions from the trapped spectrum. We control uncertainties from basis truncation, finite trap frequencies, truncation of ERE, and quark-model parameters. The extracted $DD^*$ and $BB^*$ binding energies agree with direct calculations, providing benchmarks. For the doubly strange $T_{ss}$ with $I(J^P)=0(1^+)$, we predict a $KK^*$ virtual-state pole about 30--35~MeV below threshold in the stable-$K^*$ approximation. Both the AL1 model and its modified version that better reproduces the measured $T_{cc}^+$ binding energy yield this result. This finding differs from earlier predictions of a bound state and motivates searches for the $T_{ss}$ at facilities such as the High Intensity heavy Ion Accelerator Facility (HIAF). This framework could be extended to other hadron-hadron channels to extract scattering observables and pole positions.

</details>


### [107] [Muon and Tau Anomalies and $Δα_{had}^{(5)} (M_Z^2)$ in the Standard Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20762)
*Stephan Narison*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the Standard Model (SM) prediction of the muon anomalous magnetic moment (a_μ), focusing on improved hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contributions using recent e+e− data (2π, 3π, inclusive R^ee), QCD corrections (HO PT up to α_s^5, quark mass and condensate effects), and additional γγ channels. It reports a_LO^hvp = (7094 ± 37)×10⁻¹¹, leading to Δa_μ^SM ≈ (84.9–90.0 ± 21.3_th ± 14.5_exp)×10⁻¹¹ — a ~2.1–3.5σ discrepancy with experiment — consistent with BMW20 lattice but highlighting tensions among other lattice results. It also updates a_τ^hvp and Δα_had^(5)(M_Z²).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve persistent tension between SM predictions and experimental measurement of the muon g−2, particularly by improving the dominant low-energy hadronic vacuum polarization (HVP) contribution using updated e+e− data and refined QCD theory inputs, and to assess implications for new physics.

Method: Combines recent e+e− cross-section data (2π, 3π, inclusive R^ee) from PDG and CMD3, augmented with π⁰γ/ηγ contributions; includes high-order perturbative QCD (up to α_s^5), quark mass corrections (m_q² up to α_s³, m_s⁴ up to α_s²), and QCD condensates; evaluates LO and HO HVP, then combines with QED/EW contributions; compares with lattice QCD results (e.g., BMW20) and assesses systematic consistency.

Result: a_μ^hvp,LO = (7094 ± 37)×10⁻¹¹; Δa_μ^SM = (84.9[90.0] ± 21.3_th ± 14.5_exp)×10⁻¹¹; agreement with BMW20 lattice; ~2.1–3.5σ deviation from experiment; updated a_τ^hvp,LO = (3516 ± 25)×10⁻⁹; Δα_had^(5)(M_Z²) = (2770.7 ± 4.5)×10⁻⁵.

Conclusion: The refined SM prediction reinforces a statistically significant (~2–3.5σ) discrepancy with the experimental muon g−2, sustaining motivation for new physics; however, unresolved tensions among lattice HVP determinations challenge current averaging procedures (e.g., WP25), urging improved lattice precision and independent experimental constraints (MUonE, J-PARC E34).

Abstract: I review the present status of the Standard Model (SM) determination of the muon anomaly by emphasizing the estimate of the lowest hadronic vacuum polarization contributions using recent $e^+e^-$ data on $2\pi$ and $3\pi$ and the inclusive ratio $R^{ee}$ of total cross-sections compiled by PDG to which we add the QCD continuum including HO PT QCD up to $\alpha_s^5$, quark mass $m_q^2$ up to $\alpha_s^3$, $m_s^4$ up to $\alpha_s^2$ and QCD condensates. Adding the $e^+e^-\to \pi^0\gamma + \eta\gamma$ contributions to the CMD3 data, one obtains\,: $a_\mu\vert^{hvp}_{lo}=(7094\pm 37)\times 10^{-11}$. Combining this LO result with the HO estimate of the hadronic, QED and EW contributions, one obtains\,: $\Delta a_\mu^{sm} \equiv (a_\mu^{exp}-a_\mu^{sm})= (86\pm 38_{th}\pm 14.5_{exp})\times 10^{-11} $. The average of the recent estimates quoted in Table\,3 [$\oplus$ attempt to fit all $\,e^+e^-$ data] gives\,: $\Delta a_\mu^{sm} = (84.9[90.0]\pm 21.3_{th}\pm 14.5_{exp})\times 10^{-11}$\,. These results agree with the precise BMW20 lattice one and still suggest a window for new physics beyond the SM of $(2.1\sim 3.5)\,\sigma$. Besides some future projects for improving the $e^+e^-$ data, the MUonE for extracting directly $a_\mu^{tot}\vert^{hvp}_{lo}$ and J-PARC (E34) for an independent measurement of $a_\mu$, it is important to improve the Lattice results shown in Fig.\,9 which (at first sight) disagree each others despite the large errors and may question the reliability of the {\it averages of different averages} used in the White Paper (WP25) Our analysis is completed by updating the estimate of the hadronic contribution to the tau anomaly\,: $a_\tau\vert^{hvp}_{lo}=(3516\pm 25)\times 10^{-9}$ and to $\alpha(M_Z)\,: \,\Delta \alpha_{had}^{(5)} (M_Z^2)= (2770.7\pm 4.5)\times 10^{-5}. $

</details>


### [108] [Heavy double-gluon quarkonium and $\overline{b}ggc$ mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21380)
*G. Daylan Esmer,B. Barsbay,K. Azizi,H. Sundu,S. Türkmen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses QCD sum rules to predict masses and couplings of heavy double-gluon hybrid mesons (cGGc, bGGb, bGGc) with specific quantum numbers, finding mass ranges of ~6.2–6.7 GeV, ~12.0–12.2 GeV, and ~9.2–9.6 GeV respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically predict the spectroscopic properties of heavy double-gluon hybrid mesons — exotic hadrons containing a heavy quark-antiquark pair plus two valence gluons — in order to guide experimental searches and support interpretation of future data in the heavy-quark sector.

Method: Two-point QCD sum rule method; operator product expansion (OPE) up to dimension twelve; Borel analysis with constraints ensuring dominant pole contribution and OPE convergence; current construction for $J^{PC}=0^{++}, 0^{-+}, 1^{+-}, 1^{--}$ (others vanish identically).

Result: Predicted mass ranges: $\bar{c}GGc$: 6.2–6.7 GeV; $\bar{b}GGb$: 12.0–12.2 GeV; $\bar{b}GGc$: 9.2–9.6 GeV; corresponding current couplings extracted for all four non-vanishing channels.

Conclusion: The computed masses and couplings serve as quantitative benchmarks for studying strong decays of double-gluon hybrids and for identifying such states in upcoming experiments, especially at facilities probing the heavy-quark regime.

Abstract: We investigate heavy double-gluon hybrid mesons, built from a heavy quark-antiquark pair and two valence gluons, using the two-point QCD sum rule method. The charmonium-like $\bar{c}GGc$ and bottomonium-like $\bar{b}GGb$ states are considered with $J^{PC}=0^{\pm\pm}$ and $1^{\pm\pm}$, whereas the open-flavor $\bar{b}GGc$ states are classified by $J^{P}=0^{\pm}$ and $1^{\pm}$. The currents with $J^{PC}=0^{+-}, 0^{--}, 1^{++}$, and $1^{-+}$ vanish identically, so sum rules are derived for the remaining four channels. The operator product expansion is carried out up to dimension twelve, and the Borel parameter and continuum threshold are fixed so that the pole contribution dominates and the OPE converges. The resulting masses lie in the ranges $M\simeq 6.2$--$6.7$~GeV for $\bar{c}GGc$, $M\simeq 12.0$--$12.2$~GeV for $\bar{b}GGb$, and $M\simeq 9.2$--$9.6$~GeV for $\bar{b}GGc$, and the current couplings are obtained for all channels. These predictions provide a basis for analyzing the strong decays of double-gluon hybrids and for interpreting future experimental data in the heavy-quark sector.

</details>


### [109] [Black Hole Superradiance and the Gravitational Auger Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18833)
*Joshua W. Foster,Nicholas L. Rodd*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies gravitational interactions within bosonic clouds around spinning black holes—acting analogously to the Auger effect—as a dominant, previously neglected factor that modifies superradiant level evolution, especially for QCD axions; it shows that gravity-driven scalar emission/absorption rapidly depletes the first superradiant level and suppresses higher-mode growth, significantly weakening continuous gravitational-wave signals and altering spin-down constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To account for the omission of purely gravitational interactions in prior superradiance models, which are expected to be non-negligible for ultralight bosons (e.g., QCD axions) due to their irreducible coupling to gravity, and to reassess observational implications—especially for gravitational-wave signals and black hole spin evolution.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical modeling of bosonic cloud dynamics near Kerr black holes, incorporating leading-order gravitational self-interactions (beyond mean-field $\lambda \phi^4$), including scalar emission/absorption by the black hole horizon and level-to-level scattering (including radial overtones); comparison with standard superradiance frameworks assuming only quartic self-interactions.

Result: Gravitational interactions induce rapid depletion of the saturated $n=1$ superradiant level via horizon absorption and scalar radiation; they suppress population transfer to and growth of higher azimuthal ($m$) and radial ($n$) modes; continuous GW emission is strongly suppressed; spin-down for higher modes begins earlier but proceeds more slowly than previously predicted.

Conclusion: Purely gravitational couplings fundamentally alter superradiance phenomenology for ultralight bosons, invalidating previous estimates based solely on $\lambda \phi^4$ interactions; observational searches—particularly for monochromatic GWs from axion clouds—must incorporate these effects, and existing black hole spin limits require revision for higher superradiant levels.

Abstract: Black hole superradiance is a powerful probe of nature's light degrees of freedom. In the vicinity of a spinning black hole, it exponentially amplifies ultralight bosons from vacuum fluctuations to a macroscopic cloud of enormous occupation number. Observational superradiance effects - modified black-hole spins, gravitational-wave and coherent boson emission - are known to depend sensitively on bosonic self-interactions as induced by a quartic, $\lambda \phi^4$. Here we point out that purely gravitational interactions within the cloud significantly modify superradiance dynamics, through an analog of the Auger effect. Moreover, we identify nontrivial evolution of the superradiant levels beyond the first, driven by scattering into even higher levels and their radial overtones. For the QCD axion, the irreducible gravitational coupling rapidly dissipates the first superradiant level after it reaches saturation, through scalar emission and absorption by the black hole, while truncating the growth of higher levels. This can dramatically suppress the observability of continuous gravitational-wave signals or signatures in late binary inspirals. Black hole spin-down remains more robust, but for higher modes begins earlier and proceeds more slowly, requiring revision of existing limits.

</details>


### [110] [Pulsar Timing Correlations Induced by Stochastic Spin-2 Ultralight Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21279)
*Hao-Xuan Shi,Yu-Mei Wu,Chang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives timing-residual cross-correlations in pulsar timing arrays induced by spin-2 ultralight dark matter with general polarization statistics, finding angular correlation signatures nearly degenerate with spin-1 ULDM under equipartition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable gravitational signatures of ultralight dark matter (ULDM) in pulsar timing array (PTA) data, specifically accounting for finite spatial coherence from ULDM velocity dispersion in Galactic halos.

Method: Analytical derivation of timing-residual cross-correlations induced by rapid metric oscillations of spin-2 ULDM, incorporating general polarization statistics and computing the intrinsic angular kernel under equipartition among the five spin-2 polarization modes.

Result: The derived angular kernel contains monopole and quadrupole components and closely resembles that of spin-1 ULDM, indicating a potential observational degeneracy between spin-1 and spin-2 ULDM in PTAs.

Conclusion: Spin-2 ULDM produces PTA correlation signatures that are morphologically similar to spin-1 ULDM under equipartition, complicating model discrimination and necessitating higher-order statistical or spectral analyses to break the degeneracy.

Abstract: Ultralight dark matter (ULDM) can generate time-dependent gravitational perturbations, providing a potential signal for pulsar timing arrays (PTAs). In Galactic halos, the velocity dispersion of ULDM gives rise to finite spatial coherence, which influences the correlations between pulsar timing signals. In this work, we derive the timing-residual cross-correlations induced by rapid metric oscillations of spin-2 ULDM, allowing for general polarization statistics. Under equipartition among the five polarization modes, the intrinsic angular kernel contains monopole and quadrupole components and exhibits an angular profile closely resembling that of spin-1 ULDM, suggesting a potential degeneracy between the two ULDM candidates in PTA observations.

</details>


### [111] [$K_1^*(1870)$ and $3S$-$2D$ mixing in the strange meson sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19498)
*Ting-Yan Li,Zi-Yue Bai,Xiang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the newly observed $K_1^*(1870)$ resonance (with $J^P = 1^-$) in the $K\eta'$ system, proposing it as the lower mass eigenstate of $3S$–$2D$ mixing in the kaon family; its mass and total decay width are consistent with data, a higher-mass partner is predicted, and $Kp$ production is suggested for future experimental verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the newly observed $J^P = 1^-$ strange resonance $K_1^*(1870)$ in the $K\eta'$ channel and determine whether it fits within the conventional kaon meson spectrum, particularly addressing its quantum number compatibility and decay pattern anomalies.

Method: Quark model analysis with $3S$–$2D$ mixing to assign quantum numbers and predict masses; quark pair creation model (QPCM) to compute OZI-allowed strong decays; theoretical study of $Kp \to K_1^* p$ production mechanisms.

Result: The $K_1^*(1870)$ is identified as the lower mixed $3S$–$2D$ state with mass and total width consistent with experiment; a higher-mass partner is predicted; the $K\eta'$ partial width is subdominant in calculation but observed experimentally — indicating tension requiring further scrutiny; $Kp$ reaction is found viable for experimental production.

Conclusion: The $K_1^*(1870)$ can be accommodated as a mixed $3S$–$2D$ kaonic state, supporting its assignment within the conventional meson spectrum, though the unexpected prominence of the $K\eta'$ decay mode suggests possible dynamical or structural subtleties beyond the simple quark model, warranting dedicated experimental and theoretical follow-up.

Abstract: Very recently, the BESIII Collaboration reported the first observation of a $J^P=1^-$ strange resonance in the $K\eta'$ system, the $K_1^*(1870)$. We examine whether this state can be accommodated in the kaon meson family. Although it cannot be assigned as a pure $3^3S_1$ or $2^3D_1$ state, we show that it can be described as the lower state of $3S$-$2D$ mixing, whose mass agrees with the measured value, and we predict its higher-mass partner. Using the quark pair creation model, we calculate the OZI-allowed decay properties of both \textcolor{black}{mixed} states; the total decay width of the lower state is consistent with the measured width of the $K_1^*(1870)$. We note that the subordinate $K\eta'$ decay mode of this state appears to contradict its observed $K\eta'$ decay channel, which calls for further study. We further investigate the production of these two states via the $Kp$ reaction, which may provide valuable information for future experimental searches.

</details>


### [112] [Quark + diquark description of nucleon gravitational form factors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19819)
*Peng Cheng,Zhen-Ni Xu,Dong-Yan Fu,Chen Chen,Zhao-Qian Yao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a Poincaré-covariant quark+diquark Faddeev equation to compute nucleon gravitational form factors (GFFs) — A, J, D, B, and c̄ — without adjustable parameters; it decomposes them into quark/gluon and scalar/axial-vector diquark contributions, identifies key structural origins of B(Q²), validates c̄(Q²) against symmetry constraints, benchmarks against lattice QCD (noting systematic underestimation in D-term and radii due to missing meson cloud effects—especially on gluons), and shows model dependence in predicting negative central energy density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide parameter-free, ab initio predictions of nucleon gravitational form factors (GFFs) within a relativistic bound-state framework, enabling decomposition into quark/gluon and diquark components, and to assess theoretical uncertainties—particularly from omitted meson cloud effects—by comparison with lattice QCD.

Method: Solves the Poincaré-covariant quark+diquark Faddeev equation to compute all five nucleon GFFs (A, J, D, B, c̄); performs species (quark/gluon) and diquark (scalar/axial-vector) decompositions; analyzes B(Q²) via cancellation mechanisms; evaluates spatial densities (e.g., energy density) using two extrapolation schemes; compares D-term and mechanical radii with lattice QCD results.

Result: Scalar diquarks dominate nucleon energy, spin, and mechanical properties; axial-vector diquarks are indispensable; B(Q²) is tiny but nonzero, arising from cancellations in diquark and u/d flavor sectors; c̄(Q²) satisfies fundamental symmetry requirements; D-term and radii are systematically smaller than lQCD values—attributed to gluon-sector sensitivity to missing meson cloud effects; negative central energy density is not robust and depends strongly on extrapolation scheme.

Conclusion: The quark+diquark Faddeev approach yields predictive, symmetry-respecting GFFs with clear dynamical insights, but quantitative discrepancies with lQCD—especially in gluon-sensitive observables—highlight the importance of meson cloud corrections; future improvements must incorporate these effects, particularly for gluon distributions and mechanical properties.

Abstract: We present parameter-free predictions for the nucleon gravitational form factors (GFFs) using a Poincare-covariant quark+diquark Faddeev equation. We provide the full GFFs ($A$, $J$, $D$, and $\bar{c}$) along with their species decompositions into quark and gluon contributions. Based on the diquark breakdown, we find that while the scalar diquark component dominates the nucleon's energy, spin, and mechanical properties, the axial-vector component plays an indispensable role. The GFF $B(Q^2)$ is predicted to be vanishingly small but nonvanishing at finite $Q^2$; its physical origin is deeply rooted in the nucleon' s internal structure through cancellations in both the diquark content and the $u/d$ flavor sectors. Furthermore, our results for $\bar{c}(Q^2)$ confirm the fundamental symmetry requirements of the approach. Compared with lQCD, our calculated $D$-term and various spatial radii are somewhat smaller in magnitude. This systematic underestimation is primarily driven by the gluon sector and can be attributed to the omission of meson cloud effects, suggesting that these effects impact the gluon distribution more significantly than the quark distribution. Finally, our analysis of the energy density based on two extrapolation schemes reveals that the presence of a negative central energy density is highly model-dependent.

</details>


### [113] [The Future Circular Collider as a photon-photon collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20085)
*Patricia Rebello Teles,David d'Enterria*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores photon-photon collisions at the CERN Future Circular Collider (FCC) across all operational modes (e+e−, pp, pPb, PbPb), leveraging equivalent photon approximation to access broad two-photon invariant mass ranges; it highlights exclusive C-even resonance production — especially Higgs boson, ALPs, and GLPs — with enhanced sensitivity beyond current limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the high-luminosity equivalent photon fluxes in FCC’s diverse collision modes for probing exclusive two-photon processes, particularly for C-even resonances inaccessible or poorly constrained in current experiments.

Method: Uses the equivalent photon approximation (EPA) to model quasireal photon fluxes from relativistic charged beams; computes photon-photon luminosities, invariant mass reach, and cross sections for exclusive γγ → X → γγ processes across FCC-ee and FCC-hh configurations.

Result: Demonstrates feasibility of observing Higgs boson via photon fusion in all FCC colliding modes; projects sensitivity to ALPs and GLPs up to ~100 GeV (FCC-ee) and multi-TeV (FCC-hh), significantly surpassing LHC and LEP constraints.

Conclusion: FCC’s photon-photon programme offers unprecedented reach for probing BSM physics through γγ collisions, establishing it as a unique facility for precision studies of Higgs couplings and searches for light/heavy spin-0 and spin-2 resonances coupled to photons.

Abstract: The CERN Future Circular Collider (FCC) offers a unique programme of photon-photon interactions in electron-positron, proton-proton, proton-lead, and lead-lead collisions, by exploiting the high-luminosity equivalent photon fluxes associated with the charged beams. Within the equivalent photon approximation, the electromagnetic fields of relativistic charged particles are described in terms of fluxes of quasireal photons. The complementary photon fluxes, energies, and virtualities available across the FCC operation modes provide access to a broad range of two-photon invariant masses and associated physics measurements. Physics opportunities open to study are highlighted, focusing on the exclusive production of C-even resonances, including the SM Higgs boson and searches for spin-0 axion-like (ALPs) and spin-2 graviton-like (GLPs) particles decaying back into photon pairs. These prospective studies demonstrate the potential to observe photon-fusion production of the Higgs boson for all colliding modes, as well as sensitivity to photon-coupled ALPs and GLPs with masses extending up to the kinematic reach of FCC-ee and into the multi-TeV range at FCC-hh collisions, with significant improvements over existing constraints.

</details>


### [114] [Strong Decays of the $2^-$ Heavy-Light Mesons in the Relativistic Bethe-Salpeter Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20380)
*Jie Yu,Wan-Li Ju,Qiang Li,Tianhong Wang,Guo-Li Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper conducts a relativistic analysis of heavy-light $J^P = 2^-$ mesons (e.g., $D_2$, $B_2$, etc.), computing their mass spectra, $^1D_2$–$^3D_2$ mixing angles, and OZI-allowed strong decays using the instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter (Salpeter) equation for bound states and PCAC/chiral Lagrangian methods for decay amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a consistent relativistic description of orbitally excited ($D$-wave) heavy-light mesons beyond non-relativistic approximations, particularly addressing mixing between $^1D_2$ and $^3D_2$ states and predicting measurable decay widths for experimental comparison.

Method: Solves the full instantaneous Bethe–Salpeter equation to obtain masses and wave functions; constructs wave-function representations directly in $J^P$ basis (not spectroscopic $^{2S+1}L_J$); computes strong decay amplitudes using PCAC + low-energy theorem for $H \to H'P$ and effective chiral Lagrangian for $H \to H'V$; sums OZI-allowed partial widths for total widths.

Result: Predicts mass spectra and $^1D_2$–$^3D_2$ mixing angles for $D_2$, $D'_2$, $D_{s2}$, $D'_{s2}$, $\bar{B}_2$, $\bar{B}'_2$, $\bar{B}_{s2}$, $\bar{B}'_{s2}$; provides estimates of total and partial strong decay widths for all OZI-allowed two-body channels.

Conclusion: The relativistic Salpeter framework successfully describes $2^-$ heavy-light mesons, with predicted masses, mixing angles, and decay widths offering testable benchmarks for lattice QCD and future experiments (e.g., LHCb, Belle II).

Abstract: We present a relativistic study of the mass spectra, the $^1D_2$--$^3D_2$ mixing angles, and the OZI-allowed two-body strong decays of the heavy-light $J^P=2^-$ mesons $D_2$, $D'_2$, $D_{s2}$, $D'_{s2}$, $\bar{B}_2$, $\bar{B}'_2$, $\bar{B}_{s2}$, and $\bar{B}'_{s2}$. The mass spectrum and the meson wave functions are obtained by solving the full instantaneous Bethe--Salpeter (Salpeter) equation, in which the wave-function representations are constructed directly in terms of the $J^P$ quantum numbers rather than the non-relativistic spectroscopic notation ${}^{2S+1}L_J$. For the decay amplitudes we use the partial conservation of the axial-vector current (PCAC) together with the low-energy theorem for the $H\to H'P$ channels, so that the light pseudoscalar meson enters only through its decay constant, and the effective chiral Lagrangian for the $H\to H'V$ channels. The total widths of these mesons are estimated by summing over the partial widths of all OZI-allowed strong decay channels.

</details>


### [115] [Dimension-seven spectator effects in charm-hadron lifetimes at NLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20747)
*Chia-Wei Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes NLO QCD corrections to dimension-seven spectator coefficients in the HQE for charm-hadron widths, fixes finite terms in NDR-$\overline{\rm MS}$ with consistent evanescent operator treatment, finds sizable corrections (up to 10% for $D_s^+$), resolves unphysical negative width predictions, and improves scale dependence—especially for charmed baryons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unphysical predictions (e.g., negative $D_s^+$ semileptonic width) and improve theoretical reliability of heavy-quark expansion (HQE) for charm-hadron lifetimes by including next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to dimension-seven current-current operators.

Method: Computes NLO QCD corrections to connected current-current contributions to dimension-seven spectator coefficients in the HQE; uses NDR-$\overline{\rm MS}$ renormalization scheme with standard Greek projection for evanescent operators, averages over Hermitian-conjugate pairs, and imposes a finite subtraction to eliminate mixing into dimension-six operators; estimates physical impact using tree-level matrix elements (vacuum insertion for $D$ mesons, bag model for charmed baryons) and naive $1/m_c$ expansion for dim-6 matrix element corrections.

Result: NLO corrections are numerically significant: comparable to LO in several channels; generate two new operator families absent at LO; raise $D^+$ width by 0.78 ps$^{-1}$, turning its slightly negative central value positive; contribute ~1% to $D^0$, ~10% to $D_s^+$, ≤2% to singly charmed baryon widths; reduce scale dependence markedly for baryons (whose matrix elements are evolved), but not uniformly for $D$ mesons.

Conclusion: NLO QCD corrections to dimension-seven spectator coefficients are essential for physically consistent and quantitatively reliable HQE predictions of charm-hadron lifetimes—particularly to avoid unphysical results and to stabilize theoretical uncertainties, especially for $D_s^+$ and charmed baryons.

Abstract: We compute the next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections to the connected current-current part of the dimension-seven spectator coefficients in the heavy-quark expansion of charm-hadron widths. The finite terms are fixed in NDR-$\overline{\rm MS}$, with the evanescent operators defined by the standard Greek projection and averaged over Hermitian-conjugate pairs, and with a finite subtraction that sets the finite mixing of the dimension-seven operators into the dimension-six ones to zero. In several channels the one-loop term is comparable to the LO coefficient, and two operator families absent at LO are generated. Since the NLO matrix elements of the dimension-seven operators are not known, we estimate the effect of the coefficients on the widths with tree-level matrix elements from vacuum insertion for $D$ mesons and from the bag model for charmed baryons. The $1/m_c$ corrections to the matrix elements of the dimension-six QCD operators are treated by a naive expansion. Without their $O(\alpha_s)$ part the semileptonic width of the $D_s^+$ would be negative. The NLO dimension-seven correction raises the $D^+$ width by 0.78 ps$^{-1}$, so that its central value, slightly negative as published, becomes positive. At central inputs, it is about 1% of the $D^0$ width, 10% of the $D_s^+$ width and at most 2% of the widths of the singly charmed baryons. Added to the published pole-scheme width, the $D^+$ correction gives $\tau(D^+)=1.41^{+\infty}_{-0.76}$ ps. The NLO term reduces the scale dependence of the nonleptonic dimension-seven contribution severalfold for the singly charmed baryons, whose matrix elements are evolved, but not uniformly for the $D$ mesons.

</details>


### [116] [Inferring the neutral-current neutrino--nucleon cross section from IceCube data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20887)
*Reinaldo Francener,Victor P. Goncalves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper assesses IceCube's sensitivity to the neutral-current (NC) neutrino–nucleon cross section using 12 years of data, finding only an upper limit; IceCube-Gen2 is projected to achieve actual constraints via Earth absorption/regeneration and track-to-cascade ratio.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutral-current neutrino–nucleon interactions remain experimentally unexplored at high energies, unlike charged-current interactions, motivating a dedicated sensitivity study using IceCube data.

Method: Two complementary strategies are employed: (i) modeling neutrino absorption and regeneration as they traverse Earth (energy- and zenith-dependent attenuation), and (ii) analyzing the ratio of track-like (charged-current muon) to cascade-like (neutral-current and charged-current electron/tau) events in IceCube.

Result: With 12 years of IceCube data, only an upper limit on the NC cross section can be set; IceCube-Gen2 is projected to constrain the NC cross section with both methods, owing to its larger effective volume and improved energy/zenith resolution.

Conclusion: Current IceCube data lacks sufficient statistics and resolution to constrain the high-energy neutral-current neutrino–nucleon cross section, but IceCube-Gen2 will enable the first precision measurement using Earth-filtered spectra and event topology ratios.

Abstract: Neutrinos interact only via the weak force, through the exchange of $W^{\pm}$ and $Z^{0}$ bosons in charged-current and neutral-current interactions, respectively. Recent experiments have measured the charged-current cross section at high energies, whereas neutral-current interactions remain largely unexplored experimentally. In this work, we investigate the sensitivity of the IceCube Neutrino Observatory to the neutral-current neutrino--nucleon cross section using 12 years of data. We consider two strategies to constrain this quantity: $(i)$ leveraging neutrino absorption and regeneration as neutrinos traverse the Earth; and $(ii)$ using the track-to-cascade event ratio. We find that current IceCube data can set only an upper limit on the neutral-current neutrino--nucleon cross section. We further show that IceCube-Gen2 should be able to constrain the neutral-current cross section with both methods considered here.

</details>


### [117] [Inclusive Heavy Quarkonium Production in Lepton-Hadron Scattering in a Joint QCD+QED Factorization Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21361)
*Jian-Wei Qiu,Kazuhiro Watanabe,Jia-Yue Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first joint QCD+QED factorization calculation of heavy quarkonium production in electron-proton and electron-nucleus collisions at the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC), incorporating next-to-leading power corrections in quarkonium transverse momentum and matching to NRQCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the EIC's capability for precision studies of quarkonium production in ep and eA collisions as a clean probe of heavy quark pair interactions with nuclear matter and quarkonium formation in nuclei.

Method: A joint QCD+QED factorization framework treating QCD and QED radiation on equal footing; systematic inclusion of next-to-leading power (1/qT) contributions in transverse-momentum expansion; matching to fixed-order NRQCD calculations.

Result: First theoretical predictions for quarkonium production cross sections in both ep and eA collisions at EIC energies, with quantified nuclear medium effects and improved kinematic coverage via power corrections.

Conclusion: The framework enables controlled, high-precision extraction of nuclear modifications to quarkonium formation dynamics, bridging perturbative production and nonperturbative hadronization in a nuclear environment.

Abstract: The Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) will provide an opportunity for precision studies of heavy quarkonium production in electron-proton ($ep$) and electron-nucleus ($eA$) collisions. Comparing production in $ep$ and $eA$ collisions provides a clean, well-controlled experimental environment to study interactions between a heavy quark pair and nuclear matter and the formation of quarkonia in a nuclear medium. In this talk, we present the first calculation of heavy quarkonium production in both $ep$ and $eA$ collisions at EIC energies using a joint QCD+QED factorization framework that treats collision-induced QCD and QED radiation on equal footing, and discuss the role of the nuclear medium in exploring how a physical quarkonium is emerged from a produced heavy quark pair. We also systematically extend our calculation to include next-to-leading power contributions in the inverse power expansion of quarkonium transverse momentum, and discuss the matching of our calculations to fixed-order calculations in the nonrelativistic Quantum Chromodynamics (NRQCD) approach.

</details>


### [118] [Probing the QCD Conformal Window with Four-Quark Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18828)
*Junchen Rong,Emmanuel Stamou,Tom Steudtner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes anomalous dimensions of four-quark and gluonic operators in Veneziano QCD at high loop orders, using conformal expansion and resummation, to probe infrared dynamics and the conformal window; no evidence is found for a composite operator merging with the QCD fixed point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the infrared fate of QCD-like theories and locate their conformal window—critical for understanding phase transitions between conformal and symmetry-breaking phases, with implications across high-energy and condensed-matter physics.

Method: Four-loop computation of anomalous dimensions for all four-quark operators (with/without chiral symmetry) and three-loop computation for the dimension-six gluonic operator in Veneziano QCD; systematic conformal expansion; uncertainty estimation via multiple resummation techniques.

Result: Precise anomalous dimensions across operator sectors; resummed scaling dimensions with quantified uncertainties; absence of any composite operator whose infrared scaling suggests merger with the QCD fixed point.

Conclusion: No robust evidence supports an effective composite operator bridging the conformal regime and the QCD fixed point, implying distinct infrared behavior or a narrower/no overlap of the conformal window with QCD-like dynamics.

Abstract: The infrared fate of QCD-like theories and their conformal window for different numbers or flavours and colour remains an open question with applications ranging from high-energy to condensed-matter systems. A hallmark of phase transitions from a conformal to symmetry breaking phase can be the presence of classically irrelevant operators that provide relevant deformation in the infrared. We investigate this scenario by computing anomalous dimensions of all four-quark operators, with or without chiral symmetry, at four-loop order as well as the dimension-six gluonic operator at three loops in Veneziano QCD. Scaling dimensions are derived in a systematic conformal expansion. These results are improved with various resummation techniques and we estimate their uncertainties. We find no concrete evidence for a composite operator effectively describing a merger with the QCD fixed point.

</details>


### [119] [Electromagnetic cross section of charmonium decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19654)
*X.H.Mo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates electromagnetic cross sections for charmonium decays into fifteen final states in e+e− colliders using a general lS-coupling scheme to construct covariant helicity-coupling amplitudes for two-body decays with arbitrary integer spins; decay rates are converted to collision cross sections, and examples validate the formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a systematic, covariant framework for evaluating electromagnetic cross sections of charmonium decays—key observables for testing QCD dynamics and charmonium spectroscopy—in e+e− colliders.

Method: Adopts a general lS-coupling analysis to construct Lorentz-covariant helicity-coupling amplitudes for two-body decays involving particles with arbitrary integer spins; derives decay rates from these amplitudes and converts them to electromagnetic cross sections via standard collider kinematics.

Result: Analytic expressions for electromagnetic cross sections for fifteen charmonium decay final states; validated through explicit illustrative examples demonstrating consistency and applicability of the formalism.

Conclusion: The lS-coupling–based covariant helicity-amplitude method provides a robust, general, and practical tool for computing electromagnetic cross sections in charmonium decays, facilitating precision tests of perturbative and nonperturbative QCD in e+e− experiments.

Abstract: As an important measurable quantity, the electromagnetic cross sections of charmonium decay in an e+e- collider are valuated for fifteen final states. A general scheme of lS-coupling analysis is adopted for constructing covariant helicity-coupling amplitudes involving two-body decays with arbitrary integer spins. Based on the decay rate, the collision cross section is obtained finally. A number of illustrative examples is worked out to expound and incarnate the general formulas.

</details>


### [120] [Charm quark energy loss fluctuations in clumpy color string medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19789)
*Daria Prokhorova,Shuzhe Shi,Evgeny Andronov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates charm quark energy loss in proton-proton collisions by modeling the medium as overlapping color flux tubes (string clusters), converting local energy density to an effective gluon-gas temperature, and quantifying radiative vs. collisional energy loss — finding radiative loss dominates by ~10×.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand medium-induced charm quark quenching in small systems (pp collisions), where traditional QGP-based models may not apply, and to assess how string-level spatial inhomogeneities affect energy loss mechanisms.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation of charm quark propagation through an ensemble of color flux tubes; superposition of fields to compute local energy density; conversion to effective temperature of ideal gluon gas; calculation of elastic (collisional) and inelastic (radiative) energy loss using multiple energy-density extraction prescriptions.

Result: Radiative energy loss reaches up to ~1.5 GeV, while collisional loss is limited to ~0.15 GeV; both mechanisms are sensitive to spatial inhomogeneities of the medium, but radiative loss dominates; string clustering generates complex local energy density patterns that govern quenching.

Conclusion: Spatial overlap of color flux tubes — forming inhomogeneous string clusters — is the key driver of charm quark energy loss in pp collisions; radiative processes dominate over collisional ones, and modeling the medium at the string level is essential for capturing relevant medium properties.

Abstract: We simulate charm quark propagation through an ensemble of color flux tubes formed between receding color charges in proton-proton collisions. Under the assumption of sharp string boundaries and a localized nature of the overlap regions, high flux tube densities lead to the formation of string clusters. We convert the local energy density of the superposed color field into an effective temperature of an ideal gluon gas and study the dependence of charm energy loss on its spatial inhomogeneities. By comparing different energy-density extraction prescriptions, we show that both elastic and inelastic momentum damping mechanisms are sensitive to the medium properties. However, the collisional channel is an order of magnitude smaller in absolute terms (up to $\sim0.15$~GeV) compared to the radiative loss (up to $\sim1.5$~GeV). Overall, the results demonstrate that the spatial overlap of individual strings generates a complex local energy density pattern, which predominantly governs the medium-induced quenching of charm quarks.

</details>


### [121] [Exploring X17 Couplings through Meson Dalitz Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19831)
*Xuan Wang,Tianhong Wang,SiQi Zhang,Guo-Li Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores X17 boson interpretations of ATOMKI anomalies using meson Dalitz decays, moving beyond pure vector coupling to include axial-vector couplings via Bethe–Salpeter formalism; it identifies viable charm/strange quark coupling regions and predicts a large enhancement in D*+→D+e+e− decay ratio over SM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Resolve tension between ATOMKI's X17 interpretation and existing constraints from D*0→D0e+e− and Ds*+→Ds+e+e− decays by generalizing the X17 coupling structure to include axial-vector components.

Method: Employs Bethe–Salpeter approach to compute hadronic matrix elements for meson Dalitz decays (D*→De+e−), fits X17 vector (g^V) and axial-vector (g^A) couplings to u/d-quark constrained inputs, and scans charm (c) and strange (s) quark couplings to identify allowed parameter regions.

Result: Finds four degenerate best-fit solutions with |g_{c,s}^A| ~ 10^{-3}; one solution is (g_c^V,g_c^A,g_s^V,g_s^A) = (8.32,1.09,-2.62,1.36)×10^{-3}; predicts R^{th}(D*+→D+e+e−) significantly exceeding Standard Model expectation.

Conclusion: Axial-vector couplings are essential to reconcile X17 with meson decay data; the model remains viable and testable via precision measurements of D*+→D+e+e−, offering a distinct signature from pure vector scenarios.

Abstract: The ATOMKI collaboration has reported anomalous $e^+e^-$ pair production in a series of nuclear transitions, which has been interpreted as a possible signature of a new light boson, X17. Meson Dalitz decays provide a complementary probe of the X17 interaction structure. However, the widely studied vector coupling scenario is in tension with the combined constraints from existing $D^{*0}\to D^0e^+e^-$, $D_s^{*+}\to D_s^+e^+e^-$ measurements and nuclear transition analyses. In this work, we investigate the X17 contributions to meson Dalitz decays within a framework incorporating both vector and axial-vector couplings, employing the Bethe--Salpeter approach to calculate the hadronic transition matrix elements. With $u$ and $d$ quark couplings taken from previous analyses, we identify allowed regions for the charm and strange quark couplings, which require the axial-vector coupling strength $|g_{c,s}^A|\sim10^{-3}$. We obtain four degenerate best-fit solutions, one of which is $(g_c^V,g_c^A,g_s^V,g_s^A)=(8.32,1.09,-2.62,1.36)\times10^{-3}$, and estimate the decay ratio $R^{\mathrm{th}}(D^{*+}\to D^+e^+e^-)$, which significantly exceeds the Standard Model prediction.

</details>


### [122] [Lean-Verified No-Go Theorems for $R$-Symmetries and Modular Symmetries in 4D GUTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20386)
*Mu-Chun Chen,Xiang-Gan Liu,Michael Ratz,Alexander Stewart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper formalizes and strengthens a no-go theorem on R-symmetries in 4D supersymmetric SU(5) GUTs using Lean 4, closes a gap in the original proof, and extends implications to modular symmetries—showing they act as R-symmetries in supergravity, leading to light exotic states that threaten gauge coupling unification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously verify and extend the no-go theorem prohibiting discrete Z_M^R (M ≥ 3) or continuous U(1)_R symmetries in supersymmetric 4D SU(5) GUTs that break to the MSSM, while clarifying implications for modular symmetries in supergravity contexts.

Method: Formal verification of the no-go argument using the Lean 4 theorem prover; identification and correction of a gap in the general case of the original proof; analysis of modular symmetries’ R-symmetry nature in supergravity and their linear realization at critical moduli points.

Result: A fully formalized, gap-closed version of the no-go theorem; demonstration that modular symmetries are necessarily R-symmetries in supergravity; proof that exotic states become massless (or parametrically light) at symmetry-preserving critical points in moduli space, endangering gauge coupling unification.

Conclusion: The no-go theorem is robust and formally verified; its scope extends to modular GUT constructions—implying unavoidable light exotics near critical points if R-symmetries are preserved, posing a challenge for viable, unified model building.

Abstract: $R$-symmetries are instrumental in supersymmetric model building. However, a no-go theorem restricts us from using them in a large class of four-dimensional (4D) grand unified theories (GUTs). Specifically, it has been shown that a supersymmetric 4D GUT based on SU(5), with finitely many chiral multiplets, cannot be broken spontaneously to the exact minimal supersymmetric standard model (MSSM) while preserving a discrete $\mathbb{Z}_M^R$, with $M \geq 3$, or continuous U(1) $R$-symmetry. We use Lean 4 to formalize this proof. In the process, we identify and close a gap in the general case of the published argument. Further, we discuss the implications for GUT models with modular symmetries. We point out that modular symmetries are inevitably $R$-symmetries in a supersymmetric framework that also describes gravity. At certain critical points in moduli space, discrete $R$-symmetries are linearly realized. In the context of 4D GUTs, the no-go theorem then implies that exotic states become massless at these points, provided the GUT-breaking vacuum preserves these symmetries. In the vicinity of these critical points, these exotics are parametrically light, which may spoil gauge coupling unification.

</details>


### [123] [General Formulation of Covariant Tensor Amplitudes for Purely Hadronic and Single-photon Radiative Cascade Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20482)
*Yu-chang Sun,Yu-xuan Hu,Chu-cheng Pan,Hao Cai,Liang Sun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified, Lorentz-covariant formalism for partial-wave amplitudes in multi-body hadronic and single-photon radiative cascade decays, ensuring rigorous compliance with Rarita-Schwinger constraints and photon gauge invariance via structured tensor constructions and projection operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unphysical longitudinal/trace contaminations from Lorentz boosts in cascade decays and to enforce strict gauge invariance in radiative transitions—both inadequately handled in conventional non-covariant or ad hoc approaches.

Method: Develops a hierarchical vertex contraction procedure within the covariant L-S tensor formalism; employs intermediate-state cascade bridge projectors P^(J_i)(p_i) and parent-frame spin projectors P^(S_BC)(p_A); constructs gauge-invariant radiative amplitudes using K_μν and S_μν tensors and systematic g_μν → q_μ p_Aν replacements; incorporates Blatt-Weisskopf factors and spectator-index pass-through for photon polarization.

Result: A fully Lorentz-covariant, gauge-invariant amplitude framework that guarantees Rarita-Schwinger symmetry, tracelessness, transversality across frames, and eliminates boost-induced unphysical components in both purely hadronic and γ-radiative cascade decays.

Conclusion: The formalism provides a first-principles, frame-independent foundation for high-precision partial-wave analyses in experimental hadron spectroscopy and radiative transition studies, enabling consistent treatment of spin–orbital dynamics and gauge constraints in complex decay chains.

Abstract: We present a unified method for generating Lorentz-covariant partial-wave amplitudes for multi-body purely hadronic and single-photon radiative cascade decays within the covariant $L$-$S$ tensor formalism. Based on the structural decoupling of external polarization wave functions, complex coupling parameters, and the core covariant amplitude tensor $U$, we establish a hierarchical vertex contraction procedure that explicitly incorporates intermediate-state cascade bridge projection operators $P^{(J_i)}(p_i)$ and parent-frame definite-spin projection operators $P^{(S_{BC})}(p_A)$. This rigorous treatment guarantees the Rarita-Schwinger symmetry, tracelessness, and transversality conditions across different reference frames, fundamentally eliminating the unphysical longitudinal and trace contaminations induced by Lorentz boosts in cascade decays. Furthermore, we formulate a general covariant tensor representation for single-photon radiative decays $A(J_M) \to \gamma + X(J_X)$ that strictly satisfies photon gauge invariance ($q^\lambda V_{\dots\lambda\dots} = 0$) in the Lorenz gauge. Built upon normal- and anomalous-parity fundamental transverse tensors ($K_{\mu\nu}$ and $S_{\mu\nu}$), universal recursive formulas are derived via the systematic replacement $g_{\mu\nu} \to q_\mu p_{A\nu}$, accompanied by exact rules for matching the orbital angular momentum ranks of Blatt-Weisskopf centrifugal barrier factors $B_{L_{\text{rad}}}$ and a spectator index pass-through mechanism for the photon polarization index.

</details>


### [124] [Colours and Flavours: ropes and colour reconnection as string multiplets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20705)
*Christian Bierlich*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a string-based hadronization model incorporating SU(3) colour correlations beyond leading-colour approximation to explain enhanced strange-hadron and charm-baryon yields in high-activity hadronic collisions; it introduces local escape and saturation of breakup force for nuclear collisions, achieving good agreement with light-flavour data in pp and p–Pb, but reveals a significant deficit in Ξc production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To microscopically explain the observed increase in strange-hadron yields with event activity and enhanced charm-baryon fractions—particularly Ξc—in hadronic collisions, by examining how the QCD colour environment influences hadronization.

Method: Extends the Lund string fragmentation framework by incorporating subleading-colour SU(3) correlations; models junction formation and rope hadronization as manifestations of the same colour topology; introduces a local escape criterion and phenomenological saturation of the string breakup force, motivated by multi-string (e.g., three-string) screening via background colour fields.

Result: The unified model successfully reproduces light-flavour hadron ratios (e.g., K/π, p/π, Λ/K⁰ₛ) across pp and p–Pb collisions using common parameters; however, charm-baryon ratios—especially Ξc/D⁰—show a substantial unresolved deficit, indicating limitations in current fragmentation baseline or its activity scaling.

Conclusion: Colour topology and screening in dense partonic environments are essential for describing strangeness and baryon enhancement; while the extended string picture captures light-flavour trends robustly, the persistent Ξc deficit signals the need for dedicated tests of charm fragmentation mechanisms and their modification with event activity.

Abstract: The increase of strange-hadron yields with event activity and the enhanced charm-baryon fractions in hadronic collisions motivate a microscopic examination of how the colour environment affects hadronization. We review and develop the string description of these effects, starting from the Lund fragmentation baseline in electron--positron annihilation and following colour correlations beyond the leading-colour approximation. Few-parton examples show how junction formation and rope hadronization arise from the same SU(3) colour structure, connecting baryon formation and the screening forces that govern strange-quark production. For nuclear collisions, we introduce a local escape criterion and a phenomenological saturation of the breakup force, motivated by a three-string example of screening through background colour correlations. With common settings, the combined prescription gives a satisfactory description of light-flavour ratios in pp and p--Pb and provides O--O predictions. Charm ratios reveal a substantial remaining deficit in $\Xi_c$ production, motivating separate tests of the fragmentation baseline and its extrapolation with activity.

</details>


### [125] [Longitudinal Parton Correlations in MRW-Type Unintegrated Double Parton Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20778)
*R. Kord Valeshabadi,S. Rezaie,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how parton correlations in double parton scattering manifest in unintegrated double parton distributions under MRW-type transverse momentum prescriptions, comparing three constructions (double LO-MRW, virtuality-ordered MRW, modified KMRW) using GS09 evolved inputs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe nontrivial parton correlations inside the proton beyond the factorized product approximation, via unintegrated double parton distributions (uDPDs) that encode transverse momentum dependence.

Method: Starts from non-factorized, scale-evolved GS09 double parton distributions; applies and compares three MRW-type prescriptions—double LO-MRW, virtuality-ordered MRW, and modified KMRW—to generate transverse momentum; analyzes resulting uDPD behavior, especially transverse-momentum dependence and flavor structure.

Result: Reveals distinct transverse-momentum dependencies and flavor correlations across the three MRW prescriptions, demonstrating how correlation information is preserved or modified by each scheme; highlights sensitivity of uDPDs to the choice of TMD evolution and ordering.

Conclusion: The transverse-momentum structure of unintegrated double parton distributions strongly depends on the MRW prescription used, and careful treatment is required to consistently incorporate parton correlations—essential for accurate predictions in double parton scattering at colliders.

Abstract: Double parton scattering offers a way to investigate correlations between partons inside the proton that are absent from the usual product approximation for double parton distributions. In this contribution, we discuss how these correlations enter unintegrated double parton distributions when transverse momentum is generated using MRW-type prescriptions. Starting from non-factorized GS09 distributions evolved to unequal scales, we examine the double LO-MRW, virtuality-ordered MRW, and modified KMRW constructions, focusing on their transverse-momentum dependence and flavor structure.

</details>


### [126] [Cosmological Collider Signals from Fermion Bubble Loops with a Chemical Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20919)
*Chengcheng Han,Zhanhong Lei,Zhehan Qin,Wuzhou Yin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically computes local and nonlocal cosmological collider signals from massive fermion bubble loops during inflation with an effective chemical potential, using de Sitter approximation, CC cutting rules, late-time expansions, and Mellin-Barnes techniques; it identifies two local signal origins, resolves folded singularities via MB reorganization, and shows chemical potential enhances oscillatory amplitude and frequency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute and disentangle local and nonlocal cosmological collider (CC) signals from massive fermionic loops under inflationary chemical potential—a previously unexplored regime with potential observational signatures in primordial non-Gaussianity.

Method: Analytical computation of the inflaton four-point correlator in the single-squeezed limit within de Sitter inflation; employs cosmological cutting rules, late-time expansions, and partial Mellin-Barnes (MB) representations; distinguishes contributions from spectrum/integer poles vs. UV poles; resolves singularities via MB reorganization and validates via independent MB representations.

Result: Closed-form analytical expressions for both local and nonlocal CC signals from fermion bubble loops; identification of two distinct local contributions; cancellation of apparent folded singularities in the three-point limit; convergent double-MB integral for numerical evaluation; quantitative demonstration that chemical potential amplifies oscillatory signal amplitude and increases its logarithmic frequency.

Conclusion: Chemical potential during inflation substantially modifies fermionic CC signals—enhancing detectability and enriching spectral structure—thereby establishing a rigorous analytical framework for future studies, including fermion triangle loops and phenomenological forecasts.

Abstract: We present the first systematic analytical calculation of the local and nonlocal cosmological collider (CC) signals generated by massive fermion bubble loops in the presence of an effective chemical potential during inflation. Working in the de Sitter approximation, we investigate the four-point correlator of inflaton perturbations in the single-squeezed limit. By combining CC cutting rules, late-time expansions, and partial Mellin-Barnes (MB) techniques, we derive analytical expressions for both local and nonlocal signals. In particular, we identify two distinct contributions to the local signal, arising from spectrum and integer poles and from ultraviolet poles of the loop-momentum integral. We further investigate the three-point limit, where the separately extracted local and nonlocal contributions develop apparent folded singularities. By reorganizing the MB representation, we demonstrate that these singularities cancel in the complete signal and obtain a convergent double-MB integral suitable for numerical evaluation. Independent calculations using complementary MB representations provide nontrivial consistency checks of our results. We find that the chemical potential can significantly enhance the oscillatory signal while increasing its logarithmic oscillation frequency. Our analytical results provide a foundation for further studies of chemical-potential-enhanced fermionic CC signals, including fermion triangle loops.

</details>


### [127] [A consistent (3D) EFT expansion of the bubble nucleation rate. Part I: scalar theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20938)
*Luis Gil,Javier López Miras*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a perturbative framework to incorporate higher-dimensional operators (HDOs) into bubble nucleation calculations in scalar high-temperature effective field theories, ensuring gauge-invariant bounce solutions and quantifying HDO corrections in moderately strong transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise predictions of bubble nucleation are crucial for linking cosmological phase transitions to observational signatures; existing methods lack a systematic, fully perturbative treatment of HDO effects on the bounce and its fluctuations.

Method: A general, fully perturbative framework is constructed within scalar high-temperature effective field theories, consistently including HDOs in both the bounce solution and Gaussian fluctuations—accounting for higher-derivative interactions—and proving statistical rate invariance under field redefinitions while establishing existence conditions for the perturbative bounce.

Result: The framework is validated in a scalar–fermion model, yielding modest but quantifiable HDO corrections for moderately strong transitions; it extends HDO analysis beyond critical thermodynamics into dynamical nucleation.

Conclusion: The work provides a controlled, gauge-invariant, and systematically improvable method to assess HDO impacts on bubble nucleation, laying groundwork for accurate phenomenological predictions of cosmological phase transitions.

Abstract: Precise predictions of bubble nucleation are essential for connecting cosmological phase transitions to their potential observational signatures. In this first paper of a two-part series, we develop a general, fully perturbative framework for incorporating higher-dimensional operators (HDOs) into nucleation calculations in scalar high-temperature effective field theories. Our approach consistently includes their effects on the bounce and the fluctuations around it, including interactions with higher derivatives. We prove that the resulting statistical rate is invariant under field redefinitions, and establish sufficient conditions for the existence of the perturbative bounce solution in the presence of HDOs. We illustrate the framework by applying it to a model containing a scalar and a fermion, where we find modest HDO corrections in its regime of moderately strong transitions. We thus extend the systematic study of HDO effects beyond critical thermodynamics and provide a controlled method for determining their impact on bubble nucleation.

</details>


### [128] [Probing the QCD critical point through linearized sound wave dispersion and attenuation at finite baryon density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21031)
*Abhishek Paul,Mehedi Kalam,Prabir Kumar Haldar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how sound waves attenuate in baryon-rich QCD matter near the QCD critical end point using Israel-Stewart hydrodynamics and the BEST equation of state; it finds a non-monotonic, peaked threshold wavelength for sound damping near the critical point, leading to suppressed Mach-cone-like correlations and reduced elliptic/triangular flow harmonics—providing a potential experimental signature of critical point proximity in heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify observable signatures of the QCD critical end point in heavy-ion collisions by linking critical fluctuations to measurable hydrodynamic responses—specifically sound attenuation and anisotropic flow suppression.

Method: Linearized Israel-Stewart hydrodynamics coupled to a parameterized BEST equation of state featuring a critical end point; systematic variation of non-universal EoS parameters; computation of sound attenuation length (threshold wavelength) and its impact on linearized anisotropic flow coefficients (v2, v3).

Result: A finite but sharply peaked threshold wavelength for sound attenuation emerges near the critical end point; this enhanced damping reduces linearized elliptic (v2) and triangular (v3) flow harmonics and suppresses Mach-cone-like two-particle correlations.

Conclusion: Non-monotonic beam-energy dependence of sound attenuation scale—and associated suppression of flow harmonics and correlation structures—constitutes a self-consistent phenomenological signature indicating that the system evolution traverses near the QCD critical end point.

Abstract: We investigate the linearized sound-channel response of a finite baryon density Israel-Stewart hydrodynamic system coupled to a parameterized equation of state containing a critical end point. The attenuation of sound is characterized by a threshold wavelength below which sound perturbations are strongly damped. We systematically study the dependence of this threshold scale on the non-universal parameters of the Beam Energy Scan Theory (BEST) equation of state framework. In our present study, the threshold wavelength remains finite but develops a pronounced peak in the vicinity of the critical end point, indicating strong absorption of sound modes on fireball-scale wavelengths. We further estimate the impact of this enhanced attenuation on the linearized anisotropic flow response and find a reduction of the elliptic and triangular flow harmonics near the critical region. Our results establish a phenomenological connection between critical sound attenuation, Mach cone like correlations, and the linearized flow response in baryon rich QCD matter. These results suggest a non-monotonic beam-energy dependence of the sound attenuation scale. Together with a corresponding suppression of Mach cone like correlations and the linearized anisotropic flow response, they may provide a consistent signature that the system trajectory passes close to the critical point of the chosen equation of state.

</details>


### [129] [Suppression, flow, and chemical components of heavy flavor hadrons in relativistic heavy-ion collisions within an improved Langevin framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21161)
*Shu-Qing Li,Wen-Jing Xing,Shanshan Cao,Guang-You Qin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves the Langevin model for heavy quark transport in the QGP by incorporating both perturbative (Yukawa) and non-perturbative (string) interactions, enabling accurate description of D-meson observables (R_AA, v_2) and chemical composition ratios of charm hadrons at RHIC/LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile theoretical predictions of heavy quark energy loss and hadronization with experimental data on nuclear modification and elliptic flow of D mesons, and to probe QGP properties via charm hadrochemistry.

Method: Extended Langevin dynamics with medium-modified drag/diffusion coefficients computed via a potential scattering model combining short-range Yukawa and long-range string potentials; includes realistic charm hadron chemistry and hadronization modeling.

Result: Improved agreement with measured R_AA and v_2 of D mesons; predicted spectrum ratios (e.g., D_s/D^0, Λ_c/D^0) are sensitive to both charm quark thermalization and hadronization mechanisms in QGP.

Conclusion: The hybrid interaction model enhances predictive power for heavy-flavor probes, establishing charm hadrochemistry as a novel diagnostic tool for QGP transport properties and in-medium hadron formation.

Abstract: We improve the Langevin model to incorporate both perturbative and non-perturbative interactions between heavy quarks and the quark-gluon plasma (QGP) in relativistic heavy-ion collisions. The transport coefficient of heavy quarks is evaluated by a potential scattering model in which both the short-range Yukawa interaction and the long-range string interaction are included. The updated model provides the correct thermal equilibrium limit of the heavy quark system with the medium background, and improves the accuracy in describing the nuclear modification factor and elliptic flow coefficient of $D$ mesons observed at RHIC and the LHC. Nuclear modification of different chemical components of charm hadrons, including $D^0$, $D_s^+$, $\Lambda_c^+$, $\Xi_c^0$, and $\Omega_c^0$, is investigated. Their spectrum ratios are found sensitive to not only the hadronization mechanism of charm quarks but also the medium-modified charm quark spectrum in heavy-ion collisions, offering additional constraints on the heavy quark dynamics inside a color-deconfined nuclear medium.

</details>


### [130] [Phenomenology of neutrino mass ordering synergies in reactor, accelerator, and atmospheric neutrino experiments, and the role of the detector response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21252)
*Vanessa Cerrone,Marco Grassi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how neutrino mass ordering (normal vs. inverted) affects the fitted value of Δm²₃₁ across reactor, accelerator, and atmospheric neutrino experiments; it shows that the 'wrong-ordering' Δm²₃₁ solution is a detector-dependent weighted average over spectral bins, lies on near-antidiagonal lines in the Δm²₃₁(NH)–Δm²₃₁(IH) plane, and introduces an intercept-based formalism to quantify inter-experiment synergy for mass ordering determination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the neutrino mass ordering — a fundamental open question — requires combining constraints from multiple experiments; however, when fitting under the wrong ordering, each experiment yields a biased Δm²₃₁ value whose behavior lacks a unified interpretation across experimental types (reactor, accelerator, atmospheric). The paper seeks a general, detector-aware framework to understand and compare these wrong-ordering solutions.

Method: Analytical derivation and phenomenological modeling of the wrong-ordering Δm²₃₁ solution as a weighted average over reconstructed energy (and zenith angle for atmospheric ν) bins, where weights are determined by statistical precision and detector response (e.g., energy resolution, efficiency); application to JUNO (reactor), accelerator (e.g., DUNE), and IceCube DeepCore (atmospheric); use of matter effects (MSW), oscillation probability expansions, and intercept characterization in the Δm²₃₁(NH)–Δm²₃₁(IH) plane.

Result: The wrong-ordering Δm²₃₁ is universally a weighted bin average; solutions lie on lines with slope ≈ −1 in the two-ordering Δm²₃₁ plane, characterized by experiment-specific intercepts; JUNO’s solution reduces to a closed form at an effective energy dependent on resolution; atmospheric ν_μ disappearance above MSW resonance becomes δ_CP–insensitive, shifting dependence to θ_23; IceCube DeepCore’s intercept is robust against δ_CP and θ_23 variations, unlike accelerator experiments.

Conclusion: The weighted bin average formalism unifies the treatment of wrong-ordering solutions across all neutrino oscillation experiments, extends the mass ordering sum rule to atmospheric neutrinos in Earth matter, enables quantitative assessment of synergies via intercept separation, and reveals key detector design implications — especially energy/angle resolution — for mass ordering sensitivity.

Abstract: The neutrino mass ordering is one of the main open questions of neutrino physics, addressed by reactor, accelerator, and atmospheric experiments and by their combination. When the data are fitted under the wrong ordering, each experiment returns its own value of $\Delta m^2_{31}$, its wrong-ordering solution. We show that this solution is a weighted average of the values preferred by the individual bins of the measured event spectra, in reconstructed energy and, for atmospheric neutrinos, also zenith angle, with weights set by their statistical precision. Both the values and the weights depend on the detector response. In the plane of $\Delta m^2_{31}$ fitted under the two orderings, the solutions lie on lines of slope close to $-1$, so that each experiment is described by one intercept. The weighted bin average extends the mass ordering sum rule to any oscillation experiment, including atmospheric neutrinos in Earth matter. It recovers the known closed forms for short-baseline reactors and for accelerators in vacuum, and for JUNO it equals a closed form evaluated at one effective energy, which depends on the energy resolution. Above the MSW resonance the frequency of atmospheric $\nu_\mu$ disappearance becomes nearly independent of $\delta_{CP}$, and the $\nu_\mu$ disappearance solution moves mainly with $\theta_{23}$. The atmospheric intercept depends on the energy and direction resolutions, the energy window, and the event classes, and differs between detectors.
