The synergy of two experiments is determined by the separation of their intercepts relative to their precisions on $|\Delta m^2_{31}|$. With JUNO, this separation depends strongly on $\delta_{CP}$ for an accelerator and is nearly independent of $\delta_{CP}$ and $\theta_{23}$ for IceCube DeepCore using all channels.

</details>


### [131] [Mono-Z Signatures of Vector Dark Matter Detection at Future Electron-Positron Colliders: FCC-ee and ILC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21278)
*Seyed Yaser Ayazi,Saeid Paktinat Mehdiabadi,Rouzbeh Rouzbehi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies mono-Z signatures of vector dark matter (VDM) at future e+e− colliders (FCC-ee and ILC), using Higgs-portal interactions; it performs cut-based analysis with fast simulation and shows high sensitivity, enhanced by ILC beam polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Higgs-portal vector dark matter—where a stable vector boson from an extended U(1) gauge symmetry serves as DM—via mono-Z production at high-luminosity lepton colliders, complementing LHC and direct detection constraints.

Method: Cut-based analysis of mono-Z events (Z → ℓℓ / hadrons) at FCC-ee (√s = 240, 365 GeV) and ILC (√s = 1 TeV); signal generation via MadGraph5, parton showering/hadronization with PYTHIA8, and fast detector simulation with Delphes3; background estimation and significance calculation including beam polarization effects at ILC.

Result: High statistical significance (>5σ) achievable for benchmark VDM masses and couplings; ILC with polarized beams significantly suppresses SM backgrounds (e.g., e+e− → ZH, ZZ), improving discovery reach; FCC-ee offers competitive sensitivity near the Z-pole and WW threshold.

Conclusion: Future e+e− colliders—especially the ILC with polarization—are powerful probes of Higgs-portal vector dark matter via mono-Z, offering complementary, high-precision sensitivity beyond hadron colliders and astrophysical searches.

Abstract: We investigate the mono-$Z$ signature of a vector dark matter (VDM) model at future electron-positron colliders. In this model, a stable vector boson, arising from an extended $U(1)$ gauge symmetry, serves as a viable DM candidate that interacts with the Standard Model (SM) through a Higgs portal. We perform a detailed cut-based analysis for the mono-$Z$ channel at the FCC-ee (with $\sqrt{s}=240$ and $365$ GeV) and the ILC (with $\sqrt{s}=1$ TeV), considering both leptonic and hadronic decays of the $Z$ boson. Signal significances are evaluated for benchmark points using fast event simulation with MadGraph5, PYTHIA8, and Delphes3. Our results demonstrate that future $e^+e^-$ colliders provide excellent sensitivity to Higgs-portal VDM, with the ILC's beam polarization offering an additional handle to suppress SM backgrounds. This study highlights the potential of lepton colliders in probing DM interactions.

</details>


### [132] [Generalized transverse-momentum-dependent parton distributions of a photon and orbital angular momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21289)
*Sujit Jana,Asmita Mukherjee,Sreeraj Nair*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes for the first time the leading-twist, chiral-even, time-even fermion GTMDs of an on-shell real photon at zero skewness and order α, extracting analytic expressions for coefficients 𝒻_A and 𝒢_A that encode canonical fermion orbital angular momentum ℓ_z^f and spin–orbit correlation 𝒞_z^f; it finds ℒ_z^f and 𝒞_z^f satisfy ℒ_z^f = −2𝒞_z^f for helicity +1 in the fermion–antifermion sector, consistent with TMD/GPD projections and one-loop gravitational form factors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate and quantify the canonical fermion orbital angular momentum and spin–orbit correlation—encoded in GTMD coefficients that vanish in standard TMD and GPD projections—by computing them explicitly in a controlled, analytically tractable system: the real photon.

Method: Explicit calculation of leading-twist, time-even, chiral-even fermion GTMDs for an on-shell real photon at zero skewness and order α (QED coupling) using perturbative QED in the fermion–antifermion sector; employs a common transverse regulator independent of x; extracts coefficients 𝒻_A and 𝒢_A multiplying the k_⊥×Δ_⊥ structure; verifies consistency via TMD/GPD projections and energy-momentum tensor matrix elements.

Result: Closed-form analytic expressions for 𝒻_A and 𝒢_A for photon helicity +1; derived relation 𝒞_z^f = −ℓ_z^f/2; verified reproduction of known photon TMDs, photon-to-fermion splitting kernels, and one-loop gravitational form factors F₁, F₂, F₃.

Conclusion: The calculation provides the first analytic benchmark for fermionic orbital angular momentum and spin–orbit correlation in a real photon, confirming their nontrivial GTMD origin and offering a reference for hadronic GTMD studies and spin decomposition frameworks.

Abstract: The coefficients of generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (GTMDs) that encode the canonical fermion orbital angular momentum and spin--orbit correlation do not contribute to the direct transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) and generalized-parton-distribution (GPD) projections. We present, to our knowledge, the first explicit calculation of the leading-twist, time-even, chiral-even fermion GTMDs of an on-shell real photon at zero skewness and order $\alpha$. The coefficients $\mathcal{F}_A$ and $\mathcal{G}_A$, which multiply the $k_\perp\times\Delta_\perp$ structure, yield the fermion orbital angular momentum $\ell_z^f$ and spin--orbit correlation $\mathcal{C}_z^f$. For photon helicity $+1$, both are obtained in closed form in the leading fermion--antifermion sector and obey $\mathcal{C}_z^f=-\ell_z^f/2$ for a common transverse regulator independent of $x$, because the channel with the active fermion's spin anti-aligned with its orbital motion carries three times the integrated weight of the aligned channel. The projections reproduce the known photon TMDs and leading-logarithmic photon-to-fermion kernels, and energy-momentum-tensor overlaps reproduce the known one-loop photon gravitational form factors $F_1$, $F_2$, and $F_3$. Our result provides an analytic benchmark for fermionic orbital and spin--orbit structure in the real photon.

</details>


### [133] [Non-Canonical Q-balls in Polyattractor Inflation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21413)
*Kimberley Siret,Rhys Abercromby,Kit Lloyd-Stubbs,John McDonald*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates the existence of non-canonical Q-balls in supergravity-based polyattractor inflation models—specifically those interpolating between α-attractor T-models and polynomial attractors—and identifies their potential role as stable dark matter candidates when the global U(1) symmetry remains unbroken.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether Q-balls—non-topological solitons carrying conserved charge—can arise in realistic supergravity-motivated inflationary potentials, particularly in polyattractor models that bridge α-attractor T-models and polynomial models, and to assess their cosmological relevance (e.g., dark matter).

Method: Analytical and qualitative analysis of the equations of motion for a complex scalar field with non-canonical kinetic term in supergravity-based polyattractor potentials; examination of Q-ball existence conditions across parameter space, especially near the T-model limit versus more general polynomial-like regimes.

Result: Q-ball solutions exist robustly away from the pure T-model α-attractor limit, but vanish in that limit; they are intrinsically non-canonical due to the inflaton’s curved Kähler geometry; stability analysis shows absolute stability is possible if the global U(1) symmetry is unbroken.

Conclusion: Non-canonical Q-balls are viable in supergravity polyattractor inflation and may serve as long-lived or stable dark matter relics if produced post-inflation and if the underlying U(1) symmetry is preserved.

Abstract: We show that Q-balls can exist in supergravity-based polyattractor inflation models that interpolate between $\alpha$-attractor T-models and polynomial attractor models. We consider both the original polyattractor potential and a natural generalisation. We find that Q-balls solutions exist when the model deviates significantly from the T-model $\alpha$-attractor, whereas in the T-model limit no Q-balls exist. These are non-canonical Q-balls due to the non-canonical kinetic term of the complex inflaton field. Such Q-balls could have significant cosmological consequences if they are produced at the end of inflation. In particular, if the global $\mathrm{U}(1)$ symmetry of the supergravity-based polyattractor model is unbroken, the Q-balls can be absolutely stable and could account for dark matter.

</details>


### [134] [The Skyrme-Dilaton and Nucleon Observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21537)
*Thomas Fischbacher,Roman Zwicky*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Skyrme model by including the σ/f₀(500) meson as a dilaton, improving fits to static nucleon observables and reproducing Fπ, motivated by σ’s role as the lightest scalar QCD resonance and its interpretation as a pseudo-Goldstone boson of broken scale symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The σ/f₀(500) meson is the lightest scalar resonance in QCD—making it a natural extension to pions in the Skyrme model—and recent evidence supports its interpretation as a pseudo-Goldstone boson of spontaneously broken scale symmetry, e.g., via gravitational form factor studies.

Method: The authors extend the classical Skyrme Lagrangian by incorporating the σ-meson as a dilaton field; treat it as a real (not resonant) degree of freedom for static observables; and perform a combined fit to static nucleon properties (e.g., mass, charge radius, axial coupling).

Result: The extended model achieves χ²/dof ≈ 1—a substantial improvement over the original Skyrme model—and correctly reproduces the physical pion decay constant Fπ, confirming consistency with the overall energy scale.

Conclusion: Including the σ-meson as a dilaton significantly enhances the predictive power and phenomenological consistency of the Skyrme model for static baryon properties, reinforcing the relevance of scale symmetry breaking in low-energy QCD effective descriptions.

Abstract: We extend the Skyrme model by incorporating the $\sigma/f_0(500)$-meson as a dilaton. The motivation is twofold: the $\sigma$-meson is the lightest scalar resonance in QCD and is therefore the natural extensions to the pions of the classical Skyrme model, while its interpretation as a pseudo-Goldstone boson of spontaneously broken scale symmetry is supported by recent studies of gravitational form factors and related developments. Although the $\sigma$-meson is a broad resonance, for static observables it can be treated as real particle since its line-shape is not expected to be essential. A combined fit to static nucleon observables yields $\chi^2/\mathrm{dof} $ close to unity, with substantial improvement over the original Skyrme model, while simultaneously reproducing the physical value of $F_\pi$, the overall scale of the model.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [135] [Observables and Symmetries in Causal Fermion System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18821)
*Patrick Fischer,Claudio F. Paganini,Sahil Yadav*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how observables and symmetries interact in causal fermion systems (CFS), showing covariance of position observables under unitary symmetries, deriving Heisenberg-type evolution for static CFS, and linking spacetime isometries to CFS symmetries via the Dirac equation—identifying the spatial Dirac Hamiltonian as the generator of induced unitary symmetry in static spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how physical observables (e.g., position) and spacetime symmetries are consistently realized within the background-independent, non-perturbative framework of causal fermion systems.

Method: Mathematical analysis of symmetry transformations in CFS: (i) derivation of covariance properties of position observables under unitary symmetries; (ii) Heisenberg-picture evolution for static CFS; (iii) construction of CFS from Dirac solutions; (iv) identification of conditions under which spacetime isometries induce CFS symmetries, using the Dirac equation to relate geometric and quantum generators.

Result: Position observables transform covariantly under unitary CFS symmetries; in static CFS, they obey a Heisenberg-type evolution; spacetime isometries induce CFS symmetries iff the Dirac equation intertwines their infinitesimal generators; in static spacetimes, the spatial Dirac Hamiltonian generates the induced unitary symmetry.

Conclusion: The Dirac equation serves as a fundamental bridge between geometric spacetime symmetries and quantum symmetries in CFS, enabling a physically meaningful interpretation of observables and dynamics—even in absence of a preexisting metric or manifold structure.

Abstract: We investigate the interplay of observables and symmetries in the framework of causal fermion systems (CFS). We show that position observables transform covariantly under unitary symmetries and for static CFS we derive their Heisenberg-type evolution. To give these results a geometric interpretation, we establish conditions under which spacetime isometries induce symmetries of CFS constructed from solutions of the Dirac equation. The crucial link is provided by the Dirac equation itself, which relates the infinitesimal generators of the geometric and CFS symmetries. In static spacetimes, this allows us to identify the generator of the induced unitary symmetry with the spatial Dirac Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [136] [Energy-flow bubbles at the Schwarzschild horizon and their Regge-pole clock](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20200)
*Maciej Janowicz,Arkadiusz Orłowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies energy flow patterns (recirculation cells) formed by circularly polarized electromagnetic waves scattering off a Schwarzschild black hole, linking them to interference between background fields and photon-sphere Regge waves; predicts cell births/deaths via a phase condition with π/4, confirmed experimentally; reveals duality-induced linear polarization cores and torus-knot flow topology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the fine structure of near-horizon electromagnetic energy flow under scattering of circularly polarized waves by a Schwarzschild black hole—specifically, the origin, dynamics, and topological properties of recirculation cells in the time-averaged Poynting flux.

Method: Analytical asymptotics including Regge pole theory and parabolic-cylinder approximation at the top of the effective potential barrier; numerical mapping of zero circles (Poynting vector zeros) for κ = ωrₛ/c ≤ 10; exploitation of electromagnetic duality to infer polarization and flow topology.

Result: Identified periodic birth/sliding of zero-circle pairs along the symmetry axis into the horizon; discovered off-axis cells at higher κ; established that cell death occurs exclusively at horizon zeros of the circularly polarized field; verified a π/4 phase condition predicting eight deaths in 6 ≤ κ ≤ 10 to within Δκ = 0.012; revealed linear-polarization cores and torus-knot flow structures inside main-family cells.

Conclusion: The recirculation cells are universal interference features governed by photon-sphere surface waves and horizon boundary conditions; their birth, death, and internal topology are exactly constrained by wave asymptotics and electromagnetic duality—providing a geometric–analytic framework for black-hole wave scattering beyond geometric optics.

Abstract: For a circularly polarized plane wave scattered by a Schwarzschild black hole, the time-averaged energy flow on the side of the horizon facing the wave contains small, sealed recirculation cells. In the main family, pairs of zero circles are born on the axis and slide into the horizon in periodic frequency windows; at higher frequencies further cells appear off the axis. We map the cells for $\kappa=\omega r_s/c\le10$ and show that each is the interference of a background integral with the leading photon-sphere Regge surface wave. A cell can die only at a zero of the circularly polarized field on the horizon, and all sixteen measured deaths are such zeros. A parabolic-cylinder analysis at the top of the potential barrier reduces this to a phase condition with a constant $\pi/4$ and no fitted parameter; recorded before the measurement, it predicted eight deaths in $6\le\kappa\le10$ to within 0.012 in $\kappa$. Electromagnetic duality makes the core of each main-family cell a circle of linear polarization; inside such a cell, flow lines lie on nested tori and close into torus knots of one chirality.

</details>


### [137] [A treatment of d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians with noninteger quantum numbers and their connection to the radial momentum operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18823)
*Ali Bağcı,Zühra Güneş*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a detailed theoretical analysis of Bagci-Hoggan exponential-type orbitals, identifying them as d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians with real-valued quantum numbers; it derives and verifies their radial differential equation, establishes new transformations to noninteger Slater-type orbitals, and computes fundamental quantum information measures and uncertainty products in closed form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish the identification of Bagci-Hoggan orbitals as d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians and extend their theoretical foundation—including differential structure, operator representations, and information-theoretic properties—for noninteger quantum numbers.

Method: Analytical derivation of the radial differential equation from the closed-form orbital expression; verification via power series expansion and first-order matrix formulation using the Green's function of the radial Laplacian; derivation of transformation relations to noninteger Slater-type orbitals; computation of expectation values, uncertainty products (radial and Heisenberg), Shannon entropy, and Fisher information using exact analytical techniques.

Result: The general radial differential equation for real-valued quantum numbers is derived and verified; new closed-form transformations between Bagci-Hoggan and noninteger Slater-type orbitals are obtained; expectation values of position and radial momentum operators are given analytically; radial and Heisenberg uncertainty products, Shannon entropy, and Fisher information are derived in general d-dimensional form.

Conclusion: Bagci-Hoggan orbitals are confirmed as the natural d-dimensional generalization of Coulomb-Sturmians to real quantum numbers, with a fully characterized radial operator structure and complete quantum information framework—enabling broader applications in atomic, molecular, and computational physics.

Abstract: The Bagci-Hoggan exponential-type orbitals, the generalization of Coulomb-Sturmians to noninteger quantum numbers, were recently identified as d-dimensional Coulomb-Sturmians. In the present study, this identification is treated in detail. The radial differential equation for these orbitals is derived directly from their closed form. It represents the general Coulomb-Sturmian differential equation for quantum numbers with real values. The solution is verified through a power series representation and a first-order matrix form based on the Green's function of the radial Laplacian. New transformation relationships between these orbitals and Slater-type orbitals with noninteger principal quantum numbers are established. The radial differential equation is expressed in terms of the radial momentum operator. The expectation values of the position and radial momentum operators are obtained in closed form. The radial and Heisenberg uncertainty products, the Shannon entropy and the Fisher information are derived in general form.

</details>


### [138] [Gravitational and Maxwell--Proca-Type Structures in a Variational Metric-Affine Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19001)
*Oldřich Stolín*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Einstein's nonsymmetric metric-affine variational framework, deriving gravitational equations (Einstein-type), constitutive relations for torsion, and emergent Maxwell–Proca–Lorenz structures—all purely from geometry, without introducing external matter or electromagnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether electromagnetic and gravitational dynamics can emerge jointly from a unified geometric variational principle—specifically Einstein’s nonsymmetric metric-affine theory—without ad hoc matter or field couplings.

Method: Variational calculus on a generally nonsymmetric metric and independent affine connection; irreducible decomposition of torsion; analysis of Euler–Lagrange equations for symmetric and antisymmetric metric parts; derivation of contracted Bianchi identities and their physical interpretations.

Result: Symmetric metric variation yields Einstein-type equations with geometric source terms; antisymmetric variation yields a torsion-based constitutive relation whose contracted four-divergence gives Maxwell-type (Q=0) or Proca-type (Q≠const) vector-field equations; trace of Bianchi identities yields homogeneous Maxwell equations; connection equation implies Lorenz-type condition.

Conclusion: A single metric-affine variational system self-consistently generates coupled gravitational, electromagnetic (Maxwell/Proca), and gauge-condition (Lorenz-type) structures purely geometrically—demonstrating the viability of unified field theory without external fields.

Abstract: We study Einstein's nonsymmetric metric-affine Lagrangian density with a generally nonsymmetric metric and an affine connection treated as independent variational variables. The resulting connection equation belongs to the classical Einstein--Hlavatý--Tonnelat framework, while the unrestricted metric Euler--Lagrange equation is analyzed without a divergence constraint on the antisymmetric metric density. Its symmetric part takes the form of Einstein-type gravitational equations with geometrically induced source terms and reduces to the vacuum Einstein equations in the torsion-free limit. The antisymmetric part becomes a geometric constitutive relation based on the irreducible decomposition of torsion. Its contracted four-divergence yields a general vector-field equation with geometric source and response terms. Under a trace--axial reduction, $Q=0$ gives a source-free Maxwell-type equation, whereas constant nonzero $Q$ gives a Proca-type equation. Independently, a trace of the generalized Bianchi identities yields the homogeneous Maxwell-type pair associated with the torsion trace, while the connection equation implies a Lorenz-type condition. Thus gravitational, constitutive, Maxwell--Proca-type, and Lorenz-type structures arise as compatible consequences of one metric-affine variational system. The antisymmetric dynamical level is generated by the contracted four-divergence of its constitutive level. No independent electromagnetic or matter field is introduced.

</details>


### [139] [On the Power-Law Duality in Wigner-Yang Quantum Mechanics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19246)
*Georg Junker,Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Hooke-Newton duality—linking harmonic and inverse-square potentials—to Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics, a reflection-deformed quantum framework based on Dunkl operators; it shows the duality holds for eigenvalue problems of power-law potentials, with deformation-parameter rescaling (analogous to Langer modification) replacing angular-momentum rescaling, while coupling-constant exchange remains universal across classical, standard quantum, and Wigner-Yang settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the well-known Hooke-Newton duality—and its generalisation to arbitrary power laws—from classical and standard quantum mechanics to the non-standard Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics, thereby exploring structural universality of duality under symmetry deformation.

Method: Analytical derivation and eigenvalue problem mapping: leveraging the reflection-covariant Dunkl operator formalism of Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics, the authors construct explicit duality transformations between harmonic and Coulomb-like potentials, then generalise to arbitrary power-law potentials; they identify and implement a reflection-operator-dependent rescaling of deformation parameters as the analogue of the Langer modification.

Result: The Hooke-Newton duality is rigorously established in Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics; eigenvalue problems for power-law potentials are solved via duality mapping; a novel 'Langer-type' rescaling of the Dunkl deformation parameter—incorporating the reflection operator—is derived; the energy–coupling-constant exchange relation is shown to be invariant across classical, Heisenberg–Schrödinger, and Wigner–Yang frameworks.

Conclusion: Hooke-Newton duality is robust under quantum deformation: its core structure—particularly the coupling-constant exchange—survives in Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics, albeit with a modified implementation (reflection-dependent parameter rescaling instead of angular-momentum rescaling), revealing deep algebraic and spectral continuity across classical, standard quantum, and Dunkl-deformed quantum theories.

Abstract: In this work we first revisit the Hooke-Newton duality and its generalisation to arbitrary power laws in classical and quantum mechanics. We then consider Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics on the real line, which is a deformation of standard Heisenberg-Schrödinger quantum mechanics closely related to Dunkl's differential-difference operator formalism. We consider the corresponding eigenvalue problem for the harmonic potential and a Coulomb-like potential. We observe that the Hooke-Newton duality is also applicable to these deformed quantum systems allowing us to solve the eigenvalue problem of one by mapping it onto the other system. This approach is then generalised to arbitrary power-law potentials in Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics. Whereas in standard quantum mechanics the classical re-scaling of angular momenta has to be applied to Langer-modified quantum angular momenta, Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics requires a rescaling of deformation parameters similar to the Langer-type modification but involving the reflection operator. The energy coupling-constant exchange, however, remains the same in all three cases, classical mechanics, quantum mechanics and Wigner-Yang quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [140] [Absence of binding for the helium anion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19370)
*Hao Liang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the helium anion (He⁻) with three electrons does not form a bound state in the nonrelativistic Coulomb model with infinite nuclear mass and spin-½ electrons — i.e., no stable three-electron ground state exists.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the fundamental question of whether the helium anion supports a bound three-electron state under idealized nonrelativistic quantum electrostatics, clarifying the ionization threshold and stability of negative ions.

Method: Rigorous spectral analysis using decomposition relative to the helium (two-electron) ground state and completion of the square, reducing the problem to a one-dimensional variational inequality.

Result: Proof that the three-electron energy equals the two-electron ground-state energy: $E^{\mathrm{F}}(3,2) = E^{\mathrm{F}}(2,2)$, implying absence of binding and no three-electron bound state.

Conclusion: The helium anion is unbound in this model; the third electron cannot be stably attached — confirming ionization instability at the nonrelativistic level.

Abstract: We study the helium anion in the nonrelativistic Coulomb model with an infinitely massive nucleus and spin-$\frac12$ electrons. We prove the absence of binding, with $E^{\mathrm{F}}(3,2)=E^{\mathrm{F}}(2,2)$ and no three-electron ground state. A decomposition relative to the helium ground state and completion of the square reduce the problem to a one-dimensional variational inequality.

</details>


### [141] [$Q$-operators, cluster mutations, and flavored Toda chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19436)
*Saebyeok Jeong,Taro Kimura,Norton Lee*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs Baxter Q-operators for flavored Toda chains—quantum integrable systems associated with quivers Q_N[F]—proves their commutativity, relates them to transfer matrices and Lax matrices, derives integral kernels and scalar Q-functions via Fourier transform, and connects the construction to 5D N=1 gauge theory BPS quivers and codimension-two defects on S^3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum integrability tools (Baxter Q-operators, cluster mutations) to flavored deformations of q-deformed Toda chains and establish their connections to supersymmetric gauge theory and geometric engineering.

Method: Uses quantum cluster algebra framework: constructs Q-operators as ordered products of Faddeev’s quantum dilogarithms induced by distinguished sequences of quantum cluster mutations; proves commutativity via cluster duality; identifies transfer matrices as shifted quotients of Q-operators; derives integral kernels and computes Fourier transforms; matches quiver data to BPS quivers in 5D N=1 theories.

Result: Q-operators form a commuting family; their shifted quotients yield transfer matrices whose eigenvalues are generating functions of commuting Hamiltonians; transfer matrices equal matrix elements of rank-two local Lax products; integral kernels are obtained and Fourier-transformed into factorized scalar Q-functions; flavor insertions transform canonically under local cluster moves; scalar Q-function matches perturbative partition function of a codimension-two defect on squashed S^3.

Conclusion: The flavored Toda chains admit a complete quantum integrable structure governed by cluster-algebraic Q-operators, unifying integrable systems, quantum dilogarithms, and 5D supersymmetric gauge theory via BPS quiver correspondence and defect partition functions.

Abstract: We construct Baxter $Q$-operators for a family of quantum cluster integrable systems, that we call the flavored Toda chains, associated with quivers $\mathcal{Q}_N[F]$, where $F\subseteq\{2,\dots,N\}$ specifies data of $\vert F \vert$ additional nodes compared to $\mathcal{Q}_N[\varnothing]$ for the ordinary $N$-particle open $q$-deformed Toda chain. A distinguished sequence of quantum cluster mutations yields an ordered product of Faddeev's quantum dilogarithms. We prove that these operators form a commuting family and that their shifted quotients are transfer matrices whose coefficients are commuting Hamiltonians. We identify these transfer matrices with matrix elements of products of explicit local Lax matrices of rank two. Local cluster transformations move flavor insertions along the chain while preserving their ordered flavor parameters and the transfer matrix together with the canonical transformation. We also derive integral kernels for the $Q$-operators and evaluate an associated Fourier transform as a factorized scalar $Q$-function. Finally, we relate the quivers to the BPS quivers of five-dimensional $\mathcal{N}=1$ gauge theories in the decoupling limit and identify the normalized scalar $Q$-function with the perturbative contribution of a canonical codimension-two defect wrapping a squashed three-sphere.

</details>


### [142] [Integrability of the Schrödinger Equation for a Mie-type Potential via the NU Method and Appell-type Hypergeometric Polynomials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19622)
*Kikunga Kasenda Ivan,Omalohoso Tasa Joshua,Likwolo Katamba Stanislas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a formal correspondence between Appell-type hypergeometric polynomials and exact solutions of the radial Schrödinger equation for Mie-type potentials using the Nikiforov-Uvarov method; it identifies bound states via confluent hypergeometric functions U (with quantized N ∈ ℕ) and scattering states via generalized Laguerre functions L, with s-wave scattering reducing to the Appell polynomials ẽ_n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the physical significance of recently introduced Appell-type hypergeometric polynomials ẽ_n by linking them to quantum mechanical solutions—specifically, bound and scattering states of Mie-type potentials—and to provide a unified algebraic framework for both spectral types.

Method: Application of the Nikiforov-Uvarov (NU) method to reduce the radial Schrödinger equation for Mie-type potentials to a hypergeometric differential equation parameterized by an effective principal quantum number N; asymptotic analysis of solutions to distinguish square-integrable (bound) vs. non-normalizable (scattering) behavior; identification of solution families with confluent hypergeometric function of the second kind U and generalized Laguerre function L.

Result: Bound states correspond to U(N, ·) with N ∈ ℕ yielding discrete energy spectrum and Diophantine quantization; scattering states correspond to L(N, ·), and in the l = 0 limit truncate exactly to the Appell-type polynomials ẽ_n, characterizing s-wave scattering.

Conclusion: The Appell-type polynomials ẽ_n are physically realized as exact s-wave scattering solutions for Mie-type potentials, and the NU-based analysis unifies bound and scattering state descriptions within a single hypergeometric framework, thereby defining their full quantum mechanical scope.

Abstract: We establish a formal correspondance between the Appell-type hypergeometric polynomials $\widetilde{e}_n$, rectently introduced in [1], and the exact solutions od the radial Schrödinger equation for a family of Mie-type potentials. Using the Nikiforov-Uvarov (NU) method, we show that the radial equation reduces to an hypergeometric differential equation with a parameter $N$, which plays the role of an effective principal quantum number. A careful analysis of th asymptotic behavior of the solutions reveals a dual structure: the confluent hypergeometric function of the second kind $U$ yields square-integrable bound states, for which the condition $N\in \mathbb{N}$ provides a quantization condition of Diophantine type, and a discrete energy spectrum. In contrast, the generalized Laguerre function $L$, of a certain type, yields non-normalizable scattering states. In the particular case of vanishing angular momentum $(l=0)$, these scattering stetes truncte into the Appell-type polynomial $\widetilde{e}_n$, which thus characterize the s-wave scattering of the Mie-type potential. This work therefore provides a unified algebraic framework for both bound and scattering states, and clarifies the physical scope of the Appell-type polynomials.

</details>


### [143] [High-energy asymptotics for the spectral actions of free particles in thermal equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19648)
*Fabio Ciolli,Francesco Fidaleo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives high-energy asymptotics of the spectral action for free particle gases in thermal equilibrium on compact manifolds (torus and 3-sphere), covering massless, non-relativistic massive, and relativistic massive cases under Bose, Fermi, and Boltzmann statistics; it identifies a novel logarithmic term in odd dimensions for relativistic massive particles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend spectral action analysis to thermal quantum statistical systems—particularly free gases—on compact Riemannian manifolds, filling a gap in the literature where only non-relativistic massive cases were well understood, and to uncover dimension-dependent anomalies (e.g., logarithmic terms) in the relativistic massive regime.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of the spectral zeta function and heat kernel expansion applied to the Laplace–Beltrami operator (and Klein–Gordon operator for relativistic case) on compact manifolds (d-torus, S³); use of Mellin transform techniques, contour integration, and statistical mechanical partition function representations for Bose, Fermi, and Boltzmann distributions.

Result: Exact leading-order and subleading asymptotics of the spectral action in the high-energy (large cutoff Λ) limit: (i) For massless particles on T^d, power-law scaling Λ^d with statistics-dependent coefficients; (ii) For relativistic massive particles on T^d, appearance of a Λ^{d−1} log Λ term when d is odd; (iii) On S³, same logarithmic anomaly occurs for massive relativistic particles, confirming its geometric–relativistic origin rather than boundary-condition artifact.

Conclusion: The logarithmic term in odd-dimensional relativistic massive spectral asymptotics is a universal feature tied to the interplay between relativistic dispersion, curvature-induced mode structure, and spectral geometry—not an artifact of flat-space or boundary conditions—thereby enriching the physical interpretation of spectral actions in quantum statistical field theory on curved backgrounds.

Abstract: We provide the asymptotics for high energy regime of the spectral action associated with the extensive quantity describing the average number of particles of a gas of free particles in thermal equilibrium. We analyse the case of massless particles (the massive non relativistic case being easier and well known) living in a finite volume manifold of arbitrary dimension, and subjected to periodic boundary conditions (i.e. the d-dimensional torus), obeying Bose, Fermi and Boltzmann statistics. We also treat in full generality the case of relativistic massive particles, which is more difficult and presents the appearance of a logarithmic term when the dimension of the underlying manifold is odd. The situation relative to massless and relativistic massive particles living on the 3-sphere is also studied, showing that, even in this case the asymptotics relative to the massive relativistic particles presents an analogous logarithmic term.

</details>


### [144] [Thouless's bandwidth conjecture: a proof by elliptic periods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19723)
*Yongqiang Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper proves Thouless's bandwidth conjecture for the critical rational Harper operator, establishing a universal asymptotic bandwidth scaling involving Catalan's constant and deriving the limiting even coarea density via spectral–geometric analysis, nodal-annulus methods, and rational Fourier identities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously verify Thouless's long-standing conjecture on the asymptotic behavior of the total bandwidth of the critical Harper model at rational fluxes, and to uncover the universal structure governing its spectral geometry.

Method: Combines spectral theory, algebraic geometry (genus-one family construction), nodal-annulus analysis, exact finite-matrix formulas, rational Fourier identities, uniform closed-path bounds, and careful singular matrix limit analysis with explicit normalization and subtraction.

Result: Proof that denominator × total bandwidth → 32G/π uniformly in reduced numerator; identification of universal limit of normalized even coarea density; derivation of period equation via nodal-annulus calculations; extension to all reduced numerators and both denominator parities.

Conclusion: Thouless’s bandwidth conjecture is confirmed for the critical rational Harper operator; the spectral bandwidth and coarea density exhibit universal, geometrically determined asymptotics governed by Catalan’s constant and a compact genus-one algebraic curve.

Abstract: We prove Thouless's bandwidth conjecture for the critical rational Harper operator: the denominator times the total bandwidth tends to $32G/\pi$, uniformly in the reduced numerator, where $G$ is Catalan's constant. We identify a universal limit of the normalized even coarea density. Positive real part and evenness give an integrable endpoint bound, so the local law determines the bandwidth. An exact finite-matrix formula reduces the local law to one normalized period for each fixed odd numerator. Explicit boundary polynomials, point blowups, and intersections determine a compact genus-one family. Nodal-annulus calculations fix its period equation and hence the universal density. An exact rational Fourier identity and uniform closed-path bounds extend the result to all reduced numerators and both denominator parities. The singular matrix limit, including its subtraction and normalization, is proved separately in detail.

</details>


### [145] [Matrix Star-Triangle Relations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20056)
*Rinat Kashaev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a matrix generalization of the star–triangle relation and identifies its solutions relevant to the sl_n chiral Potts models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the star–triangle relation—a key integrability condition in two-dimensional lattice models—to a matrix setting, enabling systematic construction and analysis of higher-rank integrable models such as the sl_n chiral Potts model.

Method: Develops a matrix-valued generalization of the star–triangle relation and constructs explicit solutions using representation-theoretic and algebraic methods tied to the sl_n Lie algebra.

Result: Identifies concrete matrix solutions of the generalized star–triangle relation that underlie the sl_n chiral Potts models, thereby establishing their integrability and providing a unified framework for their Boltzmann weights.

Conclusion: The matrix generalization provides a robust algebraic foundation for integrability in higher-rank chiral Potts-type models and bridges representation theory with exactly solvable lattice statistics.

Abstract: We propose a matrix generalization of the star--triangle relations and identify solutions underlying the $\mathfrak{sl}_n$ chiral Potts models.

</details>


### [146] [The Maxwell Bethe-Sommerfeld problem I: Thin-layer induction and results for planar TE modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20188)
*Habib Ammari,Yu Gao,Jiayu Qiu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces a thin-layer induction framework to construct a single periodic medium supporting infinitely many complete spectral gaps for divergence-form operators (TE-Maxwell), yielding dense counterexamples to the Bethe-Sommerfeld Conjecture and enabling arbitrary prescribed gap Chern number configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disprove the Bethe-Sommerfeld Conjecture for periodic divergence-form operators and, ultimately, for the full 3D Maxwell operator; to achieve precise topological control (Chern numbers) over spectral gaps.

Method: Inductive thin-layer insertion: iteratively embeds a 'seed' medium with a known spectral gap into progressively thinner layers of the unit cell, preserving and proliferating gaps while controlling topological invariants.

Result: Construction of a single periodic medium admitting infinitely many complete spectral gaps; generation of densely many counterexamples to the BSC for divergence-form operators; realization of any prescribed configuration of gap Chern numbers via seed selection.

Conclusion: The thin-layer induction framework is a robust and flexible tool for spectral and topological engineering of periodic elliptic operators, laying the rigorous foundation for disproving the BSC for the full 3D Maxwell system in the sequel.

Abstract: We present a framework for constructing a single medium that supports infinitely many complete spectral gaps for periodic divergence-form operators, which arise from the transverse electric (TE) polarization of Maxwell's equations. The construction proceeds by inductively inserting a seed medium with a prescribed spectral gap into a thin layer of the unit cell. As a result, we obtain densely many counterexamples to the Bethe-Sommerfeld Conjecture (BSC) for divergence-form operators. Moreover, we prove that the thin-layer construction controls the gap topology: by carefully selecting the seed medium, one can realize a single operator with any prescribed configuration of gap Chern numbers. This is the first of two papers that contains all the fundamental ingredients for the thin-layer induction. In the second paper, we will apply this framework to disprove the BSC for the full three-dimensional Maxwell operator.

</details>


### [147] [Energy and Radius Estimates for Molecules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20206)
*Ioannis Anapolitanos,Michael Hofacker*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically proves that ionizing a well-separated mixture of hydrogen and helium atoms requires energy — resolving a long-standing open question — and leverages this result to rigorously derive the van der Waals law for such systems, establish an improved lower bound on the size of stable multi-nuclei molecules, and prove energetic cost of ionizing an H₂ molecule near a conducting half-plane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the decades-old open question of whether ionization of well-separated atoms costs energy — physically intuitive but mathematically unproven — and extend insights to molecular systems where geometry may relax upon ionization, enabling rigorous justification of asymptotic interaction laws (e.g., van der Waals, plate-molecule interactions).

Method: Rigorous analytical methods from quantum many-body theory and spectral analysis; derivation of energy lower bounds using variational principles and stability-of-matter techniques; application of geometric and electrostatic constraints to bound molecular size and ionization energy.

Result: 1) Proof that ionization of a well-separated H–He atomic mixture requires positive energy; 2) Van der Waals law for H–He systems follows unconditionally as a corollary; 3) New, assumption-light lower bound on the minimal diameter of stable multi-nuclei molecules; 4) Proof that ionizing H₂ in presence of a distant perfectly conducting half-plane costs energy.

Conclusion: The fundamental physical intuition — that ionizing isolated neutral systems requires energy — is now mathematically established for H–He mixtures, with consequential improvements in the rigour of intermolecular and molecule–surface interaction asymptotics, and tighter structural constraints on stable molecules.

Abstract: The question of whether ionizing a system of well-separated atoms requires energy has remained open for decades, even though the answer is, from a physical point of view, obviously positive. The analog question for a system of molecules is even more challenging, partly because the molecular geometry is not known a priori and may change when electrons are added or removed. In this work, we investigate and analytically solve the first problem in the special case of a mixture of hydrogen and helium atoms. As a consequence, the van der Waals law for a system of hydrogen and helium atoms, which was proven in [6] assuming our result, now follows as a corollary without assumptions. Moreover, we prove a new lower bound for the `size' of a stable molecule with several nuclei, which improves upon the lower bounds from [1] with even less assumptions. Using among others this lower bound, we prove that it costs energy to ionize a system consisting of an $H_2$ molecule and a perfectly conducting half-infinite plate, provided that they are well-separated. The long-range asymptotics of their interaction energy was analyzed in [3] assuming our result.

</details>


### [148] [Lieb-Robinson Bounds for Electric Quantum Walks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20516)
*Houssam Abdul-Rahman,Christopher Cedzich,Günter Stolz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal Lieb-Robinson bounds for quantum walks under rational electric fields, showing exponentially suppressed propagation velocity with field period and proving equivalence of Lieb-Robinson and asymptotic velocities for shift-coin and split-step walks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify information propagation speed in quantum walks under external electric fields and clarify the relationship between Lieb-Robinson velocity and asymptotic transport velocity.

Method: Analytic continuation of the dispersion relation; bounding its imaginary part; spectral analysis of the Floquet operator; comparison of infimal Lieb-Robinson velocity and asymptotic velocity.

Result: Optimal Lieb-Robinson bounds with exponentially decaying velocity in field period; exact coincidence of infimal Lieb-Robinson and asymptotic velocities for homogeneous shift-coin and split-step walks; exponential tail bound outside the effective light cone; counterexample showing divergence of the two velocities in nonhomogeneous settings.

Conclusion: Lieb-Robinson bounds for electric quantum walks are tightly governed by analytic properties of the dispersion relation; velocity suppression is robust and quantifiable; the equivalence of dynamical velocities is structure-dependent, holding for translation-invariant cases but not generally.

Abstract: We derive Lieb-Robinson type bounds with optimal propagation velocity for shift-coin and split-step quantum walks in rational electric fields. The velocity decays exponentially fast in the period of the field, showing long intervals of suppressed spreading. The proof rests on bounds for the imaginary part of the analytically continued dispersion relation of the electric walk. We show that the infimal Lieb-Robinson velocity coincides with the asymptotic velocity for these walks, while a nonhomogeneous example demonstrates that the two types of velocity need not agree in general. We also obtain an exponential tail estimate on the probability of a walk to lie outside the effective light cone.

</details>


### [149] [Resurgent Analysis of the Single-Orbit Term in the Selberg Trace Formula](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20892)
*Wen Shen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the asymptotic, Borel–Laplace, and Stokes properties of the single-orbit term in the Selberg trace formula on compact hyperbolic surfaces, proving it is 1-Gevrey divergent, recovering it via Laplace integration, linking its Borel singularities to cylinder resonance spectra, and expressing Stokes jumps as convergent sums over resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the resurgent structure—i.e., the interplay between divergent asymptotics, Borel summability, and Stokes phenomena—of individual geodesic orbit contributions in the Selberg trace formula, a fundamental tool in spectral geometry and quantum chaos.

Method: Rigorous asymptotic analysis, Borel–Laplace transform theory on Riemann surfaces, meromorphic continuation of the Borel transform, analysis of Stokes lines and jumps via contour deformation, and spectral characterization of the hyperbolic cylinder.

Result: The single-orbit term admits a uniform 1-Gevrey asymptotic expansion; its Borel transform is meromorphic on the two-sheeted Riemann surface of √ξ with simple poles at cylinder resonances; Laplace integrals converge along almost all directions; Stokes discontinuities are given by exponentially decaying sums over the resonance spectrum.

Conclusion: The resurgent structure of a single closed geodesic orbit’s contribution is fully encoded in the resonance spectrum of the associated hyperbolic cylinder—establishing a precise geometric–spectral correspondence within resurgence theory.

Abstract: We study the large-time asymptotic expansion, the Borel-Laplace transforms and the Stokes phenomenon of the single-orbit term in the Selberg trace formula. By a single-orbit term we mean the contribution from a primitive closed geodesic orbit and its iterations on a compact hyperbolic surface, and its formal large-time expansion is divergent. We prove that the divergent formal series is uniformly $1$-Gevrey asymptotic to the convergent single-orbit term and recover the single-orbit term by a Laplace integral along the positive real axis. Other directions for the Laplace integration are also explored by analyzing the singularities of the Borel transform of this $1$-Gevrey formal series. It turns out that the series obtained by Borel transform is meromorphic on the two-sheeted Riemann surface of $\sqrt{\xi}$, with simple poles corresponding to the resonance spectra of the hyperbolic cylinder. The Laplace transform can be performed along almost every direction on the two-sheeted Riemann surface. Finally, the Stokes structure as the limit of the difference between Laplace integrals along different directions can be computed by a convergent sum of the exponential of resonance spectrum. These results demonstrate the phenomenon that the resurgent structure of a single-orbit term in the Selberg trace formula is reflected by its associated cylinder resonance spectra.

</details>


### [150] [Two-Dimensional Kowalevski Top Problem as the Genus Two Full Jacobi Inversion Problem -Formulation Using Kolossoff Variables-](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21012)
*Kazuyasu Shigemoto*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the Kowalevski top using Kolossoff variables, yielding two explicit equations of motion—contrasting prior work that derived only one explicit equation via Hamiltonian-Jacobi theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a more transparent formulation of the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two-variable (time + auxiliary) Kowalevski top, leveraging known solutions in reverse.

Method: Reverse use of known solutions to the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two-variable Kowalevski top; introduction and systematic application of Kolossoff variables to transform implicit Kowalevski equations into explicit ones.

Result: Derivation of two explicit equations of motion in Kolossoff variables—distinct from the classical Kolossoff approach which yields only one explicit equation and relies on Hamiltonian-Jacobi theory.

Conclusion: The Kolossoff-variable-based reformulation provides greater transparency and structural clarity for the genus two Jacobi inversion problem associated with the two-dimensional Kowalevski top.

Abstract: For the Kowalevski top, we introduce another variable in addition to time variable, and formulate as the two-dimensional model. In this paper, since the results for the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem for the two variables Kowalevski top are already known, we utilize these results in reverse, introducing Kolossoff variables, to derive a more transparent formulation of the genus two full Jacobi inversion problem. Since there are two implicit equations of motion using Kowalevski variables, we derived two explicit equations of motion using Kolossoff variables, which is different from the Kolossoff method to derive only one explicit equation and to use Hamiltonian-Jacobi theory.

</details>


### [151] [The $N$-channel white-noise Schrödinger operator in all three Dyson classes. I. Explicit stationary admittance distribution, density of states and outgoing Kontsevich matrix Airy integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21067)
*Yan V. Fyodorov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an explicit stationary distribution for the matrix Riccati variable (boundary admittance) of an N-channel Schrödinger operator with Gaussian white-noise disorder across all three Dyson classes (β=1,2,4), linking it to Kontsevich-type Airy integrals; it yields exact finite-N results connecting Lifshitz tail and large-N edge regimes, and identifies universal edge profiles governed by Painlevé-I tritronquée solutions—conjectured to apply to random band matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Halperin's scalar solution for disordered 1D systems to multichannel (N-channel) settings across all Dyson symmetry classes, and to unify spectral analysis—especially density of states and Lyapunov exponents—via the stationary distribution of the matrix Riccati (admittance) variable, while uncovering universal edge behavior.

Method: Analytical derivation of the stationary Fokker–Planck equation solution for the matrix Riccati variable under Gaussian white-noise disorder; use of uniform absorption regularization; factorization of normalization into Kontsevich matrix Airy integrals; reduction of β=4 and even-N β=1 cases to unitary orthogonal polynomials and auxiliary recursions; double-scaling analysis at the spectral edge yielding Painlevé-I tritronquée solutions.

Result: Explicit stationary distribution of boundary admittance for all β=1,2,4; exact finite-N formulas bridging Lifshitz tail (with symmetry-dependent prefactors) and collective large-N edge; edge profiles on N^{-4/5} energy scale governed by complex tritronquée solution of Painlevé-I and a linear auxiliary equation; reduction to orthogonal polynomial systems for β=4 and even-N β=1.

Conclusion: The matrix Riccati approach provides a unified framework for spectral and transport properties in disordered multichannel wires; the derived edge universality class—characterized by Painlevé-I tritronquée functions—is conjectured to govern the spectral edge of random band matrices, generalizing known 1D and scalar results.

Abstract: We obtain an explicit stationary distribution of the matrix Riccati variable---the boundary admittance---for an $N$-channel Schrödinger operator with Gaussian white-noise disorder, in all three Dyson symmetry classes $\beta=1,2,4$. Uniform absorption regularizes the Riccati dynamics, and the distribution's normalization factorizes into outgoing Kontsevich matrix Airy integrals. This gives a common route to the density of states and the sum of Lyapunov exponents, extending Halperin's scalar solution to coupled channels. Exact finite-$N$ formulas connect two spectral regimes: the Lifshitz tail, including its symmetry-dependent prefactor, and the collective large-$N$ edge. We reduce the $\beta=4$ and even-$N$ $\beta=1$ problems to unitary orthogonal polynomials and an auxiliary recursion. Their critical double-scaling limit yields edge profiles on the $N^{-4/5}$ energy scale, governed by a complex Painlevé-I tritronquée solution and a linear auxiliary equation. We conjecture that these profiles describe a universal edge regime of random band matrices.

</details>


### [152] [A hyperhamiltonian approach to symmetry of the Dirac equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21090)
*Giuseppe Gaeta,Miguel Angel Rodriguez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the symmetries of the Dirac equation—including its Poincaré and dilation invariance, plus additional 'special' symmetries—in hyperhamiltonian formalism, extending prior work that cast the Dirac equation itself in such terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified geometric description of all symmetries of the Dirac equation—including both fundamental (Poincaré, dilation) and 'special' ones—using hyperhamiltonian structure, building on earlier work casting the equation itself in that framework.

Method: Mathematical symmetry analysis using hyperhamiltonian geometry; specifically, reformulating known symmetries of the Dirac equation within the hyperhamiltonian formalism previously applied to the equation's dynamics.

Result: All symmetries of the Dirac equation—including Poincaré invariance, wavefunction dilation invariance, and additional special symmetries—are successfully expressed in hyperhamiltonian terms.

Conclusion: The hyperhamiltonian framework provides a coherent and geometrically natural language for describing the full symmetry group of the Dirac equation, unifying previously disparate symmetry properties.

Abstract: The Dirac equation is the simplest wave equation invariant under the Poincaré group, and was indeed determined by Dirac precisely by requiring this invariance. Moreover it is invariant under dilations of the wave function, as follows by its linear character. It is known that the Dirac equation possesses, besides these, other symmetries, which in this sense are the special part of the Dirac equation's full symmetry. In previous work we have shown that the Dirac equation can be cast in hyperhamiltonian terms. Here we show that the symmetries can also be described in hyperhamiltonian terms.

</details>


### [153] [Group classification and equivalence of two classes of evolution equations of arbitrary order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21124)
*Vyacheslav M. Boyko,Oleksandra V. Lokaziuk*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper revisits group classification of two classes of (1+1)-dimensional evolution equations, determines their point and contact equivalence groups, extends hodograph transformations to singular points, distinguishes classes via contact symmetry dimensions for n > 2, introduces reciprocal transformations at order three, poses a nonlocal classification problem, and classifies Lie symmetry extensions of the Burgers equation within Galilei-invariant equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify symmetry properties—including point, contact, and nonlocal equivalences—of higher-order evolution equations, resolve gaps in existing classifications (e.g., singular hodograph points), and unify understanding of symmetry structures across related equations like Burgers and generalized diffusion-convection equations.

Method: Lie group analysis: computation of maximal Lie invariance algebras, classification of equivalence groups (point and contact), extension of hodograph transformation to regions where ux = 0, construction of reciprocal transformations for n = 3, and classification of symmetry extensions under Galilei invariance constraints.

Result: Complete description of usual point equivalence groups for both classes; recovery and refinement of known contact equivalences for second-order equations with 7D algebras; distinction of classes for n > 2 via contact symmetry algebra dimensions; explicit reciprocal transformations linking the two classes at third order; formulation of a nonlocal classification problem; full classification of Lie symmetry extensions of the Burgers equation in the Galilei-invariant subclass.

Conclusion: The symmetry structure of these evolution equations is richer and more nuanced than previously recognized—especially regarding equivalence, singularity handling, and nonlocal links—and provides a foundation for further classification of nonlocal symmetries and conservation laws.

Abstract: We revisit the group classification of the $(1{+}1)$-dimensional evolution equations $u_t=F(u_n)$ and $u_t+uu_x=F(u_n)$ of arbitrary order $n\geq2$, where $u_n=\partial_x^n u$, and describe the usual point equivalence groups of the two classes. We recover the known contact equivalences between the three second-order equations with seven-dimensional maximal Lie invariance algebras and extend the hodograph construction to points where $u_x=0$. For $n>2$, the dimensions of the contact symmetry algebras distinguish the two classes. We give reciprocal transformations linking the two classes at order three and formulate a nonlocal classification problem. We also classify the extensions of the Lie invariance algebra of the Burgers equation in a wider class of Galilei-invariant equations.

</details>


### [154] [Precise Analytic Boundaries and Positive Symplectic Paths in the Stability Diagrams of Elliptic Relative Equilibria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21172)
*Jian Wang,Qinglong Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analytically characterizes spectral stability boundaries of elliptic relative equilibria in several N-body configurations (Lagrangian, Euler–Robe, (2+2)-body) by employing periodic and Hermitian Riccati decompositions, yielding explicit curves separating hyperbolic–hyperbolic from complex–saddle spectra, an eccentricity-independent hyperbolicity condition, and a sharp lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand how linear stability of elliptic relative equilibria depends on mass parameter and orbital eccentricity—particularly across classical and generalized N-body systems—where numerical studies are insufficient for precise spectral boundary characterization.

Method: Applies periodic matrix Riccati decomposition to derive analytic stability boundaries; uses Hermitian Riccati factorization to obtain eccentricity-independent sufficient conditions for operator positivity and monodromy hyperbolicity; constructs a finite trigonometric test function to derive an explicit lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve, validated at the circular limit.

Result: Explicit analytic curves separating hyperbolic–hyperbolic and complex–saddle spectra; an eccentricity-independent sufficient condition guaranteeing monodromy hyperbolicity; a sharp lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve matching its value and tangent at zero eccentricity.

Conclusion: The Riccati-based framework provides rigorous, analytic descriptions of spectral boundaries in stability diagrams for multiple N-body families, enabling precise stability classification without reliance on numerics alone.

Abstract: We investigate the dependence of the linear stability of elliptic relative equilibria on the mass parameter and eccentricity. Using a periodic matrix Riccati decomposition, we derive explicit analytic curves separating hyperbolic--hyperbolic and complex--saddle spectra. The construction applies to the Lagrangian, Euler--Robe, and $(2+2)$-body families. A Hermitian Riccati factorization also yields an eccentricity-independent sufficient condition for positivity of the associated operators under every unit-circle boundary condition, and hence for hyperbolicity of the monodromy matrix. Finally, we construct an explicit lower bound for the second antiperiodic degeneracy curve by a finite trigonometric test function. This bound agrees with the value and tangent of the degeneracy curve at the circular limit. Together, these results provide analytic descriptions and estimates for distinct spectral boundaries in the stability diagrams.

</details>


### [155] [Coalescing and annihilating random walks on the ring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21186)
*A.M. Povolotsky,R. Tribe,O.V. Zaboronski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bi-Pfaffian structure for n-point correlation functions of coalescing-annihilating random walks on the 1D periodic lattice ℤ_N under special initial conditions, generalizing the known single-Pfaffian structure on ℤ; it derives and solves kernel equations and identifies the inhomogeneous Bernoulli process as a key solvable initial condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Pfaffian point process characterization—previously established for the same particle system on the infinite line ℤ—to the finite periodic lattice ℤ_N, where translational invariance is broken and new algebraic structures emerge.

Method: Exact combinatorial and algebraic analysis of correlation functions using determinantal/Pfaffian identities; construction of 2×2 matrix kernels and reduction to scalar kernels; derivation and solution of closed evolution equations for the kernels via discrete Fourier analysis and symmetrization techniques.

Result: Proof that n-point correlation functions on ℤ_N admit a bi-Pfaffian representation—as a linear combination of two Pfaffians of 2n×2n matrices built from 2×2 matrix kernels—which reduces to two scalar kernels; explicit derivation and solution of the governing kernel equations; identification of the inhomogeneous Bernoulli initial condition as yielding this bi-Pfaffian form.

Conclusion: The coalescing-annihilating random walk on ℤ_N does not define a Pfaffian point process in general, but its correlations retain a controlled algebraic structure: a bi-Pfaffian form reflecting the interplay between periodicity and particle statistics; this provides a solvable non-equilibrium many-body system with exact correlation solutions beyond the infinite-volume limit.

Abstract: We study correlation functions in the model of coalescing and annihilating random walks on the one-dimensional periodic lattice $\mathbb{Z}_N$. We prove that, for a special class of initial conditions, the $n$-point correlation function can be represented as a linear combination of two Pfaffians of $2n\times 2n$ matrices constructed from $2\times2$ matrix kernels, which in turn can be expressed in terms of two scalar kernels. This is an analogue of the single-Pfaffian form exhibited by correlation functions of the same model on $\mathbb{Z}$, where it serves as a signature of a Pfaffian point process. We derive equations for the kernels and construct their solutions. We also show that a particular example of an initial condition leading to bi-Pfaffian correlation functions is the inhomogeneous Bernoulli point process, which includes a fixed initial configuration as a particular limiting case.

</details>


### [156] [Non-factorisable Yangian Lax matrices through oscillators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21333)
*Rouven Frassek,Henrik Jürgens*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs oscillator realisations of Yangian Lax operators for type A, focusing on non-factorisable operators linked to prime snake modules; it establishes tensor vacuum generation of finite-dimensional skew representations of Y(gl_n), derives vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials, provides a determinant-based dimension formula in terms of highest loop weights, and illustrates the framework via a 21-dimensional example with explicit basis and transfer-matrix identity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop explicit oscillator realisations of Yangian Lax operators—especially non-factorisable ones tied to prime snake modules—and to provide computationally tractable tools (e.g., determinant formulas, vacuum eigenvalues, Drinfeld polynomials) for studying finite-dimensional skew representations of Y(gl_n).

Method: Constructs Lax operators via ordered tensor products of linear oscillator representations; proves that finite-dimensional skew representations are generated by the tensor vacuum; restricts product Lax operators to corresponding submodules; computes vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials using highest loop weight data; derives dimension formula as a determinant of a matrix built from the highest loop weight; verifies with an explicit 21-dimensional example including basis construction and transfer-matrix identity.

Result: A systematic oscillator realisation of Yangian Lax operators for type A; proof of tensor vacuum generation for skew representations; explicit formulas for vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials; determinant formula for representation dimensions in terms of highest loop weights; full illustration on a 21-dimensional prime snake module, including basis and transfer-matrix identity.

Conclusion: Oscillator constructions provide an effective and explicit framework for realising Yangian Lax operators associated with prime snake modules and skew representations; the derived algebraic and combinatorial formulas—including the determinant dimension formula—offer new computational leverage in the representation theory of Y(gl_n).

Abstract: We construct oscillator realisations of Yangian Lax operators in type $A$, with particular interest in non-factorisable operators associated with prime snake modules. Our construction applies to finite-dimensional skew representations of $Y(\mathfrak{gl}_n)$. We prove that these representations are generated by the tensor vacuum in suitably ordered tensor products of oscillator representations. Their Lax operators are obtained by restricting the corresponding products of linear oscillator Lax operators to these submodules. We determine the vacuum eigenvalues and Drinfeld polynomials, and give a determinant formula for the dimensions directly in terms of the highest loop weights. A $21$-dimensional example illustrates the construction, including an explicit basis and a transfer-matrix identity.

</details>


### [157] [Conditioned Reactive-Power Tuning Manifolds in Constrained Inverse Source Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21428)
*Mohamed R. Khodja*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a constrained inverse source problem for time-harmonic Maxwell fields in a spherical region with metamaterial loading, focusing on the 'tuning manifold'—the set of material parameters yielding zero reactive power (self-tuned minimum-energy sources). It establishes differentiability, local manifold structure, and robustness bounds for the complex power functional, and derives a rigorous dipole-based asymptotic reduction in the electrically small regime, validated numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize material parameter sets that enable self-tuned (zero reactive power) minimum-energy sources for prescribed exterior electromagnetic fields—critical for designing passive or near-passive metamaterial-loaded systems with controlled energy exchange.

Method: Functional analysis of the minimum-energy source map and complex power P(θ) for time-harmonic Maxwell fields; implicit function theorem and singularity analysis to establish manifold structure of ℳ = {θ : ℑP(θ) = 0}; asymptotic analysis using vector spherical harmonics and static dipole approximations in the k₀a ≪ 1 regime; numerical continuation to validate tuning curves and conditioning.

Result: Proof of differentiability of P(θ) away from a singular set; generic local manifold structure of the tuning manifold ℳ; quantitative robustness bounds linking parameter perturbations to reactive-power error and distance to ℳ; controlled low-order asymptotic expansion of ℑP(θ) in terms of electric/magnetic dipole profiles with uniform remainder estimates; numerical confirmation of dipole reduction and identification of robust/fragile segments of ℳ.

Conclusion: The tuning manifold ℳ is generically a well-conditioned, differentiable manifold whose geometry and sensitivity can be rigorously characterized both globally (via functional-analytic tools) and locally (via dipole asymptotics), enabling systematic design of self-tuned metamaterial sources in the electrically small limit.

Abstract: We study a constrained inverse source problem for time-harmonic Maxwell fields in a homogeneous isotropic spherical region loaded by a (possibly active) metamaterial with parameter vector $\theta \coloneqq (\varepsilon,\mu)$. For a prescribed exterior field (represented by finitely many vector-spherical-wave coefficients), we consider the unique minimum-energy source that realizes the constraint and analyze its complex power $P(\theta)$. Our focus is the \emph{tuning manifold} $\mathcal{M}=\{\theta:\Im P(\theta)=0\}$, i.e., the set of material parameters for which the minimum-energy source is \emph{self-tuned} in the sense of vanishing reactive power. We prove (i) differentiability of the optimal-source map and of $P(\theta)$ away from a closed singular set, (ii) generic local manifold structure of $\mathcal{M}$ via an implicit-function argument, and (iii) quantitative conditioning/robustness bounds that relate parameter drift in $\theta$ to reactive-power error and distance to $\mathcal{M}$. In the electrically small regime $k_0a\ll 1$, we derive a controlled low-order asymptotic reduction of $\Im P(\theta)$ in terms of static electric and magnetic dipole profiles, with uniform remainder estimates on compact regular parameter sets. This yields local approximations of tuning curves and their conditioning without requiring a closed-form scalar profile. Numerical continuation experiments validate the one-parameter dipole reduction, the mixed-dipole tuning curves, and the identification of robust versus fragile segments of $\mathcal{M}$

</details>


### [158] [Normal ordering of Riemann-Liouville operators and a probabilistic formula for generalized Stirling numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21429)
*Sodbaatar Adiya,Khongorzul Dorjgotov,Bayarmagnai Gombodorj,Uuganbayar Zunderiya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies commutator relations between multiplication by x and Riemann–Liouville fractional integrals/derivatives, links them to generalized Stirling numbers S_{s;h}(n,k), constructs faithful operator representations, derives integral kernels via Dirichlet expectations, and interprets combinatorial coefficients probabilistically using Pólya-type urn models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize the algebraic and combinatorial structures arising from fractional calculus operators—specifically, to understand how multiplication by x interacts with Riemann–Liouville integrals and derivatives of arbitrary real order, and to reveal connections to known combinatorial sequences (e.g., Stirling, Bessel) and probabilistic models (e.g., Dirichlet, Pólya urns).

Method: Analyzes commutators [x, I^α] and [x, D^β], derives normal-ordering expansions for monomials like (xI^α)^n and (D^βx)^n on appropriate function spaces, computes explicit integral kernels using Mellin convolution and beta integrals, identifies kernel expressions as Dirichlet expectations, connects recurrence relations to generalized Stirling numbers S_{s;h}(n,k), and interprets combinatorial coefficients via de Finetti’s theorem and exchangeable urn schemes.

Result: Establishes that [x, I^α] induces the relation uv − vu = hv^s with s = 1 + 1/α; proves that (xI^α)^n and (I^αx)^n have kernels expressible as Dirichlet expectations; obtains a probabilistic formula for S_{s;1}(n,k) (s > 1); shows fractional integrals correspond to Pólya urns and integer-order derivatives to sampling without replacement; recovers Stirling numbers of first/second kind and Bessel numbers as special cases (s = 1, 0, 2); extends results to arbitrary words in x and fractional integrals.

Conclusion: The algebra generated by x and Riemann–Liouville operators of real order admits a faithful representation tied to generalized Stirling numbers and admits a natural probabilistic interpretation via Dirichlet-weighted urn models—unifying fractional calculus, combinatorics, and probability theory.

Abstract: We study products of multiplication by $x$ with Riemann-Liouville fractional integrals and derivatives of any real order. For the integral $I^\alpha$ of order $\alpha$, the commutator with $x$ gives the relation $uv-vu=hv^s$ with $s=1+1/\alpha$. The coefficients of the normal ordering for this relation are the generalized Stirling numbers $S_{s;h}(n,k)$. We show that these operators give a faithful representation of the algebra in which the real powers of $v$ are defined. We expand $(XI^\alpha)^n$ and $(I^\alpha X)^n$ on locally integrable functions for every $\alpha>0$, and $(XD^\beta)^n$ and $(D^\beta X)^n$ on the range of $I^{n\beta}$ for every $\beta>0$. The kernel of $(XI^\alpha)^n$ is an expectation over the Dirichlet distribution. This gives a probabilistic formula for $S_{s;1}(n,k)$, $s>1$.
