By de Finetti's theorem, the formula describes an urn with $n$ colours: fractional integrals correspond to Pólya urns, and derivatives of integer order correspond to drawing without replacement. The urn probabilities are the terms of a product formula that follows from the recurrence. The cases $s=1,0,2$ give the Stirling numbers of both kinds and the Bessel numbers. The same method gives the kernels of all words in $x$ and fractional integrals.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [159] [Nonperturbative lattice simulation of the nucleation rate in thermal first-order phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18794)
*Jing Zhou,Ligong Bian*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: A nonperturbative lattice study computes vacuum decay via bubble nucleation in 3D scalar field theory at finite temperature, combining multicanonical HMC and real-time Langevin dynamics; the continuum-extrapolated rate differs by ~8% from one-loop perturbation theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately compute the nonperturbative vacuum decay rate through bubble nucleation in a finite-temperature quantum field theory, where perturbative methods may break down or lack precision.

Method: Nonperturbative lattice field theory with expanded-ensemble multicanonical hybrid Monte Carlo to sample critical bubble configurations, coupled with real-time Langevin dynamics to evolve bubbles; continuum limit extrapolation performed.

Result: The numerically computed vacuum decay rate extrapolated to the continuum limit deviates by approximately 8% from the one-loop perturbative prediction.

Conclusion: Perturbative calculations of vacuum decay rates in this regime exhibit small but quantifiable systematic deviations from fully nonperturbative results, highlighting the need for lattice-based validation in thermal field theory.

Abstract: We present a nonperturbative lattice study of vacuum decay through bubble nucleation in a three-dimensional real scalar field theory at finite temperature. The vacuum decay rate is computed by combining an expanded-ensemble multicanonical hybrid Monte Carlo approach, which samples the critical bubble configurations, with real-time Langevin dynamics, which follows the subsequent bubble evolution. We extrapolate the nucleation rate to the continuum limit and compare it with the corresponding one-loop perturbative prediction, finding a discrepancy of about \(8\%\).

</details>


### [160] [Spatiotemporal K-Shape: Reconstructing inhomogeneous condensate profiles on the lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19306)
*Keita Horie,Rin Kagami,Chiho Nonaka*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: The paper introduces the Spatiotemporal K-Shape method—a translation- and symmetry-invariant clustering technique for lattice Monte Carlo ensembles—to extract representative real-space shapes of spatially modulated chiral condensates without assuming functional forms; it successfully recovers non-sinusoidal, multi-node profiles and distinguishes shape families in the (1+1)D Gross–Neveu model, complementing traditional two-point correlator analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spatially modulated chiral condensates in dense QCD effective models lack characterization of their real-space structure; conventional two-point correlators discard Fourier phase information, making configuration shapes indeterminate. There is a need for a model-agnostic method to identify and distinguish representative spatial profiles from ensemble data.

Method: Spatiotemporal K-Shape: an extension of K-Shape clustering to periodic lattice fields that aligns configurations modulo translations and optionally global sign/phase before computing shape centroids; uses dynamic time warping (DTW)-based distance and iterative centroid refinement; incorporates discrete chiral symmetry in alignment to handle domain-length fluctuations.

Result: Method recovers distinct known profiles with identical power spectra in controlled tests; applied to (1+1)D Gross–Neveu model with N_f=8, extracts non-sinusoidal condensate profiles with two sign changes at two chemical potentials, and identifies multiple shape families at a third where a single centroid fails; symmetry-aware alignment yields asymmetric centroids without implying ensemble asymmetry.

Conclusion: Spatiotemporal K-Shape provides a robust, assumption-free tool to characterize real-space diversity of inhomogeneous order; it complements correlator-based diagnostics and enables configuration-centric studies of modulated phases relevant to the QCD phase diagram.

Abstract: Spatially modulated chiral condensates can affect the phase structure of dense QCD effective models. Understanding such order requires characterizing its real-space structure as well as detecting a modulation. Spatial two-point correlators retain the ensemble-averaged power spectrum but discard relative Fourier phases, leaving the configuration shapes undetermined in general. We propose the Spatiotemporal K-Shape method to extract representative shapes from lattice Monte Carlo ensembles without assuming a functional form. The method extends K-Shape clustering to periodic lattice fields, aligning configurations modulo translations and, optionally, a global sign or phase before extracting cluster centroids. Comparisons across clusterings assess whether one representative shape suffices. Controlled tests recover distinct profiles with identical correlators and separate mixtures of different shapes. Applied to the $(1+1)$-dimensional Gross-Neveu model with $N_f=8$, the method extracts non-sinusoidal profiles with two sign changes at two chemical potentials and separates multiple shape families at a third, where a single centroid is insufficient. Including the discrete chiral sign symmetry in the alignment can fold domain-length fluctuations into an asymmetric centroid without implying a preferred ensemble asymmetry. The method thus complements correlator diagnostics with representative real-space shapes and information on their diversity. The application to the Gross-Neveu model provides a testing ground for configuration-based studies of inhomogeneous order relevant to the QCD phase diagram.

</details>


### [161] [Fate of chiral and $U(1)_A$ symmetries in hot QCD matter under background magnetic fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19758)
*Dan Zhang,Heng-Tong Ding,José Javier Hernández Hernández*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates how background magnetic fields affect chiral and $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking in lattice QCD using $(2+1)$-flavor HISQ ensembles; it finds magnetic-field-induced enhancement of symmetry-breaking signals at low $T$, but suppression near the chiral crossover—stronger for chiral than for singlet-axial susceptibility differences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between external magnetic fields and fundamental symmetries—specifically chiral symmetry and the anomalous $U(1)_A$ symmetry—in QCD, especially given magnetic-field-induced flavor splitting due to unequal quark electric charges.

Method: Lattice QCD simulations on fixed-scale $(2+1)$-flavor HISQ ensembles with light-quark mass tuned to $m_l = m_s^{\mathrm{phys}}/10$ ($M_\pi \simeq 220\,\mathrm{MeV}$); analysis of neutral-channel susceptibility differences $\chi_{\pi^0} - \chi_\sigma$ (chiral) and $\chi_{\pi^0} - \chi_{\delta^0}$ ($U(1)_A$) across varying magnetic field strengths and temperatures.

Result: Magnetic fields enhance both $\chi_{\pi^0} - \chi_\sigma$ and $\chi_{\pi^0} - \chi_{\delta^0}$ at low temperatures, but suppress both near the chiral crossover; suppression onset occurs at higher fields and is weaker for the $U(1)_A$-related difference than for the chiral one.

Conclusion: Magnetic fields qualitatively modify the temperature dependence of chiral and $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking: they reinforce breaking at low $T$ but impede restoration near $T_c$, with distinct sensitivity between non-singlet chiral and singlet axial channels—suggesting differing magnetic responses rooted in anomaly structure and flavor charge asymmetry.

Abstract: We summarize our recent lattice QCD study of chiral and singlet $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking in background magnetic fields. The magnetic field explicitly reduces the non-singlet flavor symmetry through the unequal light-quark electric charges. We therefore study two neutral-channel susceptibility differences: $\chi_{\pi^0}-\chi_\sigma$ for chiral symmetry breaking and $\chi_{\pi^0}-\chi_{\delta^0}$ for singlet $U(1)_A$ symmetry breaking. The calculation uses fixed-scale $(2+1)$-flavor HISQ ensembles with $m_l=m_s^{\mathrm{phys}}/10$, corresponding to a zero-field pion mass of $M_\pi\simeq220\,\mathrm{MeV}$. Magnetic fields enhance both susceptibility differences at low temperatures. Near the crossover, sufficiently strong fields instead suppress both differences. The suppression sets in at larger magnetic fields and is milder for the singlet-axial susceptibility difference than for the neutral chiral susceptibility difference.

</details>


### [162] [Canonical partition function and equation of state in the heavy quark region of finite-density lattice gauge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20178)
*Shinji Ejiri,Hideya Hashizume,Masanari Koiida*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper addresses the sign problem in lattice QCD at finite density by using canonical partition function decomposition, particularly for heavy-quark systems; it proposes and tests an algorithm in SU(2) gauge theory, resolves center-symmetry-induced vanishing of odd-sector canonical partition functions via an infinitesimal external field, and finds no softening of the equation of state—consistent with hadron resonance gas behavior at low T.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the severe sign problem in lattice QCD simulations at finite baryon density, especially for heavy quarks, by exploiting canonical partition function decomposition which suppresses phase fluctuations in even-particle sectors and enables controlled treatment of the complex quark determinant.

Method: Decomposes the grand partition function into canonical partition functions labeled by quark number; introduces an infinitesimal external field to lift center-symmetry constraints that cause vanishing of canonical partition functions for non-multiple-of-Nc particle numbers in SU(Nc); implements Monte Carlo simulation for SU(2) heavy-quark effective theory; reconstructs thermodynamics from canonical data.

Result: The sign problem is mitigated in even-particle sectors but remains severe in odd-particle sectors; center symmetry necessitates regularization via infinitesimal field to define canonical partition functions in deconfinement; reconstructed equations of state show hadron-resonance-gas-like chemical-potential dependence at low T and no softening—indicating absence of first-order phase transition or critical point in the explored regime.

Conclusion: Canonical partition function decomposition, combined with center-symmetry breaking regularization, provides a viable framework for finite-density lattice studies in heavy-quark regimes; results suggest robustness of hadronic degrees of freedom and no evidence for exotic dense matter phases (e.g., quarkyonic or color-superconducting) under the conditions probed.

Abstract: We investigate the phase structure and equations of state in lattice gauge theory at finite temperature and finite density, focusing on the canonical partition function obtained by decomposing the grand partition function with respect to particle number. We demonstrate that when quark masses are heavy, the complex phase of the quark determinant, which causes the sign problem, can be controlled by decomposing the grand partition function according to particle number. In this study, we propose an algorithm to calculate the canonical partition function for heavy-quark, high-density effective theory and test it for the first time using one-flavor $SU(2)$ gauge theory, a case where the sign problem arises. When the grand partition function of $SU(2)$ is decomposed into canonical partition functions, the sign problem is serious in odd-particle sectors, while it is mild in even-particle sectors. The odd-particle sectors also expose a fundamental issue: center symmetry makes the canonical partition function of $SU(N_c)$ vanish unless the particle number is an integer multiple of $N_c$. Although appropriate in the confinement phase, this restriction must be treated carefully in the deconfinement phase. We resolve the issue by introducing an infinitesimal external field, calculate the canonical partition functions by Monte Carlo simulation, reconstruct the grand partition function, and determine thermodynamic observables over a wide range of temperature and density. Furthermore, we find that the chemical-potential dependence of the equation of state at low temperatures exhibits behavior characteristic of the hadron resonance gas model, and that no softening of the QCD matter occurs at finite density.

</details>


### [163] [Transferable neural-network preconditioners for Wilson--Clover propagator inversions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20990)
*Shiyi Zhong,Yuanyi Zhang,Jian-Hui Zhang,Dian-Jun Zhao*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper proposes two single-layer neural network architectures—gauge-covariant parallel transport convolution (PTC) and a CNN-like network—to accelerate 4D Wilson–Clover propagator inversions in lattice QCD; both transfer without retraining, and their combination yields further solver iteration reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accelerate computationally expensive four-dimensional Wilson–Clover propagator inversions on practical lattice QCD ensembles, while ensuring generalizability across physical parameters (volume, lattice spacing, pion mass) and avoiding costly retraining.

Method: Two single-layer neural-network architectures: (1) a gauge-covariant parallel transport convolution (PTC) network that directly reduces GCR solver iterations; (2) a CNN-like network integrated with a multigrid-inspired algorithm; both are evaluated for cross-ensemble transferability and combined for synergistic acceleration.

Result: PTC transfers without retraining across lattice volumes, spacings, and pion masses with consistent acceleration; the CNN-like network transfers from small- to large-volume ensembles with strong acceleration; their combination further reduces GCR iterations; overall cost analysis shows net advantage for large-volume ensembles.

Conclusion: Neural-accelerated linear solvers—especially gauge-covariant PTC and transferable CNN-like networks—offer scalable, retraining-free speedups for lattice QCD propagator inversions, with greatest benefit in large-volume regimes.

Abstract: We investigate two single--layer neural-network architectures for accelerating four-dimensional Wilson--Clover propagator inversions on lattice ensembles in practical use; both transfer directly without retraining. The first is a gauge-covariant parallel transport convolution (PTC) network that directly reduces the number of generalized conjugate residual (GCR) solver iterations. The second is a CNN-like network used together with a multigrid-inspired algorithm. The two approaches can be combined to further reduce GCR iterations. PTC transfers without retraining across lattice volumes, lattice spacings, and pion masses while maintaining comparable acceleration, whereas the CNN-like network transfers directly from small- to large-volume ensembles with strong acceleration. A cost comparison accounting for both basis construction and solves shows that the proposed scheme is more advantageous for large-volume ensembles than for small-volume ones.

</details>


### [164] [Global analysis of the $ρ$ resonance in lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21112)
*Luka Jevsenak,Luka Leskovec,Stefan Meinel,John Negele,Srijit Paul,Andrew Pochinsky*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a lattice-QCD study of the ρ resonance using a global analysis of four N_f=2+1 ensembles, parametrizing discretization effects in lattice energy levels (not the scattering amplitude), and employing a K-matrix formalism to encode quark-mass dependence; it reports the physical-limit ρ pole position as 744(11)(7) − i 69(7)(3) MeV via model averaging.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reliably extrapolate the ρ resonance pole to the physical quark-mass and continuum limits by disentangling discretization effects from the physical scattering amplitude, thereby reducing systematic uncertainties in lattice QCD determinations of resonant states.

Method: Global coupled-channel analysis of four N_f = 2 + 1 lattice QCD ensembles; discretization effects modeled in lattice energy levels (not in the scattering amplitude); scattering amplitude defined in the continuum using a K-matrix parameterization with explicit light- and strange-quark mass dependence; systematic variation of parameterizations for quark-mass and lattice-spacing dependence; model averaging to assess robustness.

Result: Physical-limit ρ meson pole position: √s_ρ = 744(11)(7) − i 69(7)(3) MeV, where the first uncertainty is statistical and the second is systematic (model dependence).

Conclusion: Parametrizing discretization effects in the spectrum rather than the amplitude improves control over lattice artifacts; the K-matrix approach enables consistent chiral and continuum extrapolation; the resulting ρ pole is compatible with experiment and exhibits reduced model dependence compared to prior approaches.

Abstract: We present a lattice-QCD study of the $\rho$ resonance in which we address the quark-mass and lattice-spacing dependence in a novel way using a global analysis of four $N_f=2+1$ ensembles. We parametrize discretization effects in the lattice energy levels and not in the scattering amplitude, which is defined in the continuum. We describe the amplitude using a $K$-matrix parameterization, which contains the light- and strange-quark mass dependence. By varying the parameterizations of quark-mass and lattice-spacing dependence, we examine the sensitivity of the physical-limit $\rho$ pole position to these choices and obtain the result $\sqrt{s}_\rho = 744(11)(7) - i\,69(7)(3)~\text{MeV}$ from a model average.

</details>


### [165] [Finite temperature Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with matter fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21493)
*Chris Allton,Ed Bennett,Luigi Del Debbio,Ryan C. Hill,Deog Ki Hong,Jong-Wan Lee,C.-J. David Lin,Biagio Lucini,Maurizio Piai,Davide Vadacchino,Alexis Verney-Provatas*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents the first finite-temperature lattice study of Sp(4) gauge theory with two fundamental Wilson-Dirac fermions, aiming to assess its thermal phase transition nature—critical for SIMP dark matter models and gravitational wave predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether Sp(4) gauge theory with fundamental fermions exhibits a first-order thermal phase transition—key for generating detectable stochastic gravitational wave backgrounds in SIMP-type composite dark matter scenarios.

Method: Finite-temperature lattice QCD simulations with fine coupling scans; measurements of Polyakov loop and susceptibility; reweighting and finite-volume analysis; cross-validation on pure Sp(4) Yang-Mills.

Result: Pure Sp(4) Yang-Mills confirms a first-order transition; with moderately light fermions, the system shows crossover behavior; for the largest accessible fermion masses, first evidence of a first-order thermal phase transition is observed.

Conclusion: Sp(4) with heavy fundamental fermions may host a first-order thermal phase transition relevant for GW detection; this motivates large-scale continuum-extrapolated studies of Sp(2N) gauge theories.

Abstract: We perform the first finite-temperature lattice study of the Sp(4) gauge theory coupled to two Wilson-Dirac fermions transforming in the fundamental representation. Its continuum limit plays an important role in many extensions of the Standard Model for particle physics with composite dynamics. For example, it provides a compelling realisation of the strongly interacting massive particle (SIMP) paradigm for dark matter. If realised in nature, it might lead to a first-order thermal phase transition in the early universe, and produce a detectable relic stochastic background of gravitational waves (GW). Assessing whether future experiments are sensitive to the resulting GW power spectrum requires determining the strength of the transition. We scan the parameter space of this lattice theory with a fine mesh of choices of lattice coupling, for two small values of the temporal extent of the lattice, and several choices of spatial volume and fermion mass. We measure the Polyakov loop and its susceptibility on the ensembles generated, implement reweighting, and perform a finite volume analysis, both to classify the nature of the phase transition, and to estimate the critical coupling. We validate our algorithms and analysis procedures on the better understood Sp(4) Yang-Mills theory, by confirming the presence of a first-order phase transition. Upon coupling the theory to matter fields, in the presence of moderately light fermions, the Polyakov loop shows a clear crossover behaviour and does not detect singularities associated with a phase transition. We also uncover the first evidence of a first-order thermal phase transition in the case of the largest fermion masses available. This finding motivates future, large-scale studies aimed at characterising the transition in the continuum limit, for this and closely related theories with Sp(2N) gauge group.

</details>


### [166] [Hadron structure on the 16-cell honeycomb](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21543)
*Sandor Katz,Daniel Nogradi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper extends prior work on non-perturbative QCD simulations using the 4D 16-cell honeycomb lattice, demonstrating reduced cut-off effects and improved determination of hadronic structure functions due to enhanced symmetry—specifically, a larger number of irreducible representations that suppress operator mixing (reducing power divergences) and enable more continuum-like renormalization of twist-2/twist-3 matrix elements at rest.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve lattice QCD determinations of hadronic structure functions by reducing discretization artifacts—particularly power divergences from operator mixing—through a lattice discretization with higher space-time symmetry than the standard cubic lattice.

Method: Systematic group-theoretic analysis of the 16-cell honeycomb lattice symmetry group (Coxeter group B_4), comparing its irreducible representations with those of the cubic lattice (O_h); classification and symmetry-based selection rules for forward/off-forward, polarized/unpolarized, twist-2 and twist-3 hadronic matrix elements; derivation of renormalization properties and allowed moments at zero momentum.

Result: The 16-cell lattice permits determination of more moments of structure functions with continuum-like (i.e., logarithmically divergent only) renormalization and at zero hadron momentum; reduced mixing between operators of different twist/dimension due to stricter symmetry constraints; practical computational gains from increased degeneracy of symmetric states for hadrons at rest.

Conclusion: The 16-cell honeycomb lattice offers superior symmetry properties for lattice QCD studies of hadronic structure, enabling cleaner extraction of partonic observables with diminished cut-off effects and fewer power divergences compared to the cubic lattice.

Abstract: In previous work we started a program of studying non-perturbative QCD on the 4-dimensional 16-cell honeycomb lattice instead of the usually adopted cubic lattice. It was shown that in several observables much smaller cut-off effects are observed, undoubtedly a result of the larger space-time symmetry of this discretization. In the present work further consequences are worked out, relevant for the lattice determination of hadronic structure functions. The key ingredient is the larger set of irreducible representations of the 16-cell honeycomb symmetry group relative to the cubic lattice, leading to more protection from mixing and thus less power divergences. A second ingredient is that more irreducible representations are available for a hadron at rest, which also leads to computational gain in practice. The analysis is given in full detail for both polarized and unpolarized and both forward and off-forward matrix elements of twist-2 and twist-3 operators. It is shown that a larger set of moments can be determined with continuum-like renormalization and at rest, than on the cubic lattice.

</details>
